## XII *Alias* Madame Listorelle / 十二 化名里斯托雷尔太太

===Original===
The woman thus observed marched swiftly away down the deserted street in the direction of the Town Hall at the corner, and Matherfield, after one more searching look at her, dropped the slat of the Venetian blind through which he had been peeping, and turned on his companion. At the same instant he reached a hand for his overcoat and hat.
“Now, Mr. Hetherwick,” he said sharply, “this has got to be a one-man job! There’ll be nothing extraordinary in one man going along the streets to catch an early morning train, but it would look a bit suspicious if two men went together on the same errand and the same track! I’m off after her! I’ll run her down! I’m used to that sort of thing. You go to your chambers and get some sleep. I’ll look in later and tell you what news I have. Sharp’s the word, now!”
He was out of the room and the house within the next few seconds, and Hetherwick, half vexed with himself for having lingered there on a job which Matherfield thus unceremoniously took into his own hands, prepared to follow. Presently he went out into the shabby hall; the man of the house was just coming downstairs, stifling a big yawn. He smiled knowingly when he saw Hetherwick.
“Matherfield gone, sir?” he inquired. “I heard the door close.”
“He’s gone,” assented Hetherwick. “The person he wanted appeared suddenly, and he’s gone in pursuit.”

===Chinese===
就这样被盯着的那女人，快步顺着空荡荡的街道，朝街角市政厅的方向走去。马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）又朝她仔细打量了一眼，便把方才用来窥探的百叶窗窗板放下，转身冲着同伴，与此同时伸手去拿大衣和帽子。
“听着，海瑟维克先生（Mr. Hetherwick），”他语气干脆地说，“这活儿只能一个人干！大清早一个人上街去赶班早车，没什么好奇怪的；可要是两个人搭伙，奔同一桩事、走同一条道，那瞧着就可疑了！我去追她！我准能把她追查出来！这种事我干惯了。你回你的事务室去睡一觉。回头我过来把打探到的消息告诉你。眼下，就一个字——快！”
不出几秒钟，他已出了那间屋子和那所房子。这桩差事就这样被马瑟菲尔德不容分说地一手揽了过去，海瑟维克有些懊恼自己竟在那儿耽搁，于是也准备离开。不多时，他走进那间寒碜的门厅；房东正下楼来，强忍着一个大哈欠。见了海瑟维克，他会意地一笑。
“马瑟菲尔德走了吗，先生？”他问道，“我听见门关上了。”
“走了。”海瑟维克应道，“他要等的人突然露了面，他就追下去了。”

===Original===
The man, a smug-faced, easygoing sort of person, smiled again.
“Rum doings these police have!” he remarked. “Queer job, watching all night through a window. I was just coming down to make you a cup of coffee,” he continued. “I’ll get you one in a few minutes, if you like. Or tea now? Perhaps you’d prefer tea?”
“It’s very good of you,” said Hetherwick. “But to tell you the truth I’d rather get home and to bed. Many thanks, all the same.”
Then, out of sheer good nature, he slipped a treasury note into the man’s hand, and, bidding him good morning, went away. He, too, walked down the street in the direction taken by Lady Riversreade and her pursuer. But when he came to the bottom and emerged into Harrow Road he saw nothing of them, either to left or right. The road, however, was not deserted; there were already workmen going to early morning tasks, and close by the corner of the Town Hall a roadman was busy with his broom. Hetherwick went up to him.
“Did you see a lady, and then a gentleman, come down here, from St. Mary’s Terrace, just now?” he asked. “Tall people, both of them.”

===Chinese===
这人是个面相沾沾自喜、脾性随和的主儿，又笑了笑。
“这些当警察的，行事可真古怪！”他说道，“整宿守着扇窗户往外瞧，多稀罕的差事。我正要下楼去给你们煮杯咖啡，”他接着说，“您要是愿意，几分钟就能得着一杯。还是这会儿来点茶？说不定您更爱喝茶？”
“您真是好心，”海瑟维克说，“不过老实说，我宁可回家上床睡觉。尽管如此，还是多谢您。”
随后，纯粹出于一片好意，他往那人手里塞了一张纸币，道了声早安，便走开了。他也顺着里弗斯雷德夫人（Lady Riversreade）和她的追踪者所走的方向沿街而下。可走到街底、踏上哈罗路（Harrow Road）时，左右都不见他们的影子。不过路上并非空无一人：已有工人赶早去做活了，市政厅拐角近旁，一个扫街人正挥着扫帚忙个不停。海瑟维克走上前去。
“方才您瞧见一位女士、跟着又一位先生，从圣玛丽台地街（St. Mary's Terrace）那边下来了吗？”他问道，“两个人都是高个儿。”

===Original===
The man rested on his broom, half turned, and pointed towards Paddington Bridge.
“I see ’em, guv’nor,” he answered. “Tall lady, carrying a little portmantle. Gone along over the bridge yonder. Paddington station way. And, after her, Matherfield.”
“Oh, you know him, do you?” exclaimed Hetherwick, in surprise.
The man jerked a thumb in the direction of the adjacent police station.
“Used to be a sergeant here, did Matherfield,” he replied. “I knows him, right enough! Once run me in﻿—me an’ a mate o’ mine﻿—for bein’ a bit festive like. Five bob and costs that was. But I don’t bear him no grudge, not me! Thank ’ee, guv’nor.”

===Chinese===
那人拄着扫帚，侧过半个身子，朝帕丁顿桥（Paddington Bridge）一指。
“俺瞧见了，长官。”他答道，“高个儿的太太，拎着个小旅行袋。打那头的桥上过去啦，往帕丁顿车站那个方向。跟在她后头的，是马瑟菲尔德。”
“哦，您认得他？”海瑟维克惊讶地叫道。
那人朝近旁的警察局竖了竖大拇指。
“马瑟菲尔德从前是这一带的巡佐。”他答道，“俺认得他，错不了！有一回把俺抓进去过——俺跟俺的一个伙计——就为闹得有点儿欢了。罚了五个先令外带讼费。可俺不记他的仇，俺才不呢！多谢您呐，长官。”

===Original===
Hetherwick left another tip behind him and walked slowly off towards Edgware Road. The Tube trains were just beginning to run, and he caught a southbound one and went down to Charing Cross and thence to the Temple. And at six o’clock he tumbled into bed, and slept soundly until, four hours later, he heard Mapperley moving about in the adjoining room.
Mapperley, whose job at Hetherwick’s was a good deal of a sinecure, was leisurely reading the news when his master entered. He laid the paper aside, and gave Hetherwick a knowing glance.
“Got some more information last night,” he said. “About that chap I tracked the other day.”
“How did you get it?” asked Hetherwick.
“Put in a bit of time at Vivian’s,” answered Mapperley. “There’s a fellow there that I know. Clerk to the secretary chap, named Flowers. That man Baseverie has a share in the place﻿—sort of director, I think.”

===Chinese===
海瑟维克又留下一点赏钱，缓步朝埃奇韦尔路（Edgware Road）走去。地铁列车刚开班，他赶上一列南行的，坐到查令十字（Charing Cross），再从那里去了内殿（the Temple）。六点钟，他倒头上了床，睡得酣沉，直到四个钟头之后听见马珀利（Mapperley）在隔壁房间里走动。
马珀利在海瑟维克手下当的差，多少算个闲职；主人进来时，他正悠闲地读着新闻。他把报纸搁到一旁，会意地朝海瑟维克瞥了一眼。
“昨晚又弄着了点儿消息。”他说，“是关于前几天我盯梢的那个家伙的。”
“你怎么弄到手的？”海瑟维克问。
“在维维安（Vivian's）那边花了点工夫。”马珀利答道，“那儿有个我认识的伙计，是秘书手底下的办事员，名叫弗劳尔斯（Flowers）。巴斯维里（Baseverie）那人在那地方占着股份——我琢磨着，算是董事之类。”

===Original===
“What time were you at Vivian’s?” inquired Hetherwick. “Late or early?”
“Early﻿—for them,” answered Mapperley.
“Did you see the man there?”
“I did. He was there all the time I was. In and about all the time. But at first he was in what seemed to be serious conversation with a tall, handsome woman. They sat talking in an alcove in the lounge there some time. Then she went off﻿—alone.”
“Oh, you saw that, did you?” said Hetherwick. “Well, I may as well tell you, since you know what you do, that the woman was Lady Riversreade!”

===Chinese===
“你几时在维维安那边的？”海瑟维克问道，“晚还是早？”
“早——照他们的钟点。”马珀利答道。
“你在那儿见着那个人了吗？”
“见着了。我在的那阵子他一直都在，进进出出，前后不离。只是一开头，他正跟一个高个子的标致女人交谈，瞧那样子谈得挺郑重。两人坐在那边休息厅的一个雅座凹间里，谈了好一阵。后来她走了——独自走的。”
“哦，这个你也瞧见了？”海瑟维克说，“好吧，既然你已经知道了这么多，我不妨告诉你：那女人就是里弗斯雷德夫人！”

===Original===
“Oh, I guessed that!” remarked Mapperley. “I figured in that at once. But that wasn’t all. I found out more. That dead man, Hannaford﻿—from what I heard from Flowers﻿—I’ve no doubt whatever that Hannaford was at Vivian’s once, if not twice, during the two or three nights before his death. Anyway, Flowers recognised my description of him﻿—which I’d got, of course, from you and the papers.”
“Hannaford. There, eh?” exclaimed Hetherwick. “Alone?”
“No﻿—came in with this Baseverie. They don’t know him as Dr. Baseverie there, though. Plain Mister. I’m quite sure it was Hannaford who was with him.”
“Did you get the exact dates﻿—and times?” asked Hetherwick.
“I didn’t. Flowers couldn’t say that. But he remembered such a man.”

===Chinese===
“哦，这个我猜着了！”马珀利说，“我一下子就想到这上头了。可还不止呢，我还打听出更多。那个死人，汉纳福德（Hannaford）——据我从弗劳尔斯嘴里听来的——我毫不怀疑，在他死前两三个晚上之内，汉纳福德去过维维安那边一回，兴许还是两回。反正，弗劳尔斯认得出我对他的一番描述——那描述，当然，是从你和报纸上得来的。”
“汉纳福德。上那儿去了，嗯？”海瑟维克叫道，“一个人？”
“不是——是跟这个巴斯维里一道进去的。不过那边的人不知道他是巴斯维里医生（Dr. Baseverie），只称一声普普通通的先生。我敢十分肯定，跟他在一起的就是汉纳福德。”
“确切的日子——还有钟点，你打听着了吗？”海瑟维克问。
“没有。弗劳尔斯说不上来。不过他记得有这么一个人。”

===Original===
“Well, that’s something,” said Hetherwick. He turned into another room and sat down to his breakfast, thinking. “Mapperley, come here!” he called presently. “Look here,” he went on as the clerk came in. “Since you know this Vivian place, go there again tonight, and try to find out if that friend of yours knows anything of a tall man who corresponds to the description of the man whom Hannaford was seen to meet at Victoria. You read Ledbitter’s account of that, given at the inquest?”
“Yes,” replied Mapperley. “But of what value is it? None﻿—for practical purposes! He couldn’t even tell the shape of the man’s nose, nor the colour of his eyes! All he could tell was that he saw a man muffled in such a fashion that he saw next to nothing of his face, and that he was tall and smartly dressed. There are a few tens of thousands﻿—scores, perhaps﻿—of tall, smartly-dressed men in London!”
“Never mind﻿—inquire,” said Hetherwick, “and particularly if such a man has ever been seen in Baseverie’s company there.”
He finished his breakfast, and then, instead of going down to the Central Criminal Court, after his usual habit, he hung about in his chambers, expecting Matherfield. But Matherfield did not come, and at noon Hetherwick, impelled by a new idea, left a message for him in case he called, and went out. In pursuance of the idea, he journeyed once more to the regions of Paddington and knocked at the door of the house wherein he and Matherfield had kept watch on the flats opposite.
The lodging-house keeper opened the door himself and grinned on seeing Hetherwick. Hetherwick stepped inside and nodded at the door of the room which he had left only a few hours before.

===Chinese===
“嗯，这也算得上有收获。”海瑟维克说。他转身进了另一间屋子，坐下来边吃早饭边琢磨。“马珀利，过来！”过了一会儿他喊道。待那办事员进来，他又接着说：“听好，你既然熟悉维维安这个地方，今晚再去一趟，想法子探探你那位朋友，看他对一个高个子男人知道些什么——那人的相貌，正对得上人家瞧见汉纳福德在维多利亚（Victoria）会面的那一位。验尸审讯上莱德比特（Lebitter）交代的那一段，你读过了吧？”
“读过了。”马珀利答道，“可那有什么用处？照实际的用场说，一点儿没有！他连那人鼻子的形状、眼睛的颜色都说不上来！他讲得出的无非是：瞧见一个男人裹得那么严实，那张脸几乎什么都看不见，个子高，穿得时派体面。可伦敦城里高个儿、衣着笔挺的男人，少说也有几万——没准儿还更多！”
“甭管这些——去打听打听。”海瑟维克说，“尤其要问问，可曾有人瞧见这样的男人在那边跟巴斯维里做伴。”
他吃罢早饭，没有照惯常那样去中央刑事法院（Central Criminal Court），而是在事务室里守着，等候马瑟菲尔德。可马瑟菲尔德没有来。到了正午，海瑟维克被一个新念头催动着，留下一句话以防他过来，便出门了。为着盘算这个念头，他再度前往帕丁顿（Paddington）一带，敲响了那所房子的门——先前他和马瑟菲尔德就是在那屋里守望对面公寓的。
出来开门的正是分租房的房东本人，见了海瑟维克便咧嘴一笑。海瑟维克跨进门，朝几小时前才离开的那间屋子的房门点了点头。

===Original===
“I want a word or two with you,” he said. “In private.”
“Nobody in here, sir,” replied the man. “Come in.”
He closed the door on himself and his visitor, and offered Hetherwick a chair.
“I expected you’d be back during the day,” he said, with a sly smile. “Either you or Matherfield, or both!”
“You haven’t seen him again?” asked Hetherwick.

===Chinese===
“我想跟您说一两句话。”他说，“私下里说。”
“这儿没人，先生。”那人答道，“请进。”
他把门关上，把自己和来客关在里头，请海瑟维克坐了。
“我就料到您白天还要回来。”他说着，狡黠地一笑，“不是您，就是马瑟菲尔德，要不就俩人一块儿！”
“您后来没再见着他？”海瑟维克问。

===Original===
“No; he’s not been here,” replied the man.
“Well, I wanted to ask you a question,” continued Hetherwick. “Perhaps two or three. To begin with, have you lived here long?”
“Been here since before these flats were built﻿—and that’s a good many years ago; I can’t say exactly how many,” said the other, glancing at the big block opposite his window. “Twenty-two or three, anyway.”
“Then I dare say you know most of the people hereabouts?” suggested Hetherwick. “By sight, at any rate.”
The lodging-house keeper smiled and shook his head.

===Chinese===
“没有，他没来过。”那人答道。
“是这样，我想问您个问题。”海瑟维克接着说，“兴许有两三个。先说头一件：您在这儿住得久吗？”
“打这些公寓盖起来以前我就住这儿了——那是好些年前的事了；具体多少年我说不上来。”对方答道，眼睛瞟了瞟自家窗外那一片大楼，“反正总有二十二三年的光景。”
“那想必这一带的人您大多认得？”海瑟维克提点道，“至少见面认得出。”
分租房的房东笑了笑，摇了摇头。

===Original===
“That would be a tall order, mister!” he answered. “There’s a few thousand of people packed into this bit of London. Of course, I do know a good many, close at hand. But if you’re a Londoner you’ll know that Londoners keep themselves to themselves. May seem queer, but it’s a fact that I don’t know the names of my next-door neighbours on either side﻿—though to be sure they’ve only been here a few years in either case.”
“What I was suggesting,” said Hetherwick, “was that you probably knew by sight many of the people who live in the flats opposite your house.”
“Oh, I know some of ’em by sight,” assented the man. “They’re a mixed lot over in those flats! A few old gentlemen﻿—retired﻿—two or three old ladies﻿—and a fair lot of actresses﻿—very popular with the stage is those flats. But, of course, it is only by sight﻿—I don’t know any of ’em by name. Just see them going in and coming out, you know.”
“Do you happen to know by sight a tall, handsome woman who has a flat there?” asked Hetherwick. “A woman who’s likely to be very well dressed?”
The lodging-house keeper, who was without his coat and had the sleeves of his shirt rolled up, scratched his elbows and looked thoughtful.

===Chinese===
“那可是桩难办的事，先生！”他答道，“伦敦这一小块地界，挤着好几千口子人。当然，近处的一大批我确实认得。可您要是伦敦人就知道，伦敦人向来各顾各的。听着也许古怪，可我连两边紧挨着的邻居姓什么都叫不上来——虽说实在话，他们哪一边也才搬来没几年。”
“我的意思，”海瑟维克说，“是您家对面那座公寓里的住户，想必有不少您见了面认得出。”
“哦，那里头有几个我见面认得。”那人应道，“那座公寓里住的可杂了！几位老先生——退了休的——两三位老太太——还有好些个女演员——演戏的顶爱住那座公寓。当然，只是见面认得——名字我一个都不知道。无非是瞧着他们进进出出，您明白。”
“您可巧认不认得一位在那边有套房间的高个子标致女人？”海瑟维克问道，“一位多半穿得十分讲究的女人？”
分租房的房东没穿上衣，衬衫袖子卷着，挠了挠胳膊肘，一副若有所思的模样。

===Original===
“I think I do know the lady you mean,” he said at last. “Goes out with one o’ those pesky little poms﻿—a black ’un﻿—on a lead? That her?”
“I don’t know anything about a dog,” replied Hetherwick. “The woman I mean is, as I said, tall, handsome, distinguished-looking, fair hair and a fresh complexion, and about forty or so.”
“I dare say that’s the one I’m thinking of,” said the man. “I have seen such a lady now and then﻿—not of late, though.” Then he gave Hetherwick a shrewd, inquiring glance. “You and Matherfield after her?” he asked.
“Not exactly that,” answered Hetherwick. “What I want to find out﻿—now﻿—is her name. The name she’s known by here, anyway.”
“I can soon settle that for you,” said the lodging-house keeper with alacrity. “I know the caretaker of those flats well enough﻿—often have a talk with him. He’ll tell me anything﻿—between ourselves. Now then, let’s get it right﻿—a tall, handsome lady, about forty, fair hair, fresh complexion, well dressed. That it, mister?”

===Chinese===
“您说的那位太太，我想我还真认得。”他终于开口道，“出门总带着一只那种讨人嫌的小博美——黑毛的——拴着链子？是她不？”
“狗的事我一概不知。”海瑟维克答道，“我说的这个女人，方才讲过，个子高，模样标致，气度出众，金头发，气色鲜润，年纪四十上下。”
“我寻思，我想到的正是这么一位。”那人说，“这样的太太我时不时见着一回——只是近来没见着。”说着，他朝海瑟维克投来精明探究的一瞥。“您跟马瑟菲尔德在追她？”他问。
“倒也不全是。”海瑟维克答道，“我眼下要查出来的是她的名字。至少，是她在这一带为人所知的名字。”
“这个我一会儿就能替您办妥。”分租房的房东爽快地说，“那座公寓的管理员我熟得很——常跟他聊天。他什么都肯告诉我——咱们私下说说。那么，先把话说准了——高个儿的标致太太，四十上下，金头发，气色鲜润，穿得讲究。是这么一位，先生？”

===Original===
“You’ve got it,” said Hetherwick.
“Then you wait here a bit, and I’ll slip across,” said the man. “All on the strict between ourselves, you know. As I said, the caretaker and me’s pals.”
He left the room, and a moment later Hetherwick saw him cross the road and descend into the basement of the flats. Within a quarter of an hour he was back, and evidently primed with news.
“Soon settled that for you, mister!” he announced triumphantly. “He knew who you meant! The lady’s name is Madame Listorelle. Here, I got him to write it down on a bit o’ paper, not being used to foreign names. He thinks she’s something to do with the stage. She’s the tenant of flat twenty-six. But he says that of late she’s seldom there﻿—comes for a night or two, then away, maybe for months at a time. He saw her here yesterday, though; she hadn’t been there, he says, for a good bit. But there, it don’t signify to him whether she’s there or away﻿—always punctual with her money, and that’s the main thing, ain’t it?”
Hetherwick added to his largess of the early morning, and went away. He was now convinced that Lady Riversreade, for some purpose of her own, kept up a flat in Paddington, visited it occasionally, and was known there as Madame Listorelle. How much was there in that, and what bearing had it on the problem he was endeavouring to solve?

===Chinese===
“正是这么一位。”海瑟维克说。
“那您在这儿稍等，我这就溜达过去。”那人说，“这可得严严实实瞒着外人，咱们私底下的事，您知道。我说了，管理员跟我是老哥们儿。”
他出了屋。不多时，海瑟维克便瞧见他过了马路，走下那座公寓的地下室。不到一刻钟他就回来了，分明满肚子都是打探来的消息。
“一会儿就替您办妥了，先生！”他得意洋洋地宣布，“他知道您说的是谁！那位太太叫里斯托雷尔太太（Madame Listorelle）。喏，我让他把名字写在一张小纸条上了，外国名字咱们不惯。他琢磨着她跟戏剧界沾着点边。她租的是二十六号房间。不过他说，近来她难得在那儿——回来住上一两夜，随即又走，一走兴许就是几个月。可昨天他在这儿瞧见她了；照他说，她之前好一阵子没来过。不过嘛，她在不在，他才不放在心上呢——房钱从来按时不误，这才是顶要紧的，可不是吗？”
海瑟维克在清早那份赏钱之外又添了一笔，随后离去。此刻他已确信：里弗斯雷德夫人出于某种自家的目的，在帕丁顿常年赁着一处公寓房间，偶尔来住上一住，在那边以里斯托雷尔太太的名字为人所知。这里面藏着多大文章？它与自己竭力想解开的难题，又有着什么干系？

## VII Black Velvet / 七 黑天鹅绒

===Original===
Next morning, and before calling on either Kenthwaite or Rhona Hannaford, Hetherwick set out on a tour of the fashionable photographers in the West End of London. After all, there were not so many of them, so many at any rate of the very famous ones. He made a hit and began to work methodically. His first few coverts were drawn blank, but just before noon, and as he was thinking of knocking off for lunch, he started his fox. In a palatial establishment in Bond Street the person to whom he applied, showing his picture, gave an immediate smile of recognition.

“You want to know who is the original of this?” he said. “Certainly! Lady Riversreade, of Riversreade Court, near Dorking.”

Hetherwick had no deep acquaintance with Debrett nor with Burke, nor even with the list of peers, baronets and knights given in the ordinary reference books, and to him the name of Lady Riversreade was absolutely unknown﻿—he had never heard of her. But the man to whom he had shown the print, and who now held it in his hand, seemed to consider that Lady Riversreade was, or should be, as well known to everybody as she evidently was to him.

“This print is from one of our photographs of Lady Riversreade,” he said, turning to a side table in the reception-room in which they were standing and picking up a framed portrait. “This one.”

“Then you probably know in what newspaper this print appeared?” suggested Hetherwick. “That’s really what I’m desirous of finding out.”

“Oh, it appeared in several,” answered the photographer. “Recently. It was about the time that Lady Riversreade opened some home or institute﻿—I forget what. There was an account of it in the papers, and naturally her portrait was reproduced.”
===Chinese===
第二天早晨，海瑟维克（Hetherwick）还没有去拜访肯思韦特（Kenthwaite）和罗娜·汉纳福德（Rhona Hannaford）两人中的任何一位，便先动身遍访伦敦西区（West End）那些时髦的照相馆。反正这类馆子屈指可数，至少顶尖的名店没有几家。他一上手便得了手，随即有条不紊地行动起来。头几处猎丛都空空如也，可临近正午、他正想歇手去吃午饭的时候，却把狐狸惊了出来。在邦德街（Bond Street）一家富丽堂皇的大店里，他拿出照片一问，对方顿时露出了认得相中人的笑容。

“你想知道这张照片上的人是谁？”他说，“当然认得！是多金（Dorking）附近里弗斯雷德府（Riversreade Court）的里弗斯雷德夫人（Lady Riversreade）。”

海瑟维克对《德布雷特贵族年鉴》（Debrett）不熟，《伯克贵族名鉴》（Burke）也不熟，甚至连普通参考书里所附的贵族、从男爵和爵士名单也谈不上熟悉，因此里弗斯雷德夫人这个名姓在他听来全然陌生——他从来没有听说过她。可是，那个看过他出示的照片、此刻正把它拿在手里的人，却俨然认为里弗斯雷德夫人人尽皆知，或者至少理应如此——一如她在他眼里显然的那副光景。

“这张照片原是出于我们店里为里弗斯雷德夫人所摄的一帧，”他说着，转身走向两人此刻所站的那间接待室里的一张边桌，拿起一幅装框的肖像。“就是这一张。”

“那么，这张照片登在哪份报纸上，您多半知道吧？”海瑟维克提示道，“我真正想打听的正是这个。”

“哦，好几家报纸都登过，”照相师答道，“就在近来。大概是里弗斯雷德夫人开办什么疗养院还是什么机构的时候——我记不清了。报上登了记事，她的照片自然也就随之刊出来了。”

===Original===
Hetherwick made a plausible prearranged excuse for his curiosity, and went away. Lady Riversreade!﻿—evidently some woman of rank, or means, or position. But was she identical with the Mrs. Whittingham of ten years ago﻿—the Mrs. Whittingham who did the Sellithwaite jeweller out of a necklace worth nearly four thousand pounds and cleverly escaped arrest at the hands of Hannaford? And if so﻿ ﻿…

But that led to indefinite vistas; the main thing at present was to find out all that could be found out about Lady Riversreade, of Riversreade Court, near Dorking. Hetherwick could doubtless have obtained considerable information from the fashionable photographer, but he had carefully refrained from showing too much inquisitiveness. Moreover, he knew a man, one Boxley, a fellow club-member, who was always fully posted up in all the doings of the social and fashionable world and could, if he would, tell him everything about Lady Riversreade﻿—that was, if there was anything to tell about her. Boxley was one of those bachelor men about town who went everywhere, knew everybody, and kept himself fully informed; he invariably lunched at this particular club, the Junior Megatherium, and thither Hetherwick presently proceeded, bent on finding him.

He was fortunate in running Boxley to earth almost as soon as he entered the sacred and exclusive portals. Boxley was lunching and there was no one else at his table. Hetherwick joined him, and began the usual small talk about nothing in particular. But he soon came to his one point.

“Look here!” he said, at a convenient interval. “I want to ask you something. You know everybody and everything. Who is Lady Riversreade, who’s recently opened some home or institution, or hospital or something?”

“One of the richest women in England!” replied Boxley promptly. “Worth a couple of millions or so. That’s who she is﻿—who she was, I don’t know. Don’t suppose anybody else does, either. In this country, anyhow.”

“What, is she a foreigner, then?” asked Hetherwick. “I’ve seen her portrait in the papers﻿—that’s why I asked you who she is. Doesn’t look foreign, I think.”
===Chinese===
海瑟维克为这份好奇心编了一套事先备好、听来可信的托辞，便告辞出来。里弗斯雷德夫人！——显然是位有身份、有财力、有地位的女人。可她会不会就是十年前那位惠廷厄姆太太（Mrs. Whittingham）——就是那位把塞利斯韦特（Sellithwaite）珠宝商坑去了价值近四千镑的项链、又机巧地躲过汉纳福德（Hannaford）缉拿的惠廷厄姆太太？倘若果真如此……

不过，这个念头牵出的前景漫无边际；眼下最要紧的，是把多金附近里弗斯雷德府那位里弗斯雷德夫人的底细，能打听的都打听出来。那位时髦照相师本来无疑可以供给他不少消息，可他一直小心在意，不肯露出过分的好奇。何况他还认识一个人——博克斯利（Boxley），他的一个俱乐部队友，此人对社交界和时髦圈的大小动静无不了如指掌，只要肯说，就能把里弗斯雷德夫人的情形一五一十告诉他——前提是这位夫人当真有什么可说的。博克斯利属于那种满城转悠的单身汉，什么去处都去，什么人都识得，消息时刻灵通；他照例在“小大懒兽俱乐部”（Junior Megatherium）用午餐，海瑟维克当下便直奔那里，一心要把他找到。

他运气不坏，刚跨进那座神圣而非请莫入的殿堂，就把博克斯利逮了个正着。博克斯利正在用午餐，同桌没有别人。海瑟维克在他身旁坐下，照例先闲扯了一通不著边际的空话，但很快就转到了他唯一要紧的那一点上。

“我说！”他瞅准一个合适的空当开口道，“我想问你点事。你谁都识得，什么事都知道。里弗斯雷德夫人是谁？就是新近开办了什么疗养院、机构，还是医院之类的那位。”

“全英格兰数得着的有钱女人！”博克斯利应声答道，“身家两百万上下。她如今就是这么个人——从前是干什么的，我不知道。我看也没有别人知道。反正这个国家里没人知道。”

“怎么，她是个外国人？”海瑟维克问，“我在报上见过她的照片——所以才问你她的来历。照我看，倒不像外国人。”

===Original===
“I can tell you all that is known about her,” said Boxley, “and that’s not much. She’s the widow of old Sir John Riversreade, the famous contractor﻿—the man who made a pot of money building railways, and dams across big rivers, and that sort of thing, and got a knighthood for it. He also built himself a magnificent place near Dorking, and called it Riversreade Court﻿—just the type of place a modern millionaire would build. Now, old Sir John had been a bachelor all his life, until he was over sixty﻿—no time for anything but his contracts, you know. But when he was about sixty-five, which would be some six or seven years ago, he went over to the United States and made a rather lengthy stay there. And when he returned he brought a wife with him﻿—the lady you’re inquiring about.”

“American, then?” suggested Hetherwick.

“Well, he married her over there, certainly,” said Boxley. “But I should say she isn’t American.”

“You’ve met her﻿—personally?”

“Just. Run across her once or twice at various affairs, and been introduced to her, quite casually. No, I don’t think she’s American. If I wanted to label her, I should say she was cosmopolitan.”

“Woman of the world, eh?”
===Chinese===
“她的事，凡外界知道的，我都可以告诉你，”博克斯利说，“不过知道的也不多。她是老约翰·里弗斯雷德爵士（Sir John Riversreade）的遗孀——就是那位大名鼎鼎的承包商，修铁路、在大河上拦水筑坝这一类工程叫他发了大财，还因此得了爵士封号。他还在多金附近给自己盖了一处豪华宅邸，起名里弗斯雷德府——正是现代百万富翁爱盖的那种宅子。老约翰爵士打了一辈子光棍，直到六十开外——你知道，除了合同，他什么也顾不上。可到他六十五岁上下——也就是六七年前吧——他跑到美国去，盘桓了颇长一段时日。回来时身边就带了位太太——就是你要打听的这位夫人。”

“那么，是美国人喽？”海瑟维克试探道。

“嗯，他成亲是在那边成的，这没错，”博克斯利说，“不过要我说，她本人不是美国人。”

“你见过她——当面见过？”

“见过。在各种场合撞见过一两回，也有人给我引见引见，都相当随便。不，我看她不是美国人。要让我给她贴个标签，我只能说她是世界公民。”

“很见过世面的女人，嗯？”

===Original===
“Decidedly so. Handsome woman﻿—self-possessed﻿—self-assured﻿—smart, clever. I think she’ll know how to take care of the money her husband left her.”

“Leave her everything?”

“Every penny!﻿—except some inconsiderable legacies to charitable institutions. It was said at the time﻿—it’s two years since the old chap died﻿—that she’s got over two millions.”

“And this institution, or whatever it is?”

“Oh, that! That was in the papers not so long since.”

“I’m no great reader of newspapers. What about it?”
===Chinese===
“十足如此。漂亮女人——沉着——自信——时髦、精明。我看她自会看管好丈夫留给她的那笔钱财。”

“全留给她了？”

“每一便士都归她！——只除了捐给慈善机构的几笔微不足道的遗赠。老头子故世——算来两年了——当时就有传闻，说她到手了两百多万。”

“那么她办的这个机构——不管它究竟是什么——又是怎么回事？”

“哦，那个呀！不久前报上还登过。”

“我可不怎么细读报纸。究竟是怎么回事？”

===Original===
“Oh, she’s started a home for wounded officers near Riversreade Court. There was some big country house near there empty﻿—couldn’t really be sold or let. She bought it, renovated it, fitted it up, stuck a staff of nurses and servants in, and got it blessed by the War Office. Jolly nice place, I believe, and she pays the piper.”

“Doing the benevolent business, eh?”

“So it appears. Easy game, too, when you’ve got a couple of millions behind you. Useful, though.”

Boxley went away soon after that, and Hetherwick, wondering about what he had learned, and now infinitely inquisitive about the identity of Lady Riversreade with Mrs. Whittingham, went into the smoking-room, and more from habit than because he really wanted to see it, picked up a copy of *The Times*. Almost the first thing on which his glance lighted was the name that was just then in his thoughts﻿—there it was, in capitals, at the head of an advertisement:

> Required at once a Resident Lady-Secretary, fully competent to undertake accounts and correspondence and thoroughly trained in shorthand and typewriting; a knowledge of French and German would be a high recommendation. Application should be made personally any day this week between 10 and 12 and 3 and 5 to Lady Riversreade, Riversreade Court, Dorking.
===Chinese===
“哦，她在里弗斯雷德府附近办了一所负伤军官疗养院。附近有一座挺大的乡间宅子一直空着——实在卖不掉，也租不出去。她把宅子买了下来，翻修一新，布置停当，配上一班护士和仆役，又得了陆军部（War Office）的嘉许。我看那地方好得很，钱也全由她来掏。”

“做行善的买卖，嗯？”

“看样子是这么回事。况且身后垫着几百万，干这个也容易。不过倒管用。”

随后不久博克斯利便走了。海瑟维克一面咀嚼着刚听来的这些事，一面怀着按捺不住的好奇，琢磨里弗斯雷德夫人与惠廷厄姆太太究竟是否一人，走进吸烟室，多半出于习惯而非真想看报，随手拿起一份*《泰晤士报》*（*The Times*）。他的目光最先落定的东西之一，恰恰是他此刻心里正想着的那个名字——它就在那儿，用大写字母排在一则广告的顶上：

> 急聘驻邸女秘书一名，须全然胜任账目与函牍事宜，并受过速记与打字的严格训练；谙习法语、德语者将获优先考虑。有意者请于本周每日上午 10 时至 12 时、下午 3 时至 5 时，亲赴多金里弗斯雷德府，面谒里弗斯雷德夫人。

===Original===
Hetherwick threw the paper aside, left the club, and at the first newsagent’s he came to bought another copy. With this in his hand he jumped into a taxicab and set off for Surrey Street, wondering if he would find Rhona Hannaford still at Malter’s Hotel. He was fortunate in that﻿—she had not yet left﻿—and in a few minutes he was giving her a full and detailed account of his doings since his last interview with her. She listened to his story about Sellithwaite and his discoveries of that morning with a slightly puzzled look.

“Why are you taking all this trouble?” she asked suddenly and abruptly. “You’re doing more, going into things more, than the police are. Matherfield was here this morning to tell me, he said, how they were getting on. They aren’t getting on at all!﻿—they haven’t made one single discovery; they’ve heard nothing, found out nothing, about the man in the train or the man at Victoria﻿—they’re just where they were. But you﻿—you’ve found out a lot! Why are you so energetic about it?”

“Put it down to professional inquisitiveness, if you like,” answered Hetherwick, smiling. “I’m﻿—interested. Tremendously! You see﻿—I, too, was there in the train, like the man they haven’t found. Well, now﻿—now that I’ve got to this point I’ve arrived at, I want you to take a hand.”

“I? In what way?” exclaimed Rhona.

Hetherwick pulled out *The Times* and pointed to the advertisement.

“I want you to go down to Dorking tomorrow morning and personally interview Lady Riversreade in response to that,” he said. “You’ve all the qualifications she specifies, so you’ve an excellent excuse for calling on her. Whether you’d care to take the post is another matter﻿—what I want is that you should see her under conditions that will enable you to observe her closely.”
===Chinese===
海瑟维克把报纸一撂，出了俱乐部，在碰上的头一家报刊亭又买了一份。他把报纸攥在手里，跳上一辆出租车，直奔萨里街（Surrey Street）而去，一路盘算着还能不能在马尔特旅馆（Malter's Hotel）里找见罗娜·汉纳福德。这一点他运气不错——她还没有走——不出几分钟，他已把自己自上次晤面以来的种种行事，原原本本、详详细细讲给她听。听他讲塞利斯韦特那段故事和那天上午的种种发现时，她脸上带着几分困惑。

“你何必费这么大的劲？”她突然劈头问道，“你跑的比警察还勤，钻得比警察还深。马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）今天上午来过，说是要告诉我他们的进展。他们毫无进展可言！——他们连一桩发现都没有；火车上那个人、维多利亚车站那个人，他们风声也没听到一点，眉目也没查出一条——还在原地踏步。可你——你倒查出了不少东西！你对这件事为什么这样起劲？”

“你尽管把它算作职业性的好奇好了，”海瑟维克含笑回答，“我是——感兴趣，极感兴趣！你要知道——我自己也在那趟车上，同他们没找着的那个一样。好了——如今我已走到了眼下的地步，我要请你搭一把手了。”

“我？怎么个帮法？”罗娜失声道。

海瑟维克掏出那份*《泰晤士报》*，指了指那则广告。

“我要你明天上午去多金，应这则广告之约，当面见一见里弗斯雷德夫人，”他说，“她开列的条件你样样俱备，去拜会她就有了个再好不过的由头。至于你愿不愿就那份职，那是另一回事——我要的是，让你在一个能细细端详她的情形下见她一面。”

===Original===
“Why?” asked Rhona.

“I want you to see if she wears such a band as that which Hudson told Hollis and myself about,” replied Hetherwick. “Sharp eyes like yours will soon see that. And﻿—if she does, then she’s Mrs. Whittingham! In that case, I might ask you to do more﻿—still more.”

“What, for instance?” she inquired.

“Well, to do your best to get this post,” he answered. “I think that you, with your qualifications, could get it.”

“And﻿—your object in that?” she asked.

“To keep an eye on Lady Riversreade,” he replied promptly. “If the Mrs. Whittingham of ten years ago at Sellithwaite is the same woman as the Lady Riversreade of Riversreade Court of today, then, in view of your grandfather’s murder, I want to know a lot more about her! To have you﻿—there!﻿—would be an immense help.”
===Chinese===
“为什么呢？”罗娜问。

“我要你看看，她腕上系的是不是赫德森（Hudson）向霍利斯（Hollis）和我讲起过的那种带子，”海瑟维克答道，“有你那样尖利的眼睛，一看便知。还有——倘若她真戴着，那她就是惠廷厄姆太太！果真如此，我说不定还要请你做更多的事——更多得多。”

“比如说，什么事？”她问。

“嗯，就是千方百计把那份职弄到手，”他答道，“我想，凭你的条件，是拿得下来的。”

“那么——你这样做到底图的是什么？”她问。

“盯住里弗斯雷德夫人，”他应声答道，“倘若十年前塞利斯韦特的惠廷厄姆太太，就是今天里弗斯雷德府的里弗斯雷德夫人，那么，看在你祖父遇害这件事上，我还想多知道她的许多底细！有你——在那儿！——帮衬着，就是天大的助力。”

===Original===
“I’m to be a sort of spy, eh?” asked Rhona.

“Detective, if you like,” assented Hetherwick. “Why not?”

“You forget this,” she remarked. “If this Lady Riversreade is identical with the Mrs. Whittingham of ten years ago, she’d remember my name﻿—Hannaford! She’s not likely to have forgotten Superintendent Hannaford of Sellithwaite!”

“Exactly﻿—but I’ve thought of that little matter,” replied Hetherwick. “Call yourself by some other name. Your mother’s, for instance.”

“That was Featherstone,” said Rhona.

“There you are! Go as Miss Featherstone. As for your address, give your aunt’s address at Tooting. Easy enough, you see,” laughed Hetherwick. “Once you begin it properly.”
===Chinese===
“这就是让我去当密探，嗯？”罗娜问。

“说侦探也未尝不可，”海瑟维克点头认可，“有何不可？”

“有一层你忘了，”她说，“倘若这位里弗斯雷德夫人当真是十年前的惠廷厄姆太太，她会记得我的姓——汉纳福德！塞利斯韦特的汉纳福德警司，她哪能忘得了！”

“正是——这层小关节我早想到了，”海瑟维克答道，“你换个姓名就是。比方，用你母亲的。”

“那姓费瑟斯通（Featherstone），”罗娜说。

“这不就成了！你就以费瑟斯通小姐的名义去。至于住址，报你姑母在图廷（Tooting）的住址。你瞧，够容易的了，”海瑟维克笑道，“只要你开头开得像模像样。”

===Original===
“There’s another thing, though,” she objected. “References! She’ll want those.”

“Just as easy,” answered Hetherwick. “Give me as one and Kenthwaite as the other. I’ll speak to him about it. Two barristers of the Middle Temple!﻿—excellent! Come!﻿—all you’ve got to do is to work the scheme out fully and carry it out with assurance, and you don’t know what we might discover.”

Rhona considered matters awhile, watching him steadily.

“You think that﻿—somehow﻿—this woman may be at the back of the mystery surrounding my grandfather’s murder?” she suddenly asked.

“I think it’s quite within the bounds of probability,” he answered.

“All right,” she said abruptly. “I’ll go. Tomorrow morning, I suppose?”
===Chinese===
“不过还有一样，”她提出异议，“荐保人！她少不了要问这个。”

“也一样容易办，”海瑟维克答道，“报我一个，再报肯思韦特一个。我会先跟他打好招呼。两位中殿律师学院（Middle Temple）的出庭律师！——再漂亮不过！来吧！——你只消把这套计划筹划周全，再放胆去办，天晓得我们会查出什么名堂来。”

罗娜把这事掂量了一会儿，目光一直定定地望着他。

“你是不是觉得——不知怎么地——我祖父被害这团迷雾的背后，兴许就是这个女人？”她突然问。

“我认为这相当在情理之内，”他回答。

“好吧，”她干脆地说，“我去。就定在明天上午吧？”

===Original===
“Sooner the better,” agreed Hetherwick. “And, look here, I’ll go down with you. We’ll go by the 10:10 from Victoria, drive to this place, and I’ll wait outside while you have your interview. After that we’ll get some lunch in Dorking﻿—and you can tell me your news.”

Next morning found Hetherwick pacing the platform at Victoria and on the lookout for his fellow-companion. She came to him a little before the train was due to leave, and he noticed at once that she had discarded the mourning garments in which he had found her the previous afternoon; she now appeared in a smart tailor-made coat and skirt, and looked the part he wanted her to assume﻿—that of a capable and self-reliant young business woman.

“Good!” he said approvingly, as they went to find their seats. “Nothing like dressing up to it. You’re all ready with your lines, eh?﻿—I mean, you’ve settled on all you’re going to say and do?”

“Leave that to me,” she answered with a laugh, “I shan’t forget the primary object, anyway. But I’ve been wondering﻿—supposing we come to the conclusion that this Lady Riversreade is the Mrs. Whittingham of ten years ago? What are you going to do then?”

“My ideas are hazy on that point﻿—at present,” confessed Hetherwick. “The first thing, surely, is to establish identity. Don’t forget that the main thing to do at Riversreade Court is to get a good look at Lady Riversreade’s right wrist, and see what’s on it!”

Riversreade Court proved to be some distance from Dorking, in the Leith Hill district; Hetherwick chartered a taxicab and gave his companion final instructions as they rode out. Half an hour’s run brought them to the house﻿—a big, pretentious, imitation Elizabethan structure, set on the hillside amongst a grove of firs and pines, and having an ornamental park laid out between its gardens and terraces and the high road. At the lodge gates he stopped the driver and got out.
===Chinese===
“越快越好，”海瑟维克表示赞成，“还有，你听着，我陪你一道去。咱们乘 10 点 10 分从维多利亚开出的车，到了那儿坐车去那地方，你进去面谈，我在外面等。完了咱们在多金吃午饭——你也好把消息讲给我听。”

第二天早晨，海瑟维克已在维多利亚站的月台上踱来踱去，张望他的旅伴。开车前不久她来了，他立刻注意到，她已经脱下前一天下午他见她时所穿的那身丧服；此刻她换了一身时兴的定制呢外套和裙子，看上去正像他要她扮演的那个角色——一位干练而自主的年轻职业女性。

“好！”他们去找座位时，他赞许地说，“天下事全凭行头撑场面。你的台词都备齐了吧，嗯？——我是说，该说什么、该做什么，你都拿定主意了？”

“这个交给我好了，”她笑着回答，“反正头一桩正事我忘不了。不过我一直在想——倘若咱们断定这位里弗斯雷德夫人就是十年前的惠廷厄姆太太，那时候你打算怎么办？”

“在那一点上，我的主意眼下还模糊得很，”海瑟维克坦白说，“头一件事，自然是先把身份坐实。别忘了在里弗斯雷德府要办的头等大事，就是把里弗斯雷德夫人的右腕好好瞧上一眼，看看上面戴着什么！”

里弗斯雷德府离多金还有相当一段路，坐落在利斯山（Leith Hill）一带；海瑟维克包下一辆出租车，一路驶去时对同伴作了最后的嘱咐。车行半个钟头，便到了那座宅子——一幢庞大而张扬的仿伊丽莎白式建筑，坐落在山坡上一片冷杉和松树的林子里，花园、台地与公路之间还辟出一座园林化的园囿。到了门房前的大门，他叫司机停车，自己下了车。

===Original===
“I’ll wait here for you,” he said to Rhona. “You ride up to the house, get your business done, and come back here. Be watchful now﻿—of anything.”

Rhona nodded reassuringly and went off; Hetherwick lighted his pipe and strolled about admiring the scenery. But his thoughts were with Rhona; he was wondering what adventures she was having in the big mansion which the late contractor had built amidst the woods. And Rhona kept him wondering some time; an hour had elapsed before the cab came back. With a hand on its door, he turned to the driver:

“Go to the White Horse now,” he said. “We’ll lunch there, and afterwards you can take us to the station. Well?” he continued, as he got in and seated himself at Rhona’s side. “What luck?”

“Good, I should say,” answered Rhona. “She wears a broad black velvet band on her right wrist, and on the outer face is a small cameo. How’s that?”

“Precisely!” exclaimed Hetherwick. “Just what that barkeeper chap at Sellithwaite described. Wears it openly﻿—makes no attempt at concealment beneath her sleeve, eh?”

“None,” answered Rhona. “She was wearing a smart, fashionable, short-sleeved jumper. She’d a very fine diamond bracelet on the other wrist.”
===Chinese===
“我就在这儿等你，”他对罗娜说，“你坐车上去，把事办完，再回到这儿来。这会儿眼睛放尖些——对什么都留神。”

罗娜点头示意叫他放心，径自去了；海瑟维克点上烟斗，踱来踱去观赏风景。可他心里惦记的却是罗娜，一味琢磨她在这位已故承包商于林间盖起的大宅里正有什么际遇。罗娜也真让他悬了好一阵心——足足过了一个钟头，车子才回来。他一只手扶着车门，转身对司机说：

“现在去白马旅馆（White Horse），”他说，“咱们在那儿吃午饭，饭后你送我们上车站。怎么样？”他上了车，在罗娜身边坐定，接着问道，“运气如何？”

“依我看，很好，”罗娜答道，“她右腕上戴着一圈宽宽的黑天鹅绒带子，外面那一面嵌着一枚小小的浮雕宝石。这个情况如何？”

“分毫不差！”海瑟维克失声道，“正是塞利斯韦特那个酒保伙计讲的那副样子。堂而皇之地戴着——全无藏进袖中的意思，对吧？”

“一点也没有，”罗娜答道，“她穿着一件时兴时髦的短袖罩衫。另一只手腕上戴的是一只极其精美的钻石手镯。”

===Original===
“And she herself,” asked Hetherwick. “What sort of woman is she?”

“That’s a very good photograph of her that my grandfather cut out of the paper,” replied Rhona. “Very good, indeed! I knew her at once. She’s a tall, fine, handsome, well-preserved woman, perhaps forty, perhaps less. Very easy, accustomed manner; a regular woman of the world I should think. Quite ready to talk about herself and her doings﻿—she told me the whole history of this Home she’s started and took me to see it﻿—it’s a fine old house, much more attractive than the Court, a little way along the hillside. She told me that it was her great hobby, and that she’s devoting all her time to it. I should say that she’s genuinely interested in its welfare﻿—genuinely!”

“She impressed you?” suggested Hetherwick.

“I think, from what I saw and heard, that she’s a good-natured, probably warmhearted, woman. She spoke very feelingly of the patients she’s got in her Home, anyhow.”

“And the post﻿—the secretaryship?”

“I can have it if I want it﻿—of course, I told her I did. She examined me pretty closely about my qualifications﻿—she herself speaks French and German like a native﻿—and I mentioned you and Mr. Kenthwaite as references. She’s going to write to you both today. So﻿—it’s for you to decide.”
===Chinese===
“那么她本人呢，”海瑟维克问，“是个什么样的女人？”

“我祖父从报上剪下的那张照片，照得非常像她，”罗娜回答，“确实非常像！我一眼就把她认了出来。她身材高挑，仪态大方，容貌漂亮，保养得宜，大概四十岁，也许还不到。举止十分从容自在、惯见场面；照我看，十足是个见过世面的女人。谈起自己和自己种种行事来毫无遮拦——她把这所疗养院的始末原原本本讲给我听，还带我去看——那是座漂亮的老宅子，比庄园本宅讨人喜欢得多，就在山坡上往前不远的地方。她告诉我，这是她顶大的癖好，她的全部光阴都投在这上头。要我说，她是真心关切院务——真心！”

“她给你留下好印象了？”海瑟维克提点道。

“从我的所见所闻看，我觉得她是个心地不坏、多半还是副热心肠的女人。无论如何，她谈起院里收治的那些伤员，口气是相当动情的。”

“那么那份职——秘书的差事呢？”

“我想要就肯给——当然，我对她说我想要。她就我的资历盘问得相当仔细——她本人讲起法语德语来同本国人一样——我报了你和肯思韦特先生做荐保人。她今天就要给你们两位写信。所以——这就由你来定夺了。”

===Original===
“I suppose it’s really for you!”

“No!﻿—I’m willing, eager, indeed, to do anything to clear up the mystery about my grandfather’s murder. But﻿—I don’t think this woman had anything to do with it. In my opinion﻿—and I suppose I’ve got some feminine intuition﻿—she’s honest and straightforward enough.”

“And yet it looks as if she were certainly the Mrs. Whittingham who did a Sellithwaite jeweller to the tune of four thousand pounds!” laughed Hetherwick. “That wasn’t very honest or straightforward!”

“I’ve been thinking about that,” said Rhona. “Perhaps, after all, she really thought the cheque would be met, and anyway, she did send the man his money, even though it was a long time afterwards. And again﻿—an important matter!﻿—Lady Riversreade may not be Mrs. Whittingham at all. More women than one wear wristlets of velvet.”

“But﻿—the portrait!” exclaimed Hetherwick. “The positive identity!”

“Well,” answered Rhona, “I’m willing to go there and to try to find out more. But, frankly, I think Lady Riversreade’s all right! First impression, anyhow!”
===Chinese===
“我看这实在该由你来定！”

“不！——只要能解开我祖父遇害之谜，我什么都愿意做，说急切也不为过。可是——我并不认为这个女人同那件事有什么相干。依我的判断——我想我到底有几分女人的直觉——她够诚实，也够直爽。”

“可看样子，她分明就是那位把塞利斯韦特一个珠宝商坑去整整四千镑的惠廷厄姆太太呀！”海瑟维克笑道，“那可算不得多诚实、多直爽！”

“这件事我也想过了，”罗娜说，“说不定她当时真以为那张支票兑得出现款呢；再说，不管怎样，她终究把钱补给那人了，尽管隔了很久。还有一层——要紧的一层！——里弗斯雷德夫人也许压根儿就不是惠廷厄姆太太。戴天鹅绒腕环的女人，可不止一个。”

“可是——那照片呢！”海瑟维克失声道，“那可是铁定的同一张脸！”

“这个嘛，”罗娜回答，“我愿意到那儿去，再摸摸底。不过老实说，我觉得里弗斯雷德夫人没有问题！至少第一印象如此！”

===Original===
The cab drew up at the White Horse, and Hetherwick led Rhona into the coffee-room. But they had hardly taken their seats when the manager came in.

“Does your name happen to be Hetherwick, sir?” he inquired. “Just so﻿—thank you. A Mr. Mapperley has twice rung you up here during the last hour﻿—he’s on the phone again now, if you’ll speak to him.”

“I’ll come,” said Hetherwick. “That’s my clerk,” he murmured to Rhona as he rose. “I told him to ring me up here between twelve and three if necessary. Back in a minute.”

But he was away several minutes, and when he came to her again, his face was grave. “Here’s a new development!” he said, bending across the table and whispering. “The police have found the man who was with your grandfather in the train! Matherfield wants me to identify him. And you’ll gather from that that they’ve found him dead! We must lunch quickly and catch the two-twenty-four.”
===Chinese===
出租车在白马旅馆门前停住，海瑟维克领着罗娜进了咖啡厅。可两人刚落座，经理便进来了。

“先生，您是不是碰巧叫海瑟维克？”他问道，“正是——谢谢您。过去一个钟头里，有位马珀利先生（Mr. Mapperley）打到这里找过您两次——他这会儿又来电话了，您要不要去接一接？”

“我就来，”海瑟维克说，“那是我的办事员，”他起身时低声对罗娜说，“我吩咐过他，若有必要，在十二点到三点之间往这儿给我打电话。去去就回。”

可他一去就是好几分钟，再回到她跟前时，脸色已经十分凝重。“有新情况了！”他俯身隔着桌子，压低嗓音说，“警察找到火车上陪着你祖父的那个人了！马瑟菲尔德要我去认一认。由此你也想见得到——他们找到的已是个死人！咱们得赶紧吃完午饭，赶两点二十四分的那班车。

## XX Converging Tracks / 二十 合流轨迹

===Original===
It was an hour later when they pulled up at Matherfield’s headquarters and went in to find him. Matherfield, brought to them after some search, rubbed his hands at sight of them.
“Come at the right moment!” he exclaimed, “I’ve got news﻿—of Ambrose!”
Matherfield evidently expected his visitors to show deep interest, if not passive enthusiasm, in respect of this announcement, and he stared wonderingly on seeing that their faces showed nothing but gloom and concern.
“But you﻿—you look as if you’d had bad news!” he exclaimed. “Something gone wrong?”
“I forgot that we might have telephoned you from Riversreade Court,” replied Hetherwick, suddenly realising that Matherfield seemed to know nothing of the day’s happening. “But I thought the Dorking police would do that. Gone wrong!﻿—yes, and it may have to do with Ambrose﻿—we’ve heard news that seems to fit in with him. But it’s this,” he went on to give Matherfield a brief account of the day’s events. “There you are!” he concluded. “I’ve no doubt whatever that Baseverie and Ambrose are in at this﻿—kidnapping in broad daylight. Matherfield, you’ve got to find them!”

===Chinese===
一个钟头之后，他们乘车来到马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）的总部门前停了车，进去找他。着人传话之后，马瑟菲尔德被带了来，一见他们便搓起双手。
“来得正是时候！”他嚷道，“我得了消息——是关于安布罗斯（Ambrose）的！”
马瑟菲尔德显然指望来访者听了这项宣布会深感兴趣，即便不至于热切响应；可他一见两人脸上唯有阴郁忧色，不由得诧异地直瞪眼。
“可二位——二位像是得了坏消息！”他叫道，“出岔子了？”
“我倒忘了本可以从里弗斯雷德府（Riversreade Court）给你打个电话。”海瑟维克（Hetherwick）答道，忽然省悟到马瑟菲尔德对当天的事似乎一无所闻，“可我还以为多金（Dorking）警方会打给你。出岔子——可不，这岔子说不定就和安布罗斯有关——我们听到的消息，恰好能和他对上号。是这么回事，”他接着把当天发生的事向马瑟菲尔德简略讲了一遍。“事情就是这样！”他末了说，“我毫不怀疑，巴斯维里（Baseverie）和安布罗斯都掺在这件事里——光天化日之下绑人。马瑟菲尔德，你非得把他们找出来不可！”

===Original===
Matherfield had listened with close attention to Hetherwick’s story, and now he looked from him to Penteney; from Penteney to a printed bill which lay on his desk at his side. “I think I see what all this is about,” he remarked, after a pause. “Those chaps think they’ve got﻿—or they thought they got﻿—Lady Riversreade! To hold for ransom, of course. They took Miss Hannaford because she chanced to be there. What they really kidnapped﻿—and there’s more of that done than you gentlemen might think, I can tell you!﻿—was Lady Riversreade’s sister. But now, however sisters﻿—twin sisters﻿—may closely resemble each other, there comes a time when difference of identity’s bound to come out. By this time﻿—perhaps long before﻿—those men must have discovered that they laid hands on the wrong woman! And the question is﻿—what would they do then?”
“It seems to me that the more immediate question is﻿—where are the two women?” exclaimed Hetherwick. “Think of their danger!”
“Oh, well, Mr. Hetherwick, I don’t suppose they’re in any personal danger,” answered Matherfield. “They’re in the hands of brigands, no doubt, but I don’t think there’ll be any maltreatment of them﻿—set your mind at rest about that. They don’t do that sort of thing nowadays; it’s all done politely and with every consideration, I believe. As to where they are? Why, somewhere in London! And there are over seven millions of other people in London, and hundreds upon hundreds of thousands of inhabited houses﻿—a lot of needles in that bundle of hay, gentlemen!”
“They’ve got to be found!” repeated Hetherwick doggedly. “You’ll have to set all your machinery to work! This can’t﻿—”
“Wait a bit, Hetherwick,” interrupted Penteney. He turned to Matherfield. “You said you had news of this man Ambrose? What news?”

===Chinese===
马瑟菲尔德全神贯注地听海瑟维克讲完，随即目光从他移到彭特尼（Penteney）身上，又从彭特尼移到自己手边书桌上摊着的一张印刷传单。“我想我看明白这一团乱麻的底细了。”他顿了顿，说道，“那伙家伙以为他们抓到了——或者说当初以为抓到了——里弗斯雷德夫人（Lady Riversreade）！自然是要扣着勒索赎金。他们掳走汉纳福德小姐（Miss Hannaford），只因她恰好在场。可他们真正绑架的——我告诉二位，这种勾当干得可比二位想的多得多！——却是里弗斯雷德夫人的妹妹。不过，姐妹俩——孪生姐妹——再如何相像，早晚总有露馅、见真章的时候。到这会儿——也许早在此之前——那伙人必定已经发觉，他们下手抓错了人！那么问题来了——接下来他们会怎么办？”
“依我看，更要紧的问题是——两位女士眼下人在何处？”海瑟维克嚷道，“想想她们的危险！”
“噢，这个嘛，海瑟维克先生，我看她们人身不会有何危险。”马瑟菲尔德答道，“她们无疑是落在了匪徒手里，可我想不至于虐待她们——这一点您尽管宽心。如今他们不兴干那种事了；据我看，一切都办得客客气气、体贴周到。至于人在哪儿？嘿，就在伦敦城里什么地方！可伦敦城里还有七百多万人，几十万户住人的房子——从这么一大捆干草里捞针，二位先生！”
“非把人找到不可！”海瑟维克执拗地重复道，“你得把你那套机器全开动起来！这事不能——”
“稍等一下，海瑟维克。”彭特尼打断了他。他转向马瑟菲尔德。“你说有那个安布罗斯的消息？什么消息？”

===Original===
Matherfield tapped the printed bill which lay on his desk.
“I had that circulated broadcast this morning,” he answered. “And then, of course, the newspapers have helped. Well, not so very long before you came in, I was called to the telephone by a man named Killiner, who told me he was the landlord of the Green Archer Tavern, in Wood Street, Westminster﻿—”
“Westminster again!” exclaimed Hetherwick. “That seems to be the centre point!”
“And a very good thing to have a centre-point, Mr. Hetherwick,” said Matherfield. “When things begin to narrow down, one gets some chance. Well, I was saying﻿—this man rang me up to say that if I’d go down there he thought he could give me some information relative to the bill about the missing man. What he’d got to say, he said, was too long for a telephone talk. I answered that I’d be with him shortly, and I was just setting off when you arrived. Of course, I don’t know what he can tell﻿—it may be nothing, it may be something. Perhaps you gentlemen would like to go with me and hear what it is?”
“I would, but I mustn’t,” replied Penteney. “I must go to my office and hear if Lady Riversreade or the local police have had any fresh news. Keep in touch with me, though, Matherfield﻿—let me know what you hear.”

===Chinese===
马瑟菲尔德敲了敲摊在书桌上的那张印刷传单。
“今天一早我就把这张传单广为散发了。”他答道，“再有，各家报纸自然也帮了忙。喏，就在二位进门之前不大一会儿，一个名叫基利纳（Killiner）的人打电话找我，说他是在威斯敏斯特（Westminster）伍德街（Wood Street）开绿箭手酒馆（Green Archer Tavern）的掌柜——”
“又是威斯敏斯特！”海瑟维克失声道，“那地方看来正是中枢所在！”
“有个中枢所在是大好事，海瑟维克先生。”马瑟菲尔德说，“事情一旦开始收口，人就有了几分指望。喏，我刚才是说——这人打电话来讲，倘若我肯劳驾过去一趟，他想他能提供些与那张寻人传单有关的消息。他说，他要说的事太长，电话里讲不清。我回话说稍候就到，正要动身，二位就来了。当然，他能讲出什么来我不知道——也许什么都没有，也许真有些名堂。二位先生愿不愿随我一道去听听？”
“我倒愿意，可去不得。”彭特尼答道，“我得回办公室去，看看里弗斯雷德夫人或者当地警方有没有新的消息。不过，马瑟菲尔德，要同我保持联络——听到什么就告诉我。”

===Original===
“I’ll go with you,” said Hetherwick. “Westminster!” he muttered again, when Penteney had gone. “It looks as if this man Ambrose was known in that district.”
“Likely!” assented Matherfield. “But you know, Mr. Hetherwick, there are some queer spots in that quarter! People who know the purely ornamental parts of Westminster, such as the Abbey, and the Houses of Parliament, and Victoria Street, and so on, don’t know that there are some fine old slums behind ’em! But I’ll show you when we get down there. We shall go through one or two savoury slices.”
He was putting on his overcoat as he spoke, in readiness for setting out, but before he had buttoned it a constable entered with a card.
“Wants to see you particularly, and at once,” he said. “Waiting outside.”
“Bring him in﻿—straight!” answered Matherfield. He pushed the card along his desk in Hetherwick’s direction. “Lord Morradale!” he exclaimed. “Who’s he?”

===Chinese===
“我跟你去。”海瑟维克说。彭特尼走后，他又喃喃自语：“威斯敏斯特！看这样子，这个安布罗斯在那一带是个熟面孔。”
“多半如此！”马瑟菲尔德附和道，“不过您可知道，海瑟维克先生，那一带有些角落古怪得很！世人熟识的只是威斯敏斯特那些装点门面的去处，诸如威斯敏斯特教堂（the Abbey）、议会大厦（Houses of Parliament）、维多利亚街（Victoria Street）等等，却不知道这些地方背后还藏着几片地道的老贫民窟！等到了那儿，我领您开开眼。咱们还得穿过一两块够味儿的地界呢。”
他一面说话，一面穿上大衣准备动身；还没等扣上扣子，一名警员已拿着一张名片走了进来。
“说有要事非要见您不可，还得马上见。”他说，“正在外头候着。”
“带他进来——立刻！”马瑟菲尔德应声道。他把名片顺着桌面推到海瑟维克跟前。“莫罗代尔勋爵（Lord Morradale）！”他念道，“这是哪一位？”

===Original===
“The man who’s engaged to Madame Listorelle,” replied Hetherwick, in an undertone. “Hampshire magnate.”
Matherfield turned expectantly to the open door. A shortish, stoutish person, who looked more like a typical City man, prosperous and satisfied, came hustling in and gave Hetherwick and his companion a sharp, inquiring glance which finally settled on Matherfield.
“Mr. Matherfield?” he asked. “Just so! I’m Lord Morradale﻿—oh, of course, I sent in my card﻿—just so! Well, Mr. Matherfield, I’ve had an extraordinary communication from Lady Riversreade. She telephoned to my house in Hill Street this morning, but I was down in the City, and didn’t hear of her message till late this afternoon. She says her sister, Madame Listorelle, has been kidnapped! Kidnapped﻿—preposterous!”
“I’m afraid it’s neither preposterous nor improbable, my lord,” answered Matherfield. “I’m quite sure Madame Listorelle has been kidnapped, and Lady Riversreade’s secretary, Miss Featherstone, with her. I’ve been down at Riversreade Court most of the day, and there’s no doubt about it﻿—the two ladies were carried off from there by three men in a fast car, which was driven towards London. That’s a fact!”
“God bless my soul!” exclaimed Lord Morradale. “In broad daylight! Twentieth century, too! And is there no clue?”

===Chinese===
“就是同里斯托雷尔太太（Madame Listorelle）订了婚的那位。”海瑟维克低声答道，“汉普郡的豪绅。”
马瑟菲尔德满怀期待地转向敞开的房门。一个身材略矮、体形略胖的人物急匆匆走了进来，看上去更像伦敦城（the City）里那种典型的买卖人，殷实而心满意足。他朝海瑟维克和他的同伴利落地投来探究的一瞥，最终落在马瑟菲尔德身上。
“马瑟菲尔德先生？”他问，“正是这样！我是莫罗代尔勋爵——哦，对了，我递过名片——正是这样！是这样，马瑟菲尔德先生，我收到里弗斯雷德夫人一个非同寻常的信儿。今天上午她打电话到我在希尔街（Hill Street）的宅邸，可我去了伦敦城，直到今天下午很晚才得知她的口信。她说，她的妹妹里斯托雷尔太太被人绑架了！绑架——简直是岂有此理！”
“恐怕这既不岂有此理，也绝非不可能，大人。”马瑟菲尔德答道，“我确信里斯托雷尔太太是遭了绑架，和她一道被劫的，还有里弗斯雷德夫人的秘书费瑟斯通小姐（Miss Featherstone）。今天大半天我都在里弗斯雷德府，此事确凿无疑——两位女士就是在那里被三个男人用一辆高速汽车劫走的，车子朝伦敦方向开去。这是事实！”
“老天爷！”莫罗代尔勋爵叫了起来，“光天化日之下！还是在二十世纪！难道一点线索也没有？”

===Original===
“None so far, my lord. Of course, we’ve noised the affair as much as possible, and all our people are on the look out. But it’s a difficult case,” continued Matherfield. “The probability is that the ladies have been rushed to some house in London and that they’re there in captivity. Of course, one theory is that the kidnappers took Madame Listorelle for her sister. They meant to get Lady Riversreade and hold her to ransom.”
Lord Morradale pursed his lips. Then he rubbed his chin. Then he shook his head. Finally he gave Hetherwick a shrewd glance, eyeing him from head to foot.
“Um!” he said. “Ah! This gentleman? Not one of your people, I think, Matherfield?”
“No, my lord. This gentleman is Mr. Hetherwick, of the Middle Temple, who is interested very deeply in certain matters connected with the affair. Mr. Hetherwick has been down to Riversreade with me, and your lordship can speak freely before him.”
Lord Morradale gave Hetherwick a friendly, knowing nod. Then he glanced at the door, and Matherfield made haste to close it.

===Chinese===
“到目前为止还没有，大人。当然，我们已经尽力把这事宣扬出去了，我们的人全都留心查访着。不过这案子难办。”马瑟菲尔德接着说，“多半是两位女士被火速转移到伦敦某座宅子里，正被囚禁在那里。当然，有一种推断是，绑匪把里斯托雷尔太太错当成了里弗斯雷德夫人。他们原打算掳走里弗斯雷德夫人，好勒索赎金。”
莫罗代尔勋爵抿了抿嘴唇，跟着摩挲下巴，继而摇头，末了朝海瑟维克精明地瞟了一眼，把他从头到脚打量一番。
“嗯！”他说，“啊！这位先生是？我想，不是您手下的人吧，马瑟菲尔德？”
“不是，大人。这位是中殿律师学院（Middle Temple）的海瑟维克先生，与此案有牵连的几桩事，他涉入极深。海瑟维克先生随我一起去过里弗斯雷德府，大人尽可在他面前直言无妨。”
莫罗代尔勋爵朝海瑟维克友好而会意地点了点头，随后朝房门看了一眼，马瑟菲尔德连忙过去把门关上。

===Original===
“Thank ’ee,” said Lord Morradale. “Just as well to be in private. Um!﻿—I think I’d better tell you something, Matherfield. I dare say that’s a reasonable supposition of yours﻿—that these villains took Madame Listorelle for her sister. But I don’t think they did. I think they knew very well whom they were seizing. Mind you﻿—they’d have seized Lady Riversreade too if she’d happened to be there. But it was madame they were after!”
“If your lordship would explain﻿—” suggested Matherfield.
“I’m going to﻿—it’s what I came here for! I think I can just put you on the right scent. You may have heard that Madame Listorelle and I are about to marry? Very well, I accordingly knew a good deal about her affairs. Now, I don’t know whether you know or not that Madame Listorelle is actively concerned﻿—or has been﻿—in buying and selling jewels on commission? That’s her speciality.”
“Heard something of it, my lord,” replied Matherfield.
“Very well. Now, quite recently Madame Listorelle bought up in Paris a magnificent set of stones which had been at one time the property of a member of the Russian Imperial family. She brought them here to London, meaning, shortly, either to send or take them personally to America to her customer. This deal, unfortunately, got into the papers. Now, it’s my belief that these fellows have kidnapped madame in order to get hold of these jewels. Do you see?”

===Chinese===
“多谢您嘞。”莫罗代尔勋爵说，“关起门说话最好。嗯！——我看我最好告诉你们一件事，马瑟菲尔德。你那个猜想——说那伙恶棍错把里斯托雷尔太太当成了里弗斯雷德夫人——不能说不近情理。可依我看，事情不是这样。我认为，他们对自己下手劫夺的是谁，心里清楚得很。你听着——倘若里弗斯雷德夫人碰巧也在场，他们照样会把她一并抓走。可他们图谋的，正是这位太太！”
“大人可否明示——”马瑟菲尔德从旁提示。
“我正要说——我就是为这个来的！我想我恰好能把你们引上正路。你们或许听说过，我同里斯托雷尔太太就要结婚了？很好，因此之故，她的事务我知道得不少。喏，你们知不知道，里斯托雷尔太太一直在做珠宝代购代销的生意——或者说从前在做？这是她的专门行当。”
“略有耳闻，大人。”马瑟菲尔德答道。
“很好。喏，就在不久之前，里斯托雷尔太太在巴黎买进了一批成色极佳的宝石，这批宝石从前是俄罗斯皇室（Russian Imperial family）一位成员的产业。她把它们带到了伦敦，打算近日或者寄出、或者亲自送往美国，交给她的买主。不幸的是，这笔买卖走漏到了报纸上。喏，依我看，那伙家伙绑架这位太太，就是为了攫取这批珠宝。二位明白吗？”

===Original===
“Ah!” exclaimed Matherfield. “I see, my lord! That puts a new aspect on the case. But﻿—surely Madame Listorelle wouldn’t have the stones on her?”
Lord Morradale winked﻿—deliberately﻿—at both his hearers.
“No!” he said. “No﻿—she wouldn’t. But the scoundrels would figure on this﻿—that when she was fairly in their power they would be in a position to make her give them up﻿—to force her, in short, to disclose their whereabouts. If they’re desperate villains, not likely to stick at anything, I think they’ll have forced madame to compliance﻿—and in doing so give you a chance to lay hands on them!”
“How, my lord?” asked Matherfield eagerly.
Lord Morradale gave the two men a confidential glance.

===Chinese===
“啊！”马瑟菲尔德失声道，“我明白了，大人！这样一来，案子的面目就全然一新了。可是——里斯托雷尔太太总不至于把宝石随身带着吧？”
莫罗代尔勋爵有意地、不慌不忙地朝两位听客眨了眨眼。
“不至于！”他说，“不至于——她不会随身带着。可那帮恶棍打的算盘是——等她彻底落入他们掌心，他们便能使她交出宝石——一句话，逼她供出宝石的下落。这伙人若是无所顾忌的亡命之徒，我想他们必已逼得这位太太就范——而这样一来，也就给了你们下手擒拿他们的机会！”
“怎么讲，大人？”马瑟菲尔德急切地问。
莫罗代尔勋爵朝两人投来推心置腹的一瞥。

===Original===
“This way,” he replied. “The jewels were deposited, for safety, by Madame Listorelle at the Imperial Safe Deposit. She rents a safe there. Now, don’t you see what I’m suggesting? These men may force her to give them the necessary key and a signed order to the safe people to let the bearer open madame’s safe and take away a certain case in which the jewels are packed. That’s what I think will be done. And what you ought to do is to see the Imperial Safe Deposit officials at once, warn them of what I suggest may happen, and take your own means of watching for such a messenger arriving, and for tracking him when he departs. Eh?”
“Or arresting him there and then,” said Matherfield.
“No, I shouldn’t!” declared Lord Morradale. “I’m not a policeman, you know, but I can give a hint to one. Instead of arresting the man﻿—who, you must remember, will be sure to have madame’s written authority on him﻿—that is if things turn out as I suggest﻿—I should carefully follow him. For﻿—he’ll probably go back to where madame and the young lady, Miss What’s-her-name, are detained! Eh?”
Matherfield shook his head.
“I should doubt that, my lord!” he answered. “If things work out as you suggest﻿—and it’s a highly probable theory﻿—that’s about the last thing he would do! Once the jewels were in his possession﻿—”

===Chinese===
“是这样。”他答道，“为求稳妥，里斯托雷尔太太把珠宝存放在了帝国保险保管库（Imperial Safe Deposit）。她在那里租着一个保管箱。喏，二位明白我的提示了吗？那伙人可能逼她交出必需的钥匙，外加一纸签字的手令，命保管库的人准许持令人开启太太的保管箱，取走那只装珠宝的匣子。我看事情就会这样办。而你们该做的是：立刻去见帝国保险保管库的负责人，把我提示的可能发生的情形知会他们，再自行布置手段，监视这样一名来人到达，并在他离去时加以跟踪。怎么样？”
“或者当场把他逮住。”马瑟菲尔德说。
“不，我不这么办！”莫罗代尔勋爵断然道，“要知道，我不是警察，不过我能给当警察的提个醒儿。与其逮捕那个人——你们务必记住，他身上必定带着太太亲笔签发的手令——倘若事情照我估计的发展——不如小心跟踪他。因为——他多半会回到太太和那位年轻小姐——那位叫什么名字来着的小姐——被关押的地方去！怎么样？”
马瑟菲尔德摇了摇头。
“这一点我可得存疑，大人！”他答道，“倘若事情真照您说的那样发展——这推断极有可能——那却是他最不可能做的事！一旦珠宝到了他手里——”

===Original===
“You forget this,” interrupted Lord Morradale. “They may use a catspaw! Eh?”
“Well, there’s that in it, certainly,” assented Matherfield. “However, I’ll see that the Imperial Safe Deposit people are warned and that this entrance is carefully watched tomorrow morning. But﻿—the thing may have been done already! There’s been plenty of time since the ladies were carried off.”
“No!” said Lord Morradale. “Nothing’s happened so far. I called in at the Imperial Safe Deposit as I came here; they had neither seen Madame Listorelle nor had any communication from her today. And now the place is closed for the night.”
“Did you warn them, then?” inquired Matherfield.
“I didn’t. I thought it best to see you first,” replied Lord Morradale. “The warning and the rest of it will come best from you.”

===Chinese===
“你忘了这一层。”莫罗代尔勋爵打断他，“他们兴许会找个当枪使的人！怎么样？”
“嗯，这话固然在理。”马瑟菲尔德附和道，“不管怎样，我会让帝国保险保管库那边得到警示，明天一早派人对那个入口严加监视。可是——这件事也许早已办完了！两位女士被劫走至今，时间绰绰有余。”
“没有！”莫罗代尔勋爵说，“到目前为止什么都没发生。我来这儿途中顺道去了一趟帝国保险保管库；他们既没见过里斯托雷尔太太，今天也没收到她的任何音信。眼下那里已经闭门歇夜了。”
“那您警示过他们了？”马瑟菲尔德问道。
“没有。我想最好还是先来见你。”莫罗代尔勋爵答道，“这类警示连同后续的安排，由你们出面最为妥当。”

===Original===
“Very good, my lord. Much obliged to your lordship for looking in,” said Matherfield. “We’ll keep you posted up in anything that happens﻿—at Hill Street. Now,” he continued, when Lord Morradale had left the office, “we’ll get along to Westminster, Mr. Hetherwick, to the Green Archer and its landlord, Killiner.”
The Green Archer proved to be a respectable tavern which boasted a saloon bar. Behind the glass screens of this they found a middle-aged, sharp-eyed man, who at the sight of his visitors immediately opened the door of a parlour in the rear and ushered them into privacy. He pointed silently to a copy of the bill asking for news of Ambrose.
“Aye!” said Matherfield. “Just so. I had your message. You think you know this man?”
“From this description of him in that bill, yes,” replied the landlord. “I think he’s a man﻿—gentleman, by all appearances﻿—who used to come into my saloon bar pretty regularly during this last six months. Since the end of last summer, I should say, up to about three weeks or so ago.”
“Not since then, eh?” asked Matherfield. “Three weeks?”

===Chinese===
“很好，大人。多谢大人屈驾光临。”马瑟菲尔德说，“一有任何动静，我们都随时向大人禀报——送到希尔街去。”莫罗代尔勋爵离开办公室后，他接着说道：“现在，海瑟维克先生，咱们这就上威斯敏斯特去，去绿箭手酒馆，见它的掌柜基利纳。”
那绿箭手酒馆原来是家体面酒馆，还自设一间雅座酒吧。在酒吧的玻璃隔屏后面，他们见到一个目光锐利的中年人；此人一见来客，立刻打开后面一间雅室的门，把他们让进僻静处。他一言不发，指了指一份征集安布罗斯消息的传单。
“是啊！”马瑟菲尔德说，“正是。我收到你的口信了。你认为你认得这个人？”
“照那张传单上对他的描述看，认得。”掌柜答道，“我想有这么一个人——看那做派是个上流人士——过去这六个月里，到我雅座酒吧来得很规律。依我说，从去年夏末起，直到大约三个星期之前。”
“打那以后就没再来过，嗯？”马瑟菲尔德问，“三个星期？”

===Original===
“About that. No﻿—he hasn’t been in for quite that. But up to then he’d been in, well, four or five days a week. Handsome, fine man﻿—in fact, you’ve described him exactly there. I never knew who he was﻿—used to pass the time o’ day with him, you know, but that was all. He always came in about the same time﻿—one to one-thirty. He’d have sometimes a glass of bitter ale and a sandwich or two; sometimes a whisky and soda and two or three biscuits. Stood and had his snack and went away. Never talked much. I took him for some gentleman that had business hereabouts, and just wanted a bite and a sup in the middle of the day, and turned in here for it. But I don’t know what business he could be concerned in round here. He hadn’t the tradesman’s look on him, you understand. I should have said he was a professional man of some sort. Always very well dressed, you know﻿—smart. However, I did notice one peculiar thing about him.”
“What now?” asked Matherfield. “It all helps!”
“Well,” said the landlord, “I noticed that his hands and fingers were stained﻿—all sorts of colours. Sometimes it was more noticeable than at others. But there it was.”
“Um!” remarked Matherfield. He exchanged a knowing glance with Hetherwick. And when, a few minutes later, they left the tavern, he turned to him with an air of assurance. “I’m beginning to feel the end!” he said. “Feel it, if I don’t see it. Stained fingers, eh? We’ve heard of them before, Mr. Hetherwick. And I’ll tell ye what it is. Somewhere about this very spot there’s some place where men are dabbling﻿—secretly, I should think﻿—with chemicals, and Ambrose is one of ’em, and perhaps Baseverie another, and it was there that Hannaford and that man Grannet had been that night, and where they were poisoned﻿—and there, too, no doubt, these two ladies are at this minute! Well﻿—come to my place first thing in the morning.”
Hetherwick, at a loss what to do further that night, went away and dined, and, that done, strolled home to his chambers. There was a light in his parlour, and when he opened the door he found Mapperley, evidently awaiting him, and with Mapperley a curly-headed, big-nosed, beady-eyed young Jew.

===Chinese===
“差不多吧。是啊——有那么些日子没来了。可在那之前他常来，怎么说呢，一星期来个四五天。英俊体面的男子——说实在的，你们传单上把他描述得分毫不差。我从来不知道他是谁——往常跟他寒暄两句，您知道，也就到此为止。他总在差不多同一钟点来——一点到一点半之间。有时喝一杯苦啤酒，吃一两个三明治；有时一份威士忌苏打，两三块饼干。站着吃完那点东西就走。话从来不多。我原先当他是位在这一带办事的绅士，中午只想垫一口、喝一口，便拐进我这里来。可我想不出他能在这一带有什么营生。他身上没有买卖人的派头，您明白吧。要我说，他是干某种专业行当的人。穿着总是十分讲究——利落。不过，我倒确实留意到他一桩特别之处。”
“什么特别之处？”马瑟菲尔德问，“说什么都有帮助！”
“是这样，”掌柜说，“我留意到他的手上、手指上沾着斑斑渍渍——五颜六色。有时候显眼些，有时候不那么显眼。可总归是有的。”
“嗯！”马瑟菲尔德沉吟道。他与海瑟维克交换了一个心照不宣的眼色。几分钟后两人离开酒馆，他转身朝着海瑟维克，一副胸有成竹的神气。“我隐约摸到头绪了！”他说，“看不见，可摸得着。染了色的手指，嗯？这号事咱们从前就听说过，海瑟维克先生。我告诉你这是怎么一回事。就在此地附近的什么地方，有一处所在，有人在摆弄——我琢磨是偷偷摸摸地——摆弄化学药剂，安布罗斯就是其中之一，巴斯维里兴许又是一个；那晚汉纳福德和那个叫格兰内特（Grannet）的人去的就是那儿，他们就是在那儿中的毒——而且，毫无疑问，这会儿那两位女士也在那儿！好啦——明儿一早头一件事，上我那儿去。”
海瑟维克那晚一时想不出还能再做些什么，便离去用了晚饭；饭毕，信步走回自己的寓所。他起居室里亮着灯，推门一看，只见马珀利（Mapperley）等在那儿，分明是专候他回来；马珀利身边还有一个年轻人——卷头发，大鼻子，一双眼睛滴溜溜亮，是个犹太人。

## XXV Dead! / 二十五 死了！

===Original===
The rest of the searchers, hearing that startled cry from the Jew, with one accord made for the upper part of the building. Robmore and Hetherwick reached him first; he was standing at the half-opened door of a room, into which he was staring with eager eyes. They pushed by him and entered.

Hetherwick took in the general aspect and contents of that room at a glance. It had been fitted up﻿—recently, he thought, from certain small evidences﻿—as a bed-sitting-room. A camp-bed stood in one corner; there was a washstand, a dressing table, a chest of drawers, two or three pictures, a shelf of books, a small square of carpet in the centre of the floor, the outer edges of which had been roughly and newly stained. On the bed lay, open, a suitcase, already packed with clothes and linen; by it lay an overcoat, hat, gloves, umbrella; it was evident that the man to whom it belonged had completed his preparations for a departure, and had nothing to do but to close and lock the suitcase, put on his overcoat and hat, pick up the other things and go away.

But the man himself? There was a big, old-fashioned easy chair at the side of the bed﻿—a roomy, comfortable affair. A man lay, rather than sat, in it, in an attitude which suggested that he had dropped there as with a sudden weariness, laid his head back against the padded cushion, and﻿—gone to sleep. But the men knew, all of them, as they crowded into that room, that it was no sleep that they had broken in upon﻿—it was death. This, as the Jew had been quick to see, was a dead man﻿—dead!

Hetherwick took him in as quickly as he had taken in his surroundings. His head lay quietly against the padding of the chair, a little inclined to his left shoulder: the face was fully visible. It was﻿—to Hetherwick﻿—the face of a stranger; in all his and Matherfield’s investigations it had not been described to them. Yet he was certain that he was looking on the man known to them by repute as Ambrose. Disguised, of course﻿—he had shaved off the dark beard and moustache of which they had heard, and he could see at once that the loss of them had made a remarkable difference in his appearance. But nothing could disguise his height and general build. This, without doubt, was the man Matherfield and he had hunted for, the man who had met Hannaford at Victoria, who had disappeared from his flat in the Adelphi﻿—the man who was associated with Baseverie, and who﻿—

“Dead as a doornail!” muttered Robmore, bending close to the still figure. “And﻿—he’s been dead a good bit, too!﻿—some hours, anyway. Stiff! Do ye know him, Mr. Hetherwick?”

Hetherwick said what he thought. Robmore pointed to the things on the bed.
===Chinese===
其余搜查的人听见犹太人那声惊呼，不约而同地朝房子的上层奔去。罗布莫尔（Robmore）和海瑟维克（Hetherwick）最先赶到他跟前；他正站在一间房门半掩的屋子门口，急切地朝里面张望。两人从他身旁挤过去，进了屋。

海瑟维克一眼便把这间屋子的总体格局和陈设看了个大概。屋子是新近——他从若干细小的迹象上这么判断——布置成卧室兼起居室的。角落里支着一张行军床；屋里有洗脸架、梳妆台、一口五斗橱，两三幅画，一架书，地板正中铺着一小块地毯，地毯四外的新边还粗粗地染过色。床上摊开着一只小提箱，已经装满了衣裳和衬单；旁边搁着大衣、帽子、手套、雨伞；显而易见，箱子的主人已把动身的准备料理停当，只消合上箱子落了锁，穿上大衣、戴上帽子，拿起其余的东西，便可动身了。

可是人本人呢？床边摆着一把老式大安乐椅——宽宽绰绰、舒舒服服的一件家什。一个男人与其说是坐在里面，不如说是瘫在里面，那姿势像是他猛然一阵困乏，便倒进椅中，头往后靠在软垫上——睡着了。可是挤进屋来的这些人个个心里明白，他们闯进来搅破的不是睡眠——是死亡。正如那个犹太人一眼便看出的，这是个死人——死了！

海瑟维克打量这个人，同方才打量四周陈设一样快。那颗头安安静静地靠在椅子的软垫上，微微偏向左肩：那张脸整个露在外面。在海瑟维克眼里，这是一张陌生的脸；在他和马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）的历次查访之中，从没有人向他们描述过它。然而他确信，自己正望着的，就是那位他们仅闻其名的安布罗斯（Ambrose）。自然是乔装过的——他已经剃掉了他们听说过的那部黑胡子和髭须，而且海瑟维克一眼便看出，这一剃，此人的相貌大为改观。可是无论怎样乔装，也遮掩不住他的身高和大致体格。毫无疑问，这正是马瑟菲尔德和他一直追捕的那个人——在维多利亚（Victoria）车站见过汉纳福德（Hannaford）的人，从阿德尔菲（Adelphi）的公寓里销声匿迹的人——同巴斯维里（Baseverie）有勾连的人，而且——

“死得透透的了！”罗布莫尔俯身凑近那纹丝不动的身躯，喃喃道。“而且——他死得有些时候了！——起码好几个钟头。都僵了！你认得他吗，海瑟维克先生？”

海瑟维克说出了自己的想法。罗布莫尔指了指床上那些东西。

===Original===
“Looks as if he’d been taken with a seizure just as he was about to set off somewhere,” he remarked. “Well, if this is the Dr. Ambrose we’ve been seeking﻿—but let’s see if he’s got anything on him to prove his identity.”

While the rest of the men stood by watching, he put his hand into the dead man’s inside breast pocket﻿—he was wearing a smart, brand-new grey tweed suit, Hetherwick, later on, remembered how its newness struck him as being incongruously out of place, somehow﻿—and drew out a pocketbook. Touching Hetherwick’s elbow and motioning him to follow him, he went over to the window, leaving the others still staring wonderingly at the dead man.

“This is a queer business, Mr. Hetherwick,” he whispered as they drew apart. “You think this is the Dr. Ambrose we were after?”

“Sure of it!” answered Hetherwick. “He’s shaved off his beard and moustache, and that’s no doubt made a big difference in his appearance, but you may depend on it, this is the man! But what’s caused his sudden death?”

Then a keen, vivid recollection flashed up in him, and he turned sharply, glancing at the rigid figure in the background.

“What is it?” asked Robmore curiously. “Something strikes you?”
===Chinese===
“瞧这样子，倒像是他正要动身去什么地方，忽然犯了病，”他说。“呃，要是这位就是我们一直在找的安布罗斯医生——那就瞧瞧他身上有没有什么能证明身份的东西。”

其余的人站在一旁看着，他把手伸进死者的上衣内袋——死者穿一身体面的崭新灰色花呢西服，海瑟维克后来还记得，那身新衣当时给他的感觉就是不知怎的格格不入——掏出一本皮夹子。他碰了碰海瑟维克的胳膊肘，示意他跟过来，便走向窗口，留下其余人还在那儿怔怔地盯着死人。

“这可是件蹊跷事，海瑟维克先生，”两人走到一旁时他低声道。“你认为这就是我们追捕的安布罗斯医生？”

“十拿九稳！”海瑟维克答道。“他剃了胡子和髭须，相貌无疑大变，可你尽管放心，就是这个人！不过，他怎么会暴毙的？”

这时，一段鲜明生动的记忆在他心头闪起，他猛地转过身，朝后面那僵直的身形瞥了一眼。

“怎么回事？”罗布莫尔好奇地问。“想起什么了？”

===Original===
Hetherwick pointed to the dead man’s attitude.

“That’s﻿—that’s just how Hannaford looked when he died in the railway carriage!” he whispered. “After the first signs﻿—you know﻿—he laid back and﻿—died. Just like that﻿—as if he’d dropped quietly asleep. Can﻿—can it be that﻿—”

“I know what you’re thinking,” muttered Robmore. “Poisoned! Well﻿—what about﻿—eh﻿—the other man?”

“Baseverie!” exclaimed Hetherwick.

“Why not?﻿—to rid himself of an accomplice! But﻿—this pocketbook,” said Robmore. “Let’s see what’s in it. Doesn’t seem to be anything very much, by the thinness.”

From one flap of the pocketbook he drew out a wad of carefully-folded bank notes, and rapidly turned them over.
===Chinese===
海瑟维克指了指死者的姿势。

“那——汉纳福德死在火车车厢里的时候，就是那副样子！”他低声道。“初起的征兆过后——你知道的——他往后一仰——就死了。就像这样——仿佛安安静静地睡过去了。莫——莫非是——”

“我知道你在想什么，”罗布莫尔喃喃道。“中毒了！那么——呃——那另一个人呢？”

“巴斯维里！”海瑟维克失声道。

“怎么不会？——好甩掉一个同伙！不过——这皮夹子，”罗布莫尔说。“瞧瞧里头有什么。就这薄薄的样子，料想也多不到哪儿去。”

他从皮夹子的一层夹层里抽出一沓叠得整整齐齐的钞票，飞快地翻检了一遍。

===Original===
“Hundred and fifty pounds there,” he remarked. “And what’s this paper﻿—a draft on a New York bank for two hundred. New York, eh? So that’s where he was bound? And this,” he went on, turning out the other flap. “Ah! see this, Mr. Hetherwick? He’d got his passage booked by the *Maratic*, sailing tonight. Um! And Matherfield’s gone to Southampton, after Baseverie. I’m beginning to see a bit into this, I think.”

“What do you see?” asked Hetherwick.

“Well, it looks to me as if Baseverie had gone ahead to collect that box containing the jewels, and that Ambrose was to follow later, join him there, when Baseverie had secured the loot, and that they were then to be off with their harvest! But﻿—do you notice this﻿—the name under which the passage is booked? Not Ambrose﻿—Charles Andrews, Esquire. Andrews! And Baseverie is Basing. Basing and Andrews. Now I wonder if they carried on business here under these names?”

“That’s an unimportant detail,” said Hetherwick. “The important thing, surely, is﻿—that! How did that man come by his death?”

“Well, but I don’t think that is very important﻿—just now,” replied Robmore. “After all, he is dead, and whether he died as the result of a sudden seizure, or whether Baseverie cleverly poisoned him before he left, is a question we’ll have to settle later. But I’ll tell you what, Mr. Hetherwick﻿—I’ll lay anything he didn’t poison himself! Look round﻿—there isn’t a sign of anything he’s been drinking out of. No, sir﻿—the other man’s done this. And if Matherfield has the luck to lay hands on him tonight﻿—ah! But now, what was this your clerk, Mapperley, told us as we came along about the Little Smith Street landlady coming here this afternoon?”

“She was followed here by Goldmark,” replied Hetherwick. “Goldmark saw her admit herself by a key which she took from her pocket. She stayed inside a few minutes, came out looking much upset, and hurried away to her own house.”
===Chinese===
“这里头有一百五十镑，”他说。“这张纸是什么——一张向纽约一家银行兑付的汇票，票面二百镑。纽约，嗯？原来他要去的是那儿？还有这个，”他翻出另一层夹层，继续说道。“啊！瞧见没有，海瑟维克先生？他订好了*“马拉蒂克”号*（*Maratic*）的船票，今晚启航。嗯！马瑟菲尔德倒是上南安普顿（Southampton）追巴斯维里去了。我想，这桩案子的内情，我算是开始瞧出几分门道了。”

“你瞧出什么了？”海瑟维克问。

“这么说吧，依我看，是巴斯维里先走一步，去取那口装珠宝的箱子，安布罗斯随后再去，在那儿同他碰头；等巴斯维里把赃物弄到手，两人便带着这票收获远走高飞！不过——你留意到没有——船票是用什么名字订的？不是安布罗斯——是查尔斯·安德鲁斯（Charles Andrews）先生。安德鲁斯！而巴斯维里化名贝辛（Basing）。贝辛和安德鲁斯。不知他们在这里做生意，用的是不是也是这两个名字？”

“那是个无关紧要的细节，”海瑟维克说。“要紧的当然是——那个！那个人是怎么死的？”

“这个嘛，我倒不觉得眼下有多么要紧，”罗布莫尔答道。“人反正已经死了；究竟是突然犯了病，还是巴斯维里临走之前巧下毒手，这个问题只好留待日后弄清。不过我跟你说，海瑟维克先生——他绝不是自己服毒，这一点拿什么打赌我都肯！你四下里瞧瞧——连一点他喝过什么东西的影子都没有。不，先生——这是另外那个人干的。要是马瑟菲尔德今晚运气好，能把他逮住——啊！对了，咱们来的路上，你那位办事员马珀利（Mapperley）说的是怎么回事——小史密斯街（Little Smith Street）的房东太太今天下午来过这儿？”

“戈德马克（Goldmark）跟着她到了这儿，”海瑟维克答道。“戈德马克看见她掏出钥匙，自己开了门进去。她在里头待了几分钟，出来时神色大变，急急忙忙回自己家去了。”

===Original===
“And now you and I’ll just hurry after her,” said Robmore. “After all, she’s living, and we’ll make her find her tongue. Of course, she came in here expecting to find this man, and to tell him somebody was on the lookout. And﻿—she found him dead! Come round there with me, Mr. Hetherwick, at once.”

He turned to the other detective and the constable, and after giving them some whispered instructions, left the room, Hetherwick, after a word or two with Mapperley, following him. But before they had reached the outer door, they heard steps in the yard, and suddenly two men appeared in the doorway.

If Hetherwick and his companion looked questioningly at these two men, they, on their part, looked questioningly at Robmore and Hetherwick. They were youngish men﻿—Hetherwick set them down as respectably-dressed artisans. That they were surprised to find anyone confronting them at the door whereat all four now stood, was evident; their surprise, indeed, was so great that they came to a sudden halt, staring silently. But Robmore spoke. “Wanting somebody?” he asked sharply.

The two strangers exchanged a glance, and the apparently elder one replied:

“Well, no!” he said. “Not that we know of. But might we ask if you are? And how you got in here? Because this place happens to be ours!”

“Yours!” exclaimed Robmore. “Your property?”
===Chinese===
“这下就该你我赶紧追她去了，”罗布莫尔说。“她到底是个活人，咱们自有法子叫她开口。不用说，她进来是指望见到这个人，告诉他有人在盯梢。结果——她发现他死了！马上跟我到她那儿走一趟，海瑟维克先生。”

他转向另一名侦探和那名警士，低声交代了几句，便出了屋子；海瑟维克同马珀利说了两句话，也跟着走了。可两人还没走到临街的大门，就听见院子里有脚步声，猛可里两个人影出现在门口。

若说海瑟维克和他的同伴满脸疑问地打量这两个人，对方也同样满脸疑问地打量罗布莫尔和海瑟维克。两人都还年轻——海瑟维克断定他们是衣着体面的工匠。此刻四个人同站在那扇门前，那两位撞见有人迎面堵着，显然吃了一惊；他们的惊愕着实不小，竟猛地收住脚步，一言不发地瞪视着。倒是罗布莫尔先开了口。“找什么人？”他厉声问。

两个陌生人交换了一下眼色，看去年长些的那个答道：

“呃，不是！”他说。“据我们所知，没有。可我们能不能问问，你们二位是来找什么的？又是怎么进来的？因为这地方碰巧是我们的！”

“你们的！”罗布莫尔失声道。“你们的产业？”

===Original===
“Well, if buying it, paying for it, and taking a receipt and papers makes it so!” answered the man. “Bought it this morning﻿—and settled up for it, too, anyway.”

Robmore produced and handed over a professional card, and the faces of the two men fell as they read it. The elder looked up quickly.

“I hope there’s nothing wrong?” he said anxiously. “Detectives, eh? We’ve laid out a nice bit on this﻿—savings, too, and﻿—”

“I don’t suppose there’s anything wrong that way,” replied Robmore reassuringly. “But there’s something uncommonly wrong in other ways. Now look here, who are you two, and from whom did you buy this place?”

“My name’s Marshall, his is Wilkinson,” answered the leader. “We’re just starting business for ourselves as electrical engineers. We advertised for a likely place hereabouts, and Mr. Andrews came to us about this﻿—said he and his partner, Mr. Basing, were leaving, and wanted to sell it, just as it stood. We came to look at it, and as it’s just the place we need to start with, we agreed to buy it. They said it was their own property, and to save law expenses we carried out the purchase between ourselves. And we paid over the purchase money this morning, and got the papers and the key.”

“What time was that?” asked Robmore.
===Chinese===
“这个嘛，要是出钱买了、付了款、又拿了收据和契纸，它就算数的话！”那人答道。“今儿早上买的——反正款项也一笔付清了。”

罗布莫尔取出一张名片递过去，两人念着名片，脸都沉了下来。年长的那个倏地抬起头。

“但愿没出什么岔子吧？”他不安地说。“是侦探，呃？我们在这上头撂下了不小的一笔钱——连积蓄都搭上了，而且——”

“那一头我想不会有什么问题，”罗布莫尔宽慰道。“可是别的地方出了大大的乱子。听着，你们俩是什么人？这地方又是从谁手里买的？”

“我叫马歇尔（Marshall），他叫威尔金森（Wilkinson），”为首的答道。“我们正打算自己开张，做电气工程师。我们登了广告，要在这一带找个合式的铺面，这位安德鲁斯先生便来找我们——说他和他合伙人贝辛先生要走了，想把这地方原封不动、照现状一总卖掉。我们来看过，正好是起手开业的合式地方，便讲定价钱买下。他们说是他们自己的产业；为省下律师公费，我们两家私下把这笔买卖办妥了。今儿早上我们交清了价款，拿到了契纸和钥匙。”

“那是几点钟的事？”罗布莫尔问。

===Original===
“Ten o’clock or thereabouts,” replied Marshall. “By appointment, here.”

“Did ye see both men﻿—Basing and Andrews?”

“Both! In that little room to the right. We settled the business﻿—paid them in cash﻿—and settled all up. It was soon done, then they stood us a drink and a cigar, and we went.”

“Stood you a drink, eh?” said Robmore suddenly. “Where?”

“Here! Basing, he pulled out a big bottle of champagne and a cigar-box, and said we’d wet the bargain. We’d a glass apiece, Wilkinson and me, then we left ’em to finish the bottle: we were in a hurry. But﻿—is anything wrong?”
===Chinese===
“十点钟上下，”马歇尔答道。“事先约好的，就在这儿。”

“两个人——贝辛和安德鲁斯——你都见着了？”

“都见着了！就在右边那间小屋里。我们把买卖办妥——付的现款——一切两讫。一会儿就办完了，随后他们请我们喝了杯酒、抽了支雪茄，我们就走了。”

“请你们喝了杯酒，呃？”罗布莫尔突然问道。“在哪儿喝的？”

“就在这儿！贝辛，他掏出一大瓶香槟和一匣雪茄，说要庆贺成交。威尔金森和我一人一杯，随后就把瓶子留给他们两个去喝完：我们赶时间。可是——出了什么事吗？”

===Original===
“What is wrong, my lad, is that the man you know as Andrews is lying dead upstairs!” replied Robmore. “Poisoned, most likely, by his partner. But, as I said just now, I don’t suppose there’s anything wrong about your buying the property, providing you can show a title to it; you say you’ve got the necessary papers?”

Marshall clapped a hand on the pocket of his coat.

“Got ’em all here, now,” he said. “But﻿—did you say Andrews was dead﻿—poisoned? Why, he was as alive as I am when we left the two of ’em together. They were finishing the bottle﻿—”

“Look here,” interrupted Robmore. “Wait awhile until we come back﻿—we’ve some important work close by. There are people of ours upstairs﻿—tell them I said you were to wait a bit. Now, Mr. Hetherwick.”

Outside the yard and in the crowded street, Robmore turned to his companion with a cynical laugh.
===Chinese===
“出的这个事嘛，小伙子，就是你们认作安德鲁斯的那个人，正死在楼上呢！”罗布莫尔答道。“十有八九，是被他合伙人毒死的。不过，方才我说了，你们买产业这一层，我看不会有什么问题，只要你们拿得出产权凭据；你们说，必要的契纸都在手上？”

马歇尔一巴掌按在外衣口袋上。

“全在这儿呢，这会儿，”他说。“可是——你说安德鲁斯死了——被人毒死的？嗳，我们撇下他们两个的时候，他还活蹦活跳的，跟我一个样。他们正把那瓶酒往完里喝——”

“听我说，”罗布莫尔打断他。“等一等，等我们回来——我们就在左近有要紧公务。楼上有我们的人——告诉他们，是我说的，叫你们稍候片刻。走吧，海瑟维克先生。”

出了院子，走到人烟稠密的大街上，罗布莫尔转向他的同伴，发出一声冷冷的笑。

===Original===
“Champagne﻿—to wet the bargain!” he said. “Left them to finish it, eh? And no doubt what finished Ambrose was in that champagne﻿—slipped in by Baseverie when his back was turned. I’ll tell you what it is, Mr. Hetherwick, that chap’s a thorough-paced ’un﻿—he goes the whole hog! I only hope he won’t be too deep for Matherfield at Southampton! I shall be anxious till I hear.”

“Is it possible for him to escape Matherfield?” exclaimed Hetherwick. “How can he? I look on him as being as good as in custody already! He’s bound to call at the post office for that box.”

“Is he, though?” interrupted the detective, with another incredulous laugh. “I’m not so sure about that, Mr. Hetherwick. Baseverie is evidently an accomplished scoundrel, and full of all sorts of tricks! I’ll tell ye what I’m wondering﻿—will that parcel ever get to Southampton post office, where it’s to be called for?”

“Whatever do you mean?” demanded Hetherwick. “It’s in the post! Posted this morning.”

“No doubt,” agreed Robmore dryly. “By special delivery, eh? And when it gets to Southampton Station, it’s got to be taken to the head post office, hasn’t it?”
===Chinese===
“香槟——庆贺成交！”他说。“把瓶子留给他们喝完，呃？没错，要了安布罗斯性命的就是那香槟——巴斯维里趁他转身不备下的药。我跟你说句实在话，海瑟维克先生，这家伙是个彻头彻尾的狠角色——一出手就做绝！我只盼他在南安普顿别把马瑟菲尔德耍得团团转！不听到消息，我这颗心是放不下的。”

“他还能从马瑟菲尔德手里逃掉？”海瑟维克失声道。“那怎么可能？依我看，他已经跟落网没两样了！他必定要到邮局去取那口箱子。”

“是吗？”侦探插嘴道，又发出一声不信的笑。“这个我可不那么有把握，海瑟维克先生。巴斯维里分明是个手段老练的恶棍，满肚子鬼点子！我在琢磨一件事——那件包裹究竟到得了到不了南安普顿邮局——那可是指定去领取的地方啊。”

“你这话到底什么意思？”海瑟维克追问道。“东西已经在邮路上了！今儿早上寄出去的。”

“那是自然，”罗布莫尔干巴巴地应道。“走快递寄的，呃？可它到了南安普顿车站以后，总得再送到邮政总局去，是不是？”

===Original===
“Well?” asked Hetherwick.

“There’s many a slip twixt cup and lip﻿—so the old saying goes,” replied Robmore. “That parcel may slip. But isn’t this the number your clerk mentioned?”

The door of Mrs. Mallett’s house looked more closely barred than ever﻿—if possible. And no answer came to several summonses by bell and knocker. But presently Robmore tried the handle﻿—the door opened at his touch.

“Hallo!” he exclaimed. “Open! Um! That seems a bit queer. Well﻿—inside!”

For the second time that afternoon, Hetherwick walked into a place that seemed to be wholly deserted.
===Chinese===
“那又怎样？”海瑟维克问。

“杯到嘴边还会洒呢——老话说得好，”罗布莫尔答道。“那件包裹就可能在半路上出岔子。不过——你那位办事员提起的门牌，可不正是这一家？”

马利特太太（Mrs. Mallett）家的那扇门，看上去关得比先前更加严实——倘若还能更严实的话。门铃拉了好几遍，门环敲了好几遍，都没人应。可是不多时，罗布莫尔试了试门把——门应手而开。

“喂！”他失声道。“开着！嗯！这可有点蹊跷。好——进去！”

那天下午，海瑟维克已是第二次走进一处看上去人去楼空的地方。

## VIII Fligwood’s Rents / 八 弗利格伍德租屋巷

===Original===
Hetherwick went to the hotel telephone again before he had finished his lunch, and as a result Matherfield was on the platform at Victoria when the two-twenty-four ran in. He showed no surprise at seeing Hetherwick and Rhona together; his manifest concern was to get Hetherwick to himself and away from the station. And Hetherwick, seeing this, said goodbye to Rhona with a whispered word that he would look in at Malter’s Hotel before evening; a few minutes later he and Matherfield were in a taxicab together, hastening along Buckingham Palace Road.

“Well?” inquired Hetherwick. “This man?”

“I don’t think there’s any doubt about his being the man you saw with Hannaford,” replied Matherfield. “He answers to your description, anyway. But I’ll tell you how we came across his track. Last night a man named Appleyard came to me﻿—he’s a chap who has a chemist’s shop in Horseferry Road, Westminster﻿—a middle-aged, quiet sort of man, who prefaced his remarks by telling us that he very rarely had time to read newspapers or he’d have been round to see us before. But yesterday he happened to pick up a copy of one of last Sunday’s papers, and he read an account of the Hannaford affair. Then he remembered something that seemed to him to have a possible connection with it. Some little time ago he advertised for an assistant﻿—a qualified assistant. He’d two or three applications which weren’t exactly satisfactory. Then, one evening﻿—he couldn’t give any exact date, but from various things he told us I reckoned up that it must have been on the very evening on which Hannaford met his death﻿—a man came and made a personal application. Appleyard described him﻿—medium-sized, a spare man, sallow-complexioned, thin face and beard, large dark eyes, very intelligent, superior manner, poorly dressed, and evidently in low water﻿—”

“That’s the man, I’ll be bound!” exclaimed Hetherwick. “Did he give this chemist his name?”

“He did﻿—name and address,” answered Matherfield. “He said his name was James Granett, and his address Number 8, Fligwood’s Rents, Gray’s Inn Road﻿—Holborn end. He told Appleyard that he was a qualified chemist, and produced his proofs and some references. He also said that though he’d never had a business of his own he’d been employed, as, indeed, the references showed, by some good provincial firms at one time or another.
