I, too, believe that this Mrs. Whittingham is now Lady Riversreade, and I may tell you that I’m in full possession of all the facts relating to the Sellithwaite affair﻿—an affair of obtaining a diamond necklace, worth about four thousand pounds, by means of a worthless cheque, and﻿—”
Blenkinsop suddenly rose from his chair, holding up a hand.
“A moment, if you please!” he said. “Penteney,” he continued, turning to his partner, “a word with you in your room.”
Matherfield glanced triumphantly after the retreating pair, and laughed when a door had closed on them.
“That’s got ’em, Mr. Hetherwick!” he exclaimed. “They see that we know more than they reckoned for. In some way or other, it strikes me, this advertisement is a piece of bluff!”
“Bluff!” said Hetherwick. “What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
“知道不少，先生，不过还没到我打算知道的那个数。”马瑟菲尔德斩钉截铁地答道，“但有一层我确知——汉纳福德在猝死之前不久，手头存着一张里弗斯雷德夫人的画像，并且认定她就是十年前在约克郡塞利斯韦特因欺诈罪名经他手办过的那位惠廷厄姆太太。我也相信，这位惠廷厄姆太太如今就是里弗斯雷德夫人；而且我不妨告诉两位，塞利斯韦特那桩案子的全部实情，我已尽数在手——那是一桩用一张空头支票骗取一条钻石项链的案子，项链约值四千英镑，还有——”
布伦金索普突然从椅子里站起身，举起一只手。
“请稍待片刻！”他说，“彭特尼，”他转向合伙人，接着说，“到你房里说句话。”
马瑟菲尔德得意地目送那两人退了出去，待一扇门在他们身后关严，他笑出了声。
“这一军将住他们了，海瑟维克先生！”他叫道，“他们看出来了：咱们知道的多于他们的预估。依我看，这则广告八成是虚张声势，变着法儿唬人的！”
“虚张声势！”海瑟维克说，“你这话怎讲？”

===Original===
“What I say,” answered Matherfield. “Bluff! Done to prevent somebody from bringing up that old Sellithwaite affair. Lay you a thousand to one it is. You’ll see these two lawyers will be more communicative when they come back. Now they shall talk﻿—and we’ll listen!”
“If you have to do any more talking, Matherfield,” said Hetherwick, “keep Miss Hannaford’s name out of it. She’s in a rather awkward position. She went there, of course, to find out what she could, and the result’s been that she’s taken a fancy to Lady Riversreade, got a genuine interest in the work there, and wants to stop. Bit of a bother, all that, and it’ll need some straightening out. Anyway, keep her name out of it here.”
“As I say, sir, when these chaps come back to us, they’ll do the talking!” answered Matherfield, with a chuckle. “You’ll see! If you want to keep Miss Hannaford’s name out, so do they want to keep Lady Riversreade’s name out﻿—I know the signs!”
Blenkinsop and Penteney suddenly came back and seated themselves, Blenkinsop at his desk and Penteney close by. And Blenkinsop immediately turned to his callers. His manner had changed; he looked now like a man who is anxious to get a settlement of a difficult question.
“We have decided to talk freely to you,” he said at once. “That means, to tell you everything we know about this matter. You, Mr. Matherfield, as representing the police, will, of course, treat our communication confidentially. I needn’t ask you, Mr. Hetherwick, to regard all that’s said here, as﻿—you know! Now, to begin with﻿—just get one fact, an absolutely irrefutable fact, into your minds at once. Lady Riversreade is not the woman who was known as Mrs. Whittingham at Sellithwaite ten years ago, nor did Hannaford believe that she was either!”
“What?” exclaimed Matherfield. “But﻿—” he turned to Hetherwick. “You hear that?” he went on. “Why, we know﻿—”

===Chinese===
“就是我说的话。”马瑟菲尔德答道，“虚张声势！为的是拦住什么人，不让那人翻出塞利斯韦特那桩旧案。我可以拿一千对一的赌注跟你打赌，就是这么回事。你瞧吧，这两位律师回来之后，话就会多起来。这下该他们说了——咱们听着！”
“要是接下来还得费口舌，马瑟菲尔德，”海瑟维克说，“可别把汉纳福德小姐的名字牵进去。她的处境相当为难。她到那边去，本是去探听消息的，结果呢，竟喜欢上里弗斯雷德夫人了，对那边的工作动了真心，还想留下不走了。这些事都有点纠葛，得理上一理。无论如何，在这儿别提她的名字。”
“我正这么说呢，先生，等这些家伙回到咱们跟前，就该他们说了！”马瑟菲尔德呵呵笑着答道，“你瞧着吧！你想把汉纳福德小姐的名字撇开，他们也一样想把里弗斯雷德夫人的名字撇开——这路数我认得！”
布伦金索普和彭特尼突然回来了，各自落座——布伦金索普坐回书案后，彭特尼坐在近旁。布伦金索普随即转向两位来客。他的态度变了；此刻他看上去像是一个急欲了结一桩难题的人。
“我们决定向两位开诚布公。”他开门见山地说，“这就是说，把我们对此案所知的一切，统统告诉你们。马瑟菲尔德先生代表警方，自然会把我们的陈述严守机密。海瑟维克先生，这里说的话该作何看待，毋庸我多嘱咐——您是明白人！好，首先——请立刻在脑子里记下一个事实，一个绝对无可辩驳的事实：里弗斯雷德夫人并不是十年前在塞利斯韦特被称作惠廷厄姆太太的那个女人，汉纳福德也从来不曾相信她是！”
“什么？”马瑟菲尔德失声叫道，“可是——”他转向海瑟维克。“你听见了吗？”他接着说，“嗐，咱们分明知道——”

===Original===
“Let Mr. Blenkinsop go on,” said Hetherwick quietly. “He’s explaining, I think.”
“Just so,” agreed Blenkinsop. “And I’m beginning by endeavouring to clear away a few mistaken ideas from your minds. Lady Riversreade is not Mrs. Whittingham. Hannaford did not think she was Mrs. Whittingham. It was not Lady Riversreade’s portrait that Hannaford cut out of the paper.”
Hetherwick could not repress a start at that.
“Whose was it, then?” he demanded. “For I certainly believed it was!”
Blenkinsop stooped and drew out a drawer from his desk. From a bundle of documents he produced a newspaper, carefully folded and labelled. Opening this, he laid it before the two visitors, pointing to a picture marked with blue pencil. And Hetherwick at once saw that here was a duplicate of the portrait in his own pocketbook. But there was this important difference﻿—while Hannaford had cut away the lettering under his picture, it was there in the one which Blenkinsop exhibited. He started again as he read it﻿—“Madame Anita Listorelle.”
“That’s the picture which Hannaford cut out of the paper,” said Blenkinsop. “It is not that of Lady Riversreade.”

===Chinese===
“让布伦金索普先生说下去。”海瑟维克平静地说，“我想，他这是在解释。”
“正是。”布伦金索普应道，“我就从头做起，先设法把你们脑子里的几个错误想法清除出去。里弗斯雷德夫人不是惠廷厄姆太太。汉纳福德不曾认为她是惠廷厄姆太太。汉纳福德从报上剪下的那幅画像，也不是里弗斯雷德夫人的。”
听了这话，海瑟维克不由得猛然一惊。
“那么，那是谁的？”他追问，“因为我原先确信那就是她的！”
布伦金索普俯下身，从书案里抽出一只抽屉。他从一叠文件中取出一份报纸，折叠妥帖，还加了标签。他把报纸展开，摆到两位来客面前，指着一幅用蓝铅笔作了记号的图片。海瑟维克一眼便看出，这正是他自己记事本里那幅画像的复本。只是有一个重大的不同——汉纳福德把他那幅图片底下的文字剪掉了，而布伦金索普出示的这幅，文字还在。他读到那行字，又是一震——“安妮塔·里斯托雷尔太太（Madame Anita Listorelle）”。
“汉纳福德从报上剪下来的，正是这幅图片。”布伦金索普说，“它不是里弗斯雷德夫人的画像。”

===Original===
“Then it’s that of a woman who’s her double!” exclaimed Matherfield. “I’ll lay anything that if you asked a hundred men who’ve seen Lady Riversreade if that’s her picture, they’d swear it is!”
“I see,” said Hetherwick, disregarding his companion’s outburst, “that this purports to be a portrait of a Madame Listorelle, who is described in the accompanying letterpress as a famous connoisseur of precious stones. Now, in relation to what we’re discussing, may I ask a plain question? Who is Madame Listorelle?”
Blenkinsop smiled﻿—oracularly.
“Madame Listorelle,” he replied, “is the twin sister of Lady Riversreade!”

===Chinese===
“那它就是一个同她长得一模一样的女人的画像！”马瑟菲尔德嚷道，“随便赌什么都行——去问一百个见过里弗斯雷德夫人的男人，那是不是她的相片，他们准会赌咒发誓说正是！”
“我看明白了，”海瑟维克说，全不理会同伴的发作，“这幅图片号称是一位里斯托雷尔太太的画像，随图的文字把她描述成一位著名的宝石鉴赏家。那么，就着咱们正在讨论的事情，容我问一个直白的问题：这位里斯托雷尔太太是谁？”
布伦金索普莞尔一笑——一副出口神谕的架势。
“里斯托雷尔太太嘛，”他答道，“正是里弗斯雷德夫人的孪生姐妹！”

## VI Samples of Ink / 六 墨水样本

===Original===
Hollis led the way farther along the alley, between high, black, windowless walls, and suddenly turning into a little court, paused before a door set deep in the side of an old half-timbered house.

“Queer old place, this!” he remarked over his shoulder. “But you’ll get a glass of as good port or sherry from this chap as you’d get anywhere in England﻿—he knows his customers! Come in.”

He led the way into a place the like of which Hetherwick had never seen﻿—a snug, cosy room, panelled and raftered in old oak, with a bright fire burning in an open hearth and the flicker of its flames dancing on the old brass and pewter that ornamented the walls. There was a small bar-counter on one side of it; and behind this, in his shirtsleeves, and with a cigar protruding from the corner of a pair of clean-shaven, humorous lips, stood a keen-eyed man, busily engaged in polishing wineglasses.

“Good morning, gentlemen!” he said heartily. “Nice morning, Mr. Hollis, for the time o’ year. And what can I do for you and your friend, sir?”

Hollis glanced round the room﻿—empty, save for themselves. He drew a stool to the bar and motioned Hetherwick to follow his example.

“I think we’ll try your very excellent dry sherry, Hudson,” he answered. “That is, if it’s as good as it was last time I tasted it.”
===Chinese===
霍利斯（Hollis）在前头引路，顺着小巷再往里走，两旁是高高的、黑黢黢的、不见窗户的墙。他忽然一拐，折进一个小院，在一扇门前站住——那扇门深深嵌在一座半木结构老宅的侧面墙上。

“这地方，老派得古怪吧！”他回头说道。“可是从这家伙手里，你能喝上一杯全英格兰走到哪儿也未必更好的波尔图酒或者雪利酒——他懂他的主顾们！进来吧。”

他领路走进一处海瑟维克（Hetherwick）从未见过的地方——一间安适温馨的屋子，护壁板和梁架用的都是老橡木，敞开的壁炉里烧着一炉旺火，跳动的火焰映在四壁悬挂的旧铜器白镴器上，熠熠闪烁。屋子一侧设着一个小小的酒吧柜台；柜台后面，站着个目光锐利的人，只穿件衬衫，一副刮得干干净净、透着诙谐的嘴角边叼着支雪茄，正忙着擦拭酒杯。

“早上好，两位先生！”他热络地招呼道。“这个时节里，这样的早晨算是顶好的了，霍利斯先生。我能为您和您这位朋友效点什么劳，先生？”

霍利斯朝屋里扫了一眼——除了他们两个，别无他人。他拖过一张凳子放到柜台前，示意海瑟维克照他的样儿坐。

“我想咱们就尝尝你那顶呱呱的干雪利酒吧，赫德森（Hudson），”他答道。“前提是，它还跟上回我尝的那回一样好。”

===Original===
“Always up to standard, Mr. Hollis, always up to standard, sir!” replied the barkeeper. “No inferior qualities, no substitutes, and no trading on past reputation in this establishment, gentlemen! As good a glass of dry sherry here, sir, as you’d get where sherry wine comes from﻿—and you can’t say that of most places in England, I think. Everything’s of the best here, Mr. Hollis﻿—as you know!”

Hollis responded with a little light chaff; suddenly he bent across the bar.

“Hudson!” he said confidentially. “My friend here has something he’d like to show you. Now, then,” he continued, as Hetherwick, in response to this, had produced the picture, “do you recognise that?”

The barkeeper put on a pair of spectacles and turned the picture to the light, examining it closely. His lips tightened; then relaxed in a cynical smile.

“Aye!” he said, half carelessly. “It’s the woman that did old Malladale out of that diamond necklace. Of course!﻿—Mistress Whittingham!”
===Chinese===
“始终够标准，霍利斯先生，始终够标准，先生！”掌柜的答道。“本店不卖次等货，不拿代用品充数，也绝不躺在往日的名声上吃老本，两位先生！在这儿喝到的一杯干雪利，先生，同雪利酒老家喝到的一样好——依我看，英格兰大多数地方可当不起这句话。这儿样样都是头一份，霍利斯先生——这您是知道的！”

霍利斯应了几句轻松的玩笑话；忽然，他隔着柜台俯身过去。

“赫德森！”他一副交心的口气说道。“我这位朋友有样东西想给你瞧瞧。喏——”海瑟维克闻言取出了那张照片，他便接着问道，“你认得这个么？”

掌柜的戴上一副眼镜，把照片转向亮处，凑近端详。他的嘴唇绷紧了；随即又松开，化作一丝冷嘲的笑意。

“哎！”他半带随意地说道。“就是那个把老马拉代尔（Malladale）坑去了钻石项链的女人。没错！——惠廷厄姆太太（Mrs. Whittingham）！”

===Original===
“Would you know her again, if you met her﻿—now?” asked Hollis.

The barkeeper picked up one of his glasses and began a vigorous polishing.

“Aye!” he answered, laconically. “And I should know her by something else than her face!”

Just then two men came in, and Hudson broke off to attend to their wants. But presently they carried their glasses away to a snug corner near the fire, and the barkeeper once more turned to Hollis and Hetherwick.

“Aye!” he said confidentially. “If need were, I could tell that party by something else than her face, handsome as that is! I used to tell Hannaford when he was busy trying to find her that if he’d any difficulty about making certain, I could identify her if nobody else could! You see, I saw a deal of her when she was stopping at the White Bear. And I knew something that nobody else knew.”
===Chinese===
“这会儿你要是再撞见她，还认得出来么？”霍利斯问。

掌柜的拿起一只酒杯，使劲擦拭起来。

“认得！”他言简意赅地答道。“而且我认她，凭的还不光是那张脸！”

正在这时进来两个人，赫德森便打住话头去张罗。不过不多时，那两位就端着酒杯挪到炉火边一个暖和的角落里去了，掌柜的便又回过身来，对着霍利斯和海瑟维克。

“哎！”他放低声音说道。“真到了要紧关头，我认那个人，凭的就不仅仅是脸——虽说那张脸也着实标致！当年汉纳福德（Hannaford）忙着捉拿她那阵子，我常对他说，他要是拿不准对证，哪怕天下没一个人认得出，我也认得出！你要知道，她在白熊旅馆（White Bear）住着的时候，我同她打过不少交道。我还知道一件别人谁都不知道的事。”

===Original===
“What is it?” asked Hetherwick.

Hudson leaned closer across the counter and lowered his voice.

“She was a big, handsome woman, this Mrs. Whittingham,” he continued. “Very showy, dressy woman; fond of fine clothes and jewellery, and so on; sort of woman, you know, that would attract attention anywhere. And one of these women, too, that was evidently used to being waited on hand and foot﻿—she took her money’s worth out of the White Bear, I can tell you! I did a deal for her, one way or another, and I’ll say this for her: she was free enough with her money. If it so happened that she wanted things doing for her, she kept you fairly on the go till they were done, but she threw five-shilling pieces and half-crowns about as if they were farthings! She’d send you to take a sixpenny telegram and give you a couple of shillings for taking it. Well, now, as I say, I saw a deal of her, one way and another, getting cabs for her, and taking things up to her room, and doing this, that, and t’other. And it was with going up there one day sudden-like, with a telegram that had just come, that I found out something about her﻿—something that, as I say, I could have told her by anywhere, even if she could have changed her face and put a wig on!”

“Aye﻿—and what, now?” asked Hollis.

“This!” answered Hudson with a knowing look. “Maybe I’m a noticing sort of chap﻿—anyhow, there was a thing I always noticed about Mrs. Whittingham. Wherever she was, and no matter how she was dressed, whether it was in her going-out things or her dinner finery, she always wore a band of black velvet round her right forearm, just above the wrist, where women wear bracelets. In fact, it was a sort of bracelet, a strip, as I say, of black velvet, happen about two inches wide, and on the front a cameo ornament, the size of a shilling, white stone or something of that sort, with one of these heathen figures carved on it. There were other folk about the place noticed that black velvet band, too﻿—I tell you she was never seen without it; the chambermaids said she slept with it on. But on the occasion I’m telling you about, when I went up to her room with a telegram, I caught her without it. She opened her door to see who knocked﻿—she was in a dressing-gown, going to change for dinner, I reckon, and she held out her right hand for what I’d brought her. The black velvet band wasn’t on it, and for just a second like I saw what was on her arm!”
===Chinese===
“什么事？”海瑟维克问。

赫德森隔着柜台又凑近了些，压低了嗓音。

“这位惠廷厄姆太太，是个身材高大、相貌堂堂的女人，”他接着往下说。“是个招摇阔气的女人；喜好华服首饰，诸如此类；你知道，就是那种走到哪儿都惹人注目的女人。而且还是那种一看就被人伺候惯了、衣来伸手的角色——她可把白熊旅馆使唤了个够，这话我敢说！我这样那样替她办过不少事，有句话我得替她说：她花钱倒是痛快的。碰上她要你办事，事情没办妥之前她能支使你脚不沾地，可她撒起五先令的银币和半克朗来，就跟撒法新似的！打发你去递一封六便士的电报，能赏你两个先令。喏，正像我方才说的，我这样那样同她照面的回数多得很——给她叫车呀，往她房里送东西呀，办这办那呀。就是有一天，我拿着刚到的一封电报冷不丁上了她那儿，才让我发现了她的一处记认——一处，照我方才的话说，她哪怕换了脸、戴上假发，我凭它走到天边也认得出她来的记认！”

“哎——那么，是什么？”霍利斯问。

“这个！”赫德森一脸心照不宣的神气，答道。“也许我天生是个爱留意的人——反正，惠廷厄姆太太身上有一样东西，我回回都留意到。不论她在哪儿，也不论她穿得怎样，是出门的行头还是赴宴的盛装，她右臂腕上、恰好是女人家戴镯子的地方，总缠着一圈黑天鹅绒的箍儿。其实说起来，那就是一种镯子——照我说的，一条黑天鹅绒带子，宽约莫两英寸，正面缀着一枚浮雕玉石，先令大小，白石还是那一类的东西，上头雕着一个异教神像。这一带还有别人也留意过那条黑天鹅绒腕箍——我告诉你，人家从没见她摘下来过；客房女仆说她连睡觉都戴着。可在我讲的那回，我拿着电报上她房里去，偏偏撞见她没戴。她开门看是谁敲的门——她穿着晨衣，我琢磨是正要换装吃晚饭，便伸出右手来接我送上的东西。黑天鹅绒腕箍不在腕上，就那么一秒钟工夫，我瞧见了她胳膊上的东西！”

===Original===
“Yes?” said Hollis. “Something﻿—remarkable?”

“For a lady﻿—aye!” replied Hudson, with a grim laugh. “Her arm was tattooed! Right round the place where she always wore this black velvet band there was a snake, red and green, and yellow, and blue, with its tail in its mouth!﻿—wonderfully done, too; it had been no novice that had done that bit of work, I can tell you! Of course, I just saw it, and no more, but there was a strong electric light close by, and I did see it, and saw it plain and all. And that’s a thing that that woman, whoever she may be, and wherever she’s got to, can never rub off, nor scrub off!﻿—she’ll carry that to the day of her death.”

The two listeners looked at each other.

“Odd!” remarked Hollis.
===Chinese===
“哦？”霍利斯说。“有什么——了不得的？”

“对一位贵妇人来说——了不得！”赫德森答道，发出一声冷冷的笑。“她的胳膊上刺了花！就在她常年缠黑天鹅绒腕箍的那一圈，盘着一条蛇，红的绿的，黄的蓝的，尾巴衔在嘴里！——手艺也真精；干那活儿的绝不是个生手，这话我可以打包票！当然，我只瞧了一眼，仅此而已，可旁边就点着一盏雪亮的电灯，我确确实实瞧见了，而且瞧得清清楚楚。那样东西，那个女人——不管她如今是谁，不管她躲到了哪儿——永远磨不掉，也搓不掉！——她得带着它进棺材呢。”

两个听的人对望了一眼。

“蹊跷！”霍利斯评说道。

===Original===
Hetherwick turned to the barkeeper.

“Did she notice that you saw that her arm was tattooed?” he asked.

“Nay, I don’t think she did,” replied Hudson. “Of course, the thing was over in a second. I made no sign that I’d seen aught particular, and she said nought. But﻿—I saw!”

Just then other customers came in, and the barkeeper turned away to attend to their wants. Hollis and Hetherwick moved from the counter to one of the snug corners at the farther end of the room.

“Whoever she may be, wherever she may be﻿—as Hudson said just now,” remarked Hollis, “and if this woman really had anything to do with the mysterious circumstances of Hannaford’s death, she ought not to be difficult to find. A woman who carries an indefaceable mark like that on her arm, and whose picture has recently appeared in a newspaper, should easily be traced.”

“I think I shall get at her through the picture,” agreed Hetherwick. “The newspaper production seems to have been done from a photograph which, from its clearness and finish, was probably taken by some first-class firm in London. I shall go round such firms as soon as I get back. It may be, of course, that she’s nothing whatever to do with Hannaford’s murder, but still, it’s a trail that’s got to be followed to the end now that one’s started out on it. Well! that seems to finish my business here﻿—as far as she’s concerned. But there’s another matter﻿—I told you that when Hannaford came to town he had on him a sealed packet containing the secret of some invention or discovery, and that it’s strangely and unaccountably missing. His granddaughter says that he worked this thing out﻿—whatever it is﻿—in a laboratory that he had in his garden. Now then, before I go I want to see that laboratory. As he’s only recently left the place, I suppose things will still be pretty much as he left them at his old house. Where did he live?”
===Chinese===
海瑟维克转向掌柜的。

“她可曾察觉，你看见了她的胳膊上刺了花？”他问。

“没，我看她没察觉，”赫德森答道。“当然，前后不过一秒钟。我没露一点声色，表示瞧见了什么特别的东西，她也没吭一声。可是——我瞧见了！”

正在这时又来了别的客人，掌柜的转过身去张罗。霍利斯和海瑟维克离开柜台，挪到屋子那一头一个暖和的角落里坐下。

“不管她如今是谁，也不管她如今在哪儿——正像赫德森方才说的，”霍利斯说道，“倘若这个女人当真同汉纳福德之死的种种神秘情形有牵连，那要找到她应该不难。一个胳膊上带着那种磨不掉的记号、照片又新近在报纸上登过的女人，查起踪迹来该是容易的。”

“我看，我就顺着那张照片去寻她，”海瑟维克深以为然。“报上那张像，看来是从一张相片制版的，从它的清晰和精致来看，多半出自伦敦某家一流商号之手。我一回去就挨家走一遍。当然，也可能她同汉纳福德的凶案毫无干系，可既然已经上了这条道，就得追到底。好！她这一头，我在本地的事就算办完了。可还有另一桩——我对你说过，汉纳福德进城的时候，身上带着一个封了口的纸包，里面装着某项发明还是发现什么的秘密，如今离奇得没个下落地不见了。他孙女说，他这个东西——不管究竟是什么——是在自家花园里的一间实验室里钻研出来的。那么，趁我还没走，我想去看看那间实验室。他既然是不久前才离开这地方的，我想他那老宅里的一切，多半还照着他离开时的原样。他住在哪儿？”

===Original===
“He lived on the outskirts of the town,” replied Hollis. “An old-fashioned house that he bought some years ago﻿—I know it by sight well enough, though I’ve never been in it. I don’t suppose it’s let yet, though I know it’s being advertised in the local papers. Let’s get some lunch at the White Bear, and then we’ll drive up there and see what we can do. You want to get an idea of what it was that Hannaford had invented?”

“Just so,” assented Hetherwick. “If the secret was worth all that he told his granddaughter it was, he may have been murdered by somebody who wanted to get sole possession of it. Anyway, it’s another trail that’s got to be worked on.”

“I never heard of Hannaford as an inventor or experimenter,” remarked Hollis. “But there, I knew little about him, except in his official capacity: he and his granddaughter, and an elderly woman they kept as a working housekeeper, were quiet sort of folk. I knew that he brought up his granddaughter from infancy, and gave her a rattling good education at the Girls’ High School, but beyond that, I know little of their private affairs. I suppose he amused himself in this laboratory you speak of in his spare time?”

“Dabbled in chemistry, I understand,” said Hetherwick. “And, if it hasn’t been dismantled, we may find something in that laboratory that will give us a clue of some sort.”

Hollis seemed to reflect for a minute or two.

“I’ve an idea!” he said suddenly. “There’s a man who lunches at the White Bear every day﻿—a man named Collison; he’s analytical chemist to a big firm of dyers in the town. I’ve seen him in conversation with Hannaford now and then. Perhaps he could tell us something on this point. Come on! this is just about his time for lunch.”
===Chinese===
“他住在镇子边上，”霍利斯答道。“一栋老式房子，是他几年前买下的——我常常见着，认得那模样，只是从来没进去过。我想还没租出去，虽然我知道本地报纸上正登着招租的广告。咱们先上白熊旅馆吃午饭，然后坐车过去，瞧瞧能怎么办。你是想弄清楚，汉纳福德到底发明了个什么名堂？”

“正是，”海瑟维克表示赞同。“倘若那桩秘密当真值他对孙女夸下的那个价钱，那他很可能就是叫哪个想独占它的人谋害的。不管怎么说，这又是一条非下功夫追不可的线索。”

“我从没听说过汉纳福德还是个发明家、实验家，”霍利斯说道。“不过话又说回来，我对他本来就知之甚少，只在他任警官的那段公事上打过交道：他同他孙女，还有一个雇来当家操持的老妇人，都是安安静静的人家。我知道他孙女是他襁褓里就拉扯大的，在女子中学受了一等的教育，可再往里去，他们的私事我就知道得少了。我猜，他是闲工夫里在你说的那间实验室里自娱自乐吧？”

“据我所知，他摆弄过化学，”海瑟维克说。“要是那实验室还没拆除，咱们说不定能在里面找出点什么，给咱们一条线索。”

霍利斯像是沉吟了一两分钟。

“我有个主意！”他忽然说道。“有个人天天在白熊旅馆用午饭——名叫科利森（Collison）；他是镇上一家大染厂的化验师。我不时见过他同汉纳福德聊天。也许这一点上他能给咱们讲出点什么。走吧！眼下差不多正是他去用午饭的时候。”

===Original===
A few minutes later, in the coffee-room of the hotel, Hollis led Hetherwick up to a bearded and spectacled man who had just sat down to lunch, and having introduced him, briefly detailed the object of his visit to Sellithwaite. Collison nodded and smiled.

“I understand,” he said, as they seated themselves at his table. “Hannaford did dabble a bit in chemistry﻿—in quite an amateur way. But as to inventing anything that was worth all that﻿—come! Still, he was an ingenious man, for an amateur, and he may have hit on something fairly valuable.”

“You’ve no idea what he was after?” suggested Hetherwick.

“Of late, no! But some time ago he was immensely interested in aniline dyes,” replied Collison. “He used to talk to me about them. That’s a subject of infinite importance in this district. Of course, as I dare say you know, the Germans have been vastly ahead of us as regards aniline dyes, and we’ve got most, if not all, of the stuff used, from Germany. Hannaford used to worry himself as to why we couldn’t make our own aniline dyes, and I believe he experimented. But, with his resources, as an amateur, of course, that was hopeless.”

“I’ve sometimes seen him talking to you,” observed Hollis. “You’ve no idea what he was after, of late?”

“No. He used to ask me technical questions,” answered Collison. “You know, I just regarded him as a man who had a natural taste for experimenting with things. This was evidently his hobby. I used to chaff him about it. Still, he was a purposeful man, and by reading and experiment he’d picked up a lot of knowledge.”
===Chinese===
几分钟后，在旅馆的咖啡厅里，霍利斯把海瑟维克领到一位蓄须戴眼镜的先生跟前——那位刚落座要用午饭。引见之后，霍利斯把他此来塞利斯韦特（Sellithwaite）的来意扼要讲了一遍。科利森点头微笑。

“我明白了，”他们在他的桌旁落座时，他说道。“汉纳福德确实摆弄过一阵子化学——纯属业余的路数。至于发明出什么值那么大价钱的东西——得了吧！不过，以一个业余爱好者而论，他是个心灵手巧的人，没准儿还真撞上了什么值钱的玩意儿。”

“他当时在钻研什么，您就一点头绪也没有么？”海瑟维克提示道。

“近来可没有！不过前些时候，他对苯胺染料起过极大的兴趣，”科利森答道。“他从前常同我谈这个。在本地区，那可是个要紧无穷的题目。当然，我敢说你们也知道，在苯胺染料上头，德国人一直远远走在咱们前面，咱们用的原料，即使不是全部，也大半是从德国来的。汉纳福德常为咱们为什么造不出自家的苯胺染料犯愁，我相信他做过实验。可是，凭他那样一点家底，又是业余的身份，那自然是没指望的。”

“我不时看见他同您谈话，”霍利斯说。“他近来在钻研什么，您就一点不知道么？”

“不知道。他从前常问我些技术上的问题，”科利森答道。“你是知道的，我只把他当作一个天生爱摆弄东西的人。这分明是他的癖好。我常拿这个打趣他。不过，他是个有股子钻劲的人，靠读书加实验，积累了不少学问。”

===Original===
“And, I suppose, it’s within the bounds of possibility that he had hit on something of practical value?” suggested Hetherwick.

“Oh, quite within such bounds!﻿—and he may have done,” agreed Collison. “I’ve known of much greater amateurs suddenly discovering something. The question then is﻿—do they know enough to turn their discovery to any practical purpose and account?”

“Evidently, from what he told his granddaughter, Hannaford did think he knew enough,” said Hetherwick. “What I want to find out from a visit to his old laboratory is﻿—what had he discovered?”

“And as you’re not a chemist, nor even a dabbler,” remarked Hollis, with a laugh, “that won’t be easy! You’d better come with us after lunch, Collison.”

“I can give you a couple of hours,” assented Collison. “I’m already curious﻿—especially if any discovery we can make tends to throw light on the mystery of Hannaford’s death. Pity the police haven’t got hold of the man who was with him,” he added, glancing at Hetherwick. “I suppose you could identify him?”

“Unless he’s an absolute adept at disguising himself, yes﻿—positively!” replied Hetherwick. “He was a noticeable man.”
===Chinese===
“那么我想，他究竟是不是也撞出过什么有实用价值的东西，总还在可能的范围之内吧？”海瑟维克提示道。

“哦，完全在可能范围之内！——他也许真撞出来过，”科利森表示赞同。“我见过比他外行得多的人，冷不丁就发现了点什么。问题接着就来了——他们的学问够不够，能不能把这发现派上实际用场、变出实利来？”

“从他对孙女说的话来看，汉纳福德显然当真觉得自己够了，”海瑟维克说。“我到他旧实验室去走一趟，想查明的正是——他到底发现了什么？”

“可你既不是化学家，连个摆弄的爱好者都不算，”霍利斯笑道，“这可不容易！午饭以后你同我们一道去吧，科利森。”

“我可以奉陪两个钟头，”科利森应承道。“我已经起了好奇心——尤其是，倘若咱们能查出点什么，有助于照亮汉纳福德之死的谜团的话。可惜警察没把那天同他在一起的那个人抓到手，”他瞥了海瑟维克一眼，又说道。“我想你认得出他来吧？”

“除非他在化装改扮上是把登峰造极的好手——认得出，千真万确！”海瑟维克答道。“那是个叫人过目难忘的人。”

===Original===
An hour later the three men drove up to a house which stood a little way out of the town, on the edge of the moorland that stretched towards the great range of hills on the west. The house, an old-fashioned, solitary place, was empty, save for a caretaker who had been installed in its back rooms to keep it aired and to show it to possible tenants. The laboratory, a stonewalled, timber-roofed shed at the end of the garden, had never been opened, said the caretaker, since Mr. Hannaford locked it up and left it. But the key was speedily forthcoming, and the three visitors entered and looked round, each with different valuings of what he saw.

The whole place was a wilderness of litter and untidiness. Whatever Hannaford had possessed in the way of laboratory plant and appliances had been removed, and now there was little but rubbish﻿—glass, whole and broken, paper, derelict boxes and crates, odds and ends of wreckage﻿—to look at. But the analytical chemist glanced about him with a knowing eye, examining bottles and boxes, picking up a thing here and another there, and before long he turned to his companions with a laugh, pointing at the same time to a table in a corner which was covered with jars and dust-lined pots.

“It’s very easy to see what Hannaford was after!” he said. “He’s been trying to evolve a new ink!”

“Ink!” exclaimed Hollis incredulously. “Aren’t there plenty of inks on the market?”

“No end!” agreed Collison with another laugh, and again pointing to the table. “These are specimens of all the better-known ones﻿—British, of course, for no really decent ink is made elsewhere. But even the very best ink, up to now, isn’t perfect. Hannaford perhaps thought, being an amateur, that he could make a better than the known best. Ink!﻿—that’s what he’s been after. A superior, perfectly-fluid, penetrating, permanent, non-corrosive writing-ink﻿—that’s been his notion, a thousand to one! I observe the presence of lots of stuffs that he’s used.”
===Chinese===
一个钟头以后，三人坐车来到一栋房子跟前。房子离镇子有一小段路，孤零零立在荒原边上——那片荒原一直铺向西边巍峨的群山。这栋老式而冷清的宅子空着，只有一名看房人安顿在后屋里，替房子透气通风，领有可能承租的人看房。花园尽头那间实验室，是个石墙木顶的棚屋；看房人说，自打汉纳福德先生锁了门离去，就再没开过。不过钥匙很快就寻了出来，三位来客走进屋里四下打量，各人对眼前所见，各有各的掂量。

整个屋子是一片狼藉的荒摊子。汉纳福德原有的一应实验设备和器皿都已搬走，如今能看的只剩些垃圾——玻璃，囫囵的和碎的，纸张，废弃的箱子和板条筐，还有七零八碎的破烂——别无他物。可那位化验师却行家似的环顾四周，翻检着瓶瓶罐罐，这儿拾起一件，那儿拣起一样，不多时便笑着转向两个同伴，同时指着墙角一张桌子——桌上摆满了坛坛罐罐，坛口蒙着一线一线的积尘。

“汉纳福德钻研的是什么，一望便知！”他说。“他一直在琢磨造一种新墨水！”

“墨水！”霍利斯喊了起来，满心的不敢相信。“市面上墨水还少么？”

“多得很！”科利森笑着应和，又指了指那张桌子。“这些都是比较知名的各色墨水的样品——自然是英国的，因为别处根本造不出真正像样的墨水。可是，哪怕是眼下顶好的墨水，也谈不上十全十美。汉纳福德大约是想着，自己一个业余家，能造出比已知最好的还要好的来。墨水！——他钻研的就是这个。一种高级的、流动性极佳、渗透力强、永不褪色、不蚀笔尖的书写墨水——十有八九，这就是他的想头！我瞧得出他使过的那一大堆材料。”

===Original===
He showed them various things, explaining their properties and adding some remarks on the history of the manufacture of writing-inks during the last hundred years.

“Taking it altogether,” he concluded, “and in spite of manufacturers’ advertisements and boasting, there isn’t a really absolutely perfect writing-fluid on the market﻿—that I know of, anyway. If Hannaford thought he could make one, and succeeded, well, I’d be glad to have his formula! Money in it!”

“To the extent of a hundred thousand pounds?” asked Hetherwick, remembering what Rhona had told him. “All that?”

“Oh, well!” laughed Collison, “you must remember that inventors are always very sanguine; always apt to see everything through rose-coloured spectacles; invariably prone to exaggerate the merits of their inventions. But if Hannaford, by experiment, really hit on a first-class formula for making a writing-ink superior in all the necessary qualities to its rivals﻿—yes, there’d be a pot of money in it. No doubt of that!”
===Chinese===
他指给他们看各样东西，讲解各自的性能，又添了几句近百年间书写墨水制造史的闲谈。

“总而言之，”他归结道，“尽管有厂家的广告和吹嘘，市面上并没有一种真正绝对完美的书写液——反正我知道的没有。汉纳福德要是当真以为自己造得出来，而且造出来了，那好，他的方子我肯掏钱要！这里头有钱赚！”

“能值到十万镑？”海瑟维克问，想起罗娜（Rhona）对他说过的话。“值那么多？”

“哦，这个嘛！”科利森笑道，“你得记住，发明家总是异想天开地乐观；总爱隔着玫瑰色的眼镜看万事；说起自己发明的好处，从来免不了夸大。可要是汉纳福德真凭实验撞出了一张头等的方子，造出的书写墨水在各项必要品质上都压倒同行——是的，那这里头就是一注财。这一点没什么可怀疑的！”

===Original===
“I suppose he’d have to take out a patent for his invention?” suggested Hetherwick.

“Oh, to be sure! I should think that was one of his reasons for going to London﻿—to see after it,” assented Collison. He looked round again, and again laughed. “Well,” he said, “I think you know now﻿—you may be confident about it from what I’ve seen here﻿—what Hannaford was after! Ink﻿—just ink!”

Hetherwick accepted this judgment, and when he left Sellithwaite later in the afternoon on his return journey to London, he summed up the results of his visit. They were two. First, he had discovered that the woman of whom Hannaford had spoken in the train was a person who ten years before had been known as Mrs. Whittingham, appeared to be some sort of an adventuress, and, in spite of her restitution to the jeweller whom she had defrauded, was still liable to arrest, conviction, and punishment﻿—if she could be found. Second, he had found out that the precious invention of which Hannaford had spoken so confidently and enthusiastically to his granddaughter and the particulars of which had mysteriously disappeared, related to the manufacture of a new writing-ink, which might, in truth, prove a very valuable commercial asset. So far, so good; he was finding things out. As he ate his dinner in the restaurant car he considered his next steps. But it needed little consideration to resolve on them. He must find out all about the woman whose picture lay in his pocketbook﻿—what she now called herself; where she was; how her photograph came to be reproduced in a newspaper; and, last, but far from least, if Hannaford, after seeing the reproduction, had got into touch with her or given information about her. To the man in the train Hannaford had remarked that he had said nothing about her until that evening﻿—yes, but was that man the only man to whom he had spoken? So much for that﻿—and the next thing was to find out somehow what had become of the sealed packet which Hannaford undoubtedly had on him when he went out of Malter’s Hotel on the night of his death.
===Chinese===
“我想，他的发明总得领个专利吧？”海瑟维克提示道。

“哦，那是自然！我想那正是他上伦敦去的缘由之一——就为操办这个，”科利森表示赞同。他又环顾四周，又笑了起来。“好了，”他说，“我想你们如今知道了——凭我在这儿见到的种种，你们尽可放心——汉纳福德钻研的是什么！墨水——就是墨水！”

海瑟维克接受了这个论断。当天下午晚些时候，他动身返回伦敦，离开塞利斯韦特的时候，把这趟来访的收获归结了一遍。收获有两条。其一，他查明，汉纳福德在火车上说起的那女人，就是十年前名叫惠廷厄姆太太的那位，看样子是个女冒险家一流的角色，而且，尽管她已向被她诈骗的珠宝商退赔了事，只要寻得着她，仍旧随时可以将她逮捕、定罪、惩办。其二，他查明，汉纳福德对孙女说得那样笃定、那样起劲的那桩宝贵发明——其详情又莫名其妙地失踪了——同一种新书写墨水的制造有关，而这墨水没准儿当真是件很值钱的商业资产。到目前为止，一切还算顺当；他正把事情一件件查个明白。他在餐车里吃着晚饭，盘算下一步的步骤。其实这用不着怎么盘算便可定夺。他必须查清衣袋里存着照片的那女人的全部底细——她如今自称什么；人在何处；她的相片怎么会翻印到了报纸上；最后还有——虽说排在末了，分量却丝毫不轻——汉纳福德见到那张翻印的相片之后，同她接没接触过，或是向人通报过她的消息没有。在火车上，汉纳福德曾对那人说，直到那天晚上为止，他谁也没提起过她——是吗，可他开过口的，就只有那一个人么？这一层且按下——接下来要办的，是设法查明汉纳福德丧命当晚走出马尔特旅馆（Malter's Hotel）时身上无疑带着的那个封了口的纸包，如今下落如何。

## XVI Still More / 十六 更进一步

===Original===
Blenkinsop’s sudden announcement, not altogether unexpected by Hetherwick as a result of the last few minutes’ proceedings, seemed to strike Matherfield with all the force of a lightning-like illumination. His mouth opened; his eyes widened; he turned on Hetherwick as if, having been lost for a while in a baffling maze, he had suddenly seen a way pointed out to him.
“Oh, that’s it, is it?” he exclaimed. “A twin sister, eh? Then﻿—but go on, Mr. Blenkinsop; I’m beginning to see things.”
“The matter is doubtless puzzling﻿—to outsiders,” responded Blenkinsop. “To clear it up, I shall have to go into some family history. Lady Riversreade and Madame Listorelle are, I repeat, twin sisters. They are the daughters of a man who in his time was captain of various merchant ships﻿—the old sailing ships﻿—and who knocked about the world a good deal. He married an American woman, and his two daughters were born in Galveston, Texas. They were educated in America﻿—but there’s no need to go into the particulars of their early lives﻿—”
“There’s a certain particular that I should like to have some information about, if you please,” interrupted Hetherwick. “The Mrs. Whittingham who was at Sellithwaite ten years ago had the figure of a snake tattooed round a wrist, in various colours. She wore a black velvet band to cover it. Now﻿—”
Blenkinsop turned to his partner with a smile.
“I thought that would come up,” he said. “Well Mr. Hetherwick, if you want to know about that matter, both sisters are tattooed in the same fashion. That was a bit of work of the old sea-dog, their father﻿—a fancy, and a very foolish one, of his. He had the children tattooed in that way when they were quite young, much to their disgust when they grew older. Each lady wears a covering velvet armlet﻿—as I know.”

===Chinese===
布伦金索普（Blenkinsop）突然抛出的这句话，海瑟维克（Hetherwick）鉴于前几分钟的事态本已隐约料到几分，落在马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）身上，却好似一道闪电般的灵光把他照了个透亮。他张大了嘴，睁圆了眼，猛地转向海瑟维克，仿佛在令人晕头转向的迷宫里困了半晌之后，忽然看见有人给自己指出了一条出路。
“哦，原来如此，是这么回事？”他叫道，“一对孪生姐妹，嗯？那么——不过您接着讲，布伦金索普先生；我这就开始看出些门道来了。”
“这桩事对外人来说，无疑是费解的。”布伦金索普答道，“要把它讲清楚，我就得交代一点这家人的家世。我再说一遍，里弗斯雷德夫人（Lady Riversreade）和里斯托雷尔太太（Madame Listorelle）是孪生姐妹。她们的父亲当年先后做过几条商船的船长——就是老式的帆船——一辈子在世界各地闯荡惯了。他娶了一位美国太太，两个女儿都生在得克萨斯州（Texas）的加尔维斯顿（Galveston）。姐妹俩是在美国受的教育——至于她们早年生活的种种细节，没有必要细表——”
“恕我打岔，有个细节我倒想讨个明白。”海瑟维克插话道，“十年前在塞利斯韦特（Sellithwaite）的那位惠廷厄姆太太（Mrs. Whittingham），一只手腕上刺着一条盘蛇的花纹，五颜六色。她箍一条黑丝绒带子，就是为的遮住它。那么——”
布伦金索普含笑转向自己的合伙人。
“我就知道这件事早晚要被问起。”他说，“好吧，海瑟维克先生，您既然想打听，那就奉告：姐妹俩身上刺的是一模一样的花样。那是她们那位老海狗父亲的手笔——他一时兴起想的怪主意，而且蠢得很。两个孩子年纪还小的时候，他就叫人给她们刺了那玩意儿，姐妹俩长大以后，对此厌恶得很。如今两位女士各箍一条丝绒腕带遮着——这个我是知道的。”

===Original===
“Proceed, if you please,” said Hetherwick. “That’s cleared up!”
“I gather that you’ve been making inquiries on your own account,” observed Blenkinsop. “Well, since we’re determined to tell you everything, we’ll be as good as our word. So let’s come to the Sellithwaite affair. You’ve probably heard only one version﻿—you may have got it from Hannaford; you may have got it from old newspapers; you may have got it on the spot﻿—it’s immaterial to us. But you haven’t heard the version of the lady who was then Mrs. Whittingham. That puts a rather different complexion on things. For reasons of her own, with which we’ve nothing to do, Mrs. Whittingham﻿—her proper and legal name at that time﻿—stayed at Sellithwaite for a while. She had various transactions with a jeweller there; eventually she bought from him a diamond necklace at a price﻿—three thousand nine hundred pounds. She gave him a cheque for the amount, fully expecting that by the time it reached her bankers in Manchester certain funds for her credit would have reached them from America. There was a hitch﻿—the funds didn’t arrive﻿—the cheque was returned. The jeweller approached the police﻿—Hannaford, their superintendent there, got out a warrant and tracked down Mrs. Whittingham. He arrested her, and she got away from him, left England, and returned to America. For some time she was in financial straits. But she did not forget her liabilities, and eventually she sent the Sellithwaite jeweller the agreed price of the diamond necklace, and eight years’ interest at five percent on the amount. She holds his formal receipt for the money she sent him. So much for that episode﻿—whether Hannaford ever knew of the payment or not, I don’t know. We are rather inclined to believe that he didn’t. But﻿—the necklace was paid for, and paid for handsomely.”
“I may as well say that I’m aware of that,” remarked Hetherwick. “I have been informed of the fact at first hand.”
“Very good. I see you have been at Sellithwaite,” said Blenkinsop with another of his shrewd smiles. “Now then, we come to what is far more pertinent﻿—recent events. The situation as regards Lady Riversreade and Madame Listorelle some little time ago﻿—say, when Hannaford came to town﻿—was this: Lady Riversreade, widow of Sir John Riversreade, had inherited his considerable fortune, was settled at Riversreade Court in Surrey, and had founded a Home for wounded officers close by, of which my friend and partner, Major Penteney there, is London representative. Her sister, Madame Listorelle, had a flat at Paddington and another in New York. She was chiefly in New York, but she was sometimes in London and sometimes in Paris. As a matter of fact, Madame Listorelle is an expert in precious stones, and a dealer in them. But she has recently become engaged to be married to a well-known peer, an elderly, very wealthy man﻿—which possibly has a good deal to do with what I am going to tell you.”
“Probably, I think, Blenkinsop﻿—not possibly,” suggested Penteney. “Probably!﻿—decidedly.”
“Probably, then﻿—probably!” assented Blenkinsop. He leaned forward across his desk towards the two listeners. “Now, gentlemen, your closest attention, for I’m coming to the really important points of this matter﻿—those that affect the police particularly. About a fortnight ago Lady Riversreade, being in her private office at her home, close by Riversreade Court, was waited upon by a man who sent in a card bearing the name of Dr. Cyprian Baseverie. Lady Riversreade thought that the presenter of this card was some medical man who wished to inspect the Home, and he was admitted to see her. She soon found out that he had come on no such errand as she had imagined. He told her a strange tale. He let her know, to begin with, that he was fully conversant with that episode in her sister’s life which related to Sellithwaite and the diamond necklace. Lady Riversreade, who knew all about it, felt that the man’s information had been gained at first hand. He also let her know that Madame Listorelle’s whereabouts and engagement were familiar to him; in short, he showed that he was well up in the present family history, both as regards Lady Riversreade and her sister. Then he let his hand be seen more plainly. He told Lady Riversreade that a certain gang of men in London had become acquainted with the facts of the Sellithwaite matter, the warrant, the arrest, the escape, and that they were also aware of Madame Listorelle’s engagement to Lord﻿—we will leave his name out at present, or refer to him as Lord X﻿—and that they wanted a price for their silence. In other words, they were determined on blackmail. If they were not paid their price, they were going to Lord X, with all the facts, to tell him that he was engaged to a woman who, as they would put it, was still liable by the law of the land to arrest and prosecution for fraud.”

===Chinese===
“请继续讲吧。”海瑟维克说，“这一层算是弄清楚了！”
“看来您一直自己在私下查访。”布伦金索普说道，“好吧，既然我们打定主意把一切都告诉二位，我们说话就算话。这就讲塞利斯韦特那桩事。诸位听到的多半只是一个版本——也许是汉纳福德（Hannaford）告诉您的，也许是从旧报纸上读来的，也许是您实地访来的——这于我们无关紧要。可当年那位惠廷厄姆太太本人怎么讲，您还没有听过。她那一说，事情的体貌可就大不相同了。出于她自身的缘由——这与我们毫不相干——惠廷厄姆太太——这是她当时正式的法定姓名——在塞利斯韦特小住了些时日。她与当地一家珠宝商有过几番交易，末了向他购得一条钻石项链，价钱是——三千九百镑。她开出一张同额支票给他，满以为等到支票寄到她在曼彻斯特（Manchester）的银行时，美国方面有一笔存入她名下的款项早已汇到。不料出了岔子——款项没有到——支票被退了回来。珠宝商报了警——当地警司汉纳福德签发逮捕证，追查到了惠廷厄姆太太。他逮捕了她，她却从他手里脱了身，离开英国回了美国。有一阵子她手头十分拮据。可她并没有忘掉自己的债务，终于把议定的项链价款如数寄给了塞利斯韦特的珠宝商，还按年息百分之五，加付了那笔数目八年的利息。她还保存着他开具的正式收据。那桩事便是如此——汉纳福德后来究竟知不知道有这笔偿付，我不清楚。我们倒倾向于认为他并不知道。可是——那条项链是付清了价钱的，而且付得极为慷慨。”
“不妨直说，这一点我已经知道了。”海瑟维克说道，“有人把事实的第一手情况告诉过我。”
“很好。可见您到塞利斯韦特去过了。”布伦金索普说着，脸上又浮起精明的一笑，“那么，咱们这就讲到关系远为密切的部分——近来的事。就在不多几天之前——姑且说，汉纳福德到伦敦来的那阵子——里弗斯雷德夫人和里斯托雷尔太太的境况是这样的：里弗斯雷德夫人是约翰·里弗斯雷德爵士（Sir John Riversreade）的遗孀，继承了他相当可观的财产，定居在萨里郡的里弗斯雷德府（Riversreade Court），并在府邸近旁创办了一所收容伤残军官的慈善院，我这位朋友兼合伙人、坐在那边的彭特尼少校（Major Penteney），便是该院驻伦敦的代表。她的妹妹里斯托雷尔太太在帕丁顿（Paddington）有一套公寓，在纽约（New York）另有一套。她平日主要住在纽约，不过有时在伦敦，有时在巴黎（Paris）。说实在的，里斯托雷尔太太是位宝石专家，自己也做珠宝生意。可她新近订了婚，将嫁给一位知名的贵族——上了年纪、家财万贯的人物——这件事，恐怕同我接下来要告诉二位的情况大有干系。”
“依我看，布伦金索普，这不该说‘或许’，该说‘多半’。”彭特尼提意见道，“多半！——简直是笃定无疑。”
“那么，就多半——多半！”布伦金索普应道。他从书桌后面朝两位听客探过身来。“二位先生，这会儿务必凝神细听，因为我要讲到这桩案子里真正紧要的几处了——尤其是与警方利害攸关的那几处。大约两个星期以前，里弗斯雷德夫人正在自家宅中——就在里弗斯雷德府近旁——的私人办事房里，有人递进一张名片求见，名片上印着西普里安·巴斯维里医生（Dr. Cyprian Baseverie）的名字。里弗斯雷德夫人以为递名片的是位想来参观慈善院的医生，便吩咐放他进来。她很快就发觉，他此来的用意与她所想的截然是两回事。他讲了一个离奇的故事。他首先让她知道，她妹妹生平那段与塞利斯韦特和钻石项链相关的过节，他了如指掌。里弗斯雷德夫人本来自始至终知情，一听便觉出此人的消息是第一手的。他还让她知道，里斯托雷尔太太如今的行踪和婚约，他也一清二楚；总而言之，他摆出了一副对这家现下的家事了如指掌的样子——里弗斯雷德夫人这边也好，她妹妹那边也好。随后，他就把手亮得更明白了。他告诉里弗斯雷德夫人，伦敦有一伙人已经摸清了塞利斯韦特那桩事的种种事实——逮捕证、被捕、脱逃——而且他们也知道里斯托雷尔太太订婚许配了某位勋爵——眼下我们姑且隐去他的名字，就称他 X 勋爵（Lord X）吧——这伙人要为他们的缄默讨一笔代价。换言之，他们打定了敲诈的主意。倘若拿不到他们要的价钱，他们就要带着全部事实去找 X 勋爵，告诉他：他订婚要娶的这个女人，用他们的话说，依照国法至今仍可因诈骗罪被逮捕、被起诉。”

===Original===
“Isn’t she?” asked Matherfield suddenly. “No time-limit in these sort of cases, I think, Mr. Blenkinsop. Liable ten or twenty or thirty years after﻿—I think!”
“I’ve already said that the Sellithwaite affair was one of account,” replied Blenkinsop. “There was no intent to defraud, and the full amount and interest on it was duly paid. But that’s not the point﻿—we’re dealing with the presentment of this to Lady Riversreade by the man Baseverie. Of course, Lady Riversreade didn’t know how the law might be, and she was alarmed on her sister’s account. She asked Baseverie what he wanted. He told her plainly then that he could settle these men﻿—if she would find the money. He had, he said, a certain hold over them which he could use to advantage. Lady Riversreade wanted to know what that hold was; he wouldn’t tell her. She then wanted to know how much the men wanted; he wouldn’t say. What he did say was that if she would be prepared to find the money to silence them, he, during the next week, would exert pressure on them to accept a reasonable amount, and would call on her on the following Friday and tell her what they would take. She made that appointment with him.”
“And, I hope, took advice in the meantime,” muttered Matherfield. “Ought to have handed him over there and then!”
“No﻿—she took no advice in the meantime,” continued Blenkinsop. “Madame Listorelle was in Paris﻿—Major Penteney was away on business in the country. Lady Riversreade awaited Baseverie’s next coming. When he came he told her what his gang wanted﻿—thirty thousand pounds. He specified, too, the way in which it was to be paid﻿—in a fashion which would have prevented the payment being traced to the people who received it. But now Lady Riversreade was more prepared﻿—she had had time to think. She expected Major Penteney next day; she also knew that her sister would return from Paris on the following Monday. So she told Baseverie that she would give him an answer on Monday evening if he would make an appointment to meet her at some place in London. Eventually they made an appointment at Vivian’s, in Candlestick Passage. Baseverie went away; next day Lady Riversreade told Major Penteney all that had happened. As a result, he went with her to Vivian’s on Monday evening. They waited an hour beyond the fixed time. Baseverie made no appearance﻿—”
“Just so!” muttered Matherfield. “He wouldn’t﻿—the Major being there!”
“Perhaps,” assented Blenkinsop. “Anyway, he didn’t materialise. So Lady Riversreade went away, leaving Major Penteney behind her. I may say that he stopped there for some further time, keeping a sharp lookout for the man whom Lady Riversreade had described in detail﻿—a remarkable man in appearance, I understand. But he never saw him.”

===Chinese===
“难道不是吗？”马瑟菲尔德突然问道，“这一类案子是没有追诉时限的吧，布伦金索普先生？隔上十年、二十年、三十年照样跑不了——我记得没错！”
“我方才已经说过，塞利斯韦特那桩事是一桩账目纠葛。”布伦金索普答道，“当初并无诈骗的意图，欠款连同利息也早已如数偿清。可那不是眼下的问题——我们说的是巴斯维里这个人如何把这层利害摆到里弗斯雷德夫人面前。当然，里弗斯雷德夫人不知道法律上究竟如何，为了妹妹担惊受怕。她问巴斯维里意欲何为。他这才明明白白地说，他能够摆平那伙人——只要她肯出钱。他说，他手里抓着那伙人的某种把柄，可以拿来派上用场。里弗斯雷德夫人想知道那把柄是什么；他不肯讲。她又想知道那伙人要多少；他也不肯说。他只说，倘若她备妥一笔钱来封他们的口，下一星期里他就向那伙人施加压力，叫他们接受一个公道的数目，并于下星期五登门，告诉她他们肯收的数目。她同他约定了这个日子。”
“还有，我希望她这中间总该请教过律师吧。”马瑟菲尔德咕哝道，“当时就该当场把那家伙扭送官府才对！”
“没有——这中间她谁也没有请教。”布伦金索普接着说，“里斯托雷尔太太当时在巴黎——彭特尼少校正出差在外郡。里弗斯雷德夫人只等巴斯维里下次登门。他来了，告诉她那伙人要的数目——三万镑。他还指定了付款的方式——用一种教人无法追查到收钱人手里的付法。可这一回里弗斯雷德夫人有了准备——她有工夫细想了。她料定彭特尼少校次日就到；她也知道妹妹下星期一便从巴黎回来。于是她对巴斯维里说，如果他肯约个地方在伦敦见面，星期一晚上她给他答复。末了他们约定在烛台巷（Candlestick Passage）的维维安（Vivian's）碰头。巴斯维里走了；第二天，里弗斯雷德夫人把事情的前后原委都对彭特尼少校讲了。于是星期一晚上，他陪着她去了维维安。过了约定的时刻，他们又等了一个钟头。巴斯维里始终没有露面——”
“这就对喽！”马瑟菲尔德咕哝道，“他才不来呢——少校在场呢！”
“也许吧。”布伦金索普应道，“反正他没有露面。于是里弗斯雷德夫人先行离去，把彭特尼少校留在了那里。可以说，他又在那里守候了一些时候，留神提防着里弗斯雷德夫人详细描述过的那个人——据我所知，那是个相貌惹眼的人物。可他终究没有见着。”

===Original===
“No!” exclaimed Matherfield cynically. “Of course he didn’t! But she would ha’ done﻿—if she’d gone alone!”
“Well, there it was,” continued Blenkinsop. “Now for Lady Riversreade. She drove to her sister’s flat in Paddington, and found Madame Listorelle just returned from Paris. She told her all that had happened. Madame Listorelle determined to go to New York at once and get certain papers from her flat there which would definitely establish her absolute innocence in the Sellithwaite affair. Leaving Lady Riversreade in the flat, Madame Listorelle set off for Southampton before five o’clock next morning﻿—yes?”
Matherfield, uttering a deep groan, smote his forehead.
“Aye!” he muttered. “Just so! To be sure! But go on!﻿—go on, sir.”
“You seem to be highly surprised,” said Blenkinsop. “However﻿—at Southampton she booked a passage in a name she always used when travelling﻿—her maiden name﻿—by the *Tartaric*, sailing that afternoon. That done, she went to a hotel for lunch. Then she began to think things over more calmly. And in the end, instead of sailing for New York, she went back, cancelled her booking, and set off by train to Lord X’s country seat in Wiltshire, and told him the whole story. She wired to her sister as to what she had done, and in the evening wrote to her. Meanwhile, Lady Riversreade had returned, early in the morning, to Riversreade Court. Major Penteney went with her. He was confident that Baseverie would turn up. He did turn up! But he did not see Lady Riversreade. He saw Major Penteney﻿—alone. And Major Penteney, after a little plain talk to him, metaphorically kicked him out, and told him to do his worst. He went﻿—warned that if ever he showed himself there again he would be handed over to the police.”
Matherfield groaned again, but the reason of his distress was obviously of a different nature.

===Chinese===
“可不是！”马瑟菲尔德冷冷地嚷道，“他当然不来！可要是她一个人去的——他准来！”
“唉，事情就这样了。”布伦金索普接着讲，“再说里弗斯雷德夫人。她乘车赶到妹妹在帕丁顿的公寓，见里斯托雷尔太太刚从巴黎回来。她把事情原原本本都对她说了。里斯托雷尔太太当即决定赶往纽约，去取她那边公寓里的几份文件——那几份文件可以确证她在塞利斯韦特事件中完全清白。她把里弗斯雷德夫人留在公寓里，第二天早晨五点不到便动身去了南安普顿（Southampton）——怎么样？”
马瑟菲尔德发出一声深长的呻吟，一巴掌拍在自己额头上。
“可不是嘛！”他喃喃道，“正是这样！可不！可是您接着讲！——接着讲呀，先生。”
“二位像是大为吃惊。”布伦金索普说，“且不管它——到了南安普顿，她用自己出门旅行一贯使用的名字——她的闺名——订下了*“塔尔塔里克”号*（*Tartaric*）的舱位，那条船当天下午启航。订好之后，她到一家旅馆用午餐。随后她把前后的事平心静气细想了一遍。到头来，她没有启航去纽约，而是折了回去，退掉舱位，乘火车直奔 X 勋爵在威尔特郡（Wiltshire）的乡间宅邸，把事情的原委和盘托出。她拍电报把自己做的事告诉了姐姐，晚上又写了一封信去。与此同时，里弗斯雷德夫人一清早已经回到里弗斯雷德府。彭特尼少校随她同去。他断定巴斯维里准会露面。他果然露面了！可他没有见着里弗斯雷德夫人。他见着的是彭特尼少校——单独一人。彭特尼少校对他不客气地说了几句，简直是把他一脚踢出了门，还叫他有什么狠招尽管使出来。他走了——临走还得了警告：若再敢在那地方露面，就把他扭送警察。”
马瑟菲尔德又呻吟了一声，不过他这一番懊恼，缘由显然是另一码事。

===Original===
“A mistake, sir﻿—a great mistake!” he exclaimed, shaking his head at Penteney. “You shouldn’t have let that fellow go like that! You should have handed him over there and then. Go? You don’t know where he may be!”
“Oh, well, we’re not quite such fools as we seem, Matherfield,” he replied. “When I went down to Dorking with Lady Riversreade on Tuesday morning I had with me a smart man whom I can trust. He saw Baseverie arrive; he saw Baseverie leave. I think we shall be able to put our fingers on Baseverie at any moment. Our man won’t lose sight of him!”
“Oh, well, that’s better, sir, that’s much better!” said Matherfield. “That’s all right! A chap like that should be watched night and day. But now, gentlemen, about this reward! Your notion of offering it sprang, of course, from this Baseverie business. But﻿—how, exactly? Did he mention Hannaford to Lady Riversreade?”
“No!” replied Blenkinsop. “I’ll tell you how we came to issue the advertisement. All Sunday afternoon and evening, and for some time on Monday morning, Lady Riversreade, Major Penteney, and myself were in close consultation about this affair. I’ll tell you at once how and why we connected it with the poisoning of Hannaford, of which, of course, all of us had read in the newspapers.”
“Aye!﻿—how, now?” asked Matherfield.
“Because of this,” answered Blenkinsop. He tapped his desk to emphasise his words, watching Matherfield keenly as he spoke. “Because of this: Baseverie told Lady Riversreade that the gang of blackmailers had in their possession the original warrant for Mrs. Whittingham’s arrest!”

===Chinese===
“失策啊，先生——大大的失策！”他冲着彭特尼直摇头，嚷道，“您不该就那么放那家伙走！您就该当时当地把他扭送官府。放了？您可不知道他如今会在哪儿！”
“噢，这个嘛，马瑟菲尔德，我们还不至于像看上去那么傻。”他答道，“星期二早晨我陪里弗斯雷德夫人下乡去多金（Dorking）的时候，随身带了一个伶俐可靠的人。他眼看着巴斯维里到，又眼看着巴斯维里走。我想我们随时都能一把揪住巴斯维里。咱们的人不会让他脱离视线！”
“噢，这样啊，那就好，先生，这样好多了！”马瑟菲尔德说，“这就妥了！这样的家伙就该日夜盯着。可是二位先生，如今再说说那笔赏金！二位悬赏的念头，不用说，就是打巴斯维里这件事上来的。可是——究竟怎么个来龙去脉？他跟里弗斯雷德夫人提没提过汉纳福德？”
“没提过！”布伦金索普答道，“我这就告诉诸位那则启事是怎么发出来的。整个星期天的下午和晚上，再加上星期一早上好一阵子，里弗斯雷德夫人、彭特尼少校和我，一直在密议这桩事。我们又是怎样、又是为什么把它同汉纳福德中毒身亡的案子联系到一起——那案子不用说，我们都在报上读到了——我这就一并讲明。”
“可不是！——那是怎么联系上的？”马瑟菲尔德问。
“就因为这个。”布伦金索普答道。他敲着书桌来加重语气，说话时目光锐利地盯着马瑟菲尔德。“就因为这个：巴斯维里告诉里弗斯雷德夫人，那伙敲诈犯手里，握着当年逮捕惠廷厄姆太太的那张原始逮捕证！”

===Original===
Hetherwick felt himself impelled to jump in his chair, to exclaim loudly. He repressed the inclination, but Matherfield was less reserved.
“Ah!” he exclaimed sharply. “Ah!”
“Baseverie made a false step there,” continued Blenkinsop. “He should never have told that. But he did﻿—no doubt he thought a rich woman easy prey. Now, of course, when we came to consult, we knew all about the Sellithwaite affair; we knew, too, that Hannaford was superintendent at the time and that he had the warrant; it was not at all improbable that he had preserved it in his pocketbook, and had it on him when he came to London. What, then, was the obvious conclusion﻿—that the men who now held that warrant had got it, probably by foul means, from Hannaford, and were concerned in his murder? And﻿—more than that﻿—did the gang of which Baseverie spoke really exist? Wasn’t it likely that the gang was﻿—Baseverie?”
“Aye!” muttered Matherfield. “I’ve been thinking of that!”
“Yet,” said Blenkinsop, “it was on the cards that there might be a gang. We searched all the newspapers’ accounts thoroughly. We found that next to no information could be got as to Hannaford’s movements between the time of his arrival in London and the night of his death. The one man who might have given more information about Hannaford’s doings on the evening preceding his death﻿—Granett﻿—was dead, evidently poisoned, as Hannaford was poisoned. These were circumstances﻿—they’ve probably occurred to both of you﻿—which led us to believe that Hannaford had formed the acquaintance of folk here in town who were of a shady sort. And one thing was absolutely certain﻿—if the gang, or if Baseverie, had really got that warrant, they had got it from Hannaford! Eh?”
“That may be taken as certain,” assented Hetherwick. “Either directly or indirectly, it must have been from him.”

===Chinese===
海瑟维克只觉得自己要从椅子上跳起来，要放声喊出来。他强自按捺住了，马瑟菲尔德可没有那么矜持。
“啊！”他失声叫道，“啊！”
“巴斯维里在这一步上走了失着。”布伦金索普接着说，“那句话他本不该讲的。可他讲了——不用问，他把有钱的女人当成了好上钩的猎物。这下子，等到我们坐下来商议，塞利斯韦特那桩事的底细我们已经尽知；我们还知道汉纳福德当年正是那边的警司，逮捕证就在他手里；十之八九，他把它收存在随身的皮夹子里，进伦敦时也带在身上。那么，显而易见的结论是什么——如今握着那张逮捕证的人，多半是用卑鄙手段从汉纳福德手里弄到它的，并且与他被害的案子脱不了干系？还有——更进一步——巴斯维里嘴里说的那伙人，当真存在吗？那伙人，岂不很可能就是——巴斯维里自己？”
“可不是！”马瑟菲尔德喃喃道，“这一层我早就在琢磨了！”
“然而，”布伦金索普说，“也难保当真有一伙人。我们把各家报纸的种种报道彻底翻检了一遍。我们发现，关于汉纳福德从他抵达伦敦到丧命那晚之间的行踪，几乎查不出半点消息。本可以多提供一些他死前那天晚上行迹的人——格拉内特（Granett）——已经死了，显然是中毒死的，和汉纳福德中的是同样的毒。这些情形——二位恐怕也都想到过——使我们相信，汉纳福德在城里结交上了些不三不四的人物。而有一件事是板上钉钉的——倘若真有那么一伙人，或者倘若巴斯维里一个人，果真拿到了那张逮捕证，那他们准是从汉纳福德手里拿到的！嗯？”
“这一点可以算是定论了。”海瑟维克表示同意，“不管直接也好，间接也好，必定是从他手里出去的。”

===Original===
“We think they, or he, got it directly from him,” said Blenkinsop. “Our theory is that if there is a gang Baseverie is an active, perhaps the leading, member; that Hannaford was previously acquainted with him or some other member; that Hannaford was with him or them on the evening preceding his death; that he jokingly told them that he had discovered the identity of Madame Listorelle with Mrs. Whittingham; and that they poisoned him﻿—and Granett, as being present﻿—in order to keep the secret to themselves and to blackmail Madame Listorelle and her sister, Lady Riversreade. That’s our general idea﻿—and that’s why, on Monday noon, we issued the advertisement. We meant to keep things to ourselves at first, and if substantial evidence came, to pass it over to the police. Now you know everything. It may be, if there is a gang, that one member will turn traitor for the sake of five thousand pounds and if he can exculpate himself satisfactorily; it may be, too, that matters will develop until we’re in a position to fasten things on Baseverie﻿—”
“I still wish that either Lady Riversreade or Major Penteney had handed him over to custody!” said Matherfield. “You see﻿—”
“You’ve got to remember that Baseverie never demanded anything for himself,” interrupted Penteney. “He represented himself as a go-between. But our man’s safe enough﻿—a retired detective, and﻿—”
Just then a clerk opened the door and entered with a telegram. Blenkinsop tore open the envelope, glanced hurriedly at the message and flung the form on his desk with an exclamation of annoyance.
“This is from our man!” he said. “Sent from Dover. Followed Baseverie down there﻿—and Baseverie’s slipped him!”

===Chinese===
“我们认为，他们也好，他一个人也好，是直接从他手里拿到的。”布伦金索普说，“我们的推想是：倘若真有一伙人，巴斯维里便是其中活跃的一员，说不定还是领头的人物；汉纳福德先前就同他、或者同其中另一人相识；汉纳福德丧命的前一天晚上，正是同他，或者同他们在一起；他半开玩笑地告诉了他们，自己已经发现里斯托雷尔太太就是当年的惠廷厄姆太太；于是他们下了毒——连在场的格拉内特也一并毒死——好把秘密攥在自己手里，好去敲诈里斯托雷尔太太和她的姐姐里弗斯雷德夫人。我们大致是这样看的——也正因如此，星期一正午我们发出了那则启事。起初我们打算把事情攥在自己手里，待到有了确凿证据，再移交警方。现在二位尽知底细了。倘若真有一伙人，说不定哪个成员为了那五千镑——只要他自己能够洗刷干净——就会反水出卖同伙；事情也可能这样发展下去，直到我们能把罪名牢牢扣到巴斯维里头上——”
“我还是巴不得当初里弗斯雷德夫人也好，彭特尼少校也好，随便哪一位把他押送归案！”马瑟菲尔德说，“您想想——”
“您得记住，巴斯维里从头到尾没有为自己索要过一文钱。”彭特尼插话道，“他把自己说成是个居间传话的人。不过咱们盯他的人万无一失——一位退了职的侦探，而且——”
正在这时，一个文书推门进来，手里拿着一封电报。布伦金索普撕开信封，匆匆扫了一眼电文，懊恼地叫了一声，把电报纸甩在书桌上。
“是咱们的人发来的！”他说，“从多佛（Dover）发的。他一路跟到那边——巴斯维里把他甩掉了！”

## XXVII The Assurance / 二十七 定心丸

===Original===
Rhona went back to her old quarters at the little hotel in Surrey Street for that night, and next morning Hetherwick came round to her, with an armful of newspapers. Finding her alone, he laid them on the table at her side with a significant nod of his head at certain big black letters which topped the uppermost columns.
“Matherfield must have given plenty of informing news to the pressmen last night,” he remarked with a grim smile. “It’s all in there﻿—his own adventures at Southampton yesterday; mine and Robmore’s in Westminster, and all the rest of it. I believe the newspaper people call this sort of thing a story﻿—and a fine story it makes! Winding up, of course, with the dramatic arrest of Baseverie at Waterloo! I’m afraid we’re in for publicity for a time, worse luck!”
“Shall we﻿—shall I﻿—have to appear at that man’s trial?” asked Rhona.
“That’s unavoidable, I’m afraid, and at other things before that,” answered Hetherwick. “There’ll be the proceedings before the magistrate, and the adjourned inquest, and so on. Can’t be helped; and there’ll be some satisfaction in knowing that we’re ridding the world of a peculiarly cruel and cold-blooded murderer! That chap Baseverie is certainly as consummate a villain as I ever heard of. A human spider﻿—and clever in his web-spinning. But I wish one had a few more particulars on one point﻿—and yet I don’t see how one’s to get them.”
“What point?” asked Rhona.

===Chinese===
罗娜（Rhona）当晚回到了萨里街（Surrey Street）那家小旅馆里她原先的房间；第二天早晨，海瑟维克（Hetherwick）抱着一摞报纸来看她。见屋里只有她一人，他把报纸放在她手边的桌上，朝着最上面那份报纸栏目顶端的几个黑色大标题，意味深长地点了点头。
“马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）昨晚准是给报馆记者们提供了不少有内容的消息。”他带着一丝冷笑说道。“全在这儿了——他昨天在南安普顿（Southampton）的历险；我和罗布莫尔（Robmore）在威斯敏斯特（Westminster）的经历，还有其余种种。我相信办报的人管这类东西叫‘故事’——这故事可真够精彩的！收尾处，自然是巴斯维里（Baseverie）在滑铁卢（Waterloo）车站被戏剧性地逮捕！恐怕咱们要出一阵子风头了，真倒霉！”
“咱们——我是说，我——是不是得出席对那人的审判？”罗娜问。
“恐怕这避免不了，而且在那之前还有别的场合。”海瑟维克答道。“有地方法官面前的程序，还有延期再审的验尸审讯，等等。没法子；不过一想到我们正在替世间除掉一个格外残忍、冷血的凶手，也就算有几分慰藉了！巴斯维里那家伙，的确是我平生所知最老练到家的一个恶棍。一只人形蜘蛛——织起网来手段又高明。可我但愿能就一点多知道些详情——却又看不出这详情从何得来。”
“哪一点？”罗娜问。

===Original===
“That sealed packet, containing the details, or formula, or whatever it is, of your grandfather’s invention,” replied Hetherwick. “Where is it? What, precisely, is it? Did Ambrose get it from him? Has Baseverie got it? So far as I can make out, the whole thing began with that. Whether it was really worth a farthing or a fortune, your grandfather brought to London something which he honestly believed to be of great value, and there’s no doubt that he got into the hands of those two men, Ambrose and Baseverie, because of it. There’s no doubt, either, that in conversation with them, he told them, perhaps jokingly, what he knew about Madame Listorelle. Nor is there any doubt that these two murdered him. Nor is there any doubt, in my mind, as to *how* they murdered him! You must remember that both men were trained medical men, and, obviously of a scientific turn of mind into the bargain. Each had doubtless made a deep study of poisons. Such a knowledge is of value to such men as they were﻿—men of criminal tendencies. Probably they knew of a subtle poison easily administered, the effects of which would not be evident for some hours. No doubt they *timed* their work, so that their victim should die swiftly and suddenly when well away from their laboratory. And, of course, they did the same thing in the case of Granett. Granett paid the penalty of being with your grandfather. But for what did they murder your grandfather? Did they get rid of him so that they could keep his secret about Madame Listorelle to themselves, and blackmail her and her sister, or that they could rob him of his invention and turn it to their own profit? If the latter, then﻿—”
He paused, looking inquiringly at Rhona, as if he expected her to see what he was after. But Rhona shook her head.
“I don’t follow,” she said. “What then?”
“This,” replied Hetherwick. “If their desire to get hold of your grandfather’s secret was their motive, then that secret’s worth a lot of money! Money which ought to come to you. Don’t you see? Where is the secret? Where’s the sealed packet? I suppose the police would search Baseverie last night﻿—perhaps they found it on him. We shall hear﻿—but, anyway it’s yours.”
Rhona made a gesture of aversion.

===Chinese===
“就是那只封套，装着你祖父那项发明的细节——或者说配方，或者随便是什么东西。”海瑟维克答道。“它在哪儿？究竟是什么？是安布罗斯（Ambrose）从他手里拿去的吗？眼下在巴斯维里手上吗？据我判断，整件事就是打它起头的。且不论它究竟值一枚小铜板还是一笔巨财，你祖父把一件他真心相信价值连城的东西带到了伦敦，而毫无疑问，正是为了它，他才落到安布罗斯和巴斯维里那两个人手里。同样毫无疑问的是，他在同他们攀谈时，或许半开玩笑地，把自己知道的里斯托雷尔太太（Madame Listorelle）的事说了出去。这两个人谋害了他，这也没有疑问。至于他们*如何*下的手，我心里同样没有疑问！你要记住，这两个人都是受过科班训练的医学人士，而且显然还兼备一副科学头脑。两人无疑都下过深功钻研毒物。这样的知识，对他们那种人——生性倾向犯罪的人——大有用处。他们多半知道某种不易察觉、便于下手的毒药，药效要过几个钟头才显现出来。毫无疑问，他们是*掐准了时间*动手的，好让受害者死在他们实验室远远之外的某处，死得又快又突然。当然，对格拉内特（Granett），他们如法炮制。格拉内特为陪你祖父走那一遭，付出了代价。可他们究竟为什么谋害你祖父？是要除掉他，好独占他知道的里斯托雷尔太太的秘密，借此讹诈她和她妹妹，还是要夺走他的发明，据为己用、渔利自肥？若属后者，那么——”
他顿住话头，用探询的目光望着罗娜，仿佛指望她能看出自己话里的用意。可罗娜摇了摇头。
“我没听明白，”她说，“那又怎样？”
“是这样，”海瑟维克答道。“如果他们一心想弄到你祖父的秘密正是其动机，那么这秘密就值一大笔钱！这笔钱本该归你。你还不明白吗？秘密在哪儿？封套在哪儿？我料想警方昨晚搜了巴斯维里的身——说不定已在他身上搜到了。我们总会听说的——不管怎样，它是你的。”
罗娜做了一个嫌恶的手势。

===Original===
“I should hate to touch or have anything to do with it if it had been in that man’s possession!” she said. “But I don’t think there’s any doubt that they murdered my grandfather because of that secret. Only, I think, too, they’d a double motive. The secret about Madame Listorelle was their second string. Probably they believed that Lady Riversreade would be an easy prey. And I think she would have been, if she hadn’t had Major Penteney to fall back on. I know she was dreadfully upset after Baseverie’s first visit. So I put it this way﻿—always have done: they thought they could sell grandfather’s invention for a lot of money, and get another lot out of Lady Riversreade and Madame Listorelle as blackmail.”
“Black money, indeed, all of it!” exclaimed Hetherwick. “Well﻿—”
A woman-servant put her head into the little parlour in which they were sitting, and looked significantly at Rhona.
“There’s a policeman downstairs, miss, asking for you,” she announced. “Leastways, he wants to know if you can tell him if Mr. Hetherwick’s here or been here.”
Hetherwick went to the head of the stair; a policeman standing in the hall below looked up and touched his helmet.

===Chinese===
“要是那东西沾过那人的手，我连碰都不愿碰，更不愿同它有任何瓜葛！”她说。“不过我认为，他们是为了那个秘密才谋害我祖父的，这一点没什么可疑。只是我还想，他们的动机是双重的。关于里斯托雷尔太太的秘密是他们的第二手准备。他们多半以为里弗斯雷德夫人（Lady Riversreade）会是容易上钩的猎物。我也觉得她本会如此，若不是还有彭特尼少校（Major Penteney）可以倚仗。我知道巴斯维里头一回上门之后，她给吓得失魂落魄。所以我是这么看的——向来这么看：他们想着把祖父的发明卖一大笔钱，再从里弗斯雷德夫人和里斯托雷尔太太身上讹出另一笔钱来。”
“这笔钱可真是地道的黑心钱，每一文都是！”海瑟维克叫道。“唔——”
一名女仆把头探进两人坐着的小客厅，意味深长地朝罗娜望了一眼。
“小姐，楼下有位警察要见您。”她通报道。“呃，反正是，他想问问您能不能告诉他，海瑟维克先生这会儿在不在，或者来过没有。”
海瑟维克走到楼梯口；下面门厅里站着的那个警察抬头望了望，碰了碰自己的头盔。

===Original===
“Inspector Matherfield’s compliments, sir, and could you step round and bring Miss Hannaford with you?” he asked. “There’s new developments, Mr. Hetherwick. Important!”
“We’ll come at once,” assented Hetherwick. “Ten minutes!” He went back and hurried Rhona away. “What now?” he asked as they hastened towards Matherfield’s office. “Perhaps they’ve extracted something out of Baseverie? Or possibly the newspapers have attracted the attention of somebody who can give further news?”
The last suggestion strengthened itself when, on entering Matherfield’s room, they found him closeted with two strangers whose appearance was that of responsible and well-to-do commercial men. All three were discovered in what looked like a serious and deep conversation, and Hetherwick was quick to notice that the two unknown men looked at Rhona with unusual interest. Matherfield made haste to introduce her as the late ex-Superintendent Hannaford’s granddaughter, and Hetherwick as a gentleman who had been much concerned in the recent proceedings.
“These gentlemen, Miss Hannaford and Mr. Hetherwick,” he proceeded, waving his hand at the others, “are Messrs. Culthwaite and Houseover, manufacturing chemists, of East Ham﻿—incidentally, they’ve also a big place in Lancashire. And having seen this morning’s papers, in which, as you’ve no doubt noticed, there’s a good deal about our affair, they’ve come straight to me with some news which will prove uncommonly useful when Baseverie’s put in the dock before the magistrate this afternoon. The fact is, Mr. Hetherwick, these gentlemen have supplied a missing link!”
“What link?” asked Hetherwick eagerly.

===Chinese===
“探长马瑟菲尔德向您问好，先生，能不能请您挪步过去一趟，把汉纳福德（Hannaford）小姐也一并带来？”他问。“有新进展了，海瑟维克先生。要紧的！”
“我们这就去。”海瑟维克应道。“十分钟！”他回屋去，匆匆把罗娜带走了。“又出什么事了？”两人急急赶往马瑟菲尔德的办公室时，他问道。“莫非他们从巴斯维里嘴里掏出了什么？要不就是报纸引来了什么人的注意，能提供进一步的消息？”
后一种猜测，在两人走进马瑟菲尔德的房间时得到了印证：他们发现他正同两个陌生人关起门来密谈，那两人的模样像是稳重殷实的商界人士。三人谈得看上去严肃而深入；海瑟维克很快便注意到，两个生客打量罗娜的目光异乎寻常地专注。马瑟菲尔德赶紧介绍，说她是已故的前警司汉纳福德的孙女，又说海瑟维克是近来这桩案子中深度涉事的一位先生。
“汉纳福德小姐，海瑟维克先生，这几位，”他朝那两人摆摆手，接着说道，“是库思韦特（Culthwaite）先生和豪斯奥弗（Houseover）先生，伊斯特汉姆（East Ham）的化工制药制造商——顺带一提，他们在兰开夏（Lancashire）还有一家大厂。他们看了今早的报纸——想必你们也注意到了，那上面关于我们这桩案子登了不少东西——便径直来找我，带来一个消息；等今天下午巴斯维里被押到地方法官跟前的被告席上，这消息将证明格外有用。事实是，海瑟维克先生，这几位先生补上了缺失的一环！”
“哪一环？”海瑟维克急切地问。

===Original===
Matherfield nodded at the elder of the two men, Culthwaite, who produced a pocketbook, and extracted from it a sheet of paper. Silently, he passed it over to Matherfield, who turned to Rhona.
“Now, Miss Hannaford,” he said, with a note of triumph in his voice, “I dare say you can positively identify your grandfather’s handwriting and his way of making figures? Can you swear that this has been written by him?”
Rhona gave but one glance at the paper before looking up with a glance of positive assertion.
“Oh, yes!” she exclaimed. “That is his writing, without a doubt! Nothing could be more certain!”
Matherfield turned to Hetherwick.

===Chinese===
马瑟菲尔德朝两人中年长的那位、库思韦特点了点头；后者掏出一个记事本，从里头抽出一张纸，一言不发地递给马瑟菲尔德。马瑟菲尔德转向罗娜。
“好了，汉纳福德小姐，”他说，声音里带着一丝得胜的意味，“我敢说，您准能认出您祖父的笔迹，认出他写数字的那路手法？您能起誓，这张纸是他写的吗？”
罗娜只朝那张纸扫了一眼，便抬起头来，目光里是确凿无疑的肯定。
“噢，是的！”她叫道。“这正是他的笔迹，毫无疑问！再确凿不过了！”
马瑟菲尔德转向海瑟维克。

===Original===
“That’s the formula for the ink!” he said. “Now we’ve got the big thing we wanted! And Mr. Culthwaite will tell you how he got it.”
Culthwaite, after allowing Hetherwick to look at the paper, carefully replaced it in his pocketbook. There was an air of anxiety about him and about his partner concerning which Hetherwick began to make guesses﻿—they looked as if they were uncertain and uneasy. But Culthwaite was ready enough to tell his story.
“We got it in this way,” he said. “And I may as well say, as I’ve already said to you, Mr. Matherfield, that I don’t think we should have got it at all if you police people hadn’t been so reticent on that one particular point﻿—if you’d noised it abroad about Hannaford’s secret we might have been forewarned. However, some little time ago, a man whom we knew as Basing, and whom I firmly believe to be the Baseverie that we’ve read about in the papers this morning﻿—a man, mind you, that we’d done business with now and then during the last year or so﻿—came to us and offered us the formula for a new black ink which he asserted would drive every known ink off the market, all over the world! He made extravagant claims for it; he swore it was the first absolutely perfect writing fluid ever invented. He brought a sample of it which he’d made up himself﻿—he put it to various tests. But he did more﻿—he offered us the use of the secret formula so that we ourselves could make and test it before deciding whether we’d fall in with his suggestion, which was that we should offer him a lump cash sum for the formula. Well, we did make the ink, from the formula, and we did test it, and there is no doubt about it﻿—it is all, and perhaps more, that Basing, or Baseverie claimed for its excellence. I needn’t go into the drawbacks attaching to most well-known inks﻿—this has none of them. And when Basing came back to us, a few days ago, we decided to buy the formula from him. We agreed upon a cash price, and day before yesterday we paid the amount over﻿—at our office in East Ham.”
“Yes?” said Hetherwick quietly. “And﻿—what was the price agreed upon?”
The two partners exchanged a glance; it seemed to Rhona, who was watching them intently, that they looked more uneasy than before. But Culthwaite replied with promptitude.

===Chinese===
“这就是那种墨水的配方！”他说。“现在，我们要的那件大事到手了！库思韦特先生会告诉你们，他是怎么到手的。”
库思韦特让海瑟维克看过那张纸，随后小心地把它放回记事本。他和他的合伙人身上都带着一股焦虑的神气，海瑟维克开始暗自猜测缘故——两人看上去迟疑不宁。不过库思韦特倒是很愿意把事情讲出来。
“我们是这么到手的。”他说。“我不妨直说——这话我也跟您说过了，马瑟菲尔德先生——要不是你们警方偏偏在那一个问题上口风那么紧，我看我们根本到不了手；倘若你们把汉纳福德的秘密嚷嚷得满城皆知，我们说不定倒有了提防。话说不久前，有个我们称作贝辛（Basing）的人——我坚信他就是今早报上读到的那个巴斯维里——请注意，这个人过去一年上下跟我们时有些生意往来——来找我们，向我们兜售一种新式黑墨水的配方，声称它会把全世界一切已知的墨水统统挤出市场！他把这东西夸上了天；他赌咒发誓，说这是有史以来发明的头一种绝对完美的书写液。他带来了一份自己调配的样品——当着我们的面做了种种试验。可他做得更绝——他还把秘方交给我们使用，让我们在决定是否依从他的提议——也就是由我们付他一笔整付的现金，买断配方——之前，自己动手制作并检验一番。于是我们照配方制出了墨水，也检验了，这一点毫无疑问——它的品质，全然达到、兴许还超出了贝辛，或者说巴斯维里所夸称的程度。多数知名墨水各自的短处，我就不必细数了——这种墨水一样都没有。前几天贝辛再上门来，我们决定把配方买下来。我们讲定了现金价格，前天把款子付讫——在我们伊斯特汉姆的办公室里。”
“是吗？”海瑟维克平静地说。“那么——讲定的价钱是多少？”
两个合伙人交换了一个眼色；在凝神注视着他们的罗娜看来，他们显得比先前更不自在了。但库思韦特还是应声即答。

===Original===
“Ten thousand pounds!”
“How did you pay him?” asked Hetherwick. “In cash?”
“No﻿—by open cheque, at his own request. That, of course, was as good as cash. But,” continued Culthwaite, “as soon as we read the newspapers this morning, we﻿—that is, I, for I read the whole thing on my way to business﻿—went at once to our bank to see if the cheque had been cashed. It had﻿—an hour or two after we’d handed it to Basing. He’d taken the amount in Bank of England notes.”
Hetherwick looked at Matherfield.
“Of course,” he remarked, as if he were asking a question, “that formula belongs to Miss Hannaford? Baseverie had no right to sell it﻿—he stole it?”

===Chinese===
“一万英镑！”
“你们是怎么付他钱的？”海瑟维克问。“付现金？”
“不是——应他本人的要求，开了无记名支票。那自然跟现金一样管用。不过，”库思韦特接着说，“今早一读报纸，我们——准确说是我，因为我在上班路上把整桩事读了个遍——立刻去了我们的银行，查问那张支票兑了没有。兑了——在我们把支票交给贝辛之后一两个钟头。他把款子全数取成了英格兰银行（Bank of England）的钞票。”
海瑟维克望着马瑟菲尔德。
“当然，”他说，那口气像是在发问，“那配方属于汉纳福德小姐，是吧？巴斯维里无权出售——是他偷来的？”

===Original===
“That’s the fact, Mr. Hetherwick,” assented Matherfield. “These gentlemen, innocently enough, bought stolen property. But I’ve just told them something that I’ll now tell you. We found the money﻿—notes﻿—on Baseverie, last night. Intact﻿—in his pocketbook. Of course, with that, and the jewels which his accomplice succeeded in getting at Southampton, he’d got a nice haul. But now we can easily prove how he came by that ten thousand﻿—and it’ll go back to Messrs. Culthwaite and Houseover there. We can prove, too, from their evidence, that Baseverie poisoned Mr. Hannaford for the sake of that formula. Baseverie’s done!”
“These gentlemen will recover their ten thousand pounds, then?” said Hetherwick. “In that case”﻿—he turned to the two partners﻿—“I don’t see that you’ve anything to worry about?” he suggested. “The formula, of course, must be handed over to﻿—”
“Well, now, that’s just it, Mr. Hetherwick,” interrupted the partner who until then had kept silent. “The fact is, sir, we don’t want to lose that formula! We gave this man Baring or Baseverie ten thousand pounds for it, but﻿—”
“But you really believe it to be worth more, eh?” said Hetherwick with a smile. “I see! Then in that case﻿—”
“If we get back our ten thousand, sir, we shall be pleased to treat with the rightful owner,” said Culthwaite, after an exchange of looks with his partner. “In the meantime, the formula is safe and secret with us. We are well-known people﻿—”

===Chinese===
“正是这么回事，海瑟维克先生。”马瑟菲尔德表示同意。“这几位先生毫不知情，买下了赃物。不过我刚对他们说了一件事，现在也说给你们听。昨晚我们在巴斯维里身上找到了那笔钱——钞票。分文未动——就在他的记事本里。当然，再加上他同伙在南安普顿成功弄到手的那批珠宝，他这一票捞得着实不小。可现在我们能轻而易举地证明，那一万英镑是怎么来的——钱会物归原主，还给他们二位库思韦特先生和豪斯奥弗先生。凭他们的证词，我们还能证明，巴斯维里正是为了那份配方才毒害汉纳福德先生的。巴斯维里完了！”
“这么说，这几位先生能把他们那一万英镑收回来了？”海瑟维克说。“既然如此”——他转向那两位合伙人——“依我看，你们没什么可发愁的了？”他提了一句。“那配方，自然得移交——”
“唉，可不是嘛，毛病正出在这儿，海瑟维克先生。”先前一直没作声的那位合伙人插话道。“实情是，先生，我们不想失去那配方！我们为它付给了这个人——贝林（Baring）也好，巴斯维里也好——一万英镑，可是——”
“可你们当真觉得它值更多的钱，嗯？”海瑟维克微笑道。“我明白了！既然那样——”
“只要收回我们那一万英镑，先生，我们乐于同正当的主人洽谈。”库思韦特同合伙人交换了一下眼色，然后说道。“在此期间，配方在我们这里万无一失、严守机密。我们是有名有姓、体体面面的人——”

===Original===
“We’ll leave it at that, just now,” answered Hetherwick. “Miss Hannaford will trust you to keep your word about safety and secrecy. And later﻿—business!” He got up, and Rhona rose with him. “Shall you want us today, Matherfield?” he asked. “If not﻿—”
“No!” replied Matherfield. “Merely formal business today﻿—then, this afternoon, he’ll be brought up. Only evidence of arrest and application for adjournment. You can go away, Mr. Hetherwick﻿—we’ll let you both know when you’re wanted.”
Hetherwick led Rhona out, and once clear of the police precincts, smote his stick on the pavement.
“When we’re through with this business I’m hanged if I ever dabble in crime affairs again, personally!” he exclaimed. “Baseverie has been a pretty vile example to tackle! And that you should be dragged into it, too!” he added, suddenly. “That upset me more than anything. However, it’s getting to an end, and then﻿—”
He paused, while she looked up at him with a little wonder at his vehemence. Then, and as they were at that moment walking along a quiet stretch of the less frequented side of the Embankment, she timidly laid a hand on his arm. He turned sharply, laying his hand on hers.

===Chinese===
“眼下就先这样吧。”海瑟维克答道。“汉纳福德小姐信得过你们，相信你们会言而有信，妥善又保密。至于往后——再谈买卖！”他站起身，罗娜随他一同起来。“今天还需要我们吗，马瑟菲尔德？”他问。“要是不需要——”
“不需要！”马瑟菲尔德答道。“今天全是例行公事——然后，今天下午，他会被提审。只有逮捕经过的证词和延期审理的申请。你们可以走了，海瑟维克先生——需要你们的时候，我们会通知你们二位。”
海瑟维克领着罗娜出来，一离开警局地界，便把手杖往人行道上顿了一下。
“等这桩案子了结，我若再亲手沾刑事案子的边，就把我绞死好了！”他叫道。“巴斯维里这家伙，可真是个够阴狠难缠的对头！而竟然连你也给拖了进来！”他突然又补了一句。“这件事比什么都让我难受。不管怎样，总算快到头了，然后——”
他顿住了；她抬头望着他，对他这份激切略感讶异。这时两人正走在堤岸（Embankment）行人稀少那一侧的一段僻静处，她怯怯地把一只手搭上他的手臂。他霍然转身，把手覆在她的手上。

===Original===
“I think you’ve been very considerate and thoughtful for me,” she said. “After all﻿—it wasn’t quite mere interest in crime that made you﻿—”
“Good Lord, no!” he exclaimed quickly. “At first, perhaps, half that﻿—half you! I felt﻿—somehow﻿—that I’d got to look after you. And then﻿—and when you disappeared﻿—but I believe I’m a bit muddleheaded! I’ll tell you something﻿—all that time you were lost, I﻿—well, I scarcely ever slept! Wondering, you know. And when you turned up yesterday afternoon﻿—but I want to ask you something that I’m not quite clear about﻿—I was certainly muddled just then!”
“What is it?” she asked.
Hetherwick bent down to her and dropped his voice.
“I was so glad, so relieved to see you, yesterday afternoon,” he said, “that﻿—that I felt dazed﻿—eh? And I want to know﻿—did I kiss you?”

===Chinese===
“我想，你对我一直十分体贴周到。”她说。“说到底——当初促使你上心的，并不单纯是对罪案的兴趣——”
“天哪，当然不是！”他急急地叫道。“起初，或许，一半是那个——一半是你！我总觉得——不知怎么——非照应你不可。再后来——等你失踪了——可我想我这会儿有点语无伦次了！跟你说件事——你失踪的那段时间里，我——唉，我几乎就没怎么睡过！你知道，一直在牵挂。而昨天下午你露面的时候——可有件事我想问问你，我自己也不大清楚——我当时准是晕了头！”
“什么事？”她问。
海瑟维克俯下身凑近她，压低了嗓音。
“昨天下午见到你，我实在太高兴、太如释重负了，”他说，“以致——以致我都有点晕晕乎乎——呃？我想知道——我吻了你吗？”

===Original===
Rhona suddenly looked up at him﻿—and laughed.
“Oh, really, how amusing you are!” she said. “Why, of course, you did! Twice!”
“That’s good!” he exclaimed. “I﻿—I thought perhaps I’d dreamt it. But﻿—did you kiss me?”
“Do you really want to be dead sure?” asked Rhona mischievously. “Very well﻿—I did!”
“That’s better!” said Hetherwick.

===Chinese===
罗娜猛地抬头看他——随即笑了。
“噢，真的，你可真逗！”她说。“嗐，当然吻了！还吻了两回呢！”
“那就好！”他叫道。“我——我还当或许是我做梦来着。可是——你吻了我吗？”
“你当真要一个准话？”罗娜调皮地问。“好吧——吻了！”
“那就更好了！”海瑟维克说。

## IV The Diamond Necklace / 四 钻石项链

===Original===
The conviction that there was more than met the eye in Hannaford’s cutting out and putting away the handsome and distinguished woman’s photograph grew mightily in Hetherwick’s mind during the next few days. He recalled all that Hannaford had said about it in the train in those few short minutes before his sudden death. Why had he been so keen about showing it to the other man? Was he taking the other man specially to his hotel to show it to him﻿—at that time of night? Why did the recollections which his possession of it brought up afford him﻿—obviously﻿—so much interest and, it seemed, amusement? And what, exactly, was meant by the pencilled words in the margin of the cutting?﻿—“Through my hands ten years ago!” Under what circumstances had this woman been through Hannaford’s hands? And who was she? The more he thought of it, the more Hetherwick was convinced that there was more importance in this matter than the police attached to it. They had proved utterly indifferent to Hetherwick’s account of the conversation in the train﻿—that, said Matherfield, with official superiority, was nothing but a bit of chat, reminiscence, recollection, on the ex-superintendent’s part; old men, he said, were fond of talking about incidents of the past. The only significance Matherfield saw in it was that it seemed to argue that whoever the man who had disappeared was, he and Hannaford had known each other ten years ago.

At the end of a week the police had heard nothing of this man. Nor had they made any discovery in respect of the other man whom Ledbitter swore he had seen with Hannaford at Victoria. The best Scotland Yard hands had been hard and continuously at work, and had brought nothing to light. Only one person had seen the first man after he darted up the stairs of Charing Cross calling out that he was going for a doctor; this was a policeman on duty at the front of the Underground Station. He had seen the man run out; had watched him run at top speed up Villiers Street, and had thought no more of it than that he was some belated passenger hurrying to catch a last bus in the Strand. But with that, all news and trace of him vanished. Of the tall man in the big blue spectacles and white muffler there never was any trace, nor any news beyond Ledbitter’s. Yet Ledbitter was a thoroughly dependable witness, and there was no doubt that he had seen Hannaford in this man’s company. So, without question, Hannaford, during his last few hours of life, had been with two men﻿—neither of whom could be found. Within twenty-four hours of his death several men came forward voluntarily who had had dealings or conversation with Hannaford since his arrival in London. But there was a significant fact about the news which any of them could give﻿—not one knew anything of the tall man seen by Ledbitter, or of the shabby man seen by Hetherwick, or of the secret which Hannaford carried in his sealed packet. The story of that sealed packet had been told plentifully in the newspapers﻿—but nobody came forward who knew anything about it. And when a week had elapsed after the ex-Superintendent’s burial, the whole mystery of his undoubted murder seemed likely to become one of the many which are never solved.

But Hetherwick was becoming absorbed in this affair into which he had been so curiously thrown headfirst. He had leisure on his hands; also, he was well off in this world’s goods, and much more concerned with the psychology of his profession than with a desire to earn money by its practice. From the moment in which he heard that the doctors had found that Hannaford had been poisoned, he felt that here was a murder mystery at the bottom of which he must get﻿—it fascinated him. And all through his speculations and theorisings about it, he was obsessed by the picture in his pocket. Who was that woman﻿—and what did the dead man remember about her?
===Chinese===
汉纳福德（Hannaford）把那张仪容俊美、气度不凡的妇人相片剪下来又郑重收好，此中必有蹊跷——随后几天里，这个念头在海瑟维克（Hetherwick）心里愈发滋长起来。他把汉纳福德猝死之前那短短几分钟里在火车上就此所说的话，从头到尾回想了一遍。他何以那样急切地要拿给另一个人看？他莫不是正要特意带那个人回他的旅馆去给他看——在那么深更半夜的时辰？他占有这张相片所勾起的种种旧忆，何以给他带来——明摆着的——那么大的兴味，而且看样子还有乐子？剪报边上天头的那行铅笔字，究竟是什么意思？——“十年前曾过我手！”这个女人是在什么情形之下经过汉纳福德的手的？她又是谁？他越想越认定，这件事的干系远比警方所承认的要大。警方对海瑟维克所述火车上的那番对话全然无动于衷——马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）摆出官府的优越腔调说，那不过是那位退职老警司的一点闲谈、追忆、怀旧罢了；他说，上了年纪的人就爱念叨过去的旧事。在马瑟菲尔德看来，此事唯一的意味只是：那个失踪的人不管是谁，总归同汉纳福德早在十年前就相识了。

满了一个礼拜，警方对这个人的下落仍一无所闻。至于另外那个人——莱德比特（Ledbitter）一口咬定在维多利亚（Victoria）车站看见他同汉纳福德在一起——警方也不曾有任何发现。苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）最得力的干探们日以继夜地苦干，却什么也没有查出来。头一个人一边嚷着要去请医生、一边冲上查令十字（Charing Cross）的楼梯之后，只有一个人见过他：那是守在地铁站门前的一名警察。他看见那人奔出来，眼看着他撒开腿飞也似的跑上维利尔斯街（Villiers Street），过后便不再多想，只当他是哪个赶着去斯特兰德街（the Strand）搭末班车的晚归客。可是打那以后，此人的一切音讯和踪迹就此湮灭。至于那个戴蓝色大墨镜、围白围巾的高个子，则从来不曾有过一丝踪影，除了莱德比特的话，再无半点消息。然而莱德比特是个十足可靠的证人，他确曾看见汉纳福德同这个人在一起，这点毫无疑问。于是无可置疑：汉纳福德在世的最后几个钟头里，先后同两个男人在一起——而这两个人，一个也寻不着。他死后二十四小时之内，有几个人自动出面，说是自汉纳福德抵京以后，曾同他有过交易或交谈。可是他们随便哪一个所能提供的消息，都有一样耐人寻味之处——没有一个人知道莱德比特见到的高个子，没有一个人知道海瑟维克见到的寒碜汉子，也没有一个人知道汉纳福德那只密封纸包里携带的秘密。那只密封纸包的故事，报上已经连篇累牍地登过了——可是知情的一个人也没有出面。待到这位老警司下葬后又过了一个礼拜，这桩铁定无疑的谋杀谜案，眼见得就要归入那许多永远破不了的悬案里去了。

而海瑟维克呢，对这个把他如此离奇地一头栽进去的案子，越来越全神贯注了。他手头有的是闲暇；再者，他在这世上的资财也算宽裕，比起靠执业挣钱的心思，他更在意的是本行当里的种种心理问题。打从他听说医生们查出汉纳福德是中了毒的那一刻起，他就觉得摆在自己面前的是一桩非得探到根底的凶杀谜案——它把他迷住了。而在他对案情的种种推测和立论之中，始终萦绕心头、挥之不去的，是他口袋里那张相片。那个女人是谁——死者关于她又记着些什么呢？

===Original===
Suddenly, one morning, after a visit from Matherfield, who looked in at his chambers casually, to tell him that the police had discovered nothing, Hetherwick put on his hat and went round to Surrey Street. He found Rhona Hannaford busy in preparing to leave Malter’s Hotel: she was going to live, for a time at any rate, with Mrs. Keeley. Hetherwick went straight to the matter that had brought him.

“That print of a woman’s photograph which your grandfather had in his pocketbook,” he said, “and that’s now in mine. Out of what paper did he cut it?﻿—a newspaper, evidently.”

“Yes, but I don’t know what paper,” answered Rhona. “All I know is that it was a paper which he got by post, the morning that he left Sellithwaite. We were just leaving for the station when the post came. He put his letters and papers﻿—there were several things﻿—in his overcoat pocket, and opened them in the train. It was somewhere on the way to London that he cut out that picture. He threw the paper away﻿—with others. He had a habit of buying a lot of papers, and used to cut out paragraphs.”

“Well﻿—I suppose it can be traced,” muttered Hetherwick, thinking aloud. He glanced at the evidences of Rhona’s departure. “So you’re going to live with your aunt?” he said.
===Chinese===
一天早晨，马瑟菲尔德顺路到他事务寓所里坐了坐，告诉他说警方毫无发现；他前脚一走，海瑟维克后脚就戴上帽子，转身往萨里街（Surrey Street）去了。他发现罗娜·汉纳福德（Rhona Hannaford）正忙着收拾行装，准备搬出马尔特旅馆（Malter's Hotel）：她要去同基利太太（Mrs. Keeley）住在一处——至少先住上一阵。海瑟维克开门见山，直奔来意。

“你祖父夹在皮夹子里的那张妇人相片剪样，”他说道，“如今到了我手里。他是从哪张报上剪下来的？——明摆着，是份报纸。”

“是报纸，可我不知道是哪一份，”罗娜答道，“我只知道那是他动身离开塞利斯韦特（Sellithwaite）那天早晨从邮局寄到的。邮件送来的时候，我们正要动身去车站。他把信件和报纸——东西有好几样——塞进大衣口袋，到了火车上才拆开来看。那张相片，就是在来伦敦路上的什么地方剪下来的。报纸他随手扔了——连同另外几份一道。他有个习惯，爱买上一大堆报纸，惯常把一段段的文字剪下来。”

“唔——我想总归查得出来，”海瑟维克喃喃自语道。他扫了一眼罗娜动身在即的种种迹象。“这么说，你要去同姑母一块儿住了？”他问。

===Original===
“For a time﻿—yes,” she answered.

“I hope you’ll let me call?” suggested Hetherwick. “I’m awfully interested in this affair, and I may be able to tell you something about it.”

“We’d be pleased,” she replied. “I’ll give you the address. I don’t intend to be idle though﻿—unless you call in the evening, you’ll probably find me out.”

“What are you thinking of doing?” he asked.

“I think of going in for secretarial work,” she answered. “As a matter of fact, I had a training for that, in Sellithwaite. Typewriting, correspondence, accounts, French, German﻿—I’m pretty well equipped.”
===Chinese===
“先住一阵——是的，”她答道。

“我想上门拜访，不知你可容许？”海瑟维克提议道，“这桩案子我关切极了，说不定还能把它的一些消息讲给你听。”

“我们欢迎得很，”她答道，“我把地址写给你。不过我不打算闲着——你要不是傍晚来，多半会扑个空。”

“你打算做些什么呢？”他问。
