“我想投身秘书工作，”她答道，“其实这一行我在塞利斯韦特受过训练。打字、函牍、账目，还有法文、德文——我这身装备倒还算齐全。”

===Original===
“Don’t think me inquisitive,” said Hetherwick, suddenly. “I hope your grandfather hasn’t forgotten you in his will﻿—I heard he’d left one!”

“Thank you,” replied Rhona. “He hasn’t. He left me everything. I’ve got about three hundred a year﻿—rather more. But that’s no reason why I should sit down, and do nothing, is it?”

“Good!” said Hetherwick. “But﻿—if that sealed packet could be found? What was worth a hundred thousand to him, would be worth a hundred thousand to his sole legatee. Worth finding!”

“I wonder if anything will be found?” she answered. “The whole thing’s a mystery that I’m not even on the edge of solving.”

“Time!” said Hetherwick. “And﻿—patience.”
===Chinese===
“别嫌我多问，”海瑟维克忽然说道，“但愿你祖父没在遗嘱里忘掉你——我听说他立了遗嘱来着！”

“谢谢你，”罗娜答道，“他没有忘。他把一切都留给了我。我一年大约有三百镑进款——还不止。可总不能因此就安坐着，什么也不干吧？”

“好！”海瑟维克说，“不过——那只密封的纸包要是寻得回来呢？于他值十万的东西，于他唯一的继承人也就值十万。值得去寻！”

“也不知究竟还能寻出点什么来？”她应道，“整个儿就是一桩谜案，我连解决的边儿都还没沾上呢。”

“要靠时间！”海瑟维克说，“还有——耐性。”

===Original===
He went away presently, and strolled round to Brick Court, where Kenthwaite had his chambers.

“Doing anything?” he asked, as he walked in.

“Nothing,” replied Kenthwaite. “Go ahead!”

Hetherwick sat down, and lighted his pipe.

“You know Sellithwaite, don’t you?” he asked when he had got his tobacco well going. “Your town, eh?”
===Chinese===
不多时他便告辞出来，信步绕到布里克院（Brick Court）——肯思韦特（Kenthwaite）的寓所就在那里。

“忙着吗？”他一边走进去一边问。

“没忙什么，”肯思韦特答道，“尽管说！”

海瑟维克坐下，点着了烟斗。

等烟丝烧得顺畅了，他问道：“塞利斯韦特这地方你知道吧？你家乡的镇子，嗯？”

===Original===
“Born and bred there, and engaged to a girl there,” replied Kenthwaite. “Ought to! What about Sellithwaite?”

“Were you there ten years ago?” demanded Hetherwick.

“Ten years ago? No﻿—except in the holidays. I was at school ten years ago. Why?”

“Do you remember any police case at Sellithwaite about that time in which a very handsome woman was concerned﻿—probably as defendant?”

“No! But I was more interested in cricket than in crime, in those days. Are you thinking about the woman Hannaford spoke of in the train to the chap they can’t come across?”

“I am! Seems to me there’s more in that than the police think.”
===Chinese===
“生在那儿，长在那儿，还跟那儿的一位姑娘订了亲，”肯思韦特答道，“岂止知道！塞利斯韦特怎么了？”

“十年前你在那儿吗？”海瑟维克追问。

“十年前？不在——除了放假的日子。十年前我还在念书呢。问这做什么？”

“你可记得那个年头上，塞利斯韦特有过什么案子，牵涉到一位十分标致的女人的——多半是当被告？”

“不记得！不过那时候我对板球的兴趣比对罪案的兴趣大。你是在想汉纳福德在火车上跟那个怎么也寻不着踪影的家伙提起的那个女人吧？”

“正是！依我看，那里面头的名堂，比警方想的要多。”

===Original===
“Shouldn’t wonder. Let’s see: Hannaford spoke of that woman as﻿—what?”

“Said she’d been through his hands, ten years ago.”

“Well, that’s easy! If she was through Hannaford’s hands, as Superintendent of Police, ten years ago, that would be at Sellithwaite. And there’ll be records, particulars, and so on at Sellithwaite.”

Hetherwick nodded, and smoked in silence for awhile.

“Think I shall go down there,” he said at last.

Kenthwaite stared, wonderingly.
===Chinese===
“那倒不足为怪。我且想想：汉纳福德说那女人——是怎么说来着？”

“说她十年前曾经过他的手。”

“嗐，这就好办了！她要是以警司身份在十年前经过汉纳福德的手，那地方就必定是塞利斯韦特。塞利斯韦特那儿准保有案卷、细目，诸如此类。”

海瑟维克点了点头，闷头抽了一会儿烟。

“我看我要往那儿跑一趟，”他终于说道。

肯思韦特诧异地瞪大了眼。

===Original===
“Keen as all that!” he exclaimed.

“Queer business!” said Hetherwick. “Like to solve it.”

“Oh, well, it’s only a four hours’ run from King’s Cross,” observed Kenthwaite. “Interesting town, too. Old as the hills and modern as they make ’em. Excellent hotel﻿—White Bear. And I’ll tell you what, my future’s brother is a solicitor there﻿—Michael Hollis. I’ll give you a letter of introduction to him, and he’ll show you round and give you any help you need.”

“Good man!” said Hetherwick. “Write it!”

Kenthwaite sat down and wrote, and handed over the result.

“What do you want to find out, exactly?” he asked, as Hetherwick thanked him, and rose to go.
===Chinese===
“兴致这么大！”他失声道。

“蹊跷的案子！”海瑟维克说，“我想解开它。”

“哦，那也不妨事，从国王十字（King's Cross）坐车过去不过四个钟头，”肯思韦特说，“那镇子也有意思。老得同亘古的山丘一样，又时新得无以复加。旅馆好得很——白熊旅馆（White Bear）。还有一桩告诉你，我未来的内弟就在那儿当律师——迈克尔·霍利斯（Michael Hollis）。我给你写封引荐信，他会领你各处转转，你要什么帮助，他都能给你。”

“好伙计！”海瑟维克说，“这就写！”

肯思韦特坐下写了，把写好的信递了过来。

海瑟维克道了谢，起身要走时，他问道：“你到底想查明些什么？”

===Original===
“All about the woman, and why Hannaford cut her picture out of the paper,” answered Hetherwick. “Well﻿—see you when I get back.”

He went off to his own chambers, packed a bag, and drove to King’s Cross to catch the early afternoon train for the North. At half-past seven that evening he found himself in Sellithwaite, a grey, smoke-laden town set in the midst of bleak and rugged hills, where the folk﻿—if the railway officials were anything to go by﻿—spoke a dialect which, to Hetherwick’s southern ears, sounded like some barbaric language. But the White Bear, in which he was presently installed, yielded all the comforts and luxuries of a first-class hotel: the dining-room, into which Hetherwick turned as soon as he had booked his room, seemed to be thronged by a thoroughly cosmopolitan crowd of men; he heard most of the principal European languages being spoken﻿—later, he found that his fellow-guests were principally Continental business men, buyers, intent on replenishing exhausted stocks from the great warehouses and manufactories of Sellithwaite. All this was interesting, nor was he destined to spend the remainder of his evening in contemplating it from a solitary corner, for he had scarcely eaten his dinner when a hall-porter came to tell him that Mr. Hollis was asking for Mr. Hetherwick.

Hetherwick hastened into the lounge, and found a keen-faced, friendly-eyed man of forty or thereabouts stretching out a hand to him.

“Kenthwaite wired me this afternoon that you were coming down, and asked me to look you up here,” he said. “I’d have asked you to dine with me, but I’ve been kept at my office until just now, and again, I live a good many miles out of town. But tomorrow night﻿—”

“You’re awfully good,” replied Hetherwick. “I’d no idea that Kenthwaite was wiring. He gave me a letter of introduction to you, but I suppose he thought I wanted to lose no time. And I don’t, and I dare say you can tell me something about the object of my visit﻿—let’s find a corner and smoke.”

Installed in an alcove in the big smoking-room, Hollis read Kenthwaite’s letter.
===Chinese===
“那女人的一切，还有汉纳福德为什么把她的相片从报上剪下来，”海瑟维克答道，“好——等我回来见。”

他回到自己的寓所，收拾了一只提包，驱车赶到国王十字车站，搭上午后早班开往北方的火车。当天傍晚七点半，他已经身在塞利斯韦特——一座灰色煤烟笼罩的镇子，坐落在一群荒凉嶙峋的山丘中间；那里的人——如果拿铁路员工做个凭据——说的话在海瑟维克这双南方耳朵听来，简直像一种蛮夷语言。可是他随即下榻的白熊旅馆，却有一流旅馆应有尽有的舒适与豪华：他订好房间便径直走进餐厅，那里似乎挤满了彻头彻尾五方杂处的一群男人；欧洲几种主要语言，他大多听见在耳边响着——后来他才知道，同旅馆的客人多半是欧洲大陆来的生意人、采买客商，一心要从塞利斯韦特那些大货栈和大工厂里补足告罄的存货。这一切都挺有意思，而他这个夜晚也不至于独自坐在角落里对着这一切出神，因为晚饭刚吃完，一个门房就来传话，说霍利斯先生求见海瑟维克先生。

海瑟维克急忙走进休息厅，见一个四十岁上下、面目精明、目光和善的男人正向他伸出手来。

“肯思韦特今天下午拍电报给我，说你要来，托我到这儿来寻你，”他说道，“我本想请你来同我共进晚餐，可是在事务所里一直耽到方才才脱身；再说，我住在城外好几英里的地方。不过明天晚上——”

“您太好了，”海瑟维克答道，“我压根儿不知道肯思韦特拍了电报。他给了我引荐信，不过我料想，他是知道我一刻也不肯耽搁的。我也确实不肯，而且我敢说，我这趟来意的底细，您准能告诉我一些——咱们找个角落抽口烟去。”

两人在大吸烟室的一个僻静凹座里坐定，霍利斯看过了肯思韦特的信。

===Original===
“What is it you’re after?” he asked. “Kenthwaite mentions that my knowledge of Sellithwaite is deeper than his own﻿—naturally, it is, as I’m several years older.”

“Well,” responded Hetherwick. “It’s this, briefly. You’re aware, of course, of what befell your late Police-Superintendent in London﻿—his sudden death?”

“Oh, yes﻿—read all the newspapers, anyway,” assented Hollis. “You’re the man who was present in the train on the Underground, aren’t you?”

“I am. And that’s one reason why I’m keen on solving the mystery. There’s no doubt whatever that Hannaford was poisoned﻿—that it’s a case of deliberate murder. Now, there’s a feature of the case to which the police don’t seem to attach any importance. I do attach great importance to it. It’s the matter of the woman to whom Hannaford referred when he was talking﻿—in my presence﻿—to the man who so mysteriously disappeared. Hannaford spoke of that woman as having been through his hands ten years ago. That would be some experience he had here, in this town. Now then, do you know anything about it? Does it arouse any recollection?”

Hollis, who was smoking a cigar, thoughtfully tapped its long ash against the edge of his coffee-cup. Suddenly his eyes brightened.

“That’s probably the Whittingham case,” he said. “It was about ten years ago.”
===Chinese===
“你究竟想查的是什么？”他问道，“肯思韦特信里提到，我对塞利斯韦特的底细比他知道得深——自然，事实如此，我比他年长好几岁呢。”

“是这样，”海瑟维克应道，“简单说吧。你们那位已故的警司在伦敦遭的事——他猝然死去，你当然是知道的？”

“哦，知道——反正报纸全读了，”霍利斯应道，“你就是地铁火车上在场的那个当事人，对吧？”

“正是在下。这也是我一心要解开这谜案的缘故之一。汉纳福德是中了毒，这一点毫无疑问——这是一桩蓄意的谋杀。如今案情里有一个关节，警方似乎丝毫不予重视，我却极为重视。就是汉纳福德同那个神秘得无影无踪的男人谈话时——当着我的面——所提到的那位女人的事。汉纳福德说起那女人，说她十年前曾经过他的手。那就是他在此地、在这个镇上的一段经历。那么，你知道些内情么？这可勾起了什么回忆？”

霍利斯抽着一支雪茄，若有所思地把它长长的烟灰在咖啡杯沿上轻轻磕着。忽然，他的眼睛亮了。

“那多半是惠廷厄姆太太（Mrs. Whittingham）案，”他说道，“大约是十年前的事。”

===Original===
“And what was the Whittingham case?” asked Hetherwick. “Case of a woman?”

“Of a woman﻿—evidently an adventuress﻿—who came to Sellithwaite about ten years ago, and stayed here some little time, in this very hotel,” replied Hollis. “Oddly enough, I never saw her! But she was heard of enough﻿—eventually. She came here, to the White Bear, alone, with plenty of luggage and evident funds. I understand she was a very handsome woman, twenty-eight or thirty years of age, and she was taken for somebody of consequence. I rather think she described herself as the Honourable Mrs. Whittingham. She paid her bills here with unfailing punctuality every Saturday morning. She spent a good deal of money amongst the leading tradesmen in the town, and always paid cash. In short, she established her credit very successfully. And with nobody more so than the principal jeweller here﻿—Malladale. She bought a lot of jewellery from Malladale﻿—but in his case, she always paid by cheque. And in the end it was through a deal with Malladale that she got into trouble.”

“And into Hannaford’s hands!” suggested Hetherwick.

“Into Hannaford’s hands, certainly,” assented Hollis. “It was this way. She had, as I said just now, made a lot of purchases from Malladale, who, I may tell you, has a first-class trade amongst our rich commercial magnates in this neighbourhood. Her transactions with him, however, were never, at first, in amounts exceeding a hundred or two. But they went through all right. She used to pay him by cheque drawn on a Manchester bank﻿—Manchester, you know, is only thirty-five miles away. As her first cheques were always met, Malladale never bothered about making any inquiry about her financial stability; like everybody else he was very much impressed by her. Well, in the end, she’d a big deal with Malladale, Malladale had a very fine diamond necklace in stock. He and she used to discuss her acquisition of it: according to his story they had a fine old battle as to terms. Eventually, they struck a bargain﻿—he let her have it for three thousand nine hundred pounds. She gave him a cheque for that amount there and then, and he let her carry off the necklace.”

“Oh!” exclaimed Hetherwick.
===Chinese===
“惠廷厄姆案是怎么一回事？”海瑟维克问，“一个女人的案子？”

“一个女人的案子——明摆着是个女冒险家——大约十年前来到塞利斯韦特，在这儿盘桓了一小段时日，住的正是这家旅馆，”霍利斯答道，“说来也怪，我竟从来没有见过她！可她后来名声大得很——到头来。她只身来到这儿，住进白熊旅馆，行李累累，资财显然充裕。据我所知，她是个非常标致的女人，二十八到三十岁的年纪，人家都把她当成一位有身份的人物。我料想她自称是尊敬的惠廷厄姆太太。她在这里的账目，每逢礼拜六上午必定分毫不爽地结清。她在城里几家头等商号里花去大笔的钱，回回都付现款。总而言之，她的信用建立得非常成功。而在谁身上都不如在本地头号珠宝商马拉代尔（Malladale）身上建立得成功。她从马拉代尔手里买了许多珠宝——不过同他的交易，她一概用支票付款。到头来，也正是同马拉代尔的一笔买卖，叫她栽了跟头。”

“也落进了汉纳福德的手里！”海瑟维克接口道。

“落进汉纳福德的手里，那是千真万确，”霍利斯应道，“事情是这样的。方才说过，她从马拉代尔手里买过许多东西——不妨告诉你，马拉代尔同我们这一带有钱的商界巨头们做的是头一流的生意。不过她同他的交易，起初数目从没有超过一两百镑。可回回都兑现无误。她惯常付他支票，从曼彻斯特（Manchester）一家银行支取——你要知道，曼彻斯特离这儿不过三十五英里。她起初开的支票张张照付，马拉代尔便懒得去打听她资力的虚实；同所有人一样，他对她印象极深。唔，到末了，她同马拉代尔做了一笔大买卖：马拉代尔手里存着一条极精美的钻石项链。他同她屡屡商量她把它买下来的事；照他自己的说法，两个人在价钱上着实鏖战了一场。末了，他们讲定了——他以三千九百镑的价钱把项链让给了她。她当场开出同额的支票给他，他便任她把项链提了去。”

“哦！”海瑟维克失声道。

===Original===
“Just so!” agreed Hollis. “But﻿—he did. However, for some reason or other, Malladale had that cheque specially cleared. She handed it to him on a Monday afternoon; first thing on Wednesday morning Malladale found that it had been returned with the ominous reference to drawer inscribed on its surface! Naturally, he hurried round to the White Bear. But the Honourable Mrs. Whittingham had disappeared. She had paid up her account, taken her belongings, and left the hotel, and the town, late on the Monday evening, and all that could be discovered at the station was that she had travelled by the last train to Leeds, where, of course, there are several big main lines to all parts of England. And she had left no address: she had, indeed, told the people here that she should be back before long, and that if any letters came they were to keep them until her return. So then Malladale went to the police, and Hannaford got busy.”

“I gather that he traced her?” suggested Hetherwick.

Hollis laughed sardonically.

“Hannaford traced her﻿—and he got her,” he answered. “But he might well use the expression that you mentioned just now. She was indeed through his hands﻿—just as a particularly slippery eel might have been﻿—she got clear away from him.”
===Chinese===
“正是如此！”霍利斯附和道，“可是——他确实让她提走了。不过不知怎么的，马拉代尔把那张支票专门送交兑取了一道。礼拜一下午她把支票交到他手里；礼拜三一早头一件事，马拉代尔发现支票退了回来，票面上赫然批着‘请与出票人接洽’那不祥的字样！他自然急急赶到白熊旅馆。可是尊敬的惠廷厄姆太太已经无影无踪。她在礼拜一深夜付清了账目，收拾了行李，离开了旅馆和镇子；车站上能打听出来的全部消息，不过是她搭了开往利兹（Leeds）的末班车——利兹那地方，不用说，有好几条通往英格兰各地的大干线。她也没有留下地址：她确实同这儿的人说过，她不久就回来，倘若有信寄到，就替她收着，等她回来再取。于是马拉代尔报了警，汉纳福德便着手办起来了。”

“我料想，他把她的踪迹追出来了？”海瑟维克提示道。

霍利斯讥诮地笑了。

“汉纳福德追出了她的踪迹——也把她拿到了手，”他答道，“不过你方才提到的那句说法，他正好用得上。她的确曾经过他的手——正像一条格外滑溜的鳗鱼可能经过的那样——她从他手里脱身，跑得干干净净。”

## XXII The Highly-Respectable Solicitor / 二十二 道貌岸然的律师

===Original===
Lord Morradale, who kept up honest, country-squire habits even in London, had gone to bed when Hetherwick and Mapperley arrived at his house, but he lost little time in making an appearance, in pyjamas and dressing-gown, and listened eagerly to Hetherwick’s account of the recent transactions.
“Force!” he muttered, nodding his head at each point of the story. “Force! got it out of her by force. That is, if the order’s genuine.”
Mapperley produced the sheet of paper, which he had filched under the caretaker’s eyes, and silently handed it over.
“Oh, that’s Madame Listorelle’s handwriting!” exclaimed Lord Morradale. “Hers, without doubt. Difficult to imitate, of course. Oh, yes﻿—hers! Well, that proves what I’ve just said, Mr. Hetherwick﻿—force! She’s in their power﻿—with the young lady, Miss﻿—Miss﻿—Featherstone, to be sure﻿—and they’ve made her write that. Next, they’ll make her write an order on the Imperial Safe Deposit. We must be beforehand with them there. Early﻿—early as possible in the morning. Meet me at Matherfield’s﻿—I think he’s pretty keen. Bless me! what a pack of villains! Now I wonder where, in all London, these unfortunate ladies are?”
“That’s precisely what all this ought to help us to find out,” remarked Hetherwick. “I’m not so much concerned about the valuables these men are after as about the safety of﻿—”

===Chinese===
莫罗代尔勋爵（Lord Morradale）即便身在伦敦，也依然保持着乡下士绅那种规规矩矩的做派——海瑟维克（Hetherwick）和马珀利（Mapperley）赶到他府上时，他早已就寝；但他不多时便穿着睡衣、罩着晨袍现身，热切地听海瑟维克讲说新近的这几桩事。
“强迫！”他喃喃道，故事讲到每一处，他便点一下头。“强迫！是逼她写出来的。当然，前提是那纸凭单是真的。”
马珀利掏出那页纸——他方才是在看门人的眼皮底下把它顺了来的——一声不响地递了过去。
“哦，这是里斯托雷尔太太（Madame Listorelle）的笔迹！”莫罗代尔勋爵失声道，“是她写的，错不了。当然，这字要模仿可不容易。哦，没错——是她的！好，这正印证了我方才的话，海瑟维克先生——强迫！她落在他们手里了——同那位年轻小姐，费——费——费瑟斯通小姐（Miss Featherstone）在一起，没错——他们逼她写下了这个。下一步，他们就会逼她写一张向帝国保险保管库（Imperial Safe Deposit）取件的凭单。在那上头，咱们得抢在他们前头。赶早——明儿一早，越早越好。到马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）那儿同我会合——我看他劲头十足。老天爷！好一伙恶棍！可我倒纳闷，偌大一个伦敦，这两位落难的女士究竟在哪儿呢？”
“这恰恰正是眼下的线索该帮咱们查明的事。”海瑟维克说，“这伙人图谋的珍宝财物，我倒不怎么挂心；我挂心的是——”

===Original===
Lord Morradale gave him a quick, understanding glance.
“Of Miss Featherstone, eh?” he said. “I see﻿—I see! And I’m concerned, too, about Madame Listorelle. Well, this, as you say, ought to help. But look here﻿—we must be cautious﻿—very cautious! We mustn’t let Matherfield﻿—you know what the police are﻿—we mustn’t let him be too precipitate. Probably﻿—if a man comes to the safe place, he’ll go away from it to where these scoundrels are. We must follow﻿—follow!”
“I agree,” said Hetherwick.
“Nine o’clock, then, at Matherfield’s,” concluded his lordship. “And may we have a strong scent, a rousing one, and a successful kill!”

===Chinese===
莫罗代尔勋爵迅疾而会意地瞥了他一眼。
“挂心的是费瑟斯通小姐，嗯？”他说，“我明白——我明白！里斯托雷尔太太那边，我也挂心着呢。好，正如你说的，这东西该能帮上忙。可是听着——咱们务必谨慎——十二分的谨慎！咱们可不能让马瑟菲尔德——你晓得警察那班人是什么路数——不能让他操之过急。多半是这样——倘若有人到保管库那儿去取东西，他取完总得离开，回到这伙恶棍所在的地方去。咱们就得跟踪——跟踪！”
“我同意。”海瑟维克说。
“那么，九点钟，马瑟菲尔德那儿见。”勋爵大人一锤定音，“但愿咱们这回嗅迹浓烈、追猎起劲、一举得手！”

===Original===
With this bit of sporting phraseology in their ears, Hetherwick and Mapperley returned to the Middle Temple and retired for the rest of the night, one to bed, the other to a shakedown on the sitting-room sofa. But when Hetherwick’s alarm clock awoke him at seven-thirty and he put his head into the next room to rouse the clerk, he found that Mapperley had vanished. The cushions, rugs, and blankets with which he had made himself comfortable for the night were all neatly folded and arranged﻿—on the topmost was pinned a sheet of brief-paper, with a message scrawled in blue pencil.

> You won’t want me this morning; off on an important notion of my own. Look out for message from me about noon.
> M.

===Chinese===
耳畔带着这句打猎的行话，海瑟维克和马珀利回到中殿律师学院（Middle Temple）去消磨下半夜，一个上床安睡，一个在起居室的沙发上打了个地铺。可七点半海瑟维克被闹钟叫醒，探头到隔壁房间去唤那位办事员时，却发现马珀利已经无影无踪。他夜里铺盖取暖用的坐垫、小毯子和毯子，全都叠得整整齐齐、码放停当——最上面别着一页公文笺，用蓝铅笔潦草地涂了几句话。

> 今晨用不上我；我自有个要紧的主意，出门去了。约莫正午，留意我捎来的消息。
> 马。

===Original===
Muttering to himself that he hadn’t the least idea as to what his clerk was about, Hetherwick made a hurried toilet, and an equally hurried breakfast, and hastened away to meet Matherfield and Lord Morradale. He found these two together, and with them a quiet, solemn-faced individual, clad in unusually sombre garments, whom Matherfield introduced as Detective-Sergeant Quigman. Matherfield went straight to business.
“His lordship’s just told me of your adventure last night, Mr. Hetherwick,” he said, “and I’m beginning to get a sort of forecast of what’s likely to happen. It was, of course, Baseverie who went to madame’s flat last night﻿—that’s settled. But what do you suppose he went for?”
“Can’t say that I’ve worked that out,” answered Hetherwick, with a glance at the others. “But I imagine that he went there to get, say, certain keys﻿—having forced Madame Listorelle to tell him where they were. The keys of her safe at the Deposit place, I should think.”
“No!” replied Matherfield, shaking his head knowingly, and with a sly smile at Quigman. “No, not that. I’ll tell you what he went for﻿—a very simple thing. He went to get some of Madame’s private notepaper! He knew well enough that if he was to take an order on that Safe Deposit to allow the bearer access to Madame’s safe it would have to be what the French, I believe, call *en régle*﻿—eh? Written on her own notepaper in her own handwriting, and so on. See?”
“I think you’re right, and I think he got it,” said Hetherwick. “A drawer in her desk containing boxes of stationery had been pulled out, and some of its contents lay about the desk. As a matter of fact, though I scarcely know why I did it, I put some paper and some envelopes in my pocket﻿—here they are! I had a faint idea that they might be useful﻿—somehow.”

===Chinese===
海瑟维克一面暗自嘀咕，实在摸不透他的办事员要搞什么名堂，一面匆匆梳洗，又同样匆匆地用了早餐，便赶去同马瑟菲尔德和莫罗代尔勋爵会合。他见这两位已经聚在一处，同他们在一起的还有一个安静而面孔严肃的人物，穿一身异常深暗的衣服，马瑟菲尔德介绍说是侦探警佐奎格曼（Detective-Sergeant Quigman）。马瑟菲尔德开门见山，直入正题。
“勋爵大人方才把你们昨晚的遭遇讲给我听了，海瑟维克先生，”他说，“我心里渐渐有了个大致的预感，知道往后可能要出什么事。昨儿夜里去太太公寓的，自然是巴斯维里（Baseverie）——这已是定论。可你猜，他是去干什么的？”
“这个我还没琢磨出来。”海瑟维克答道，朝旁人瞥了一眼，“不过我想，他是去取——比方说——某几把钥匙，逼着里斯托雷尔太太说出钥匙放在哪儿。依我看，是她那口存放在保管库的保险柜的钥匙。”
“不对！”马瑟菲尔德答道，会意地摇着头，朝奎格曼狡黠地一笑，“不对，不是那个。我告诉你他去取什么——一件顶简单的东西。他去取那位太太的私人信纸！他心里透亮得很：要凭一纸凭单，让保管库准许持单人去动太太的保险柜，这凭单就得办得——我想法国人的说法是——*en régle*（规矩齐全），对不对？要用她自己的信纸、她自己的笔迹写成，如此等等。明白吗？”
“我想你说得对，而且我想他已经到手了。”海瑟维克说，“她书桌里有个装着成盒文具的抽屉，被人拉了出来，有些东西摊在书桌上。说实在的，虽则我自己也说不清当时为什么那么做，我往口袋里装了几张信纸、几只信封——喏，就在这儿！我当时隐隐觉得，这些东西兴许能派上什么用场。”

===Original===
“Well, that’s the notion, depend on it,” asserted Matherfield, glancing at the paper which Hetherwick produced. “I’ve no doubt that somebody, representing Madame Listorelle, and bearing an authorization from her, written on her notepaper in her own writing, will present himself at the Imperial Safe Deposit this morning. But﻿—it won’t be Baseverie! And it won’t be Ambrose!”
“A stranger, eh?” suggested Hetherwick.
“We shall see. Now,” continued Matherfield, glancing at the clock, “we’ll be off to the scene of operations. This Imperial Safe Deposit is in Kingsway﻿—Holborn end﻿—and very fortunately situated for our job, being close to the Tube station; there’ll be lots of people about there, and we shan’t attract attention. And this is the way of it﻿—his lordship and myself will go into the Safe Deposit, see the people in charge, explain matters, and get them to tell us at once if and when the expected ambassador arrives. We shall let him﻿—”
“Or her,” interrupted Quigman solemnly.
“Just so, my lad﻿—it might be a she,” assented Matherfield. “Quite likely! We shall let him or her get what is wanted from the safe and go away, closely followed by all four of us. While Lord Morradale and I are inside, you and Quigman, Mr. Hetherwick, will be outside, talking, casually. When we come out﻿—and you’ll both keep a sharp watch on the entrance hall﻿—I’ll give you the office as to the particular person we’re following, and wherever that person goes, you two will go. But don’t come near us﻿—we’ll keep one side of the street, you the other. If the person takes to a cab or a bus﻿—well, we’ll have to do the same. But I’ve reasons for thinking he or she will stick to his feet!”

===Chinese===
“嗯，就是这么回事，错不了。”马瑟菲尔德断言道，一面看着海瑟维克掏出的那几张纸，“我毫不怀疑，今天上午准会有人以里斯托雷尔太太的代表身份露面，手持她的授权书——写在她自己的信纸上、出诸她本人的笔迹——到帝国保险保管库去。可是——那人不会是巴斯维里！也不会是安布罗斯（Ambrose）！”
“是个生人，嗯？”海瑟维克提示道。
“到时候便见分晓。喏，”马瑟菲尔德接着说，瞥了一眼时钟，“咱们这就动身，开赴阵地。这座帝国保险保管库在金斯威（Kingsway）——靠霍尔本（Holborn）那一头——位置对咱们这桩差事来说再凑巧不过，紧挨着地铁车站；那一带人来人往，咱们不致惹眼。事情这么办——勋爵大人和我进保管库，见一见负责的人，把原委讲明，请他们一俟那位翘盼之中的‘使者’到来，即刻知会我们。咱们就放他——”
“或者是她。”奎格曼一本正经地插话道。
“正是，老弟——没准儿是个‘她’。”马瑟菲尔德附和道，“很有可能！咱们就放他或者她把要的东西从保险柜里取出、走人，咱们四个紧紧跟上。莫罗代尔勋爵和我在里面的时候，海瑟维克先生，您和奎格曼就在外头，随随便便地聊天。等我们出来——你们两位要把门厅盯紧——我会把暗号递给你们，指明咱们跟的是哪一个；那人走到哪儿，你们俩就跟到哪儿。可是别凑近我们——咱们占街的一边，你们占另一边。那人要是雇车或者乘公共汽车——唔，咱们也只好照办。不过我有理由认为，他或者她，多半还是靠一双脚板！”

===Original===
“How do we go?﻿—all together?” asked Hetherwick. “Because﻿—it’s a mere idea﻿—how do you know, Matherfield, that these people﻿—there would appear to be more than one concerned﻿—aren’t keeping an eye on you?”
“I’ve thought of that,” answered Matherfield. “No﻿—we’re all going separately. It’s now nine-fifteen. That Imperial Safe Deposit doesn’t open its doors till ten﻿—nobody can get in there until that time, anyway. We all four go out of this office on our own hook. Each takes his own method of getting to the top of Kingsway. As soon as I get there, I go straight in and ask for the manager. As soon as Lord Morradale gets there, he follows suit﻿—he and I forgather in the manager’s room. As for you two, go how you like﻿—fly, if it suits you﻿—or wander round the side streets. But﻿—you meet right opposite the Safe Deposit entrance at precisely ten o’clock, and under pretence of casual meeting and conversation keep your eyes on it, noticing everybody who goes in and comes out. That clear? Then we all clear out﻿—one by one.”
Outside, and left to his own devices, Hetherwick walked a little way and then hailed a taxicab. He gave his driver a confidential smile.
“You can just help me to employ forty minutes,” he said, as he got in. “Drive round﻿—anywhere you like﻿—up and down﻿—as long as you put me down at the corner of the Holborn Restaurant at precisely two minutes to ten. Got that?”

===Chinese===
“咱们怎么个去法？——一块儿走吗？”海瑟维克问，“因为——不过是随口一个念头——马瑟菲尔德，你怎么知道这伙人——看样子插手的不止一个——没在暗中盯你的梢？”
“这层我想到了。”马瑟菲尔德答道，“不——咱们分头去。现在九点一刻。帝国保险保管库十点才开门——无论如何，十点之前谁也进不去。咱们四个各自离开这间办公室，各走各的。各人用各人的法子赶到金斯威北头。我一到，就径直进去求见经理；莫罗代尔勋爵一到，也照此办理——他跟我在经理室里碰头。至于你们二位，爱怎么走就怎么走——对胃口的话，飞过去都行——或者在横街窄巷里绕一绕。但是——十点整，你们务必在保管库大门正对面碰头，装作偶然相逢、随口闲谈，眼睛却盯住大门，进出的人一个不落全看在眼里。清楚了吗？那就散伙——一个一个地走。”
出了门，一切由自己拿主意，海瑟维克走了一小段路，便招呼了一辆出租汽车。他冲司机送过去一个心照不宣的微笑。
“你正好帮我消磨四十分钟。”他一边上车一边说，“兜着圈子开——去哪儿随你的便——来回转悠——只要十点差两分整，把我放在霍尔本餐馆（Holborn Restaurant）门口的拐角上。记住了吗？”

===Original===
The driver comprehended, and began a leisurely journey round certain principal streets and thoroughfares. Two minutes before ten he pulled up at the Holborn-Kingsway corner and gave his fare a grin.
“Done it to the second, sir,” he announced, nodding at an adjacent clock.
“Good man!” said Hetherwick, handing out something over the registered fare. Then an idea struck him. “Look here!” he continued confidentially. “I﻿—and another man﻿—may have to follow somebody from here, presently. Just drive down the street here, keep your flag down, and wait﻿—if I want you, I shall be close at hand.”
The driver showed his understanding by a nod and a wink and moved a little distance off to the kerbstone. Hetherwick walked slowly down the west side of Kingsway. And precisely as the clock struck ten he saw Lord Morradale come from one direction and enter the formidable-looking and just opened door of the Safe Deposit, and Matherfield appear from the other: looking round again he was aware of the solemn-faced Quigman who sauntered round the corner of Parker Street and came towards him. Hetherwick went on to meet him.

===Chinese===
司机心领神会，慢悠悠地绕着几条主要街道和通衢大道转起圈来。十点差两分，他在霍尔本—金斯威拐角把车停住，冲乘客咧嘴一笑。
“掐得一分不差，先生。”他宣告道，朝近旁一座时钟点了点头。
“好样的！”海瑟维克说着，递出的车钱比计价器上的数目多出了一截。这时他忽然心生一计。“听着！”他推心置腹地接着说，“我——还有一个人——待会儿说不定得从这儿跟踪什么人。你把车顺着这条街往前开，旗子压着别竖，等着——我要用你的时候，人就在近旁。”
司机点头又挤眼，表示明白，把车挪开一段，靠到路缘石旁。海瑟维克沿着金斯威西侧缓缓走去。时钟正敲十点，他看见莫罗代尔勋爵从一头走来，进了保管库那扇气象森严、刚刚开启的大门，马瑟菲尔德则从另一头现身；再一环顾，又见那副严肃面孔的奎格曼从帕克街（Parker Street）拐角悠然转出，朝他走来。海瑟维克迎上前去。

===Original===
“There you are!” he said, doing a little acting in case any inimical eyes were on him. “To the minute! We’d better appear to be doing a bit of talk, eh? The others have just gone in.”
“I saw ’em, sir,” replied Quigman, coming to a halt on the kerb, and affecting an interest in anything rather than on what he was really working. “Ah! But the question is﻿—when will they come out? Might be in a few minutes﻿—so to speak. Mightn’t be for hours﻿—as it were!”
“You seem to be a melancholy chap,” observed Hetherwick.
“Melancholy job!” muttered Quigman. “Watching isn’t my line. But Matherfield﻿—he particularly wanted me to be in at this.”
“Why?” asked Hetherwick.
“Peculiar knowledge of solicitors and their clerks in this part o’ London,” replied Quigman. “My line. Matherfield, he’s an idea that the order to open this safe’ll be presented by a solicitor.”

===Chinese===
“你可来了！”他说，为了提防有敌意的眼睛望着，还特意做了几分戏，“准到一分钟不差！咱们顶好装出闲谈几句的样子，嗯？那几位刚进去。”
“我瞧见他们了，先生。”奎格曼答道，在路缘上站定，装出一副对什么都关心、唯独不关心他真正差事的样子，“啊哈！可问题来了——他们几时才出来呢？兴许几分钟里头就出来——可以这么讲。也兴许几个钟头都出不来——好比这么讲！”
“你瞧着是个愁眉苦脸的家伙。”海瑟维克说。
“愁人的差事嘛！”奎格曼咕哝道，“守望盯梢不是我的本行。可马瑟菲尔德——他特意点了我来经手这一桩。”
“为什么点你？”海瑟维克问。
“对伦敦这一带的律师跟律师的办事员，我肚里有一本特别的账。”奎格曼答道，“这正是我的本行。马瑟菲尔德他有个念头，认定来递这开柜凭单的，会是位律师。”

===Original===
“Good Lord!﻿—has he?” exclaimed Hetherwick. “I wonder! But﻿—”
“Big help to these chaps, don’t you see, if they can make a solicitor do the cat’s-paw work,” suggested Quigman. “Who’d suspect a solicitor of the High Court? And as I know pretty nearly all of ’em﻿—there’s one I know now coming up t’other side of the street,” he continued suddenly. “That tallish, thin, pale-faced chap﻿—see him? Look at him without seeming to look. Now I wonder if he’s the party we want?”
Hetherwick looked in the direction indicated. He saw a youngish, spectacled man in a silk hat, morning coat, and the corresponding additions of professional attire, who was walking rapidly along from south to north. He was a very mild, gentle-looking person, not at all the sort to be concerned in dark plots and mysterious aims, and Hetherwick said so.
“Aye, well, you never know!” remarked Quigman lugubriously. “But, as I say, I know him. Mr. Garrowell﻿—Mr. Octavius Garrowell﻿—solicitor, of St. Martin’s Lane, that is. Been in practice for himself about four years or so. Nice young feller!﻿—quiet. And he is going in there﻿—see?”

===Chinese===
“老天爷！——是吗？”海瑟维克失声道，“难说！可是——”
“这帮家伙要是能拉个律师当猫爪子使，帮衬可就大了，您瞧。”奎格曼点拨道，“谁会疑心高等法院的律师呢？再说，这路人我差不多个个认识——喏，正有一个我认识的打街对面上来了，”他忽然接着说，“那个高挑个儿、瘦瘦的、脸色苍白的家伙——瞧见没有？别正着眼瞧，拿眼角瞄着他。唔，我倒纳闷，他会不会正是咱们要等的那位？”
海瑟维克朝指点的方向望去。他看见一个年纪轻些、戴眼镜的男人，头戴大礼帽，身穿常礼服，配着职业装束应有的一应零星，正由南往北疾步走来。那是个眉目温婉、模样和善的人，全然不像同阴暗密谋、神秘行当有牵连的角色，海瑟维克便这么说了。
“嗳，唉，这话可说不准！”奎格曼凄凄惨惨地说，“不过，像我方才讲的，这人我认识。加罗韦尔先生（Mr. Garrowell）——奥克塔维厄斯·加罗韦尔先生（Mr. Octavius Garrowell）——圣马丁巷（St. Martin’s Lane）的律师，就是他。自己开业有四年上下了。好一个和气的后生！——安安静静的。他这不是正往里去——瞧见没有？”

===Original===
Hetherwick saw. There were several people, men and women, entering the Safe Deposit just then, but Mr. Garrowell’s silk hat and sloping shoulders made him easily identifiable.
“I dessay it’s him!” observed Quigman, with a sigh. “Just the sort to be took in, he is! Innocent, unsuspecting sort o’ gentleman. However﻿—it mayn’t be. Deal o’ people use these Safe Deposits nowadays.”
Mr. Garrowell disappeared. The two watchers waited. Five, ten, fifteen, twenty minutes went by; then Mr. Garrowell came out. He came out just as any man would come out after transacting his business, quietly. Nobody followed him: nobody seemed to be watching him﻿—from the Safe Deposit. But Hetherwick noticed at once that whereas he had entered carrying nothing but an umbrella, he now carried a small, square, leather-covered box. With this in his left hand he crossed the roadway, and advanced straight towards Hetherwick and Quigman.
“No need to move, sir,” whispered the detective. “Take no notice﻿—spot him, though.”

===Chinese===
海瑟维克瞧见了。当时正有好几个人，男女都有，往保管库里走，可加罗韦尔先生的大礼帽和溜肩膀，教人一眼就能把他认出来。
“我看八成就是他！”奎格曼叹了口气说，“天生就是让人家坑的那种人！天性老实、不疑心人的那路绅士。不过——也未必。如今使这种保管库的人多着呢。”
加罗韦尔先生进去了。两个守望的人等着。五分、十分、十五分、二十分钟过去了；随后加罗韦尔先生出来了。他出来时同任何人办完了事走出来一样，安安静静。没有人跟着他：看样子也没有人——从保管库那边——在望他。可海瑟维克立刻注意到，他进去时除了一把伞什么都没带，出来时却提着一只四四方方的小皮箱。他把箱子提在左手，穿过车马道，径直朝海瑟维克和奎格曼走来。
“不必挪窝，先生。”侦探低声道，“只当没瞧见——可要把他认准了。”

===Original===
Mr. Garrowell, seen at close quarters, looked to be a somewhat absentminded gentleman. But, chancing to look up as he stepped on the pavement, his eyes encountered Quigman, who touched his hat.
“Morning, Mr. Garrowell,” said the detective. “Nice morning, sir.”
“Morning, Quigman,” responded Mr. Garrowell. “A very nice morning!”
He nodded smilingly and went on his way, and round the corner into Parker Street. Quigman glanced at Hetherwick and shook his head.
“Not him!” he said. “Matherfield’s not following. And, as I said, we may have to wait﻿—hours!”

===Chinese===
隔近了看，加罗韦尔先生像是个有些心不在焉的绅士。可他跨上人行道时偶然抬头，目光正撞上奎格曼，奎格曼碰了碰帽檐。
“早安，加罗韦尔先生。”侦探说，“今儿天气真好，先生。”
“早安，奎格曼。”加罗韦尔先生应道，“的确是个好早晨！”
他笑吟吟地点了点头，自顾自去了，转过拐角进了帕克街。奎格曼瞥了海瑟维克一眼，摇了摇头。
“不是他！”他说，“马瑟菲尔德没有跟出来。再说，像我讲的，咱们兴许得等——等上几个钟头！”

===Original===
But at the end of another ten minutes Matherfield and Lord Morradale came together out of the entrance hall opposite. An official, smiling and talking, accompanied them to the threshold; when they left him they came straight across the road. And it was obvious to Hetherwick that each was in a state of surprise﻿—possibly, of perplexity. Matherfield hailed them as soon as he was within speaking distance.
“Here’s a queer business!” he said. “Did you see a professional-looking chap come away just now who carried a small leather box?”
“We saw Mr. Garrowell, solicitor, St. Martin’s Lane,” answered Quigman. “I know him. Gone down Parker Street.”
“It was Garrowell,” assented Matherfield. “I know him, too. Well,” he turned to Hetherwick, “it’s a queer business. They knew Garrowell across there﻿—he’s been to Madame Listorelle’s safe for her before. He came there just now, with the usual authorisation, on her notepaper, went to the safe, got that small box, and went. Garrowell﻿—a highly﻿—respectable legal practitioner!”
“Why didn’t you stop him and ask him questions?” inquired Hetherwick.

===Chinese===
可又过了十分钟，马瑟菲尔德和莫罗代尔勋爵一同从对面的门厅里出来了。一名馆方职员笑吟吟地陪着说话，送他们到门口；两人辞了他，径直穿过马路过来。海瑟维克一看便知，两人都面带讶异——说不定还是困惑。马瑟菲尔德一进说话听得见的距离，便招呼开了。
“这儿有桩蹊跷事！”他说，“方才你们见没见一个职业模样的人走出来，手里提着一只小皮箱？”
“我们见着加罗韦尔先生了，圣马丁巷的律师。”奎格曼答道，“我认识他。下帕克街去了。”
“正是加罗韦尔。”马瑟菲尔德附和道，“我也认识他。嗯，”他转向海瑟维克，“这事透着蹊跷。那边的人认识加罗韦尔——他从前替里斯托雷尔太太到她那口保险柜取过东西。方才他到那儿，带着惯常的授权书，用的她的信纸，走到保险柜跟前，取了那只小箱子，就走人了。加罗韦尔——一位多么——受人尊敬的法律执业师！”
“那你们怎么不拦住他问个明白？”海瑟维克问道。

===Original===
Matherfield exchanged a glance with Lord Morradale.
“Not there!” he said. “It﻿—well, it looks as if Madame really had sent him! Her business.”
“Of course she’d sent him!” exclaimed Hetherwick. “Sent him under compulsion! The whole thing’s a clever plant! These fellows probably know that she’s employed Garrowell now and then, and they forced her to write a letter to him, authorising him to come here again, and enclosing an order on the Safe Deposit people! Don’t you see?”
“By Gad, there’s something in that, Matherfield!” said Lord Morradale. “Didn’t strike me, though! ’Pon my honour, I really thought he had come direct from her. Couldn’t think why, exactly, but then, as Matherfield says, a highly-respectable solicitor﻿—eh?”

===Chinese===
马瑟菲尔德同莫罗代尔勋爵交换了一个眼色。
“那儿不成！”他说，“这——唉，看上去倒真像是太太本人差他来的！她自己的正事。”
“当然是她差他来的！”海瑟维克嚷道，“是被迫差他来的！整件事就是一个巧妙的圈套！这伙家伙多半知道她时不时会用加罗韦尔，便逼她给他写了封信，授权他再到这儿来一趟，随信还附上一张给保管库方面的凭单！你们还不明白吗？”
“天哪，这话有点道理，马瑟菲尔德！”莫罗代尔勋爵说，“我可没想到！凭良心讲，我还真当他直接从她那儿来呢。也说不清为什么会这么想，不过嘛，正如马瑟菲尔德说的，一位极其——受人尊敬的律师——嗯？”

===Original===
“We’ll soon settle it!” exclaimed Matherfield suddenly. “We’ll go to Garrowell’s office. Better discuss it there than have tackled him here. Anyway, he’ll have the square box. Quigman, call a taxi!”
“There’s a man here waiting for me,” said Hetherwick. He signalled to his former driver who quickly came alongside. “For anything we know,” he continued, as all four took their seats, and were driven off, “Garrowell may have gone straight away somewhere to hand that box over! We ought to have followed.”
“I don’t think so,” replied Matherfield. “The whole thing’s queer, and not at all what I expected. Lord Morradale says that he never heard of madame employing Garrowell, and yet the Safe people say he’s been here two or three times on her business. But we’ll soon have it out of him.”

===Chinese===
“咱们很快就能弄个水落石出！”马瑟菲尔德忽然高声道，“咱们到加罗韦尔的事务所去。到那儿再从容计议，总好过在这儿半路截住他。无论如何，那只方箱子总还在他手里。奎格曼，叫辆出租车！”
“这儿正有个车夫候着我使呢。”海瑟维克说。他向自己先前的司机招了招手，那辆车很快靠了过来。“谁知道呢，”待四人一一落座、车子开动，他接着说，“加罗韦尔兴许已经径直到什么别的地方，把箱子交出去了！咱们本该跟上去的。”
“我看不至于。”马瑟菲尔德答道，“整件事透着古怪，全不是我意料中的样子。莫罗代尔勋爵说，他从来没听说太太用过加罗韦尔，可保管库的人却说，他为了她的事来过两三回。不过，咱们很快就能从他嘴里掏出实话。”

## XXIV The House in the Yard / 二十四 院子里的房子

===Original===

The Jew silently and promptly set out in the wake of the hurrying woman; presently she and her pursuer disappeared round a corner.

“That’s the result of our call, Mapperley!” said Hetherwick. “She’s gone somewhere﻿—to tell somebody!”

“Likely!” assented Mapperley. “But wherever she’s gone, Issy Goldmark’ll spot her. He’s the eyes of a lynx.”

“He let Baseverie slip him, the other night, though,” remarked Hetherwick.

“Well, there was some excuse for that,” said Mapperley, “to begin with, he was only instructed to find out where Baseverie went, and to end with he had found out! He’ll not let this woman slip him. She’s good to follow﻿—plenty of her.”

“I wish we knew what she’d left in that house,” said Hetherwick. “We’ll have to find out, somehow!”

===Chinese===

那犹太人一言不发，脚下麻利，径直跟上了那匆匆而去的女人；不多时，她和盯梢的人便一齐在街角那边没了影。

“这就是咱们登门拜访的结果，马珀利！”海瑟维克说。“她奔什么地方去了——去给什么人报信儿！”

“八成是！”马珀利附和道。“不过不管她奔哪儿去了，伊西·戈德马克（Issy Goldmark）都盯得住她。他那双眼，跟山猫似的。”

“不过那天夜里，他可是让巴斯维里从他手里溜了，”海瑟维克说。

“嗐，那回情有可原，”马珀利说，“头一条，交待给他的差事不过是查明巴斯维里去了哪儿；末一条，他查明了呀！这回他不会再让这女人溜掉。她好跟——目标大得很。”

“但愿咱们能知道她把什么留在了那所房子里，”海瑟维克说。“总得想个法子查出来！”

===Original===

“That’s a police job,” replied Mapperley. “Can’t walk into people’s houses without a warrant. And you say Matherfield’s on the other track? However, I should say that this woman’s gone off now to find somebody who’s principally concerned﻿—she looked afraid, in my opinion, when she saw me.”

“She’s in it, somehow,” muttered Hetherwick.

“That house looks mysterious enough for anything. We’ll keep a close watch on it, anyway, until Goldmark comes back, however long that may be.”

But the Jew was back within twenty minutes. So was the woman. She came first, hurrying up the street quicker than when she had left it. As far as the watchers could make out from their vantage point, twenty yards away from her door, she looked flustered, distressed, upset. After her, on the opposite pavement, came Mr. Issy Goldmark, his hands in his pockets.

The woman reentered the house; they heard the door bang. A moment later the Jew turned into the entry in which Hetherwick and Mapperley stood, half hidden from the street. He smiled, inscrutably.

===Chinese===

“那是警察的差事，”马珀利答道。“没有搜查证，可不能闯进人家屋里去。你不是说马瑟菲尔德追另一条线去了么？不过依我看，这女人这会儿准是去找那个顶要紧的正主儿了——她一瞧见我，神色里就带着怕。”

“她多少脱不了干系，”海瑟维克咕哝道。

“那房子瞧着怎么都透着十二分的邪气。反正咱们紧紧盯住它，等戈德马克回来，等多久都成。”

可不到二十分钟，那犹太人就回来了。女人也回来了。她先到的，顺着街一路急赶，比出去时还快。两个放哨的从他们那处据点——离她家大门二十码开外——望过去，只觉得她神色慌乱、满脸愁苦、心绪不宁。她身后，对面人行道上，跟着伊西·戈德马克先生，两手插在兜里。

女人重新进了屋；他们听见门砰的一声关上。片刻之后，犹太人拐进了海瑟维克和马珀利站立的那条过道——从街面上看，这里半隐半露。他微微一笑，笑得叫人捉摸不透。

===Original===

“Thee her go back to her houth?” he asked. “Well, I followed. I thaw where thee’th been, too.”

“Where, then?” demanded Hetherwick, impatiently.

Goldmark jerked his head in the direction from whence he had come.

“Round that corner,” he said, “you get into a regular thlum. Little thtreeth, alleyth, pathageth, and tho on. In one of ’em, a narrow plathe, where there’th a thort of open-air market, there’th a good thithed pieth of blank wall, with an iron-fathen’d door in it. Well, the woman went in there﻿—let herthelf in with a key that thee took from her pocket. Ath thoon ath thee’d gone in, I took a clother look. The door’th fathen’d with iron, or thteel, ath I thaid﻿—jolly thtrong. There ain’t no name on it, and no keyhole that you can look through. The wall’th a good nine or ten feet high, and it’th covered with broken glath at the top. Not a nithe plathe to get into, nohow!”

“Well?” inquired Hetherwick. “She went in?”

“Went in, ath I thay, mithter, and the door clothed on her. After I’d taken a glimpth at the door I got a potht behind one of the thtalls in the thtreet and watched. She came out again in about ten minitth﻿—looked to me, too, ath if thee hadn’t had a very plethant time inthide. Upthet! And thee thet off back here, fathter than vhat thee came. Now thee’th gone into her houth again﻿—ath you no doubt thaw. And that’th all. But if I wath you, mithter,” concluded Issy, “I should jutht find out vhat there ith behind that door and the wall it’th thet in﻿—I thhould tho!”

===Chinese===

“瞧见她回她自个儿家了没？”他问。“嗯，我跟上她啦。她去过哪儿，我也瞧见来着。”

“那么，去了哪儿？”海瑟维克不耐烦地追问。

戈德马克朝自己来的方向一甩头。

“拐过那个街角，”他说，“你就进了一片地道道的贫民窟喽。小街小巷、过道夹弄，如此等等。其中有条窄巷，算是个露天市场似的地方，那儿有一大截光秃秃的墙，墙上开着一扇包铁皮的门。喏，那女人就进那儿去了——用从兜里掏出来的钥匙自己开的门。她一进去，我就凑近瞧了瞧。那门包的是铁皮，要不就是钢——跟我说的似的——结实得很哩。门上没名号，也没有能往里瞅的锁眼。墙足有九英尺到十英尺高，墙头上还插满了碎玻璃。横竖不是个进去的好地方哟！”

“后来呢？”海瑟维克问。“她进去了？”

“进去啦，跟我说的似的，先生，门跟着就在她身后关上了。我朝那门张了一眼，就在街里一个摊子后头占了位子，盯着。约莫十分钟她就出来了——依我看哪，她在里头那会儿可不太痛快。气急败坏的！她拔腿回这儿来，比去时还快。这会儿她又进了她自己家——二位想必也瞧见了。就这么些。可我要是您呐，先生，”伊西末了说，“我就非查明那扇门后头、那道墙里头到底有什么不可——我说真格的！”

===Original===

“That’s a police job,” said Mapperley once more. “If we’d only got Matherfield with us, we could﻿—” Hetherwick paused﻿—thinking. “Look here, Mapperley,” he continued, with a sudden inspiration. “I know what we’ll do! You get a taxicab, as quickly as possible. Drive to the police station where I usually meet Matherfield. There’s another man there whom I know, and who’s pretty well up in this business﻿—Detective-Sergeant Robmore. Ask for him. Tell him what we’ve discovered, and ask him to come back with you and to bring another man if he thinks it necessary. Now then, Goldmark! Tell Mapperley exactly where this place is.”

The Jew pointed along the street to its first corner.

“Round that corner,” he said. “Firtht turning to the right; then firtht to the left; then firtht to the right﻿—that’th the thpot. Lot’th o’ little thtallth in it﻿—a bithy, crowded plathe.”

“Didn’t ye notice the name?” demanded Mapperley, half scoldingly.

“To be thure I did!” grinned Goldmark. “Pencove Thtreet. But it’th better to dethcribe it than to name it. And don’t you go tellin’ no tackthy-driver to drive you in there!﻿—cauth’ there ain’t room!”

Mapperley gave no answer to this piece of advice; he shot off in the direction of Victoria Street, and Hetherwick turned to the Jew.

===Chinese===

“那是警察的差事，”马珀利又说了一遍。“这会儿马瑟菲尔德要在跟前就好了，咱们就能——”海瑟维克顿住了——正琢磨着。“听我说，马珀利，”他接着道，灵机一动。“我知道该怎么办了！你尽快叫一辆出租车。赶到我平常见马瑟菲尔德的那个警察局。那儿还有个我认识的人，办这种案子是行家里手——侦探警佐罗布莫尔（Detective-Sergeant Robmore）。点名找他。把咱们的发现告诉他，请他跟你一块儿回来，必要的话再带上一个人。好了，戈德马克！把那地方的确切位置说给马珀利听。”

犹太人顺着街指向前头第一个拐角。

“拐过那个街角，”他说。“头一个岔口往右；接着头一个岔口往左；再头一个岔口往右——就是那地方喽。里头净是小摊儿——一个又热闹又拥挤的去处。”

“你没留意街名？”马珀利追问道，带着几分责备。

“那可不，当然留意啦！”戈德马克咧嘴一笑。“彭科夫街（Pencove Street）。不过光报名号，不如把模样说清楚。您可别叫哪个出租车司机把车往里开！——那儿可没地儿转车身呐！”

对这条忠告，马珀利没搭腔；他朝维多利亚街方向飞奔而去，海瑟维克则转向犹太人。

===Original===

“We’ll go and have another look at this place, Goldmark,” he said. “But we’ll go separately﻿—as long as we’re in this street, anyway. You stroll off to that first corner, and I’ll join you.”

He crossed the street when the Jew had lounged away, and once more took a narrow look at the house into which the big woman had vanished. It was as close barred and curtained as ever; a veritable place of mystery. For a moment Hetherwick doubted whether he ought to leave it unwatched. But the descriptions of the wall and door in Pencove Street had excited his imagination, and he went on, turned the corner, and rejoined Goldmark. Goldmark at once went in front, piloting him into a maze of unusually dirty and crowded streets, and finally into one, narrower than the rest, on each side of which were tent-like stalls whereon all manner of cheap wares were being offered for sale by raucous-voiced vendors. He saw at once that this was one of those open-air markets of which there are many in the poorer neighbourhoods of London, and wherein you can buy a sixpenny frying-pan as readily as a paper of fried fish, and a gay neckerchief alongside a damaged orange.

Threading his way behind Issy, and between the thronged stalls and the miserable shops that lined the pavement, Hetherwick presently came to the piece of blank wall of which the Jew had told him. The houses and shops round about were old and dilapidated, but the wall was either modern or had been rebuilt and strengthened. It stretched between two low houses, one used as a grocer’s, the other as a hardware shop. In length, it was some thirty feet; in height, quite ten; its coping, as Goldmark had said, was liberally embattled with broken glass. The door, set flush with the adjoining masonry, was a solid affair, faced with metal, newly painted, and the lock was evidently a patent one. A significant fact struck Hetherwick at once﻿—there was no sign of a bell and none of a knocker.

“You say the woman let herself in here?” he asked, as he and Issy paused.

“That’th it, mithter Hetherwick﻿—let herthelf in,” replied Issy. “I thee her take the key from her pocket.”

===Chinese===

“咱们再去把那地方细看一遍，戈德马克，”他说。“不过得分开走——至少在这条街上得如此。你先溜达到头一个拐角去，我随后就来跟你碰头。”

犹太人闲闲荡开之后，他穿过街去，把那大块头女人消失其中的房子又细细打量了一眼。它依旧门窗紧闭、帘幕低垂，十足一个神秘所在。有那么一瞬，海瑟维克拿不准该不该撂下它无人盯守。可彭科夫街那道墙、那扇门的种种描述已经勾起了他的想象，于是他继续前行，拐过街角，重新与戈德马克会合。戈德马克立刻在前头引路，领着他钻进一座格外肮脏拥挤的街道迷宫，最后拐进其中比别处更窄的一条：两旁摆着帐篷似的小摊，嗓门沙哑的小贩们在摊上叫卖各式廉价货色。他一眼看出，这是那种露天市场之一——伦敦穷人聚居的街区里比比皆是，在那儿，你买一把六便士的煎锅跟买一纸包炸鱼一样便当，挑一条鲜亮围巾的旁边，就搁着一只磕碰坏的橘子。

海瑟维克跟在伊西身后，在挤挤挨挨的摊子和沿人行道排开的破铺子之间穿行，不多时便来到了犹太人说起的那截光墙跟前。四周的房屋店铺都老旧破败，唯独这道墙，要么是新砌的，要么翻修加固过。它横亘在两座低矮的房子之间，一座开着杂货铺，另一家是五金行。长有三十英尺上下，高足有十英尺；墙头压顶，正如戈德马克所言，密密麻麻插满了碎玻璃。那扇门与两侧砖石砌得严丝合缝，敦实厚重，包着金属皮，油漆簇新，门锁一望便知是专利货。一个意味深长的事实当即撞进海瑟维克眼里——门上不见门铃的影子，也没有门环。

“你是说，那女人就是在这儿自己开的门？”他问，这时他和伊西停下了脚。

“就是这么回事儿，海瑟维克先生——她自己开的门，”伊西答道。“我亲眼瞧见她从兜里掏钥匙来着。”

===Original===

Hetherwick glanced at the top of the wall.

“I wonder what’s behind?” he muttered. “Building of some sort, of course.” He turned to a man whose stall stood just in front of the mysterious door, and who at that moment had no trade. “Do you know anything about this place?” he asked. “Do you know what’s behind this wall? What building it is?”

The stall-keeper eyed Hetherwick over, silently and carefully. Deciding that he was an innocent person and not a policeman in plain clothes, he found his tongue.

“I don’t, guv’nor!” he answered. “ ’Aint a bloomin’ notion! I been comin’ here, or hereabouts, this three year or more, but I ’aint never seen behind that wall, nor in at that there doorway. S’elp me!”

“But I suppose you’ve seen people go in and come out of the door?” suggested Hetherwick. “It must be used for something!”

“I reckon it is, guv’nor, but I don’t call nobody to mind, though, to be sure, I see a woman come out of it a while ago﻿—big, heavy-jawed woman, she was. But queer as it may seem, I don’t call to mind ever seeing anybody else. You see, guv’nor, I comes here at about ten o’clock of a morning, and I packs up and ’ops it at five﻿—if there’s folks comes in and out o’ that spot, it must be early in a morning and late at night, and so I shouldn’t see ’em. But it’s my belief this here wall and door is back premises to something﻿—the front o’ the place’ll be on the other side.”

===Chinese===

海瑟维克瞥了一眼墙头。

“不晓得后头是什么？”他咕哝道。“总归是座什么建筑就是了。”他转向一个摊子正摆在那神秘之门跟前的男人，这会儿他正好闲着没买卖。“这地方您知道些什么吗？”他问。“您知道这道墙后头是什么？是座什么建筑？”

摆摊的把海瑟维克上上下下打量了一番，不出声，还挺仔细。断定了他是个无干的寻常人、并非便衣警察，这才开了口。

“不知道，老爷！”他答道。“压根儿半点影儿都没有！我上这儿、上这左近来，都三年多了，可那堵墙后头，还有那扇门里头，我从来没瞧见过。老天爷作证！”

“可我猜，总见过有人从这门里进进出出吧？”海瑟维克提点道。“它总归派什么用场！”

“我看是有人用，老爷，可想不起见过谁。不过说实在的，方才倒瞧见一个女人从里头出来——大块头、下巴宽厚的一位。可怪就怪在，我想不起还见过第二个人。您瞧，老爷，我每天早上十点来钟到这儿，五点钟就收摊儿撒腿走人——要真有人打那地方进出，准是清早头一会儿和深夜里，所以我瞧不见他们。可依我看呐，这墙这门是哪家铺子的后院库房——那地方的正门，保准开在另一头的街面上。”

===Original===

“That’s a good idea,” said Hetherwick, with a glance at Goldmark. “Let’s go round.”

But there was no going round. Although they tried various alleys and passages and streets that ought to have been parallel to Pencove Street, they failed to find any place that could be a frontage to the mysterious wall and its close-set door. But the Jew’s alert faculties asserted themselves.

“We can thee vhat’th behind that vail, mithter, eathy enough if we get one o’ them thop-keeperth oppothit to let uth go upthtairth to hith firtht floor,” he said. “Look right acroth the thtreet there, thtallth and all, into vhatever there ith. Try that one,” he went on, pointing to a greengrocer’s establishment which faced the close-set door. “Tell him we’re doin’ a bit o’ land thurveyin’﻿—which ith thtrue!”

Hetherwick made his request﻿—the greengrocer’s lady showed him and Goldmark upstairs into a bow-windowed parlour, one of those dismal apartments which are only used on Sundays, for the purpose of adding more gloom to a gloomy day. She observed that there was a nice view both ways of the street, but Hetherwick confined his inspection to the front. He saw across the wall easily enough, now. There was little to see. The wall bounded a yard, bounded on its left and right sides by the walls of the adjoining houses, and at its further extremity by a low, squat building of red brick, erected against the rear of a high, windowless wall beyond. From its mere aspect, it was impossible to tell what this squat, flat-roofed structure was used for. Its door﻿—closed﻿—was visible; visible, too, were the windows on either side. But it was easy to see that they were obscured, as to their lower halves, by coats of dark paint. There was no sign over the building; no outward indication of its purpose. In the yard, however, were crates, boxes, and carboys in wicker cases; a curiously-shaped chimney, projecting from the roof above, suggested the presence of a furnace or forge beneath. And Hetherwick, after another look, felt no doubt that he was gazing at the place to which Hannaford had been taken, and where he had been skilfully poisoned.

===Chinese===

“这话说得好，”海瑟维克说着，朝戈德马克递了个眼色。“咱们绕到那头去。”

可这一带绕不过去。他们试了一条又一条理应与彭科夫街平行的巷子、过道和街路，却始终找不到一处地方，能与那道神秘的墙和那扇紧闭的门对上正脸。倒是犹太人那股机灵劲儿占了上风。

“咱们能瞧见那堵墙后头是啥，先生，只消说动对面哪位铺子掌柜，让咱们上他家二楼去，容易得很，”他说。“隔着整条街，连摊带人，把那边不管什么都望个透。就试那家，”他接着说，指了指正对那扇紧闭之门的一家菜铺。“就说咱们是干土地测量的——这还不假！”

海瑟维克说明了来意——菜铺的老板娘领他和戈德马克上了楼，进了一间带凸窗的客厅：那种阴惨惨的屋子，只在礼拜天才启用，专为给阴沉的日子再添几分阴沉。她夸口说从这屋朝街两头望景致都好，可海瑟维克只顾朝正面察看。这下，越过那道墙看得一清二楚了。没什么可看的。墙圈出一方院子，左右两侧以毗邻房屋的墙为界，顶里头则贴着一道更高且无窗的大墙的背面，立着一栋低矮敦实的红砖建筑。光凭外表，看不出这座方方正正、平屋顶的矮墩子派什么用场。它的门——关着——望得见；两边的窗户也望得见。但不难看出，那几扇窗的下半截都让几层黑漆糊了个严实。房上没有任何招牌；没有任何表明用途的外在迹象。可院子里堆着板条箱、木箱，还有柳条筐罩着的大玻璃瓶；屋顶上探出一根形状古怪的烟囱，暗示底下装着炉子或锻炉。海瑟维克又端详了一阵，再无疑窦：他此刻望着的，正是汉纳福德被带去的那个所在，也正是他被人手法娴熟地下了毒的地方。

===Original===

Goldmark suddenly nudged his arm, and nodded at the crowded street below.

“Mapperley!” he whispered. “And two men with him!”

Hetherwick, glancing in the direction indicated, saw Robmore and another man, both in plain clothes, making their way down the street, between the stalls and the shops. With them, and in close conversation, was a uniformed constable. He turned to leave the room, but Goldmark again touched his elbow.

“Before we go, mithter,” he said, “jutht take another glanth at that plathe oppothite, and it’ths thurroundin’th. I thee where we can get in! D’ye thee, mithter Hetherwick, the wall between that yard and the next houth﻿—the right-hand thide one﻿—’ith fairly low at the far end. Now, if the man in that houth would let uth go through to hith backyard﻿—vhat?”

“I see!” agreed Hetherwick. “We’ll try it. But Robmore first﻿—come along.”

===Chinese===

戈德马克忽然用胳膊肘碰了碰他，朝楼底下人潮拥挤的街上点头示意。

“马珀利！”他悄声道。“还带了两个人！”

海瑟维克顺着指的方向望去，看见罗布莫尔和另一个人，都一身便衣，正顺着街在摊子和店铺之间往这边走。与他们同行、且正凑在一处密谈的，还有一名穿制服的警察。他转身要出屋，戈德马克却又碰了碰他的胳膊肘。

“咱们走之前，先生，”他说，“再朝对面那地方瞅一眼，还有它四周围的情形。我瞧出咱们能打哪儿进去了！您瞧见没，海瑟维克先生，那院子跟隔壁房子之间那道墙——右手边的那道——到了顶里头那端相当矮。这会儿，要是那房子里的人肯让咱们打他家后院穿过去——您说呢？”

“我明白了！”海瑟维克表示赞成。“就依这个办。不过先去迎罗布莫尔——走吧。”

===Original===

He slipped some silver into the hand of the greengrocer’s lady, and went down to the street. A few brief explanations to the two detectives supplemented the information already given them by Mapperley, and then Robmore nodded at the constable who stood by, eagerly interested.

“We’ve been talking to him, Mr. Hetherwick,” he said. “He’s sometimes on day duty here, and sometimes he’s on night. He says he’s often wondered about this place, and it’s a very queer thing that though he’s known this district more than a year, he’s never seen a soul go in or out of that door, and hasn’t the least notion of what business, if it is a business, is carried on there!”

“Never seen anything or anybody!” corroborated the constable. “At any time﻿—day or night. When I first came on this beat, maybe fifteen months ago, that door had been newly set and painted, and the glass had just been stuck atop of the wall. But it’s a fact﻿—I’ve never seen anybody go in or come out!”

“I propose to go in,” said Hetherwick. “I think we’ve abundant cause, knowing what we do. It may be that the two missing ladies are there. I’ve been having a look into the yard, and we could get into it easily by going through the grocer’s shop there, on the right, and climbing the wall from his back premises. What do you say, Robmore?”

“Oh, I think so!” agreed Robmore. “Now we’re on the job, we’ll carry it through. Better let me tackle the grocer, Mr. Hetherwick﻿—I’ll see him first and then call you in.”

===Chinese===

他往菜铺老板娘手里塞了几枚银币，下了楼来到街上。向两位侦探三言两语补充了马珀利已提供的情报之后，罗布莫尔便朝那位在旁听得入神的警察点了点头。

“我们刚才正跟他聊来着，海瑟维克先生，”他说。“他有时在这片儿当白班，有时当夜班。他说他常为这地方纳闷；怪就怪在，他在这辖区一年多了，从没见过一个活人打那扇门进出，那里面——倘若真算门生意——做的是什么营生，他更是连一点头绪也没有！”

“什么也没见过，什么人也没见过！”警察帮腔道。“什么时候都算上——白天也好，夜里也好。我刚接这条巡段那阵儿，约莫十五个月前吧，那扇门才新装新漆，墙头上的玻璃也才插上去。可这是实情——我从来没见谁进去过、出来过！”

“我提议进去，”海瑟维克说。“凭咱们眼下掌握的种种，我认为理由绰绰有余。说不定那两位失踪的女士就在里头。我方才朝院子里望过一眼，打右手边那家杂货铺穿过去，从他家后院翻墙进去，易如反掌。你说呢，罗布莫尔？”

“哦，我看行！”罗布莫尔表示赞同。“既然接了这活儿，就一竿子干到底。杂货铺主还是让我去打交道，海瑟维克先生——我先去见他有话，回头再叫你们进来。”

===Original===

The other waited while Robmore entered the shop and spoke with its owner. They saw him engaged in conversation for several minutes; then he came to the door and beckoned the rest to approach.

“That’s all right,” he said in an aside to Hetherwick. “We can go through to his backyard, and he’ll lend us a stepladder to get over the wall. But he’s told me a bit﻿—he knows the two men who have this place in the next yard, and there’s no doubt at all, from his description of them, that one’s Ambrose and the other Baseverie. He says they’ve had the place almost eighteen months, and he thinks they use it as a laboratory﻿—chemicals, or something of that sort. But he says they’re rarely seen﻿—sometimes he’s never seen them for days and even weeks together. Usually they’re there of a night﻿—he’s seen lights in the place at all hours of the night. Well﻿—come on!”

The posse of investigators filed through the dark little shop to a yard at its rear, the grocer’s apprentice going in front with a stepladder, which he planted against the intervening wall at its lowest point. One by one, the uniformed constable going first, the six men climbed and dropped over. But for their own presence, the place seemed deserted and lifeless. As Hetherwick had observed from the greengrocer’s parlour the windows were obscured by thick coats of paint; nevertheless, two or three of the men approached and tried to find places from which the paint had been scratched, in an effort to see what lay inside. But the constable, bolder and more direct, went straight to the entrance.

“Door’s open!” he exclaimed. “Not even shut!” He pushed the door wide, and went into the building, the rest crowding after him. “Hullo!” he shouted. “Hullo!”

No answer came to the summons. The constable crossed the lobby in which they were all standing, and opened an inner door. And Hetherwick saw at once that the grocer’s surmise as to the purpose to which the place was put had been correct﻿—this was a chemical laboratory, well equipped, too, with modern apparatus. But there was not a sign of life in it.

===Chinese===

众人候着，罗布莫尔进店同店主交涉。他们瞧他一谈谈了好几分钟；随后他走到门口，招呼其余人上前。

“都妥了，”他侧过身对海瑟维克低声道。“咱们可以打他家后院穿过去，他还肯借一架折梯供我们翻墙。不过他也跟我说了点儿底——隔壁院里盘下这地方的那两个人，他认得，听他一形容，一个是安布罗斯，另一个是巴斯维里，半点疑问没有。他说这地方他们到手快十八个月了，他琢磨着是当实验室用的——化学药品，或者那一类东西。可他说这俩人轻易不露面——有时候一连几天甚至几个礼拜见不着人影。他们通常夜里才在——夜里无论什么钟点，他都见过那屋里亮着灯。好了——走吧！”

这一行查案的人鱼贯穿过那间昏暗的小铺，进到铺子后头的院子，杂货铺的学徒扛着折梯走在前头，把梯子支在隔墙最低矮的一处。六个人一个接一个翻过墙跳下去，那穿制服的警察打头。若不是他们自己这几个人在，这地方简直就是一片死寂、了无生气。正如海瑟维克方才在菜铺客厅里瞧见的，窗玻璃都刷了厚厚的漆遮死；尽管如此，还是有那么两三个人凑上前去，想找找漆皮剥落的地方，好窥探里面的光景。倒是那位警察更大胆、更直截，径直奔向入口。

“门开着！”他失声道。“压根儿没关！”他把门推到最大，走进屋去，其余人一拥而入。“喂！”他扬声喊道。“喂——！”

喊声无人应答。警察穿过他们众人立足的门厅，推开了一扇内门。海瑟维克立刻看出，杂货铺主对这处所用途的猜测一点不错——这是一间化学实验室，而且设备精良，仪器都是新式的。可里面没有一丝活气。

===Original===

“Nobody here, apparently,” murmured one of the men. “Flown!”

Robmore went forward to another door, and opening it, revealed a room furnished as an office. There was a roll-top desk in it, and papers and documents lying there; he and Hetherwick began to finger and examine them. And Hetherwick suddenly saw something that made a link between this mysterious place and the house he had called at earlier in the afternoon. There, before his eyes, lay some of the azure-tinted notepaper which Mapperley had traced with the embossed address on it of which the stationer had told.

“There’s no doubt we’ve hit on the place at last, Robmore,” he said. “I wish we’d had Matherfield here. But﻿—”

Before he could say more, a sudden shout came from Goldmark, who, while the others were investigating the lower regions, had courageously, and alone, gone up the low staircase to the upper rooms.

“Mithter!” he called. “Mithter Hetherwick! come up here﻿—come up, all of you. Here’th a man here, a-thittin’ in a chair﻿—and th’elp me if I don’t believe he’th a thtiff ’un﻿—dead!”

===Chinese===

“看样子人不在，”其中一个咕哝道。“跑光了！”

罗布莫尔走向另一扇门，推开一看，是间布置成办公室模样的屋子。屋里有一张卷盖写字台，台上摊着纸张文书；他和海瑟维克动手翻检起来。冷不丁地，海瑟维克看见了一样东西，把这座神秘院落与他下午早些时候登门的那所房子连到了一处。就在他眼前，摊着几张浅蓝色信笺——正是马珀利循着那压花地址一路查到的那种，也是文具商说起过的那种。

“毫无疑问，咱们总算找对地方了，罗布莫尔，”他说。“可惜马瑟菲尔德不在跟前。不过——”

他话没说完，戈德马克那边猛地爆出一声喊——趁着众人在楼下各处查看，他已独自一人壮着胆子，顺着那道低矮的楼梯上二楼房间去了。

“先生！”他喊道。“海瑟维克先生！上这儿来——你们全都上来。这儿有个人，直挺挺坐在椅子上——老天爷作证，我瞧他分明是具死尸——死透啦！”

## XXIII The Landlady of Little Smith Street / 二十三 小史密斯街的房东太太

===Original===
Garrowell’s office proved to be up two flights of stairs in St. Martin’s Lane. They were dark and dingy stairs, and none of the four men clambering up them noticed that an office-boy, rushing unceremoniously downward carried a small parcel with which he fled out of the door and away down the street. They were, indeed, thinking of Garrowell﻿—and within five minutes they were all in his private room. For another five minutes Matherfield was explaining matters﻿—explaining to an obviously startled and much astonished listener.
“That’s how it stands,” concluded Matherfield. “You’ve evidently got the explanation, Mr. Garrowell. Now﻿—”
“But you surprise me!” broke in the solicitor. “I’ve acted for Madame Listorelle in two or three matters﻿—I’ve got things from her safe for her before, once or twice. And I saw nothing unusual in the letter she sent me this morning. Here it is! You can see it. Her usual notepaper﻿—certainly her handwriting﻿—nobody, I think, could imitate that successfully. You see what she says﻿—I was to give the enclosed authorisation to the Safe people, take out a small, square, brown-leather-covered box from the safe, pack it up, and send it off to Mr. C. Basing, Post Office, Southampton, at once, by express delivery. Nothing unusual in all that, I think. Of course, I carried out her wishes. But look at the letter.”
All four men were looking at the letter. It was as Garrowell described, and whether it had been written under duress or not, the writing was bold and firm. But Matherfield seized on the envelope, and after a glance at it, pointed to the postmark.
“See that!” he exclaimed. “Posted in the SW district late last night. If madame had been at home in Paddington the postmark would have been different. Well﻿—but the square box, Mr. Garrowell! You’ve got it, of course? Do you know that that box probably contains jewels worth﻿—”

===Chinese===
加罗韦尔（Garrowell）的事务所原来设在圣马丁巷（St. Martin’s Lane）一幢楼的两段楼梯之上。楼梯又暗又旧，四个人攀援而上，谁都没留意一个事务所的听差正冒冒失失地往下冲，手里拎着一个小包裹，一出大门便顺着街道飞奔而去。他们心里想着的，也确实是加罗韦尔——不出五分钟，四个人便都进了他的私人办公室。接下来的五分钟里，马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）一一说明事情原委——听他讲的人显然又惊又诧。
“情况就是这样。”马瑟菲尔德收束道，“解释显然在您手里，加罗韦尔先生。那么——”
“这可真叫我吃惊！”律师插嘴道。“我替里斯托雷尔太太（Madame Listorelle）办过两三件事——以前也替她去保险库取过一两回东西。今天早上她给我送来的这封信，我瞧着毫无异样。信就在这儿！诸位请过目。她平素用的信笺——千真万确是她的笔迹——我想，这笔迹谁也仿不像。你们看她信里交代的——让我把随信附来的授权书交给保险保管库那边，从库房里取出一只小小的方形棕色皮箱，包装妥当，立刻以快递专递寄往南安普顿（Southampton）邮局，交 C. 贝辛先生（Mr. C. Basing）留局待取。这一切在我看来都平平常常。我自然照她的意思办了。可你们再瞧瞧这封信。”
四个人都凑过去看那封信。信正如加罗韦尔所描述的那样，且不论是否出于胁迫，笔迹遒劲沉稳。但马瑟菲尔德一把抓过信封，扫了一眼，便指着上面的邮戳。
“瞧这个！”他叫道，“昨天深夜在西南（SW）邮区投寄。要是太太当时在帕丁顿（Paddington）家中，邮戳就不是这个样子了。好——那么，那只方箱子呢，加罗韦尔先生！想必在您手上吧？您可知道，那只箱子里装的多半是珠宝，价值——”


===Original===
“The box?” ejaculated Garrowell. “Got it? Of course not! It’s gone! The boy went off to the post office with it﻿—oh, just before you came.”
“Gad!” muttered Lord Morradale. “Well﻿—the post office, at once, Matherfield!”
But Matherfield suddenly laughed, throwing up both hands as if with a sudden inspiration.
“No, my lord, no!” he said. “No! The box is safe enough in the post. It’s off to Mr. C. Basing, Post Office, Southampton. And when Mr. Basing calls to collect it﻿—he’ll find me!”

===Chinese===
“箱子？”加罗韦尔脱口道，“在我手上？当然不在！已经寄走啦！听差拎着它上邮局去了——哦，就在诸位来之前。”
“天哪！”莫罗代尔勋爵（Lord Morradale）咕哝道，“那——赶紧上邮局，马瑟菲尔德！”
谁知马瑟菲尔德忽然大笑起来，双手一扬，仿佛灵光乍现。
“不，勋爵大人，不！”他说，“不！箱子待在邮路里再稳妥不过。它这就要奔南安普顿邮局的 C. 贝辛先生那儿去。等贝辛先生前去领取时——他会发现我等在那儿！”


===Original===
There was triumphant conviction in Matherfield’s tone: there was the impulse to immediate action in the way in which he pulled out a railway guide from his pocket, and rapidly turned its pages. But Hetherwick and Lord Morradale looked at each other. And each saw that the other was dubious.
“Yes,” said Lord Morradale slowly. “Um﻿—no doubt, Matherfield. But I say, you know﻿—those jewels are worth no end! Safe enough, perhaps, in the hands of the postal authorities, now they are there, but﻿—there’s many a slip, you know, and﻿—”
“You might take the postal authorities into your confidence,” suggested Hetherwick. “These people are up to all sorts of wily tricks﻿—”
Matherfield laughed quietly. It was the laugh of a man who knows his own business thoroughly, and is a little impatient of outside criticism.
“I know what I’m doing, gentlemen,” he answered. “Leave it to me as to what I do with the post office people. I’ve as good as got the handcuffs on Baseverie or on Ambrose﻿—perhaps on both! This is how I figure the thing,” he went on, with a final glance at the timetable. “These two men have got Madame Listorelle and the young lady-secretary in their power, safe somewhere in London. They forced madame, last night, to write that letter to Mr. Garrowell here﻿—we know what they made her write. Mr. Garrowell got the small box containing the jewels, and he’s sent it off, already, by express delivery, to Southampton. It will be there early this evening, and one or other of the men will be there to meet it. If Baseverie calls for it, Ambrose will be round the corner. If Ambrose calls for it, Baseverie will be close at hand. Probably they’re already in Southampton﻿—they’d go this morning, to be on the spot. As soon as the box is in their hands they’ll be off﻿—probably to the Continent, by Southampton and Havre. They won’t try the Atlantic﻿—the five days’ voyage would be too risky. They’ll make for France. But they won’t get to France﻿—they’ll find themselves in the lockup at Southampton before bedtime! You see if that doesn’t come off, gentlemen, as sure as my name’s what it is. Now, Quigman, you come with me. We’ve just nice time to catch the one-thirty, and to get in touch with the Southampton police, and lay our plans and make our arrangements. Some time tonight, gentlemen, you’ll hear from me!”

===Chinese===
马瑟菲尔德的语气里透着稳操胜券的笃定；他从口袋里掏出一册火车时刻表，飞快地翻检书页，那股子劲头是一刻也不肯耽搁的劲头。可海瑟维克（Hetherwick）和莫罗代尔勋爵对视了一眼，彼此都看出对方心里犯着嘀咕。
“是啊，”莫罗代尔勋爵慢吞吞地说，“唔——没说的，马瑟菲尔德。不过我说，你可知道——那些珠宝可价值连城！如今既已交到邮政当局手里，也许够稳妥的了，可是——世事总有闪失，你知道，再说——”
“您不妨也让邮政当局知情。”海瑟维克提议道，“这伙人诡计多端，什么花招都使得出来——”
马瑟菲尔德淡淡一笑。那是一种对自己行当烂熟于心、对外行指摘稍感不耐的人的笑。
“我心中有数，诸位先生，”他答道，“跟邮局那边怎么打交道，就交给我吧。巴斯维里（Baseverie）也好，安布罗斯（Ambrose）也好——没准儿两个一起——我等于已经给他们铐上了手铐！我是这样盘算这档子事的，”他朝时刻表又扫了最后一眼，接着说道，“这两个人把里斯托雷尔太太和那位年轻的女秘书捏在手心里，藏在伦敦城里哪处稳妥地方。昨天夜里，他们逼着太太给这位加罗韦尔先生写了那封信——他们逼她写了什么，咱们都清楚。加罗韦尔先生取到了装珠宝的小箱子，而且已经用快递专递寄往南安普顿了。箱子今晚早些时候就到，两个家伙里必有一个到场接货。若是巴斯维里去取，安布罗斯就在拐角守着；若是安布罗斯去取，巴斯维里就在近旁。多半他们已经到了南安普顿——今儿一早就动身，好守在那儿。箱子一到手，他们立刻就溜——多半取道南安普顿和勒阿弗尔（Havre）奔欧洲大陆去。他们不会走大西洋那条线——五天的航程太冒险。他们要奔法国。可他们到不了法国——不到就寝的钟点，他们就会发现自己已经蹲在南安普顿的班房里了！诸位瞧着吧，这事准这么收场，就像我的名字货真价实一样。喏，奎格曼（Quigman），你跟我来。咱们赶一点半那班车，工夫正好，还要跟南安普顿警方接上头，把计划部署安排停当。今晚什么时候，诸位就会得到我的信儿！”


===Original===
Then Matherfield hurried Quigman away, and the three men left behind looked at each other. Mr. Garrowell was obviously much concerned, and his hands, thin and nervous, trembled as he began to arrange the papers on his desk.
“This is a most distressing business, gentlemen,” he said. “It is very painful to me to think that I have been made an instrument in a crime of this sort, however innocent a one! But how could I tell that this letter was forced out of Madame Listorelle? On the face of it﻿—”
“Oh, there’s no blame attaching to you, Mr. Garrowell!” interrupted Lord Morradale. “On the face of it, the letter’s genuine enough. But I wanted to ask you a question: How much do you know of Madame Listorelle? I mean, how often has she employed you?”
“Two or three times only,” replied Garrowell. “She came to me first about an agreement which I had had to send her on behalf of another client. She seemed very friendly, and was kind enough to say that next time she had any legal business she would remember me as she hadn’t any regular solicitor of her own. I think,” he added with a deprecating smile, “she probably saw that I was beginning, and hadn’t much to do.”

===Chinese===
说完，马瑟菲尔德便带着奎格曼匆匆走了，留下的三个人面面相觑。加罗韦尔先生显然心绪不宁，他开始收拾桌上的文件时，那双瘦削而神经质的手直发颤。
“这真是桩叫人痛心的事，诸位先生，”他说，“一想到自己竟被利用，成了这种罪案里的一件工具，哪怕是无辜的工具，我心里就万分难受！可我哪里想得到，这封信是逼着里斯托雷尔太太写的呢？从表面上看——”
“哦，这可怪不到您头上，加罗韦尔先生！”莫罗代尔勋爵打断道，“从表面上看，这封信够真的了。不过我想请教您一个问题：您对里斯托雷尔太太了解多少？我是说，她找您办过几回事？”
“总共不过两三回。”加罗韦尔答道，“她头一回找我，是为一份协议书——那是我先前不得不代表另一位当事人寄给她的。她显得很热络，还好心说，往后再有什么法律上的事一定想着我，因为她自己没有固定的律师。我想，”他带着自谦的笑容补充道，“她多半是看我刚起步，没什么生意。”


===Original===
“I see,” said Lord Morradale, looking round at the somewhat humble appointments of the office. “And you’ve been to that Safe Deposit place on her behalf﻿—how often?”
“Twice. On each occasion Madame Listorelle wrote her instructions from abroad. Once she was in Paris. The other time she was at Nice. The instructions were similar on both occasions: I was to go to the Safe Deposit, get a certain parcel or article and post it to an address given. The first time I sent a small parcel to Amsterdam﻿—I have the exact address and name; the second, to New York. So that, of course, when I got Madame’s letter this morning, I saw nothing unusual in it.”
“Just so!” agreed Lord Morradale. “You wouldn’t. Well, I hope Matherfield will clap the irons on the men who forced her to write it! Eh, Hetherwick?”
“With all my heart!” responded Hetherwick. “But I, too, want to ask Mr. Garrowell a question. How long,” he continued, “have you been here, in St. Martin’s Lane?”

===Chinese===
“我明白了。”莫罗代尔勋爵说着，环顾了一下这间陈设颇为寒素的办公室。“那么，您替她去过那家保险保管库——去过几回？”
“两回。每一回里斯托雷尔太太都是从国外写信来吩咐的。一回她在巴黎（Paris），另一回在尼斯（Nice）。两回的吩咐都相仿：让我去保险保管库，取出某件包裹或物件，寄到指定的地址。头一回我把一个小包裹寄到了阿姆斯特丹（Amsterdam）——确切的地址和姓名我都留着呢；第二回寄到了纽约（New York）。所以，今儿早上接到太太的信，我自然瞧不出半点异样。”
“正是这么回事！”莫罗代尔勋爵附和道，“您本来就看不出来。好，但愿马瑟菲尔德能把逼她写这封信的那两个家伙铐起来！呃，海瑟维克？”
“那真是求之不得！”海瑟维克应道，“不过我也要问加罗韦尔先生一个问题。”他接着说，“您在这儿——在圣马丁巷——开业多久了？”


===Original===
“Oh, four or five years,” replied Garrowell.
“Then you know this district pretty well, of course. Have you ever come across a man whom I’ll try to describe to you?” He went on to give an accurate, if concise, description of Baseverie. “That man,” he concluded, “is sometimes seen around here.”
Garrowell nodded.
“I know him!” he said. “In fact, he’s been in this very room﻿—to see me. But I don’t know his name, nor anything much about him. He was brought here by another man and he only stayed a few minutes.”
“How much do you know about him﻿—however little?” asked Hetherwick.

===Chinese===
“哦，四年五年了吧。”加罗韦尔答道。
“那么，这一带您想必相当熟了。您可曾碰见过一个人——我来试着给您描述描述？”他接着把巴斯维里的模样精当而扼要地刻画了一番。“那个人，”他末了说，“这一带有时能见得着。”
加罗韦尔点了点头。
“我认得他！”他说，“不瞒您说，他就来过这间屋子——来见我。可我不知道他的名字，别的情况也不甚了了。他是让另一个人带来的，只坐了几分钟。”
“关于他您知道多少——哪怕一星半点？”海瑟维克问。


===Original===
“This much. You know that people who have invented things come to solicitors for legal advice, and sometimes to get information as to how they can best dispose of their inventions? Well, about nine months ago a man came to me who claimed to have invented a drop-bottle﻿—that is, a bottle from which you could only drop one drop of stuff at a time. He said such a thing was badly wanted, and that there ought to be a pile of money in it. He wanted to know how best to get it on the market. I didn’t know, but I mentioned the matter to one or two people, and a man I know﻿—or knew at that time, for he’s since dead, unfortunately﻿—said that he knew a man who was a sort of commission agent for inventions﻿—took up a good idea, don’t you see, and introduced it﻿—and he promised to bring him to see me. He brought him; the man he brought was, without doubt, the man you describe. His name was not mentioned, but I am sure he was that man. I don’t know what your man is, but I felt sure that the man I am talking about either was or had been a medical man.”
“Ah!” exclaimed Hetherwick. “What made you think that?”
“From his conversation﻿—from the remarks he made about the bottle. He didn’t take it up; he said my client was too late and was wrongly informed into the bargain: there was such a thing, and a superior one, already on the market. He went away then, and, as I say, I never heard his name, and I’ve never seen him since.”
“That’s the man we want!” said Hetherwick. “If Matherfield can only lay hands on him! But we shall know more by midnight.”
Outside, he turned to Lord Morradale with a shake of the head.

===Chinese===
“知道这么一些。您知道，发明了东西的人常来找律师讨法律上的主意，有时是想打听怎样处置自己的发明最合算？喏，大约九个月前，有个人来找我，自称发明了一种滴瓶——就是一次只淌得出一滴药水的瓶子。他说这东西正用得着，里头准压着一大笔钱。他想知道怎么把它推上市场最合适。我不在行，不过我跟一两个人提过这事；我认识的一个——应该说是当时认识，不巧他如今已经过世了——说他认得一个人，是替发明做经纪的那路人——您明白，就是相中一个好点子，替它引荐门路——他答应领他来见我。他果真领来了；领来的那个人，不容置疑，正是您描述的这位。姓名没有提起过，可我拿得准就是他。我不知道您要找的这位是干什么的，可我那时就拿得准，我说的这个人不是当下行医、就是行过医。”
“啊！”海瑟维克叫道，“您凭什么这么想？”
“凭他的谈吐——凭他就那瓶子说的一番话。他没有接这个茬；他说我的当事人动手太晚，况且还听信了讹传：市面上早有这么个东西了，还是个更高级的。随后他就走了，正如我所说的，我再没听说过他的名字，也再没见过他。”
“这正是我们要找的人！”海瑟维克说，“但愿马瑟菲尔德能逮住他！到午夜，咱们就知道更多了。”
到了外面，他摇着头转向莫罗代尔勋爵。


===Original===
“We’re no nearer to any knowledge of where the two women are!” he exclaimed.
“Oh, I don’t know!” responded Lord Morradale. “I think we are, you know. You see, if Matherfield nabs those chaps, or even one of them, he or they will see that the game’s up, and will give in and say where their captives are. Odd business, Hetherwick, that people can be kidnapped and imprisoned in broad daylight in London!”
“I don’t think anything’s impossible or odd﻿—in London,” answered Hetherwick dryly. “If one had only the least idea as to which quarter of the town that car was driven, one might be doing something!”
“Lots of subsections in every quarter, and subsections again in each of those,” replied Lord Morradale with equal dryness. “Take some time to comb out this town! No! I think we must trust to Matherfield. Nothing else to trust to, in fact.”
But Hetherwick suddenly thought of Mapperley. He began to wonder what the clerk was after, what his notion had been. Then he remembered Mapperley’s admonition to look out for a message about that time, and excusing himself from Lord Morradale, he jumped on a bus and went along to the Temple. There, in the letter-box, he found a telegram:

===Chinese===
“那两位太太的下落，咱们还是一点眉目都没有！”他叫道。
“哦，那也未必！”莫罗代尔勋爵应道，“要我说，倒是有眉目了。您想啊，马瑟菲尔德要是逮住那两个家伙，哪怕只逮住一个，他们就会明白大势已去，乖乖服输，招出关人的地方。真是桩怪事，海瑟维克，在伦敦，光天化日之下居然能把人绑架关押起来！”
“在伦敦，我看没有什么事是不可能的，也说不上怪。”海瑟维克干巴巴地答道，“哪怕只摸到一丁点儿那辆汽车朝城里哪个方向开去的线索，也还能有点作为！”
“每个城区里又分好几片，每片里再分好几小块。”莫罗代尔勋爵同样干巴巴地答道，“把这座城梳上一遍可得些功夫呢！不！我看咱们只能指望马瑟菲尔德了。说实在的，旁的也没什么可指望的。”
可海瑟维克忽然想起了马珀利（Mapperley）。他开始琢磨这位办事员究竟在忙什么，打的什么主意。接着他记起马珀利叮嘱过，大约这个时辰要留意有什么口信送来，于是向莫罗代尔勋爵告了罪，跳上一辆公共汽车，直奔内殿（the Temple）而去。在那儿的信箱里，他发现一封电报：


===Original===
> Meet me Victoria three o’clock.
> Mapperley.
Hetherwick set off for Victoria there and then. But it was only a quarter-past two when he got there, and as he had had no lunch, he turned into the restaurant. There, when he was halfway through a chop, Mapperley found him, and slipped into a chair close by before Hetherwick noticed his presence.
“Thought I might find you in here, sir,” said Mapperley. They were alone in a quiet corner, but the clerk lowered his voice to a whisper. “Well,” he continued, bending across the table, “I’ve done a bit, anyhow.”
“In what way?” asked Hetherwick.
Mapperley produced from his breast pocket some papers, and from amongst them selected an envelope﻿—the azure-tinted envelope which he had picked up from the caretaker’s supper table at St. Mary’s Mansions.

===Chinese===
> 维多利亚车站三点会面。
> 马珀利。
海瑟维克当即动身前往维多利亚（Victoria）。可他到那儿才两点一刻，午饭还没吃，便进了餐馆。一份肉排刚吃到一半，马珀利寻了过来，趁他没留神，悄悄在近旁一把椅子上坐了下来。
“估摸着能在这儿找着您，先生。”马珀利说。两人独坐在僻静的角落里，他却把嗓门压成了耳语。“喏，”他隔着桌子探过身来，接着说道，“反正，我办成了一点事。”
“办了什么？”海瑟维克问。
马珀利从怀里掏出几样纸件，从中拣出一只信封——正是那只淡蓝色的信封，他从圣玛丽公寓（St. Mary’s Mansions）管理员的晚饭桌上顺出来的那只。


===Original===
“You recognise this?” he said, with a sly smile. “You know where I got it. This is the envelope which Baseverie took to the caretaker, with the order to enter Madame Listorelle’s flat. You knew that I carried it off, from under the man’s nose, last night. But you didn’t know why. I only laughed when you asked me.”
“Well, why, then?” inquired Hetherwick.
“This reason,” replied Mapperley. “We both noticed that the sheet of paper on which the order had been written by Madame had been shortened﻿—there was no doubt that a printed or embossed address had been trimmed off, rather roughly, too. We noticed that, I say, both of us. But I don’t think you noticed something far more important﻿—far, far more important﻿—for our purposes.”
“No,” admitted Hetherwick. “I didn’t. What?”
“This,” said Mapperley, turning back the broken flap of the envelope. “You didn’t notice that here, on the envelope, is the name and address of the stationer who supplied this stuff! There you are﻿—W. H. Calkin, 85, Broadway, Westminster. You never saw that, Mr. Hetherwick. But I did!”

===Chinese===
“认得这个吧？”他说，狡黠地一笑，“您知道我打哪儿弄来的。这就是巴斯维里拿给管理员的那只信封，附带着进里斯托雷尔太太公寓的指令。您知道，昨天夜里我当着那人的面把它顺了出来。可您不知道图的是什么。您问我时，我只是笑。”
“那么，图的是什么呢？”海瑟维克追问。
“图的是这个缘故。”马珀利答道，“咱们俩都注意到，太太写指令的那张纸裁短了一截——毫无疑问，原本印着或压印着地址的部分给铰掉了，还铰得挺毛糙。我说，这一点咱们俩都看出来了。可有一样要紧得多的东西——对咱们的差事来说，要紧得不知多少倍的东西——我想您没瞧出来。”
“是没有，”海瑟维克承认道，“我没瞧出来。是什么？”
“是这个。”马珀利说着，翻开信封撕破的封盖，“您没留意，这儿，信封上，印着供应这批纸品的文具商的姓名地址！瞧——威斯敏斯特（Westminster）百老汇（Broadway） 85 号，W. H. 卡尔金（W. H. Calkin）。这个您从来没瞧见吧，海瑟维克先生。可我瞧见了！”


===Original===
Hetherwick began to comprehend. He smiled﻿—gratefully.
“Smart of you, Mapperley!” he exclaimed. “I see! And﻿—you’ve been there?”
“I’ve been there,” answered Mapperley. “I saw a chance of tracking these men down. I couldn’t get hold of Calkin till nearly noon, but I got on like a house afire when I did get him. You see,” he went on, “that paper is, to start with, of an unusual tint, in colour. Secondly, it’s of very superior quality, though very thin﻿—intended chiefly for foreign correspondence. Thirdly, it’s expensive. Now, I felt certain its use would be limited, and what I wanted to find out from the stationer was﻿—to whom he’d supplied it. That was easy. He recognised the paper and envelope at once. Of the handwriting on the paper, he knew nothing whatever﻿—Madame’s writing, you know﻿—that he’d never seen before. But he said at once that he’d only supplied that particular make of paper and envelopes to three people, and for each person he’d prepared a die, to emboss the addresses. The embossing had been done at his shop, and he showed me specimens of each. One was for the Dowager Lady Markentree, 120, Grosvenor Gardens. That was no use. The second was for Miss Chelandry, 87, Ebury Street. That was out of count, too. But the third was what I wanted. It was just the address, 56, Little Smith Street, SW1. As soon as I saw it, I knew I’d got on the right track.”
“Go on!” said Hetherwick.

===Chinese===
海瑟维克恍然领悟。他笑了——笑得满怀感激。
“你可真机灵，马珀利！”他叫道，“我明白了！那么——你去过那儿了？”
“去过了。”马珀利答道，“我瞅准了机会，要顺着这条线把那两个人挖出来。快到晌午我才见着卡尔金，可一接上头，事情就办得风风火火。您瞧，”他接着说，“这种纸，头一条，颜色就少见。第二，纸质极上乘，虽然极薄——主要是供国外通信用的。第三，价钱贵。我当时就笃定，用这纸的人屈指可数，我要从文具商嘴里问出来的正是——他都供货给了谁。这不难。他一眼就认出了这种纸和信封。纸上写的字他毫不知情——太太的笔迹，您知道——他从来没见过。可他立刻说，这种特制的纸和配套信封，他统共只供过三个人，还替每个人制了一副压印地址的铜模。压印都是在他铺子里做的，他还在我面前展示了每一副的印样。一副是马肯特里太夫人（Dowager Lady Markentree）的，格罗夫纳花园（Grosvenor Gardens） 120 号——那副没用。第二副是切兰德里小姐（Miss Chelandry）的，伊伯里街（Ebury Street） 87 号——那副也不作数。可第三副正合我意。上头只有个地址：小史密斯街（Little Smith Street） 56 号，SW1。一瞧见它，我就知道摸对路了。”
“接着说！”海瑟维克道。


===Original===
“The stationer, Calkin, didn’t know the name of the man who ordered this paper and gave this address,” continued Mapperley. “He knew him well enough as a customer, though, and described him. Baseverie, without a doubt! Calkin says that Baseverie, during the last few months, bought various items of stationery from him﻿—notebooks, duplicating paper, office requisites, and so on. He never knew his name, but as he always carried away his own purchases, and paid spot cash for them, that didn’t matter. Calkin supplied him with ten quires of this paper and envelopes to match, a couple of months ago. So﻿—there you are! And there I was﻿—sure at last that Baseverie’s mysterious hiding-place was 56, Little Smith Street!”
“Good﻿—good!” said Hetherwick. “What next?”
“Well, I thought we could do with a bit of help,” replied Mapperley, smiling. “So I left Calkin﻿—bound to secrecy, of course﻿—and telephoned to Issy Goldmark. Issy is just the sort of chap for games of this sort! Issy came﻿—and he and I took a stroll round. Do you know Little Smith Street?”
“Not I!” answered Hetherwick. “Never heard of it!”

===Chinese===
“文具商卡尔金并不知道订这纸的人姓甚名谁，”马珀利接着说，“不过作为主顾，他把那人认得很熟，还描画了一番。千真万确，就是巴斯维里！卡尔金说，近几个月来，巴斯维里陆续从他手里买过各式各样的文具——笔记本、誊印纸、办公用品等等。名字他始终不知道，可那人回回自己拎走所买的货，一律现钱交易，知不知道名字也就无妨。两个月前，卡尔金卖给了他十刀这种纸和配套的信封。于是——您瞧！我呢——总算确凿了，巴斯维里那处神秘的藏身之所，就是小史密斯街 56 号！”
“好——好！”海瑟维克道，“下一步呢？”
“喏，我想咱们用得上一把帮手，”马珀利笑吟吟地答道，“于是我就撇下了卡尔金——当然，先叫他赌咒保密——给伊西·戈德马克（Issy Goldmark）打了电话。干这种事儿，伊西正是那块料！伊西来了——我俩就围着那一带遛了个圈。小史密斯街，您知道吗？”
“不知道！”海瑟维克答道，“压根儿没听说过！”


===Original===
“Oh, well, but it is a street,” said Mapperley. “It lies between Great Smith Street and Tufton Street, back o’ the Church House﻿—not so far from the Abbey. Bit slummy down those quarters, round about﻿—sort of district that’s seen decidedly better days. Still, there’s good, solid houses here and there﻿—56 is one of ’em. From outside, it looks the sort of house you can’t get into﻿—dark, silent, heavily-curtained windows﻿—sort of place in which you could murder anybody on the quiet. Very substantial front door, painted dark green, with an old-fashioned brass knocker﻿—that sort of house. We took a good look at it.”
“See anything?” asked Hetherwick.
“Nothing but what I’ve told you﻿—lifeless sort o’ place,” answered Mapperley. “However, having once seen it, I wasn’t going to leave it unwatched, so I posted Issy there, in the window of a convenient public-house, and came away to telegraph to you. And there Issy is﻿—either in his pub, or loafing round. And now we ought to go and hear if he’s anything to report. And if he hasn’t﻿—what then?”
“Just so,” said Hetherwick. “That’s it﻿—what then? But before we do anything at all, Mapperley, I’d better post you up as to what’s happened elsewhere this morning. You see,” he continued, when he had finished his story, “if Matherfield’s theory is correct, and Baseverie has already gone to Southampton to collect that parcel on its arrival, and if Ambrose has gone with him, we shan’t find Baseverie at this address. But﻿—we might inquire if he’s known there.”

===Chinese===
“哦，话虽如此，那确是一条街，”马珀利说，“夹在大史密斯街（Great Smith Street）和塔夫顿街（Tufton Street）之间，背倚教会大楼（Church House）——离威斯敏斯特教堂（the Abbey）不远。那一带四下里多少有点贫民窟的味道——是那种眼见过好日子的地界。不过，零零星星也立着几所结实的好宅子，其中一所便是 56 号。打外面瞧，那是一处你休想进得去的宅子——黑黢黢，静悄悄，窗户上挂着厚厚的帘子——是那种能叫人悄没声儿谋害掉性命的地方。非常结实的大门，漆成墨绿色，装着老式的黄铜门环——就是那么一所宅子。我们上上下下端详了个够。”
“瞧出什么来了吗？”海瑟维克问。
“除了说过的那些，什么也没瞧见——一处死气沉沉的所在。”马珀利答道，“不过，既然见着了它，我就不能撒手不管，于是我把伊西安插在那儿，守着一家就近酒馆的窗口，自己脱身来给您打电报。这会儿伊西就在那儿——不是泡在酒馆里，就是在左近闲晃。眼下咱们该去听听他有没有什么可报的信儿。要是没有——那又怎么办？”
“正是，”海瑟维克说，“就是这话——那又怎么办？不过，在咱们行动之前，马珀利，我最好先把今儿早上别处的种种讲给你听，让你心里有个底。”他把经过讲完，又接着说道：“你瞧，倘若马瑟菲尔德的推断不错，巴斯维里已经赶去南安普顿，单等包裹一到就取，安布罗斯也随他去了，那咱们在这个地址就扑不着巴斯维里了。不过——咱们可以打听打听，那一带的人认不认得他。”


===Original===
Mapperley reflected a while. Then an idea seemed to suggest itself.
“Pay your bill, sir, and let’s get out to a Post Office Directory somewhere,” he said. “We’ll get the name of the occupier of 56, Little Smith Street.”
Ten minutes later they were looking down the long columns of names in a directory; Mapperley suddenly pointed to what they wanted.
“There we are!” he said. “Mrs. Hannah Mallett﻿—boardinghouse proprietor.”

===Chinese===
马珀利沉吟了一会儿。随后，像是忽然来了个主意。
“请您结了账，先生，咱们找个地方去查《邮政名录》（Post Office Directory），”他说，“查一查小史密斯街 56 号的住户姓甚名谁。”
十分钟后，两人已在名录长长的姓氏栏里逐行翻检；马珀利忽然手指一点，正是他们要找的。
“有了！”他说，“汉纳·马利特太太（Mrs. Hannah Mallett）——寄宿公寓的老板娘。”


===Original===
“Come along!” said Hetherwick. “We’ll see Mrs. Mallett, anyhow.”
But on arrival at Little Smith Street, Mapperley looked round first, for his friend, Mr. Goldmark. Mr. Goldmark materialised suddenly﻿—apparently from nowhere﻿—and smiled.
“Afternoon, mithter!” he said politely to Hetherwick. “Lovely weather, ithn’t it? Ain’t theen nothing, Mapperley, old bean! Ain’t been a thoul in or out o’ that houth, thinth you hopped it! Theemth to me it’th locked up.”
“We’ll see about that,” remarked Hetherwick. “Come with me, Mapperley. You stay here. Goldmark, and keep your eyes as open as before.”

===Chinese===
“走！”海瑟维克说，“不管怎样，咱们总得会一会这位马利特太太。”
可一到小史密斯街，马珀利先四下张望，找他的朋友戈德马克先生（Mr. Goldmark）。戈德马克先生陡然现了身——简直像凭空冒出来似的——笑吟吟的。
“午后好啊，先生！”他客客气气地对海瑟维克说，“天儿真不赖，是不？啥也没瞅见，马珀利，老伙计！打您开溜那阵儿起，那宅子进进出出连个鬼影儿都没有！照我瞅啊，那屋里八成儿是上了锁喽。”
“那咱们就瞧瞧看，”海瑟维克道，“跟我来，马珀利。你待在这儿，戈德马克，眼睛还照先前那么睁得大大的。”


===Original===
He advanced boldly, with the clerk at his heels, to the door of number 56, and knocked loudly on the stout panel, supplementing this with a ring at the bell. This dual summons was twice repeated﻿—with no result.
“Somebody coming!” whispered Mapperley, suddenly. “Bolted﻿—inside﻿—as well as locked!”
