“我也不行！——我太扎眼了，”海瑟维克笑道。“我要是矮上一头半肩的，兴许还能派点用场。不过我有现成的人——我的办事员，一个叫马珀利的。跟踪盯梢这种事，他正是那块料，跟多久也不叫人察觉。等你有机会见到他，你自己也会说，马珀利是你平生所见最普通、最不起眼的家伙——随便混进哪一群伦敦佬里头，都绝没有人会看他第二眼。可他精明透顶，在那副毫不出奇的面孔底下藏着一份过人的机敏。我的打算是这样——我要把西普里安·巴斯维里医生的模样详详细细描述给马珀利：你描述的我已经记熟了；我复述的，马珀利再记熟。巴斯维里不管他究竟是谁，多半会从这儿乘 10:10 的车下多金去；马珀利也乘那趟车。只要马珀利认准了他的对象，就再不会让他离开视线。”

“那他会去做——什么？”罗娜问。

“一路跟到多金——盯住他——再跟他回伦敦——查明他回来后去哪儿——总之，把他追个水落石出。然后他向我回报——我们就能比现在知道得多些，也知道下一步该怎么办。”

“真不知道这一切到头来会引出什么？”罗娜说。“简直是一座大迷宫，是不是？”

===Original===

“It is,” agreed Hetherwick. “But if we can only get a firm hold on a thread﻿—”

“And that might break!” she laughed.

“Well, then, one that won’t break,” he said. “There are several loose ends lying about already. Matherfield’s got a hold on one or two.”

He went to see Matherfield next morning and told him the story that he had heard from Rhona. Matherfield grew thoughtful.

===Chinese===

“正是，”海瑟维克表示同意。“可只要我们能牢牢抓住一根线头——”

“那线头兴许还会断呢！”她笑道。

“那就找一根断不了的，”他说。“眼下的线头本来就已经散着好几根了。马瑟菲尔德已经抓住了一两根。”

第二天上午，他去见马瑟菲尔德，把从罗娜那儿听来的事讲了一遍。马瑟菲尔德陷入了沉思。

===Original===

“Well, Mr. Hetherwick,” he said, after a pause, “it’s as I’ve said before﻿—if this Lady Riversreade is mixed up in it, the thing to do is to go back and get as full a history as can possibly be got of her antecedents. We’ll have to get on to that﻿—but we’ll wait to see what that clerk of yours discovers about this man. There may be something in it﻿—in the meantime I’m hard at work on my own clues.”

“Any luck?” asked Hetherwick.

“Scarcely that. But, as I say, we’re at work. The five-pound note is a difficult matter. Given in change, of course, at Vivian’s Night Club﻿—but they tell me there that it’s no uncommon thing to change ten, twenty, and even fifty-pound notes for their customers﻿—it’s a swell lot who forgather there﻿—and of course they’ve no recollection whatever about that particular note or night. Still, the fact remains﻿—that note came through Vivian’s, and through one of its frequenters, to Granett, and I’m in hopes.”

“And the medicine bottle?” suggested Hetherwick.

===Chinese===

“呃，海瑟维克先生，”他顿了顿说，“还是我从前说过的那句话——倘若这位里弗斯雷德夫人搅在这案子里，那就该回过头去，把她先前的履历尽可能查个周全。这一层我们迟早要着手去办——不过先等一等，看看你那位办事员查出这人的什么来。这里头兴许有名堂——这期间，我也在自己那几条线索上下着死功夫。”

“有进展吗？”海瑟维克问。

“谈不上。不过，正如我说的，我们都在使劲。那张五镑票子是桩难办的事。它当然是在维维安夜总会找零找出去的——可那边的人告诉我，替客人兑开十镑、二十镑甚至五十镑的大票，在他们那儿是家常便饭——聚在那儿的全是阔客——那一晚、那张票子，他们自然一点也记不得了。不过事实终归是事实——那张票子是经维维安、经那里的某个常客，到了格拉内特手里的，我还抱着指望呢。”

“那么药瓶呢？”海瑟维克提示道。

===Original===

“Ah, there is more chance!” responded Matherfield, with a lightening eye. “That’s only a question of time! I’ve got a man going round all the chemists in the West Central district﻿—stiff job, for there are more of ’em than I believed. But he’s bound to hit on the right one eventually. And then﻿—well, we shall have a pretty good idea, if not positive proof, as to how Granett got hold of the stuff that poisoned him.”

“I suppose there’s no doubt that there was poison in that bottle?” inquired Hetherwick.

“According to the specialists, none,” replied Matherfield. “And in the glass too. What sort of poison, I don’t know﻿—you know what these experts are﻿—so mysterious about things! But they have told me this﻿—the stuff that settled Granett was identical with that which finished off Hannaford. That’s certain.”

“Then it probably came from the same source,” said Hetherwick.

“Oh, my notion is that the man or men who poisoned one man poisoned the other,” exclaimed Matherfield. “And at the same time. At least, I think Granett got his dose at the same time﻿—probably carried it off in his pocket and drank it when he got home. But﻿—we shall trace that bottle! Let me know what you find out about this man Baseverie, Mr. Hetherwick﻿—every little helps.”

Hetherwick duly coached Mapperley in the part he wanted him to play, and Mapperley, with money in his pockets and a pipe in his mouth, lounged off to Victoria on the following Friday morning. His principal saw nothing and heard nothing of him all that day.

===Chinese===

“啊，那个希望就大些！”马瑟菲尔德答道，眼睛亮了起来。“那只是个时间问题！我派了一个人，把西区中央区所有的药房挨家跑一遍——活儿不轻，药店比我原先想的多。可他迟早准能撞上那家对号的。到那时——哪怕拿不到铁证，格拉内特怎么弄到那害死他的毒药，我们也就能猜个八九不离十了。”

“我想，瓶子里有毒是确定无疑了吧？”海瑟维克问道。

“据专家们说，毫无疑问，”马瑟菲尔德答道。“杯子里也有。是哪种毒，我不知道——你知道这些专家的做派——对什么都故弄玄虚！可他们告诉我这么一点——要了格拉内特命的那种东西，同结果了汉纳福德的是同一种。这是确定无疑的。”

“那多半是同一个来源，”海瑟维克说。

“噢，我的看法是，毒死一个的那个人——或那几个人——就是毒死另一个的，”马瑟菲尔德大声说。“而且同时下的手。至少，我认为格拉内特那份是在同一时间到手的——多半揣在口袋里带走，到了家才喝下去。不过——那只瓶子我们总会查出来的！关于这个巴斯维里，你查出了什么，随时告诉我，海瑟维克先生——点滴线索都是助力。”

海瑟维克循例把要他扮演的角色细细教给了马珀利。于是到了下礼拜五早上，马珀利兜里揣着钱，嘴里叼着烟斗，晃晃悠悠地往维多利亚车站去了。他的东家一整天没见到他的人影，也没听到他一点音信。

## XXI The Order in Writing / 二十一 书面指令

===Original===
Hetherwick realised at once that Mapperley had news, and was waiting there to communicate it. But he looked not so much at Mapperley as at Mapperley’s companion. Mapperley, as Hetherwick had remarked to more than one person in the course of those proceedings, concealed his sharpness under an unusually commonplace exterior; he looked, as a rule, like a young man whose ideas rarely soared above a low level. But the Jew was of a different aspect﻿—Hetherwick was not quite sure whether he was rat or ferret. There was subtlety and craft written all over him, from his bright beady eyes to his long, thin, dirty fingers, and before Mapperley spoke his employer felt sure that in this son of Israel the clerk had found a valuable associate.

“Hullo, Mapperley!” exclaimed Hetherwick. “Waiting for me? You’ve some news, I suppose?”

Mapperley, grave and formal, pointed a finger at the Jew.

“Mr. Isidore Goldmark, sir,” he said. “Friend of mine. I got him to give me a bit of assistance in this Baseverie and Vivian affair. The fact is, sir, he knows Vivian’s﻿—don’t you, Issy?”
===Chinese===
海瑟维克（Hetherwick）立刻看出马珀利（Mapperley）带来了消息，正候在那里等着向他转达。不过他的目光落在马珀利身上的少，落在马珀利那位同伴身上的多。正如海瑟维克在此案进程中不止对一个人评论过的那样，马珀利把自己的精明藏在一副异乎寻常的平庸外表底下；他平日瞧上去，活像个心思难得高出地平面多少的年轻人。可那犹太人却是另一副气象——海瑟维克吃不准他究竟是老鼠还是白鼬。此人从头到脚都写着机巧与狡诈，从那双亮闪闪的圆眼珠到那十根又长又细的脏手指；马珀利还没开口，他的东家就已认定：在这位以色列子孙身上，办事员觅得了一位可贵的帮手。

“喂，马珀利！”海瑟维克扬声道。“在这儿等我？我猜你是带着消息来的吧？”

马珀利神色郑重、一本正经，伸手指了指那犹太人。

“这位是伊西多·戈德马克（Isidore Goldmark）先生，先生，”他说。“我的一位朋友。巴斯维里和维维安这档子事，我拉他来帮了点忙。实情是，先生，他熟门熟路，认得维维安——你说是不是，伊西？”

===Original===
“Thome!” replied Mr. Goldmark, with a grin.

“And he knows Baseverie, too,” continued Mapperley. “By sight, anyhow. So I got him﻿—for a consideration﻿—to watch for Baseverie’s next appearance on that scene, and then, when he did come, to keep an eye on him﻿—trick him, in fact. And Issy’s seen him tonight, Mr. Hetherwick, and followed him. Then Issy came to me, and I brought him here.”

“Good!” said Hetherwick. “Sit down, both of you, and I’ll hear about it.” He dropped into his own easy chair and again regarding the Jew decided that he was probably a creditable witness. “What do you do at Vivian’s?” he asked. “Employed there?”

Mr. Goldmark glanced at Mapperley and smiled knowingly. Mapperley nodded.
===Chinese===
“熟着哩！”戈德马克先生咧嘴一笑，答道。

“他还认得巴斯维里，”马珀利接着说。“至少认得那张脸。于是我出了点酬劳，请他守在那地方，等巴斯维里下次露面；等他真来了，就盯住他——说白了，就是耍着他玩。伊西今儿晚上果真见着他了，海瑟维克先生，还跟了他一程。随后伊西来找我，我就把他领到这儿来了。”

“好！”海瑟维克说。“你们俩都坐，说来我听听。”他一屁股坐进自己的安乐椅，再把那犹太人打量一遍，认定此人多半算个可信的证人。“你在维维安做些什么？”他问。“在那儿当差？”

戈德马克先生瞟了马珀利一眼，会心地笑了。马珀利点了点头。

===Original===
“All confidential, Issy,” he said reassuringly. “Going no further.”

“Of course this is all confidential﻿—and secret,” remarked Hetherwick. “I only want to know the precise connection between Vivian’s and Mr. Goldmark.”

“It’th a thort of themi-official, mithter,” answered the Jew. “The fact ith, I do a bit o’ commith’on work for Vivian’th cuthtomerth, turf you know. Though﻿—I’m in and out of an evening. Thee?”

“I see,” said Hetherwick. “All right! And you know Baseverie?”
===Chinese===
“一律保密，伊西，”他宽慰道。“出了这屋子，一个字也不会走。”

“当然，这全是机密——还是绝密，”海瑟维克说。“我只想弄明白，维维安与戈德马克先生之间究竟是何等关系。”

“算是半个挂名的自己人吧，先生，”犹太人答道。“实情是，我替维维安的客人们跑跑马注、抽点佣金——您懂的。所以嘛——一到晚上我就在那儿里里外外。明白不？”

“明白了，”海瑟维克说。“好！那么你认得巴斯维里？”

===Original===
“Ath well ath I know my own nothe,” replied Mr. Goldmark.

“How long have you known him?”

“Thome time.”

“Do you know what he is?”

“Aint an idea, mithter﻿—and noboody elthe that I knowth of! Liv’th on hith wit’th, I should thay, if you athk me. Wrong ’un!”
===Chinese===
“就跟认得我自个儿的鼻子一样熟，”戈德马克先生答道。

“认得他多久了？”

“有些日子喽。”

“知道他是干什么的吗？”

“一点影儿都没有，先生——就我所知，别人也没一个知道的！照我看哪，您要问我，他是靠耍心眼子过活的主儿。不是个好东西！”

===Original===
“Nor where he lives?”

“No, mithter! All I knowth ith that he come’th to Vivian’th﻿—now and then.”

“And you saw him tonight?”

“I did, mithter﻿—tonight ath ever wath!”

“What time was that?”

“About eight o’clock, mithter﻿—near ath I can fix it.”
===Chinese===
“也不知道他住在哪儿？”

“不知道，先生！我只晓得他隔三差五上维维安去——时不时露个面。”

“今晚你见着他了？”

“见着啦，先生——千真万确，就是今儿晚上！”

“几点钟的事？”

“八点来钟，先生——我估摸着是这个点儿。”

===Original===
“Well, what happened?”

“Thith, mithter. He came in about eight, ath I thay. I wath there, doing a bit o’ bithneth with another cuthmur. Batheverie, he didn’t thtop. He wathn’t in the plathe three minuteth, and while he wath in he theemed﻿—to me﻿—to be a bit fidgety﻿—thuthpithious, like. Looked round and about﻿—cautiouth. Then he went﻿—and I followed him. According to inthructionth from Mapperley there.”

“Where did he go?”

“Well, mithter, I’ll give you the particularth﻿—in full: when I theth out on a job o’ that thort I do it proper. He turned out o’ Candlethtick Pathage into the Lane, and he had a drink at a bar there. Then he went to Trafalgar Square Tube. I wath clothe behind him when he booked﻿—”
===Chinese===
“好，出了什么事？”

“是这么回事，先生。他八点来钟进的门，跟我说的对得上。我那会儿正在那儿，跟另一位主顾谈着一桩小买卖。巴斯维里呢，脚不停。他在那地方前后没待满三分钟，待着的当口，瞧着——在我眼里——有点儿心神不宁——像是起了疑心似的。东张西望——提防着什么。随后他就走了——我就跟了上去。这可是遵照那边马珀利的吩咐行事。”

“他去了哪儿？”

“得嘞，先生，我把细情一五一十全报给您：我揽下这种差事，向来办得妥妥帖帖。他出了烛台巷拐进圣马丁巷，在巷里一家酒吧喝了口酒。然后他去了特拉法加广场地铁站。他买票的时候，我就紧跟在他身后——”

===Original===
“A moment. Does he know you?”

“May jutht know me by thite, mithter, but not enough to exthite any thuthpithion in hith mind if he thaw me there behind him. I never had no truck with him﻿—never thpoke to him.”

“Well, go on. Where did he book to?”

“Warwick Avenue, mithter. Tho did I﻿—of courth. When we got there, I followed him out﻿—at a thafe dithtance. He turned down to the Canal, crothed the bridge, and went down to Thant Mary’th Manthion’th. And there he went in.”
===Chinese===
“慢着。他认得你吗？”

“兴许照面能认出我这个人，先生，可认不到那个份儿上——我在他后头跟着，也犯不着叫他心里犯疑。我跟他素来没有瓜葛——话都没说过一句。”

“好，接着说。他买的到哪站的票？”

“沃里克大道（Warwick Avenue），先生。我也买了一张——那还用说。到了站，我跟着他出来——隔着一段稳妥的距离。他转身往运河那边去，过了桥，一路走到圣玛丽公寓。得，他就进去了。”

===Original===
Hetherwick glanced at Mapperley. Mapperley permitted himself to wink at his employer﻿—respectfully, but knowingly.

“Went into St. Mary’s Mansions, eh?” said Hetherwick. “Walked straight in?”

“Straight in, mithter﻿—front entranth. I thee him, from acroth the road, talking to the man in livery﻿—porter or whatever he hith. I could thee through the glath doorth. Then I thee both of ’em go up in the lift. Tho I waited about a bit, jutht to thee if he’d come out. He did.”

“Soon?” asked Hetherwick.

“He wath inthide about ten minuteth. Then he came out. Alone. Thith time he went in t’other direction. I followed him acroth Paddington Green to Edgware Road Tube, and there﻿—well, to tell you the truth, mithter, there I lotht him! There wath a lot o’ people about, and I made thure he’d be going thouth. But he mutht ha’ gone wetht. Anyway, I lotht him altogether.”
===Chinese===
海瑟维克瞥了马珀利一眼。马珀利冲着东家挤了挤眼——毕恭毕敬，却心照不宣。

“进的是圣玛丽公寓，嗯？”海瑟维克说。“径直走进去的？”

“径直进的，先生——走正门。我隔着马路，瞧见他跟那个穿号衣的家伙说话——门房还是什么角色吧。隔着玻璃门我都看得真真的。接着我就瞧见他们俩一块儿上了电梯。于是我在附近转悠了一阵，就为瞧瞧他出来不出来。他出来了。”

“没等多久？”海瑟维克问。

“他在里头待了约莫十分钟。然后就出来了。就他一个人。这回他朝另一头走。我跟着他穿过帕丁顿绿地，到了埃奇韦尔路地铁站，可在那儿——唉，跟您说实话吧，先生，就在那儿我把他跟丢啦！那会儿四下里人多得乌泱乌泱，我拿准了他是往南去。可他准是往西去了。反正，我彻底把他跟丢了。”

===Original===
“Well﻿—I think you saw enough to be of help,” said Hetherwick. “Now﻿—just keep this to yourself, Goldmark.” He motioned Mapperley into another room, gave him money for his assistant, and waited until the Jew had gone, shown out by the clerk. “Eleven o’clock!” he remarked, glancing at his watch as Mapperley came back. “Mapperley! we’re going out﻿—to St. Mary’s Mansions. And after we’ve been there, and made a call, you’d better come back here with me and take a shakedown for the night﻿—I shall want you in the morning, unless I’m mistaken.”

It was one of Mapperley’s chief virtues that he was always ready to go anywhere and do anything, and he at once accompanied Hetherwick to the top of Middle Temple Lane, found a taxicab within five minutes, and proposed himself to sit up and shakedown that night and the next, if necessary.

“Scent’s getting hot, I think, sir,” he remarked as they drove off, after bidding the driver carry them to Paddington Green. “Things seem to be coming to a head.”

“Yes﻿—but I don’t think you know everything,” answered Hetherwick. He proceeded to give the clerk an epitomised account of the day’s doings as they had related to himself, concluding with Matherfield’s theory as expressed after leaving the Green Archer. “You’re a smart chap, Mapperley,” he added. “What do you think?”
===Chinese===
“唔——我想你瞧见的这些已经够帮上忙了，”海瑟维克说。“还有——这件事只许烂在你自己肚里，戈德马克。”他示意马珀利到另一个房间去，给了他一笔钱，作为他那位帮手的酬劳，随后一直等到那犹太人由办事员送出门去。“十一点了！”马珀利回来时，他看了一眼表，说道。“马珀利！咱们这就出去——去圣玛丽公寓。到过那儿、再作一处的拜访之后，你顶好跟我回这儿来，胡乱搭个铺过夜——要是我没弄错，明儿早上我还用得着你。”

马珀利顶要紧的美德之一，便是随叫随到、什么事都肯干。他当即陪海瑟维克走到中殿律师学院巷（Middle Temple Lane）巷口，不出五分钟就寻着一辆出租车，还主动提出：如有必要，今晚明晚他都可以在此守夜搭铺。

开动之前，他吩咐司机送他们去帕丁顿绿地；车子一动身，他便议论道：“我看哪，这踪迹是越追越热了，先生。事情像是快要见分晓喽。”

“是热了——不过我想，事情的原委你还没全知道，”海瑟维克答道。接着他便把这一天里落到自己头上的种种情由，择要向办事员讲了一遍，末了说到离开绿箭手酒馆之后马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）表述的那番推想。“你是个机灵人，马珀利，”他补了一句。“你怎么看？”

===Original===
“I see Matherfield’s point,” answered Mapperley. “I can follow his line. He thinks like this: Hannaford, when he came to London, wanted to get rid, advantageously, of that formula of his about a new ink. He got into touch with Ambrose, whom, of course, he’d known before at Sellithwaite. Ambrose introduced him to some men who deal or dabble in chemicals, of whom one, no doubt, is Baseverie, and who seem to have a laboratory or something of that sort somewhere in the Westminster district. On the night of the murder Ambrose met Hannaford, by appointment, at Victoria, and took him there. Probably, Hannaford left the sealed packet﻿—opened by that time﻿—with these fellows. Probably, too, while there he told them﻿—jokingly, very likely﻿—what he’d discovered, from the picture in the papers, about the identity of Mrs. Whittingham and Madame Listorelle. And now comes in﻿—Granett!”

Hetherwick gave an exclamation that denoted two or three things﻿—surprise, for one.

“Ah!” he said. “Granett! To be sure! I’d forgotten Granett!”

“I hadn’t,” remarked Mapperley with a cynical laugh. “Granett﻿—and his murder﻿—is an essential factor. What I think is this: We know that Hannaford met Ambrose at Victoria Station that all-important evening. Ambrose, without doubt, took him to the place I hinted at just now﻿—the exact location of which is a mystery. I think Hannaford stopped there until late in the evening. But﻿—I also think he went back again! With﻿—Granett!”
===Chinese===
“马瑟菲尔德的用意我明白，”马珀利答道。“他那条思路我跟得上。他是这么想的：汉纳福德（Hannaford）到伦敦来的时候，想把他那份新墨水配方脱手个好价钱。他联络上了安布罗斯——此人在塞利斯韦特他自然早就认识。安布罗斯把他引荐给几个做化工买卖、或是沾着化工边儿的人物，其中一个毫无疑问便是巴斯维里；这伙人似乎在威斯敏斯特一带的什么地方，有座实验室之类的处所。凶案当晚，安布罗斯依约在维多利亚接上汉纳福德，把他带到了那儿。多半，汉纳福德把那个原本封口的纸包——那时节已经拆开了——留在了这伙人手里。多半，他还在那儿顺口说起——十有八九是当笑谈讲的——自己从报上照片里看出的、惠廷厄姆太太与里斯托雷尔太太实为一人的发现。这下子——格拉内特（Granett）就登场了！”

海瑟维克一声惊叹，里头含着两三层意思——头一层便是惊讶。

“啊！”他说。“格拉内特！可不是嘛！我把格拉内特给忘了！”

“我可没忘，”马珀利冷笑着说。“格拉内特——连同他那条人命——是少不了的一环。我是这么想的：我们知道，在那个干系重大的傍晚，汉纳福德在维多利亚车站跟安布罗斯碰了头。安布罗斯无疑把他带到了我方才暗指的那个地方——那地方的确切所在还是个谜。我想汉纳福德在那儿一直待到夜里很晚。可是——我还想，他后来又折回去了！带着——格拉内特！”

===Original===
“Ah!” exclaimed Hetherwick. “I see!”

“We know,” continued Mapperley, “that Granett went that evening to see the chemist who gave information about him; we know, too, that he and the chemist went and had a drink together, and parted at about closing time, Granett then, according to the chemist, going towards Victoria Street. Now I think that Granett then met Hannaford﻿—accidentally. They’d known each other in Sellithwaite. They talked﻿—Granett told Hannaford he was down on his luck. Hannaford, evidently, was a kindhearted man, and I think he did two things out of kindness for Granett. He gave him that five-pound note﻿—”

“That was got at Vivian’s!” interrupted Hetherwick quickly.

“To be sure!” assented Mapperley. “But we know that Hannaford had been at Vivian’s﻿—with Baseverie﻿—undoubtedly. Taken there by Baseverie, which makes me certain that for two or three days before his death he’d been in touch with both Baseverie and Ambrose. Hannaford got that fiver in change at Vivian’s. And he gave it to Granett, on hearing his story. But he did something else﻿—something that was far more important﻿—that is far more important﻿—to us!”
===Chinese===
“啊！”海瑟维克失声道。“我明白了！”

“我们知道，”马珀利接着说，“格拉内特那天傍晚去找过那位后来告发他行迹的药剂师；我们还知道，他和药剂师一同去喝了杯酒，约莫在打烊时分分的手，据药剂师说，格拉内特随后是朝维多利亚街方向去的。如今我琢磨着，格拉内特正是在那时撞见了汉纳福德——纯属偶然。他们俩在塞利斯韦特就相识。两人聊了起来——格拉内特对汉纳福德诉苦，说自己走了背运。汉纳福德分明是个心慈面软的人，我想他出于好心，为格拉内特做了两件事。他给了他那张五镑钞票——”

“那张钞票就是在维维安找的零！”海瑟维克急忙插话道。

“可不是！”马珀利附和道。“不过我们知道，汉纳福德去过维维安——跟巴斯维里一道——这是板上钉钉的。是巴斯维里带他去的；这就让我断定，他死前那两三天里，与巴斯维里和安布罗斯两方都有往来。那张五镑票子是汉纳福德在维维安找零得来的。听了格拉内特的境遇，他就把票子给了他。可他还做了一件事——一件要紧得多的事——一件对咱们而言要紧得多的事！”

===Original===
“What?” asked Hetherwick.

“He turned back to the place he’d just left, and took Granett with him!” answered Mapperley with confidence. “He knew Granett was a trained and qualified chemist; he thought he could get him a job with these men who, presumably, were going to take up his own invention. It would be little more than half-past ten then. Where else than at this place are Hannaford and Granett likely to have been between that time and the time at which they got into your carriage at St. James’s Park? Of course they were there﻿—with Ambrose and Baseverie.”

“As you put it﻿—highly probable,” said Hetherwick. “Two and a half hours﻿—doing what?”

“Ah, now we come to the real thing!” exclaimed Mapperley. “My own belief is that Hannaford was fatally poisoned when he left those two men the first time! They’d two objects in poisoning him﻿—or, to put it another way, he’d entrusted them with two secrets﻿—one about Madame Listorelle; the other about his invention. They wanted to keep both to themselves and to profit by both. The invention, no doubt, has considerable value﻿—Hannaford believed it had, anyway. They thought they could blackmail Madame and her sister, Lady Riversreade. So, before Hannaford left them the first time, they poisoned him﻿—cleverly, subtly, devilishly﻿—knowing that so many hours would elapse before the poison worked, and that by that time he’d be safe in bed at his hotel and would die in his sleep. But﻿—he went back to them again, and took another man with him! So﻿—that man had to die, too!”
===Chinese===
“什么事？”海瑟维克问。

“他转身回到方才离开的地方，还把格拉内特一并带了去！”马珀利笃定地答道。“他知道格拉内特是科班出身、有执照的药剂师；他想，在这些多半要接手他那项发明的人手下，没准能给格拉内特谋个差事。那时节顶多十点半过一点儿。从那个时辰，到他们在圣詹姆斯公园上了你马车的那一阵，汉纳福德和格拉内特不在那地方还能在哪儿？他们当然在那儿——跟安布罗斯和巴斯维里在一块儿。”

“照你这么讲——极有可能，”海瑟维克说。“两个半钟头——都在干些什么？”

“啊，这可就说到要害了！”马珀利扬声道。“依我看，汉纳福德头一回离开那两个人时，就已经吃下了致命的毒！他们毒他，有两重目的——或者说，他向他们托付了两桩秘密——一桩关乎里斯托雷尔太太；另一桩关乎他的发明。他们要把两桩都攥在自己手里，靠两桩都渔利。那项发明无疑相当值钱——反正汉纳福德是信它值钱的。他们又觉得，可以拿这个去讹诈那位太太和她的姐姐里弗斯雷德夫人。于是，趁汉纳福德头一回动身之前，他们给他下了毒——下得巧，下得阴，下得歹毒——算准了毒性发作还要隔上好几个钟头，到那时他早已稳稳当当躺进旅馆的床上，会在睡梦里死去。可是——他后来又回他们那儿去了，还带了另一个男人同去！于是——那个男人也就非死不可了！”

===Original===
Hetherwick thought awhile in silence.

“All very good theory, Mapperley,” he said at last. “But﻿—it may be nothing but theory. Why did Granett run off at Charing Cross?”

“Because Granett knew that Ambrose lived in John Street, close by,” replied Mapperley with promptitude. “He may have known it before; he may not have known it until that evening. But﻿—he knew it! Most likely he thought that Ambrose had returned home from the place in Westminster: Ambrose may have left there before Hannaford and Granett did. Anyway, we may be reasonably certain that when Granett left you with the dying or dead man, he ran off to Ambrose’s flat﻿—a few minutes away.”

“Why didn’t he come back?” demanded Hetherwick. “I’m only wanting to get at probabilities.”
===Chinese===
海瑟维克默然想了一会儿。

“这一套推得都好，马珀利，”他终于开口道。“可是——它没准儿也就止于推想。格拉内特为什么在查令十字撒腿就跑？”

“因为格拉内特知道安布罗斯就住在近旁的约翰街（John Street），”马珀利应声答道。“这个他兴许早就晓得，兴许到那天晚上才晓得。可反正——他是晓得的！十有八九，他是想着安布罗斯已经从威斯敏斯特那地方回了家：安布罗斯没准儿比汉纳福德和格拉内特先一步离开那儿。无论如何，咱们可以有相当的把握断言：格拉内特把你和那个奄奄一息——或者已经咽气——的人撂下之后，撒腿奔安布罗斯的寓所去了——不过几分钟的路。”

“那他怎么不回来呢？”海瑟维克追问。“我不过是想把事情往或然处靠一靠。”

===Original===
“I’ve thought of that, too,” replied Mapperley. “I think he found Ambrose out. But by that time he’d had time to reflect. He knew something was wrong. He knew that if he went back, he’d find the police there, and would be questioned. He might be suspected. And so﻿—he went home, with the bottle in which Ambrose had given him a drop of whisky for himself. And﻿—died in his sleep, as they thought Hannaford would.”

“Why should Ambrose have that bottle down at Westminster?” asked Hetherwick.

“Why shouldn’t he?” retorted Mapperley. “A man who’s taking a tonic takes it at least three times a day﻿—regularly. He’d have his bottle with him. Probably there are several similar empty bottles there at that place.”

“Where is that place?” exclaimed Hetherwick. “Where?”

“Got to be found,” said Mapperley, as the cab came to a stand. “But﻿—here’s this!”
===Chinese===
“这一层我也想到了，”马珀利答道。“我想他扑了个空，没见着安布罗斯。可到那时，他也有了掂量的工夫。他知道事情不对头了。他知道，要是回去，准会撞见警察在那儿，就得受盘问。他说不定要被疑上。于是——他就回家了，兜里揣着那只瓶子——安布罗斯先前往里头倒过一口给他自用的威士忌。然后——在睡梦里死去，正如他们指望汉纳福德会死的那样。”

“安布罗斯干吗要把那瓶子带到威斯敏斯特去呢？”海瑟维克问。

“怎么就不能带？”马珀利反诘道。“一个正吃着补药的人，一天至少得服三回——顿顿不落，一顿不误。他自然随身带着药瓶。那地方多半还撂着好几只一模一样的空瓶子呢。”

“那地方到底在哪儿？”海瑟维克提高嗓门道。“在哪儿？”

“总得把它找出来，”车子停稳的当口，马珀利说道。“不过——眼前有这个！”

===Original===
Hetherwick led his companion across Paddington Green and to the house from which he and Matherfield had watched the flats opposite. Late as it was, the lodging-house keeper was up, and lent a willing ear to Hetherwick’s request that he should go with him to his friend the caretaker of the Mansions. That functionary was at supper. He continued to sup as Hetherwick, morally supported by the lodging-house man, explained matters to him, but at last he allowed his cheek to bulge with unswallowed food and turned a surprised and knowing eye on his principal visitor.

“Blamed if I didn’t wonder whether it was all OK with that chap!” he exclaimed, banging the table with the haft of his knife. “For all he was quite the gentleman, I somehow suspicioned him! And yet, he’d a straight tale to tell: come here on Madame’s behalf, to get something for her out of her rooms, had her keys, and give me a note from her saying as how I was to allow the bearer to go up to her flat! What more could I expect﻿—and what could I do﻿—under the circs? I asks yer!”

“Oh, he had a note, had he?” inquired Hetherwick. “In Madame’s writing?”
===Chinese===
海瑟维克领着同伴穿过帕丁顿绿地，来到他先前和马瑟菲尔德监视对面公寓所用的那所房子。夜虽然深了，那房东还没有歇下，听海瑟维克求他陪自己去见他那位管理公寓的朋友，便慨然应允。那位管理员正用着晚饭。海瑟维克——有房东在旁给他壮胆——向他说明来意时，他照吃不误；可到了末了，他腮帮子鼓起一口没咽下去的饭，把一只又惊又会意的眼睛，转向了这位主要的来客。

“嘿，我当时还真犯过嘀咕，拿不准那位爷是不是全然正路！”他嚷道，拿刀柄砰砰地敲桌子。“别看他一副十足的绅士派头，我不知怎么就是有点儿疑心他！可他又有套说得圆的来历：说是替太太上这儿来，替她从屋里取些东西，带着她的钥匙，还拿出她的一张条子给我，说上面交代了，要我准许来人上她的公寓去！那种情形之下，我还能指望什么——又能怎么办呢？你说说看！”

“哦，他有一张条子，是吗？”海瑟维克问道。“是太太的亲笔？”

===Original===
The caretaker laid down his knife, and thrusting his hand in his breast-pocket, drew forth an envelope and silently handed it over. It was an azure-tinted envelope, of a very good quality of paper, such as is only sold in high-class stationery shops, and the sheet inside matched it in tint and quality. But Hetherwick at once noticed something about that sheet; so, too, did Mapperley, peering at it from behind his elbow. About an inch and a half had been rather roughly cut off at the top; obviously some address had been engraved, or embossed, or printed on the missing portion. As for what was written on the sheet, it was little﻿—a simple order that the caretaker should allow bearer to go into Madame Listorelle’s flat.

“You recognised that as Madame’s handwriting?” suggested Hetherwick.

“Oh, that’s her fist, right enough, that is!” replied the caretaker. “I knew it at once. And no wonder! I ain’t no scholard, not me!﻿—but I knows enough to know that it ’ud puzzle one o’ them here forgers as ye reads about to imitate that there sort o’ writing﻿—more like as if it had been done with a wooden skewer than a Christian pen! Oh, that’s hers.”
===Chinese===
管理员放下刀子，把手伸进胸前口袋，掏出一只信封，一言不发地递了过来。那是一只天蓝色的信封，纸质极佳，唯有高档文具店才出售这等货色；里头的信笺，色泽与质地也正与信封相称。可海瑟维克一眼便注意到那信笺上有些蹊跷；从他胳膊肘后头窥看的马珀利，也同样看在了眼里。信笺顶端约有一英寸半，被相当毛糙地裁了去；显见得，缺掉的那一截上原先刻着、压着或者印着某处地址。至于笺面上写的字，倒寥寥无几——不过一道简单的手令：命管理员准许持函人进入里斯托雷尔太太的公寓。

“你认出那是太太的笔迹了？”海瑟维克提示道。

“可不是，那就是她的笔道儿，错不了，就是她！”管理员答道。“我一眼就认了出来。也难怪！我不是什么有学问的人，我可不是！——可我也懂得：就是你们在报上念到的那种造假文书的主儿，要摹仿那一路字也得抓瞎——那字与其说出自一支正经笔，倒不如说像拿根木头签子戳出来的！错不了，是她的。”

===Original===
Hetherwick handed the letter and envelope to Mapperley, who was holding out a hand.

“Well,” he said. “I wish ye’d just let me have a look into Madame’s flat. There’s something seriously wrong, and﻿—”

“Oh, you can do that﻿—’long as I’m with you,” said the caretaker readily. He rose and led the way to the left, and presently ushered them into a smart flat and turned on the electric light. “Don’t see nothing wrong here,” he observed. “The chap wasn’t here ten minutes, and he carried nothing heavy away, whatever he had in his pockets.”

Hetherwick and Mapperley looked round. Everything seemed correct and in order﻿—the surroundings were those of a refined and artistic woman, obviously one who loved order and system. But on a desk that stood in the centre of the sitting-room a drawer had been pulled open, and in front of it lay scattered a few sheets of Madame Listorelle’s private notepaper, with her engraved address and crest. Near by lay some envelopes, similarly marked. And with a sudden idea in his mind, Hetherwick picked up a sheet or two of the paper and a couple of envelopes and put them in his pocket.
===Chinese===
海瑟维克把信和信封递给了正伸着手来接的马珀利。

“那么，”他说。“劳驾让我进太太的公寓瞧上一眼。事情大大地不妙，而且——”

“哦，这个使得——只要我陪着你们，”管理员爽快地说。他起身在前引路向左，不多时便领他们走进一套漂亮的公寓，打开了电灯。“这儿瞧不出什么不对劲，”他说。“那位爷在这儿没待上十分钟，不管他兜里揣了什么，沉家伙可一件也没扛走。”

海瑟维克和马珀利环视四周。样样东西似乎都妥帖齐整——这里是一位娴雅而有艺术趣味的太太的居处，一看便知是个讲究条理章法的人。只是起居室正中那张书桌的一只抽屉被拉开了，抽屉跟前散落着几张里斯托雷尔太太的私人信笺，上面印着她的地址和纹章。近旁还搁着几只信封，也是这般标记的。海瑟维克心头忽然生出一念，便捡起一两张信笺、两只信封，揣进了口袋。

===Original===
A few minutes later, once more in the cab which they had kept waiting, and on the way to Hill Street, whither Hetherwick had bidden the driver go next, Mapperley turned to his employer with a sly laugh, and held up something in the light of a street lamp by which they were passing.

“What’s that?” asked Hetherwick.

“The order written by Madame Listorelle,” answered Mapperley, chuckling. “The caretaker didn’t notice that I carried it off, envelope and all, under his very eyes! But I did﻿—and here it is!”

“What do you want to do with it?” demanded Hetherwick. “What’s your notion?”

But Mapperley only chuckled again and without giving any answer restored the azure-tinted envelope and its contents to his pocket.
===Chinese===
几分钟后，两人重新坐上那辆一直候着的出租车，往希尔街去——那是海瑟维克吩咐司机去的下一站。途中，马珀利狡黠地笑着转向东家，趁车子经过一盏路灯，把手里的东西举到了灯光底下。

“那是什么？”海瑟维克问。

“里斯托雷尔太太亲笔的那道手令呗，”马珀利抿着嘴笑道。“管理员愣是没瞧见我把它连信封带信笺一并顺走了——就在他眼皮子底下！可我干成了——喏，在这儿！”

“你要拿它做什么？”海瑟维克追问。“你打的什么主意？”

可马珀利只是又抿嘴一笑，并不答话，把那只天蓝色信封连同里面的东西，放回了口袋。

## V The Police Return / 五 警方归来

===Original===
Hetherwick now began to arrive at something like an understanding of a matter that had puzzled him ever since and also at the time of the conversation between Hannaford and his companion in the train. He had noted then that whatever it was that Hannaford was telling, he was telling it as a man tells a story against himself; there had been signs of amused chagrin and discomfiture in his manner. Now he saw why.

“Ah!” he exclaimed. “She was one too many for him. Then?”

“A good many times too many!” laughed Hollis. “She did Hannaford completely. He strove hard to find her, and did a great deal of the spadework himself. And at last he ran her down﻿—in a fashionable hotel in London. He had a Scotland Yard man with him, and a detective from our own police-office here, a man named Gandham, who is still in the force﻿—I’ll introduce you to him tomorrow. Hannaford, finding that Mrs. Whittingham had a suite of rooms in this hotel﻿—a big West End place﻿—left his two men downstairs, or outside, and went up to see her alone. According to his own account, she was highly indignant at any suspicions being cast upon her, and still more so, rose to a pitch of most virtuous indignation when he told her that he’d got a warrant for her arrest and that she’d have to go with him. During a brief interchange of remarks she declared that if her bankers at Manchester had returned her cheque unpaid it must have been merely because they hadn’t realised certain valuable securities which she’d sent to them, and that if Malladale had presented his cheque a few days later it would have been all right. Now, that was all bosh!﻿—Hannaford, of course, had been in communication with the bankers; all they knew of the lady was that she had opened an account with them while staying at some hotel in Manchester, and that she had drawn all but a few pounds of her balance the very day on which she had got the necklace from Malladale and fled with it from Sellithwaite. Naturally, Hannaford didn’t tell her this﻿—he merely reiterated his demand that she should go with him. She assented at once, only stipulating that there should be no fuss﻿—she would walk out of the hotel with him, and he and his satellites could come back and search her belongings at their leisure. Then Hannaford﻿—who, between you and me, Hetherwick, had an eye for a pretty woman!﻿—made his mistake. Her bedroom opened out of the sitting-room in which he’d had his interview with her; he was fool enough to let her go into it alone, to get ready to go with him. She went﻿—and that was the very last Hannaford ever saw of her!”

“Made a lightning exit, eh?” remarked Hetherwick.
===Chinese===
海瑟维克（Hetherwick）此刻才算对一桩事情有了几分领悟——打从汉纳福德（Hannaford）在火车上同旅伴交谈的时候起，这桩事便一直令他纳闷，当时也是如此。他那时便留了心：不论汉纳福德讲的是什么，他讲起来，就像一个人在讲拆自己台的旧事；举止之间，分明带着几分又觉好笑、又觉懊恼的狼狈。如今他看出缘故来了。

“啊！”他失声道，“原来他到底斗不过她。后来呢？”

“岂止斗不过，还叫她占了好几回上风呢！”霍利斯（Hollis）笑道，“她把汉纳福德耍了个透。他费尽心机寻她，许多跑腿掘地的苦工都是亲手做的。末了总算把她寻着了——在伦敦一家时髦旅馆里。他随身带着一名苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的人，还有咱们本地警察局的一名侦探，名叫甘达姆（Gandham），至今还在当差——明天我引见你。汉纳福德打听得惠廷厄姆太太（Mrs. Whittingham）在这家旅馆里——西区一家大店——占着一套房，便把两名手下留在楼下，或者说门外，独自上楼去见她。据他自己讲，她一听有人把疑心落到她头上，已是十二分恼怒；待到他告知手中握有拘捕她的拘票、她必须随他前去，她愈发义愤填膺，一副贞节凛然的做派。短短几句话交锋之间，她声言：若说她在曼彻斯特（Manchester）的往来银行退了她的支票，那必定只因他们未曾把她送去托收的几笔值钱证券变现；又说马拉代尔（Malladale）那张支票若迟几日提示，本可安然无事。哼，这全是瞎话！——汉纳福德当然早就同那银行通过信息；他们对于这位太太所知的一切，无非是她在曼彻斯特某家旅馆小住时开了个户头，以及在从马拉代尔手中取得项链、携赃逃离塞利斯韦特（Sellithwaite）的当天，便把存项提取得只剩几镑。汉纳福德自然没有当面戳破——只再三重申她必须随他走。她一口应承，唯独约定不许张扬——她亲自随他步出旅馆，他与他的随从尽可折回头，从从容容搜检她的行李什物。于是汉纳福德——你我私下说一句，海瑟维克，他见了俊俏女人是有眼睛的！——便铸成了大错。她的卧室通着他与她会谈的那间起居室；他竟糊涂到容她独自入内更衣整装。她进去了——从此汉纳福德再没见过她一面！”

“来了个闪电开溜，嗯？”海瑟维克说道。

===Original===
“She must have gone instantly,” asserted Hollis. “A door opened from the bedroom into a corridor﻿—she must have picked up hat and coat and walked straight away, leaving everything she had there. Anyway, when Hannaford, tired of waiting, knocked at the door and looked in, his bird was flown. Then, of course, there was a hue-and-cry, and a fine revelation. But she’d got clear away, probably by some side door or other exit, and although Hannaford, according to his own account, raked London with a comb for her, she was never found. Vanished!”

“And the necklace?” inquired Hetherwick.

“That had vanished too,” replied Hollis. “They searched her trunks and things, but they found nothing but clothing. Whatever she had in the way of money and valuables she’d carried off. And so Hannaford came home, considerably down in the mouth, and he had to stand a good deal of chaff. And if he found this woman’s picture in a recent paper﻿—well, small wonder that he did cut it out! I should say he was probably going to set Scotland Yard on her track!﻿—for, of course, there’s no time-limit to criminal proceedings.”

“This is the picture he cut out,” observed Hetherwick, producing it from his pocketbook. “But you say you never saw the woman?”
===Chinese===
“她必定是即时便走的，”霍利斯断言道，“卧室另有一道门通着走廊——她准是顺手抄起帽子外套，径自去了，把身边的东西撂了个精光。总之，等到汉纳福德候得不耐烦，叩门张看时，他那只鸟儿早已飞了。于是自然是一场缉捕，一场好大的曝光。可她已脱身远去，多半走的什么边门或旁的出口；虽说据汉纳福德自己讲，他把伦敦像篦头发似的篦了一遍，她却始终没有寻见。人间蒸发了！”

“那么项链呢？”海瑟维克问道。

“也一同蒸发了，”霍利斯答道，“他们搜了她的箱笼什物，除了衣裳一无所获。但凡钱财珠宝，她都随身裹挟而去。于是汉纳福德回了家，好不没精打采，还着实捱了不少取笑。他既然在近期的一份报上见到这女人的相片——嗯，他把相片剪了下来，何足为奇！依我说，他多半是要叫苏格兰场去追她的踪迹！——要知道，刑事诉讼原没有时效的限制。”

“这就是他剪下的那张相片，”海瑟维克说着，从皮夹里取了出来。“可你说，你从未见过那女人？”

===Original===
“No, I never saw her,” assented Hollis, examining the print with interested curiosity. “So, of course, I can’t recognise this. Handsome woman! But you meet me at my office﻿—close by﻿—tomorrow morning, at ten, and I’ll take you to our police-station. Gandham will know!”

Gandham, an elderly man with a sphinx-like manner and watchful eyes, laughed sardonically when Hollis explained Hetherwick’s business. He laughed again when Hetherwick showed him the print.

“Oh, aye, that’s the lady!” he exclaimed. “Not changed much, neither! Egad, she was a smart ’un, that, Mr. Hollis!﻿—I often laugh when I think how she did Hannaford! But you know, Hannaford was a softhearted man. At these little affairs, he was always for sparing people’s feelings. All very well﻿—but he had to pay for trying to spare hers! Aye, that’s her! We have a portrait of her here, you know.”
===Chinese===
“没有，我从未见过她，”霍利斯应道，一面饶有兴味地端详那张印片，“所以我自然无从辨认。好一位标致妇人！明天早上十点，你到我的事务所来——就在左近——我带你上我们的警察局去。甘达姆准知道！”

甘达姆是个上了年纪的人，一副斯芬克斯式的莫测神气，一双眼时刻留神。霍利斯道明海瑟维克的来事，他讪笑了几声；海瑟维克把印片给他看，他又笑了。

“哦，可不，正是这位太太！”他叫道，“也没见怎么变样儿！乖乖，那可真是个精明绝顶的人儿，霍利斯先生！——想起她怎么摆布汉纳福德的，我如今还时常要笑呢。不过你是知道的，汉纳福德是个软心肠的人。遇上这等小小事端，他总存心给人留脸面。留脸面原也不错——可为着顾全她的脸面，他到底吃了亏！可不，就是她！咱们这儿还存着她一幅画像呢，你是知道的。”

===Original===
“You have, eh?” exclaimed Hetherwick. “I should like to see it.”

“You can see it with pleasure, sir,” replied the detective. “And look at it as long as you like.” He turned to a desk close by and produced a big album, full of portraits with written particulars beneath them. “This is not, strictly speaking, a police photo,” he continued. “It’s not one that we took ourselves, ye understand﻿—we never had the chance! No!﻿—but when my lady was staying at the White Bear, she had her portrait taken by Wintring, the photographer, in Silver Street, and Wintring was that suited with it that he put it in his window. So, of course, when her ladyship popped off with Malladale’s necklace, we got one of those portraits, and added it to our little collection. Here it is!﻿—and you’ll not notice so much difference between it and that you’ve got in your hand, sir.”

There was very little difference between the two photographs, and Hetherwick said so. And presently he went away from the police-office wondering more than ever about the woman with whose past adventures he was concerning himself.
===Chinese===
“你们存着，嗯？”海瑟维克叫道，“我倒想见识见识。”

“请尽管看，先生，”侦探答道，“愿意看多久看多久。”他转身到近旁一张书案前，取出一本大册子，里面贴满画像，每幅底下都附着手写的履历。“严格说起来，这不算一张警务照相，”他接着道，“不是我们自己拍得的，你老明白——我们始终不曾有过机会！不是！——不过那位太太下榻白熊旅馆（White Bear）期间，曾请银街（Silver Street）的照相师温特林（Wintring）造了一幅小照，温特林中意得很，把它陈进了橱窗。于是呢，待她老人家携着马拉代尔的项链拔腿开溜，我们便取到一幅那样的画像，添进了我们这份小小的收藏。就在这儿！——你瞧瞧它同你手里那幅，先生，出入不很大吧。”

两幅照相果然没有什么出入，海瑟维克也这般说了。随后他辞出警局，心里对那个女人越发疑讶起来——他此刻关切的，正是她往年的那些行藏。

===Original===
“May as well do the thing thoroughly while you’re about it,” remarked Hollis, as they walked off. “Come and see Malladale﻿—his shop is only round the corner. Not that he can tell you much more than I’ve told you already.”

But Malladale proved himself able to tell a great deal more. A grave, elderly man, presiding over an establishment which Hetherwick, unaccustomed to the opulence of provincial manufacturing towns, was astonished to find outside London, he ushered his visitor into a private room, and listened to the reasons they gave for calling on him. After a close and careful inspection of the print which Hetherwick put before him, he handed it back with a confident nod.

“There is no doubt whatever﻿—in my mind﻿—that that is a print from a photograph of the woman I knew as the Honourable Mrs. Whittingham,” he said. “And if it has been taken recently, she has altered very little during the ten years that have elapsed since she was here in this town.”
===Chinese===
“既办就办个彻底，”两人步出时霍利斯说道，“去见见马拉代尔吧——他的铺子只在此间拐角上。倒不是说他还能告诉你多少我未曾讲过的。”

不想马拉代尔能讲的远不止此。他是个端重的老人，主持着一爿字号——海瑟维克不惯见外省工商市镇的豪阔，想不到竟会在伦敦城外遇见——他把来客让进一间私室，听他们陈明登门的缘由。他把海瑟维克摆到面前的印片凑近端详、仔细验看一番，然后递还回去，胸有成竹地点一点头。

“毫无疑义——在我心里——这就是一幅照相的印片，相片上那个女人，我当年识得时，人称尊敬的惠廷厄姆太太（the Honourable Mrs. Whittingham），”他说道，“倘若相片是近期所摄，那么自她来本镇至今，隔了这十年，她也几乎没有改样。”

===Original===
“You’d be glad to see her again, Mr. Malladale﻿—in the flesh?” laughed Hollis.

The jeweller shook his head.

“I think not,” he answered. “No, I think not, Mr. Hollis. That’s an episode which I had put out of my mind﻿—until you recalled it.”

“But﻿—your loss?” suggested Hollis. “Close on four thousand pounds, wasn’t it?”
===Chinese===
“你乐得再见她一面吧，马拉代尔先生——见她活生生的本人？”霍利斯笑道。

珠宝商摇了摇头。

“我看不想，”他答道，“不想，霍利斯先生。那是一段旧事，我本已撇出脑海——直到你们又提起。”

“可是——你的损失呢？”霍利斯提点道，“将近四千镑，是不是？”

===Original===
Mr. Malladale raised one of his white hands to his grey beard and coughed. It was a cough that suggested discretion, confidence, secrecy. He smiled behind his moustache, and his spectacled eyes seemed to twinkle.

“I think I may venture a little disclosure﻿—in the company of two gentlemen learned in the law,” he said. “To a solicitor whom I know very well, and to a barrister introduced by him, I think I may reveal a little secret﻿—between ourselves and to go no further. The fact of this matter is, gentlemen﻿—I had no loss!”

“What?” exclaimed Hollis. “No﻿—loss?”

“Eventually,” replied the jeweller. “Eventually! Indeed, to tell you the truth plain, I made my profit, and﻿—er, something over.”
===Chinese===
马拉代尔先生抬起一只白净的手，抚上花白胡须，咳了一声。那一声咳嗽，透着审慎、信托与守密的意味。他在髭须后面含笑，戴眼镜的那双眼睛似乎熠熠地眨。

“我想我不妨小小吐露一点——当着两位深于法律的先生的面，”他说，“对一位我深为熟识的律师，同他引荐的一位大律师，我想我可以揭一个小小的秘密——只在你我之间，不得外传。这桩事的实情是，两位先生——我并没有损失！”

“什么？”霍利斯叫道，“没——损失？”

“到头来没有，”珠宝商答道，“到头来没有！老实对你说了吧，我不但没有赔，反倒得了利，而且——呃，还有一点富余。”

===Original===
Hollis looked his bewilderment.

“Do you mean that﻿—eventually﻿—you were paid?” he asked.

“Precisely! Eventually﻿—after a considerable interval﻿—I was paid,” replied Mr. Malladale. “I will tell you the circumstances. It is, I believe, common knowledge that I sold the diamond necklace to Mrs. Whittingham for three thousand, nine hundred pounds, and that the cheque she gave me was dishonoured, and that she cleared off with the goods and was never heard of after she escaped from Hannaford. Well, two years ago, that is to say, eight years after her disappearance, I one day received a letter which bore the New York postmark. It contained a sheet of notepaper on which were a few words and a few figures. But I have that now, and I’ll show it to you.”

Going to a safe in the corner of his parlour, the jeweller, after some searching, produced a paper and laid it before his visitors. Hetherwick examined it with curiosity. There was no name, no address, no date; all that appeared was, as Malladale had remarked, a few words, a few figures, typewritten:﻿—
===Chinese===
霍利斯满脸的迷惑。

“你是说——到头来——有人把钱还给你了？”他问。

“正是！到头来——隔了相当一段时候——钱到了我手，”马拉代尔先生答道，“我把原委讲给你们听。那条钻石项链，我作价三千九百镑卖与惠廷厄姆太太；她开给我的支票横遭退票；她携货遁去，自脱出汉纳福德掌心之后再无音讯——这些，我想已是人尽皆知。喏，两年以前，就是说，在她失踪八年之后，我有一天收到一封信，盖着纽约（New York）的邮戳。信内只有一页便笺，笺上寥寥数语、几个数码。那张纸我如今还留着，这就拿给你们过目。”

珠宝商走到客堂角落的保险柜前，翻检了一阵，取出一张纸来，摆在两位来客面前。海瑟维克好奇地察看。纸上无名姓，无住址，无日期；如马拉代尔所言，所有者无非寥寥数语、几个数码，打字机打的：

===Original===
> Principal
> £3,900
> 8 years’ Interest @ 5%
> £1,560
> £5,460
> Draft £5,460 enclosed herein: kindly acknowledge in *London Times*.
===Chinese===
> 本金

> 3,900 镑

> 8 年利息，按 5% 计

> 1,560 镑

> 5,460 镑

> 兹随函附上 5,460 镑汇票一纸；请在*伦敦《泰晤士报》*上惠予见复。

===Original===
“Enclosed, as is there said, was a draft on a London bank for the specified amount,” continued Mr. Malladale. “£5,460! You may easily believe that at first I could scarcely understand this: I knew of no one in New York who owed me money. But the first figures﻿—£3,900﻿—threw light on the matter﻿—I suddenly remembered Mrs. Whittingham and my lost necklace. Then I saw through the thing﻿—evidently Mrs. Whittingham had become prosperous, wealthy, and she was honest enough to make amends; there was my principal, and eight years’ interest on it. Yet, I felt somewhat doubtful about taking it﻿—I didn’t know whether I mightn’t be compounding a felony? You gentlemen, of course, will appreciate my little difficulty?”

“Um!” remarked Hollis in a noncommittal tone. “The more interesting matter is﻿—what did you do? Though I think we already know,” he added with a smile.

“Well, I went to see Hannaford, and told him what I had received,” answered the jeweller. “And Hannaford said precisely what I expected him to say. He said ‘Put the money in your pocket, Malladale, and say nothing about it!’ So﻿—I did!”

“Each of you feeling pretty certain that Mrs. Whittingham was not likely to show her face in Sellithwaite again, no doubt!” observed Hollis. “Very interesting, Mr. Malladale. But it strikes me that whether she ever comes to Sellithwaite again or not, Mrs. Whittingham, or whatever her name may be nowadays, is in England.”
===Chinese===
“如笺上所云，随函附来一纸伦敦银行的汇票，票面即如所列之数，”马拉代尔先生接着说，“ 5,460 镑！你们不难相信，起初我简直摸不着头脑：我在纽约并不认得亏欠我钱的人。可是开头的几个数码—— 3,900 镑——点破了这一层——我蓦地想起惠廷厄姆太太和我那条失去的项链。随即我便看穿了内里——显然，惠廷厄姆太太境况兴旺，发了财，而且良心未泯，肯来补偿；本金在此，外加八年的利息。只是接下这笔钱，我心中不无踌躇——我拿不准，这样是否算私和重罪？两位先生自然体谅我这一点小小的难处吧？”

“唔！”霍利斯不置可否地说，“更有趣的题目是——你怎么料理的？不过我想我们已经知道了，”他含笑补了一句。

“喏，我去见汉纳福德，把收到的东西告诉了他，”珠宝商答道，“汉纳福德说的恰是我料定他要说的。他说：‘把钱揣进口袋，马拉代尔，只字休提！’于是——我照办了！”

“你们两位，八成都拿定惠廷厄姆太太再不会回塞利斯韦特来露面了吧！”霍利斯说道，“十分有趣，马拉代尔先生。不过我看，她回不回塞利斯韦特且不管，这位惠廷厄姆太太——无论她如今姓甚名谁——人可就在英国。”

===Original===
“You think so?” asked the jeweller.

“Her picture’s recently appeared in an English paper, anyway,” said Hollis.

“But pictures of famous American ladies appear in English newspapers,” suggested Mr. Malladale. “I have recollections of several. Now my notion is that Mrs. Whittingham, who was a very handsome and very charming woman, eventually went across the Atlantic and married an American millionaire! That’s how I figured it. And I have often wondered who she is now.”

“That’s precisely what I want to find out,” said Hetherwick. “One thing is certain﻿—Hannaford knew! If he’d been alive he could have told us. Because in whatever paper it was that this print appeared there would be some letterpress about it, giving the name, and why it appeared at all.”
===Chinese===
“你这样看？”珠宝商问。

“反正，她的相片新近在一份英国报纸上露过面，”霍利斯说。

“可是英国报纸上，本来也登有名的美国太太们的相片呀，”马拉代尔先生提醒道，“我记得的就颇有几幅。依我的想头，惠廷厄姆太太——一位十分标致、十分迷人的女人——到头来横渡了大西洋，嫁了一位美国的百万富翁！我便是这般推想的。我还常常好奇，她如今究竟是谁。”

“我要查明的恰恰就是这个，”海瑟维克说，“有一件事是确定的——汉纳福德知道！他若在世，本可以讲给我们听。因为这张印片当初不论登在哪份报纸上，相片总配着一段文字，载明姓名，并说明刊登的缘由。”

===Original===
“You can trace that,” remarked Hollis.

“Just so,” agreed Hetherwick, “and I may as well get back to town and begin the job. But I think with Mr. Hollis,” he added, turning to the jeweller, “I believe that the woman is here in England: I think it possible, too, that Hannaford knew where. And I don’t think it impossible that between the time of his cutting out her picture from the paper and the time of his sudden death he came in touch with her.”

“You think it probable that she, in some way, had something to do with his murder﻿—if it was murder?” asked Mr. Malladale.

“I think it possible,” replied Hetherwick. “There are strange features in the case. One of the strangest is this. Why, when Hannaford cut out that picture, for his own purposes, evidently with no intention of showing it to anyone else, did he cut it out without the name and letterpress which must have been under and over it?”
===Chinese===
“那个查究得出，”霍利斯说道。

“正是，”海瑟维克附和道，“我不如这就回城动手。不过我赞同霍利斯先生的意见，”他转向珠宝商添了一句，“我信这女人如今就在英国：我还想，汉纳福德兴许知道她身在何处。再者，从他把她的相片从报上剪下，到他猝然身故，这中间他与她搭上过线，这在我也并非绝无可能。”

“那么你认为，她多少同他的被害有干系——倘若那算得上谋害的话？”马拉代尔先生问道。

“我认为有此可能，”海瑟维克答道，“这案子有几处奇处。最奇的要数这一处：汉纳福德剪下那张相片，既为着自己的用途，分明无意给任何人看，何以剪的时候，把必然印在相片上下方的姓名与文字一并剪去不要？”

===Original===
“Queer, certainly!” said Hollis. “But, you know, you can soon ascertain what that name was. All you’ve got to do is to get another copy of the paper.”

“Unfortunately, Hannaford’s granddaughter doesn’t know what particular paper it was,” replied Hetherwick. “Her sole recollection of it is that it was some local newspaper, sent to Hannaford by post, the very morning that he left here for London.”

“Still﻿—it can be traced,” said Hollis. “It was in some paper﻿—and there’ll be other copies.”

Presently he and Hetherwick left the jeweller’s shop. Outside, Hollis led his companion across the street, and turned into a narrow alley.

“I’ll show you a man who’ll remember Mrs. Whittingham better than anybody in Sellithwaite,” he said, with a laugh. “Better even than Malladale. I told you she stayed at the White Bear when she was here? Well, since then the entire staff of that eminent hostelry has been changed, from the manager to the boots﻿—I don’t think there’s a man or woman there who was there ten years ago. But there’s a man at the end of this passage who was formerly hall-porter at the White Bear﻿—Amblet Hudson﻿—and who now keeps a rather cosy little saloon-bar down here: we’ll drop in on him. He’s what we call a bit of a character, and if you can get him to talk, he’s usually worth listening to.”
===Chinese===
“蹊跷，的确！”霍利斯说，“不过你要知道，那名字是什么，你很快便能查明。你只须另觅一份那份报纸。”

“不巧，汉纳福德的孙女不知那究竟是哪一份报，”海瑟维克答道，“她对它仅有的记忆是：那是某份地方报纸，恰在汉纳福德由此地动身赴伦敦的那个早晨，经邮局寄到他的手里。”

“话虽如此——总查究得出，”霍利斯说，“它总归登在某份报上——旁的本子总还有。”

少顷，他同海瑟维克离了珠宝铺。出到街上，霍利斯领着同伴横过马路，拐进一条窄巷。

“我领你去见一个人，他记惠廷厄姆太太，比塞利斯韦特的谁都牢，”他笑道，“比马拉代尔还牢。我告诉过你，她当年在此下榻白熊旅馆吧？喏，打那以后，那家显赫客栈的上下人等换了个遍，从掌柜到擦靴的杂役——我看如今那里的男人女人，没有一个是十年前的旧人。可这条巷子尽头有一个人，从前在白熊旅馆当门房——阿姆布利特·赫德森（Amblet Hudson）——如今在此间开着一家相当惬意的小雅座酒馆：咱们进去望望他。此人便是我们所谓有点儿脾性的角色，你若能引得他开口，他的话通常都值得一听。”

## III The Potential Fortune / 三 潜在的财富

===Original===
Elder and younger woman alike took Matherfield’s intimation quietly. Rhona made no remark. But Mrs. Keeley spoke impulsively.
“There never was a more popular man than he was﻿—with everybody!” she exclaimed. “Who should want to take his life?”
“That’s just what we’ve got to find out, ma’am,” said Matherfield. “And I want to know as much as I can﻿—I dare say Miss Hannaford can tell me a lot. Now, let’s see what we do know from what you told me this morning. Mr. Hannaford had been Superintendent of Police at Sellithwaite for some years. He had recently retired on his pension. He proposed to live in London, and you and he, Miss Hannaford, came to London to look for a suitable house, arrived three days ago, and put up at this hotel. That’s all correct? Very good﻿—now then, let me hear all about his movements during the last three days. What did he do? Where did he spend his time?”
“I can’t tell you much,” answered Rhona. “He was out most of the day, and generally by himself. I was only out with him twice﻿—once when we went to do some shopping, another time when we called on Mr. Kenthwaite at his rooms in the Temple. I understood he was looking for a house﻿—seeing house agents and so on. He was out morning, afternoon and evening.”
“Did he never tell you anything about where he’d been, or whom he’d seen?”
“No. He was the sort of man who keeps things to himself. I have no idea where he went nor whom he saw.”
===Chinese===
老少两个女人听了马瑟菲尔德这话，都显得很平静。罗娜没有说什么，基利太太却按捺不住，冲口开了腔。
“再没有比他更得人心的人了——对谁都是一样！”她叫起来。“谁会想要他的命呢？”
“我们要查的正是这个，太太，”马瑟菲尔德说。“我想尽量多了解些情况——我看汉纳福德小姐能告诉我不少。来，先把今早你们告诉我的话对一对。汉纳福德先生在塞利斯韦特当了几年警司，不久前领了退休金退了职。他打算在伦敦定居，于是您和他——汉纳福德小姐——来伦敦找一处合适的房子，三天前到的，落脚在这家旅馆。都对吧？很好——那么，把这三天他的行踪都讲给我听。他都做了些什么？时间都花在哪儿了？”
“我讲不出多少，”罗娜答道。“他白天多半在外头，而且总是独自一人。我统共只跟他出去过两回——一回是去买些东西，另一回是去内殿肯思韦特先生的寓所拜访。据我了解，他是在找房子——见见房产经纪人什么的。他上午、下午、晚上都出去。”
“他从来没跟您说起过他去了哪儿、见过什么人吗？”
“没有。他是那种什么事都闷在自己心里的人。他去了哪儿、见了谁，我一点儿也不知道。”

===Original===
“Didn’t say anything about where he was going last night?”
“No. He only said that he was going out and that I should find him here when I got back from the theatre, to which I was going with Mrs. Keeley. We got back here soon after eleven. But he hadn’t come in﻿—as you know.”
“You never heard him speak of having enemies?”
“I should think he hadn’t an enemy in the world! He was a very kind man and very popular, even with the people he had to deal with as a police-superintendent.”
“And I suppose he’d no financial worries﻿—anything of that sort? Nor any other troubles﻿—nothing to bother him?”
“I don’t think he’d a care in the world,” said Rhona confidently. “He was looking forward with real zest to settling down in London. And as to financial worries, he’d none. He was well off.”
===Chinese===
“他没说过昨晚要去哪儿吗？”
“没说。他只说要出去一趟，说我和基利太太看完戏回来，在这儿准能找到他——我就是和基利太太一块儿去看戏的。我们十一点刚过就回到了这儿。可他还没进来——这您是知道的。”
“您从没听他说起过有什么仇人吧？”
“我看他在这个世上一个仇人也没有！他是个非常和善的人，人缘好得很——就连他当警司时不得不管束的那班人，也都喜欢他。”
“那么钱上头他也没有什么愁事——没有那一类的事吧？别的烦恼也没有——没有什么让他牵心的事？”
“我看他天大的心事也没有一个，”罗娜笃定地说。“他正满心起劲地盼着在伦敦安顿下来呢。至于钱上的愁事，他一点儿也没有。他手头宽裕。”

===Original===
“Always a saving, careful man,” remarked Mrs. Keeley. “Oh, yes, quite well off﻿—apart from his pension.”
Matherfield glanced at Hetherwick, who had listened carefully to all that was asked and answered. Something in the glance seemed to invite him to take a hand.
“This occurs to me,” said Hetherwick. He turned to Rhona. “Apart from this house-hunting, do you know whether your grandfather had any business affair in hand in London? What I’m thinking of is this﻿—from what I saw of him in the train, he appeared to be an active, energetic man, not the sort of man who, because he’d retired, would sit down in absolute idleness. Do you know of anything that he thought of undertaking﻿—any business he thought of joining?”
Rhona considered this question for a while.
“Not any business,” she replied at last. “But there is something that may have to do with what you suggest. My grandfather had a hobby. He experimented in his spare time.”
“What in?” asked Hetherwick. Then he suddenly remembered the stained fingers that he had noticed on the hands of both men the night before. “Was it chemicals?” he added quickly.
===Chinese===
“他向来是个省吃俭用、仔细小心的人，”基利太太说。“哦，是啊，相当宽裕——这还是不算他那笔退休金呢。”
马瑟菲尔德朝海瑟维克瞥了一眼；后者对这一问一答始终仔细听着。这一眼里似乎带着几分意思，是请他也来插一手。
“我想到一点，”海瑟维克说。他转向罗娜。“撇开找房子这件事，您知不知道您祖父在伦敦手上还有什么正经事务？我是这么想的——就我在火车上对他的观察，他是个积极主动、精力充沛的人，不是那种因为退了休便安坐下来、彻底闲住的人。您知不知道他想过要着手做什么——想过要投身什么营生没有？”
罗娜把这个问题斟酌了一会儿。
“没有什么营生，”她终于答道。“不过有一件事，也许同您说的这点沾边。我祖父有一样癖好，闲下来就做实验。”
“做什么方面的？”海瑟维克问。随即他猛地想起前一天晚上在两个人手上注意到的那些染色指头。“是化学吗？”他赶紧补了一句。

===Original===
“Yes, in chemicals,” she answered with a look of surprise. “How did you know that?”
“I noticed that his hands and fingers were stained,” replied Hetherwick. “So were those of the man he was with. Well﻿—but this something?”
“He had a little laboratory in our garden at Sellithwaite,” she continued. “He spent all his spare time in it﻿—he’d done that for years. Lately, I know, he’d been trying to invent or discover something﻿—I don’t know what. But just before we left Sellithwaite, he told me that he’d solved the problem, and when he was sorting out and packing up his papers he showed me a sealed envelope in which he said were the particulars of his big discovery﻿—he said there was a potential fortune in it and that he should die a rich man. I saw him put that envelope in a pocketbook which he always carried with him.”
“That would be the pocketbook I examined last night,” said Matherfield. “There was no sealed envelope, nor one of which any seal had been broken, in that. There was nothing but letters, receipts and unimportant papers.”
“It is not in his other pocketbooks,” declared Rhona. “I went through all his things myself very early this morning﻿—through everything that he had here. I know that he had that envelope yesterday﻿—he pulled out some things from his pocket when we were lunching with Mr. Kenthwaite in a restaurant in Fleet Street, and I saw the envelope. It was a stout, square envelope, across the front of which he had drawn two thick red lines, and it was heavily sealed with black sealing-wax at the back.”
“That was yesterday, you say?” asked Matherfield sharply. “Yesterday noon? Just so! Then as he had it yesterday at noon, and as it wasn’t in his pockets last night and is not among his effects in this house, it’s very clear that between, say, two o’clock yesterday and midnight he parted with it. Now then, to whom? That’s a thing we’ve just got to find out! But you’re sure he wasn’t joking when he told you that this discovery, or invention, or whatever it was, was worth a potential fortune?”
===Chinese===
“是的，化学方面，”她答道，面露惊讶。“您怎么知道的？”
“我注意到他的手上、指头上染着颜色，”海瑟维克答道。“跟他在一起的那个人也是如此。哦——您刚才说的那件事是？”
“他在塞利斯韦特我们家花园里有个小实验室，”她接着说。“他把全部空余时间都花在那上面——这样有好些年了。我知道，近来他一直在试着发明或者发现点什么——我不知道是什么。可就在我们离开塞利斯韦特之前，他告诉我，说他把那个难题解决了；他整理收拾文件的时候，还给我看过一只封了口的信封，说里面装着他那项重大发现的详情——他说那里头是一笔潜在的财富，说他终归能以富家翁的身份了此一生。我亲眼看见他把那只信封放进一只皮夹子，那夹子他一向随身带着。”
“那想必就是我昨晚检查过的那只皮夹子，”马瑟菲尔德说。“里面没有封着口的信封，也没有封过口又拆开了的。除了信件、收据和几张无关紧要的纸片，什么也没有。”
“他别的皮夹子里也没有，”罗娜斩然说道。“今天一大早，我亲手把他所有的东西都翻过了一遍——凡是他在这里的物件，件件翻遍。我知道他昨天还带着那只信封——那天我们和肯思韦特先生在舰队街（Fleet Street）一家饭馆吃午饭，他从口袋里往外掏东西，我看见了那只信封。那是只厚实的方形信封，正面横着画了两道粗红线，背面用黑火漆封得严严实实。”
“您是说，那是昨天？”马瑟菲尔德尖锐地问。“昨天中午？正是如此！那么，昨天中午他还带着它，昨晚他口袋里却没有，这幢房子里的遗物中也没有，这就很清楚了：大约就在昨天两点到午夜之间，他把它脱手了。那么，给了谁呢？这正是我们非查个水落石出不可的事！不过，他告诉您这项发现——或者发明，或者随便是什么——值一笔潜在的财富的时候，您敢肯定他不是说笑？”

===Original===
“On the contrary, he was very serious,” replied Rhona. “Unusually serious for him. He wouldn’t tell me what it was, nor give me any particulars﻿—all he said was that he’d solved a problem and hit on a discovery that he’d worked over for years, and that the secret was in that envelope and worth no end of money. I asked him what he meant by no end of money and he said: ‘Well, at any rate, a hundred thousand pounds﻿—in time.’ ”
The two men exchanged glances; silence fell on the whole group.
“Oh!” said Matherfield at last. “A secret worth a hundred thousand pounds﻿—in time. This will have to be looked into﻿—narrowly. What do you think, Mr. Hetherwick?”
“Yes,” answered Hetherwick. “You’ve no idea, of course, as to whether your grandfather had done anything about putting this discovery on the market﻿—or made any arrangement about selling it? No! Well, can you tell me this: What sort of house did your grandfather want to rent here in London? I mean, do you know what rent he was prepared to pay?”
“I can answer that,” remarked Mrs. Keeley. “He told me he wanted a good house﻿—a real good one﻿—in a convenient suburb, and he was willing to go up to three hundred a year.”
“Three hundred a year,” said Hetherwick. He exchanged a meaning glance with Matherfield. “That,” he added, “looks as if he felt assured of a considerable income, and as though he had already realised on his discovery or was very certain of doing so.”
===Chinese===
“恰恰相反，他非常认真，”罗娜答道。“认真得出乎寻常。他不肯告诉我那是什么，也不肯透露任何详情——他只说，他解开了一道难题，得到了一样钻研多年的发现，秘密就在那只信封里，值数不尽的钱。我问他数不尽的钱是什么意思，他说：‘呃，至少十万英镑——只是迟早的事。’”
两个男人对视了一眼；一屋子人都沉默下来。
“哦！”马瑟菲尔德终于开口。“一个值十万英镑的秘密——只是迟早的事。这件事非查个明白不可——仔仔细细地查。您看呢，海瑟维克先生？”
“是啊，”海瑟维克答道。“当然，您不知道您祖父有没有为把这项发现推向市场做过什么——或者作过什么出售的安排吧？不知道！那么，您能告诉我这一点吗：您祖父想在伦敦租一幢什么样的房子？我是说，您知道他打算出多少租金吗？”
“这个我答得上，”基利太太说。“他跟我说过，他要一幢好房子——真正的好房子——坐落在近便的郊区，一年出到三百镑他也愿意。”
“一年三百镑，”海瑟维克说。他同马瑟菲尔德交换了一个意味深长的眼色。“这个数目，”他补了一句，“看上去就像他笃定有一笔可观的进项，仿佛那项发现他已经兑现成了钱，再不然也稳有把握兑现。”

===Original===
“To be sure,” agreed Matherfield. “Of course, I don’t know what his private means were, but I know what his retiring pension would be﻿—and three hundred a year for rent alone means﻿—a good deal! Um!﻿—we’ll have to endeavour to trace that sealed envelope.”
“It seems to me, Matherfield,” observed Hetherwick, “that the first thing to do is to trace Hannaford’s movements last night, from the time he left this hotel until his death in the train.”
“We’re at that already,” replied Matherfield. “We’ve a small army of men at work. But as we want all the help we can get, I’m going to stir up the newspaper men, Mr. Hetherwick﻿—the Press, sir, is always valuable in this sort of thing!﻿—and I want Miss Hannaford, if she’s got one, to give me a recent photograph of her grandfather so that it can appear in the papers. Somebody, you know, may recognise it﻿—somebody who saw him last night with somebody else.”
Rhona had a new photograph of the dead man, taken in plain clothes just before he left Sellithwaite, and she gave Matherfield some copies of it. Reproductions appeared in the *Meteor* and other evening papers that night, and in some of the dailies next morning. And, as a result, a man came forward at the inquest, a few hours later, who declared with positive assurance that he had seen Hannaford early in the evening of the murder. His appearance was the only sensational thing about these necessarily only preliminary proceedings before the coroner; until he stepped forward nothing had transpired with which Hetherwick was not already familiar. There had been his own evidence; somewhat to his surprise neither coroner nor police seemed to pay much attention to his account of the conversation about the woman’s portrait; they appeared to regard Hannaford’s observations as a bit of garrulous reminiscence about some criminal or other. There had been Rhona’s﻿—a repetition of what she had told Matherfield and Hetherwick at Malter’s Hotel: police and coroner evidently fixed on the missing sealed envelope and its mysterious secret as a highly important factor in the case. Then there had been the expert testimony of the two doctors as to the cause of death﻿—that had been confined to positive declarations that Hannaford died from the administration of some subtle poison, the exact details being left over until experts could tell more at the adjourned proceedings. And the coroner was about to adjourn for a fortnight when a man, who had entered the court and been in conversation with the officials, was put into the witness-box to tell a story which certainly added information and, at the same time, accentuated mystery.
This man was a highly-respectable person in appearance, middle-aged, giving the name of Martin Charles Ledbitter, manager of an insurance office in Westminster, and residing at Sutton, in Surrey. It was his habit, he said, to travel every evening from Victoria to Sutton by the 7:20 train. As a rule he arrived at Victoria just before seven and took a cup of tea in the refreshment-room. He did this on the night before last. While he was drinking his tea at the counter, an elderly man came in and stood by him, whom he was sure beyond doubt was the same man whose photograph was reproduced in some of last night’s and some of this morning’s newspapers. He had no doubt whatever about this. He first noticed the man’s stained fingers as he took up the glass of whisky-and-soda which he had ordered; he had, at the time, wondered at the contrast between those fingers and the general spick-and-spanness of the man and his smart attire; also he had noticed his gold-headed walking-cane and that the head was fashioned like a crown. They stood side by side for some minutes, then the man went out. A minute or two later he saw him again﻿—this time at the right-hand side bookstall; he was there obviously looking out for somebody.
===Chinese===
“可不是，”马瑟菲尔德附和道。“我自然不知道他私人有多少财产，可我知道他那份退休金有多少——光房租一年就出到三百，这意味着——手面相当阔！嗯！——我们得设法去追那只封口的信封了。”
“依我看，马瑟菲尔德，”海瑟维克说道，“头一件事就是把汉纳福德昨晚的行踪查个清楚——从他离开这家旅馆起，直到死在火车上为止。”
“这我们已经在办了，”马瑟菲尔德答道。“我们有一小队人马在跑。不过，帮手是越多越好，所以我要去鼓动一下报馆里的人了，海瑟维克先生——报界，先生，在这类事情上向来顶用！——另外，我想请汉纳福德小姐给我一张她祖父的近照，如果她有的话，好登到报上去。您知道，兴许有人认得出他——兴许昨晚有人瞧见他同另外什么人待在一起。”
罗娜手头有死者的一张便装新照，是他离开塞利斯韦特前不久照的；她给了马瑟菲尔德几张复本。当晚，《流星报》（*Meteor*）和其他几家晚报就登出了这张照片的复制版，第二天早上又有几家日报刊出。结果，几个小时之后，验尸审讯上便有一个男人站了出来，一口咬定、确信无疑地说，凶案发生那天傍晚早些时候他见过汉纳福德。此人的出场，是这场本来就只能是初步程序的验尸审讯中唯一轰动的事；在他站出来之前，披露出来的一切，没有一样是海瑟维克不早已熟知的。有他本人的证词；让他有点诧异的是，无论验尸官还是警方，对他讲的那段关于女人画像的谈话似乎都没怎么放在心上；他们看来只把汉纳福德那些话当作对某个罪犯之类的絮絮叨叨的追忆。有罗娜的证词——不过是把她在马尔特旅馆对马瑟菲尔德和海瑟维克说过的话重述了一遍：警方和验尸官显然都盯住了那只失踪的封口信封和它那神秘的秘密，把它当作本案一个极其重要的因素。然后是两位医生关于死因的专家证言——内容只限于明确断言汉纳福德死于某种隐秘毒药的下毒，确切细节留待复审时专家们进一步查明。而验尸官正要宣布休庭两星期，一个男人——先前走进法庭、同官员们交谈过的那个——被传进证人席，要讲一段固然增添了情况、却又同时加深了疑团的话。
此人外表极其体面，中年模样，报的姓名是马丁·查尔斯·莱德比特（Martin Charles Ledbitter），威斯敏斯特一家保险公司的经理，住在萨里郡的萨顿（Sutton）。他说，他习惯每天傍晚从维多利亚乘 7:20 的火车回萨顿。照例他七点前一点到维多利亚，在茶点室里喝上一杯茶。前天晚上他也是这么做的。他正在柜台边喝茶，一个上了年纪的男人走进来，站在他身旁——他毫不怀疑，此人正是昨晚和今早几家报纸上刊出照片的那个人。这一点他毫无疑义。他最先注意到那人的手指染着颜色，是在那人端起自己叫的那杯威士忌苏打的时候；当时他还暗暗诧异，那几根手指同那人浑身上下干净齐整的气派、同他那身漂亮的衣装，反差何其之大；他还注意到那人的一根金头手杖，杖头打造成一顶王冠的样式。两人并肩站了几分钟，随后那人走了出去。一两分钟后他又见到了他——这回是在右手边的书报摊旁；他站在那里，分明是在等什么人。

===Original===
This was the point where the interest really began; everybody in court strained eyes and ears as the coroner put a direct question.
“Looking out for somebody? Did you see him meet anybody?”
“I did!”
“Tell me what you saw.”
“I saw this. When I approached the bookstall, to buy some evening papers, the man whom I had seen in the refreshment-room was standing close by. He was looking about him, but chiefly at the entrances to the big space between the offices and the platforms. Once or twice he looked at his watch. It was then﻿—by the station clock﻿—about ten minutes past seven. He seemed impatient; he moved restlessly about. I passed him and went to the bookstall. When I turned round again he was standing a few yards away, shaking hands with another man. From the way in which they shook hands, I concluded that they were old friends, who perhaps had not seen each other for some time.”
===Chinese===
真正有看头的地方就从这里开始；验尸官直接发问时，法庭上人人瞪大了眼睛、竖起了耳朵。
“等什么人？你看见他见到什么人了吗？”
“见到了！”
“把你看见的讲给我听。”
“我看见的是这样。我走近书报摊去买几份晚报的时候，先前在茶点室见到的那个人就站在近旁。他朝四下里张望，但主要是望着办公处所和月台之间那片大场地的几个入口。有一两回他看了看表。那时——照车站大钟——是七点过十分光景。他似乎等得不耐烦，焦躁地走来走去。我打他身边走过，到了书报摊。等我再回过头来，他已站在几码开外，正同另一个男人握手。从他们握手的那副样子，我断定他们是老朋友，也许有些日子没见面了。”

===Original===
“Their greeting was cordial?”
“I should call it effusive.”
“Can you describe the other man?”
“I can describe a sort of general impression of both. He was a tall man, taller than Hannaford, but not so broadly built. He wore a dark ulster overcoat, with a strap at the back; it was either a very dark blue or a black in colour. He had a silk hat﻿—new and glossy. He gave me the impression of being a smartly-dressed man﻿—smart boots and gloves and that sort of thing﻿—you know the general impression you get at a quick glance. But as to his features, I can’t tell you anything.”
“Why not?” asked the coroner.
“Because, to begin with, he wore an unusually large pair of blue spectacles, which completely veiled his eyes, and to end with, his throat and chin were swathed in a heavy white muffler, which covered the lower part of his face as well. Between the rim of his hat and the collar of his coat it was all muffler and spectacles!”
===Chinese===
“他们见面时亲热吗？”
“要我说，亲热得过了头。”
“你能描述一下另一个男人吗？”
“对这两个人，我能说出个笼统的总印象。他是个高个子，比汉纳福德还高，但没那么壮实。他穿一件深色阿尔斯特（ulster）长大衣，背后有束带；颜色不是极深的蓝，就是黑的。他戴一顶丝质礼帽——崭新，锃亮。他给我的印象是穿着十分讲究——讲究的靴子呀手套呀那一类——你知道，就是匆匆一瞥间得到的那种总体印象。至于他的五官相貌，我可说不上来。”
“为什么说不上来？”验尸官问。
“因为，头一条，他戴着一副大得出奇的蓝色眼镜，把两眼完全遮没了；末一条，他的喉咙和下巴裹在一条厚厚的白围巾里，连脸的下半截也一并盖住了。从帽檐到衣领之间，露出来的根本不是脸——全是围巾和眼镜！”

===Original===
The coroner looked disappointed. His interest in the witness seemed to evaporate.
“Did you notice anything else?” he asked.
“Only that the newcomer took Hannaford’s arm and that they walked away towards the left-hand entrance hall, evidently in earnest conversation. That was the last I saw of them.”
“There’s just one question I should like to put to you in conclusion,” said the coroner. “You say that you are confident that the photograph in the newspapers is that of the man you saw at Victoria. Now, have you seen the dead man’s body?”
“I have. The police took me to see it when I volunteered my evidence.”
“And you recognised it as that of the man you saw?”
===Chinese===
验尸官显出失望的样子。他对这证人的兴致似乎一下子散了个干净。
“你还注意到别的什么吗？”他问。
“只注意到那新来的人挽住汉纳福德的胳膊，两人朝左手边的进口大厅走去，分明在专心谈着话。那是我最后一次见到他们。”
“最后我还想问你一个问题，”验尸官说。“你说你确信报纸上那张照片，就是你在维多利亚见到的那个人的。那么，死者的遗体你见过没有？”
“见过。我主动提供证词之后，警方就带我去看了。”
“你认出那遗体就是你见过的那个人吗？”

===Original===
“Without doubt! There is no question of that in my mind.”
Five minutes later the inquest stood adjourned, and those chiefly concerned gathered together in the emptying court to discuss the voluntary witness’s evidence. Matherfield manifested an almost cheerful optimism.
“This is better!﻿—much better,” he declared, rubbing his hands as if in anticipation of laying them on something. “We know now that Hannaford met, at any rate, two men that night. It’s easier to find two men than one!”
Rhona, whom Hetherwick had escorted to the coroner’s court, looked her astonishment. “How can that be?” she asked.
“Mr. Hetherwick understands,” answered Matherfield with a laugh. “He’ll tell you.”
But Hetherwick said nothing. He was always wondering﻿—always wondering﻿—about the woman whose picture lay in his pocket.
===Chinese===
“毫无疑问！在我心里，这一点毫无可疑。”
五分钟后，验尸审讯宣布休庭，主要关系人聚在渐渐散空的法庭里，议论起这位自动出面的证人的证词。马瑟菲尔德显出一种近乎欢畅的乐观。
“这下好多了！——好多了，”他宣称道，搓着双手，仿佛巴望着马上要把什么东西抓到手里。“现在我们知道了，那天晚上汉纳福德至少会过两个男人。找两个人可比找一个人容易！”
罗娜——她是海瑟维克护送到验尸法庭来的——满脸惊讶之色。“这怎么可能呢？”她问。
“海瑟维克先生明白，”马瑟菲尔德笑着回答。“他会告诉你的。”
可海瑟维克什么也没说。他一直在纳闷——一直在纳闷——那个女人，那幅此刻正躺在他口袋里的画像的主人。

## XVIII The Telegram / 十八 电报

===Original===

Hetherwick followed his companion across the Strand, into the Adelphi, and to the house they wanted﻿—an old Adams mansion, now divided into flats. Matherfield did not take the trouble to ascend to the upper regions; he sought and found a caretaker and put a question to him. The man shook his head.

“Dr. Ambrose, sir?” he replied. “Oh, yes, Dr. Ambrose lives here﻿—38. But he ain’t in, sir﻿—ain’t at home, in fact. He’s been away three weeks or so﻿—don’t know where he is.”

With a meaning look at Hetherwick, Matherfield drew the caretaker aside and talked to him for a few moments; the man presently turned and went downstairs to the basement from which they had summoned him.

“That’s all right,” remarked Matherfield, with a wink. “He’s going to let us into Ambrose’s flat. Didn’t I tell you we shouldn’t find Ambrose here? Not he! I should say he’s off!”

===Chinese===

海瑟维克跟着同伴穿过斯特兰德街，走进阿德尔菲，来到他们要找的那所宅子跟前——一幢亚当风格的老公馆，如今隔成了公寓。马瑟菲尔德懒得劳神往楼上跑；他寻着了看门人，问了他一句。那人摇了摇头。

“安布罗斯医生吗，先生？”他答道。“哦，有的，安布罗斯医生就住这儿—— 38 号。不过他这会儿不在，先生——说实在的，人压根儿不在。他出门三个礼拜光景了——去了哪儿，可说不上来。”

马瑟菲尔德朝海瑟维克递了个会意的眼色，把看门人拉到一旁，同他说了几句；那人随即转身下楼，回他被打唤上来的地下室去了。

“这就妥了，”马瑟菲尔德使个眼色，说道。“他答应放咱们进安布罗斯的公寓。我不是早说了么，在这儿准找不着安布罗斯本人！他才不回来呢！要我说，人早溜了！”

===Original===

“Supposing he returns﻿—while we’re here?” asked Hetherwick.

“Wish he would!” chuckled Matherfield. “Nobody I want to see more! If he did, why, I should just ask him to take a little walk with me﻿—to explain a few matters. But he won’t! Here’s the man. We’ll go up.”

The caretaker reappeared with a bunch of keys and led the way to a flat at the top of the old house. He unlocked a door and stood aside.

“You needn’t wait,” said Matherfield. “I’ll shut the place up again when we leave and let you know. All right.”

===Chinese===

“可要是他回来了呢——趁咱们还在的时候？”海瑟维克问。

“巴不得他回来！”马瑟菲尔德呵呵一笑。“再没有比他更叫我盼着见的人了！他要真回来，嘿，我就请他跟我出去溜达一小趟——把几桩事情交代交代。可他不会回来！人来了。咱们上楼。”

看门人拿着一串钥匙重新现身，领着路上了老宅顶层的一套公寓。他开了锁，侧身让开。

“你不必在这儿守着，”马瑟菲尔德说。“我们走的时候，我自会把屋子重新锁好，再知会你一声。就这样。”

===Original===

He walked in, with Hetherwick at his heels, as soon as the caretaker had gone, and, once inside, closed the door carefully upon himself and his companion. But Hetherwick, after a first glance at the sitting-room into which they had entered, a somewhat untidy, shabbily furnished place, went straight to the hearth and pointed to a framed photograph, time-stained and faded, which hung over the mantelpiece.

“There’s a striking and significant piece of evidence﻿—at once!” he exclaimed. “Do you know what that is, Matherfield?”

Matherfield looked in the direction indicated, and shook his head.

“Not the slightest idea!” he answered. “I see it’s a photograph of some old church or other﻿—that’s all.”

===Chinese===

看门人一走，他便跨进门去，海瑟维克紧随其后；一进屋，他就仔细地把门带上，把自己和同伴关在了里面。海瑟维克对走进的这间起居室先扫了一眼——屋内颇为凌乱，家具陈旧寒素——随即径直走到壁炉跟前，指着壁炉台上挂的一幅装框相片：相片年深日久，已经泛黄褪色。

“瞧，一上来就是件扎眼又紧要的物证！”他叫道。“马瑟菲尔德，你可知道那是什么？”

马瑟菲尔德朝他所指的方向望去，摇了摇头。

“半点儿也猜不着！”他答道。“我看就是哪座老教堂的相片——仅此而已。”

===Original===

“That’s the famous Parish Church of Sellithwaite!” said Hetherwick. “One of the very finest in England! I had a look at it﻿—only a mere look﻿—when I was down there. Now then, what’s this man doing with a picture of Sellithwaite Parish Church in his rooms? Hannaford came from Sellithwaite!”

“That’s a mighty significant thing, anyway,” agreed Matherfield. “We’re getting at something this morning!” He looked more carefully at the photograph. “Grand old building, as you say,” he continued. “Of course, the mere fact of his having it put up there shows that he’s some interest in it. Sellithwaite man, likely. But we’ll find all that out. Now let’s look round.”

There was little to see, Hetherwick thought. The flat consisted of a sitting-room and bedroom and a small bathroom. The furniture was plain, old, rather shabby; the whole place suggested that its occupant was not over well-to-do; the only signs of affluence to be seen were manifested in the toilet articles on the dressing-table, in a luxurious, if well-worn, dressing-gown which hung on the rail of the bed, and in the presence of carefully folded and pressed garments laid out in the bedroom. There were a few books, chiefly medical treatises, in shelves in the sitting-room; a few personal pictures, mainly of college and school groups, on the walls; and a desk in the centre, littered with more books, writing materials, and papers. Matherfield began to turn them over.

===Chinese===

“那可是大名鼎鼎的塞利斯韦特教区教堂！”海瑟维克说。“在英格兰也算得数一数二的精美！我上回下去的时候，看过它一眼——只匆匆看了一眼。那么请问，这人屋里挂一张塞利斯韦特教区教堂的照片做什么？汉纳福德正是塞利斯韦特来的！”

“不管怎么说，这可是桩大有分量的线索，”马瑟菲尔德深表赞同。“今天上午咱们算摸着东西了！”他把相片又细看了一遍。“确是座气派的老建筑，正如你所说，”他接着讲。“当然，单是他把这张相片挂在那里这件事，就说明他同它有几分干系。多半是塞利斯韦特人。不过这些都会查个水落石出。现在咱们四下里瞧瞧。”

依海瑟维克看，可瞧的东西不多。这套公寓是一间起居室、一间卧室，外加一间小浴室。家具朴素、陈旧，相当寒素；整处屋子都透着房客不大宽裕的光景；唯一露出几分阔绰痕迹的，是梳妆台上的盥洗用具，是床栏上挂的那件晨衣——虽已穿旧，质料却讲究——还有卧室里摊放着的几件仔细叠好、熨烫平整的衣裳。起居室书架上有几本书，多是医学论著；墙上挂着几帧私人照片，大都是大学和中学时代的合影；屋子正中一张书桌，堆着更多的书、文具纸张。马瑟菲尔德动手翻检起来。

===Original===

“See that?” he exclaimed suddenly, pointing to a movable calendar which stood on the top ledge of the desk. “Notice the date? March 18th! That’s the day on which Hannaford got his quietus. At least, strictly speaking, it was the day before. Hannaford actually died on the nineteenth﻿—about﻿—what was it?﻿—very early in the morning, anyway. What’s one to gather from this?﻿—that Ambrose hasn’t been here since the eighteenth. So﻿—hallo!”

Turning over the loose papers that lay about the blotting-pad, he had suddenly lighted upon a telegram; just as suddenly he thrust it into Hetherwick’s hands.

“Look at that!” he exclaimed. “Now, that is a find! Biggest we’ve ever had﻿—so far!”

Hetherwick read the apparently innocent message.

===Chinese===

“瞧见没有？”他忽然叫道，指着立在书桌后沿的一本活页日历。“留意到日期没有？3 月 18 日！那正是汉纳福德一命归西的日子。至少，严格说起来，是前一天。汉纳福德实际死在十九日——大约——是几点来着？——反正是一大清早。从这一点能得出什么？——可见打十八号起，安布罗斯就不曾踏进过这里。这么一想——咦！”

他翻着吸墨纸板四周散放的零纸，忽然翻出了一封电报；他又同样突然地把它塞进海瑟维克手里。

“瞧这个！”他叫道。“这才叫大收获！是咱们到手的东西里最大的一份——到今儿为止！”

海瑟维克读了那封字面上看似全然无事的电文。

===Original===

> All right. Will meet you Victoria bookstall this evening as suggested.

> Hannaford.

“See the date?” said Matherfield excitedly. “March 18th! Now we’ve got at it! Ambrose was the man that met Hannaford at Victoria, the tall, muffled-up man that Ledbitter saw! That’s﻿—certain!”

“Seems so,” agreed Hetherwick. He was still studying the telegram. “Sent off from Fleet Street twelve-fifteen that day,” he muttered. “Yes﻿—there doesn’t seem much doubt about this. I wonder who this man Ambrose is?”

“We’ll soon get to know something about that, Mr. Hetherwick!” exclaimed Matherfield briskly. “Now, I’m just going to put that wire in my pocket, lock up this flat again, have another word or two with that caretaker chap, and go in search of the information you refer to. Come with me! Later, I shall get a search warrant, and make a thorough examination of this flat. Let’s be moving.”

===Chinese===

> 好。今晚如约在维多利亚书报摊碰头。

> 汉纳福德。

“瞧见日期没有？”马瑟菲尔德激动地说。“3 月 18 日！这回咱们可抓住要害了！在维多利亚接汉纳福德的正是安布罗斯——莱德比特瞧见的那高大裹严实的人就是他！这——板上钉钉！”

“看来是这样，”海瑟维克表示同意。他还在琢磨那封电报。“当天十二点一刻自舰队街发出，”他喃喃道。“是啊——这事看来没什么可怀疑的了。真不知道这位安布罗斯究竟是什么人？”

“用不了多久，这方面咱们就能摸出点门道，海瑟维克先生！”马瑟菲尔德干脆利落地叫道。“眼下呢，我打算把这封电报装进口袋，重新锁好这套公寓，同那看门的家伙再攀谈一两句，然后去查您说的那份底细。跟我来！回头我再去弄一张搜查令，把这公寓里里外外彻底翻检一遍。这就走吧。”

===Original===

Downstairs again, Matherfield called up the caretaker.

“You say Dr. Ambrose has been away for a bit?” he asked. “Is there anything unusual in that?”

“Well, not so very,” answered the man. “Ever since he came here, two or three years ago, he’s been used to going away for a while. I believe he used to go over to Paris. But I never remember him being away more than a week at a time before.”

“Evidently he’s a doctor,” suggested Matherfield. “Did he ever have patients come to see him here?”

The caretaker shook his head.

“No,” he replied. “He never had anybody much come to see him here﻿—never remember anybody, unless it was somebody he brought in at night for a smoke, you know. He generally went out early in a morning, and came home late﻿—very late.”

===Chinese===

重新下了楼，马瑟菲尔德又把看门人唤了上来。

“你说安布罗斯医生出门有些日子了？”他问。“这里头有什么反常的么？”

“呃，也说不上多反常，”那人答道。“打两三年前他搬来这儿起，就惯常要出门一阵子。我看他往常是上巴黎去。可在我记性里，他哪一回一走也没超过一个礼拜。”

“他分明是个行医的，”马瑟菲尔德提示道。“从前可有病人上这儿来找他？”

看门人摇了摇头。

“没有，”他答道。“从来就没有什么人上这儿看他——我谁也记不起来，除非是他夜里带个把人来抽口烟，那样儿的。他平常一大清早就出门，回来到得很晚——很晚很晚。”

===Original===

“What about his meals?” asked Matherfield.

“He’d no meals here﻿—unless he made himself a cup of coffee or so in a morning,” said the caretaker. “All his meals out﻿—breakfast, too. Sundays as well as weekdays. We saw very little of him.”

“Who does up his rooms﻿—makes the bed and so on?” inquired Matherfield.

“My wife,” answered the caretaker. “She does all that.”

“And she hasn’t had anything to do for﻿—how long?”

“Well, it’ll be three weeks, I’m sure. He never used to say anything at any time when he went off﻿—just went. He’d call downstairs when he came back and let us know he was back, d’ye see? But we never thought he’d be as long away as this, this time. It was only this morning, just before you came, that my missus said to me that it seemed queer.”

===Chinese===

“那他一日三餐怎么解决？”马瑟菲尔德问。

“他在家一顿也不开——顶多早上自己煮一杯咖啡喝喝，”看门人说。“顿顿都在外头吃——连早饭也一样。礼拜天同寻常日子一个样。我们轻易见不着他的人影儿。”

“他那几间屋子谁给收拾——铺床叠被这些活计？”马瑟菲尔德问。

“我老婆，”看门人答道。“全是她一手料理。”

“那么她没活儿可干有多久了——多长时间？”

“呃，总有三个礼拜了，管保。他往常出门，从来一句话不说——抬脚就走。回来了就朝楼下喊一嗓子，让我们知道人回来了，您明白么？可这一回谁也没想到他会一去这么久。就在今儿早上，您几位来之前，我家老婆子还跟我说，这事瞧着邪性呢。”

===Original===

“Why queer?”

“Because he’s taken nothing with him. However short a time he might be away before, he always took a suitcase, clean linen, shaving things, so on﻿—he was a very particular gentleman about his appearance﻿—always dressed like a swell and had a clean shirt every day; used to have a nice heavy washing-bill, anyhow!”

“Did he seem to be pretty well supplied with money?” asked Matherfield. “Or﻿—the opposite?”

“Couldn’t rightly say,” replied the caretaker. “Always paid his rent, and us, and the washing regular, but as for anything else, why, we’d no means of knowing. Of course, as I tell you, he always looked the gentleman.”

“I see!” said Matherfield. “All right﻿—you’ll see me again this afternoon.”

===Chinese===

“怎么个邪性法？”

“因为他这回什么也没带走呀。往常不管出门的日子多短，他总要带只手提箱、干净衬衣、剃须的家什，等等——他是对仪表顶讲究的一位先生——永远穿得像个人物，天天换干净衬衫；那洗衣账单，回回都是沉甸甸的一大笔，可不！”

“他看上去手头很宽裕么？”马瑟菲尔德问。“还是——恰恰相反？”

“这个可说不准，”看门人答道。“房租、我们的工钱、洗衣钱，样样按时不误，可再往深里说，我们无从知晓。当然喽，我方才说了，他瞧着始终是位体面先生。”

“明白了！”马瑟菲尔德说。“好——今天下午我还会再来见你。”

===Original===

He strode away towards the Strand, and there ushered Hetherwick into the first empty taxicab they met.

“Where now?” asked Hetherwick as Matherfield followed him into the cab after a word to its driver.

“We’re going now, sir, to Hallam Street, to the offices of the General Medical Council,” answered Matherfield promptly. “I’ve had experience of inquiring into the antecedents of medical men before, and I know where to find out all about any of ’em. I’m going to find out all about this Dr. Charles Ambrose﻿—that is, of course, if he’s an English doctor.”

“Probably he isn’t,” remarked Hetherwick, “any more than Baseverie is.”

“Ah, Baseverie!” exclaimed Matherfield. “I’d forgotten that man for the time being! Well, while we’re about it, we’ll see if we can unearth a bit of information concerning him. We’ve done a bit of good work this morning, ye know, Mr. Hetherwick!” he went on, rubbing his hands with satisfaction. “We’ve practically made certain that Ambrose was the man who met Hannaford at Victoria, and we’re sure he’s the man to whom Macpherson supplied the bottle in which the poison was discovered at Granett’s room. And now we’ll hope for a bit more illumination in the darkness!”

===Chinese===

他大步流星朝斯特兰德街走去，到了那边，便把海瑟维克让进他们碰上的头一辆空出租车。

“现在去哪儿？”海瑟维克问——马瑟菲尔德向司机交代了一句，跟着上了车。

“这一趟，先生，是去哈勒姆街（Hallam Street），上医学总会（General Medical Council）的办公处，”马瑟菲尔德应声答道。“查行医人士的来历底细，我从前的差事里有过历练，要打探他们哪一个的老底，该上哪儿我门儿清。我这就去把这位查尔斯·安布罗斯医生的底细查个透——当然喽，前提是他得是位英国医生。”

“多半不是，”海瑟维克说，“跟巴斯维里一个样，怕都不是。”

“啊，巴斯维里！”马瑟菲尔德叫道。“这个人我一时竟撂在脑后了！好，既然顺路，咱们就瞧瞧能不能也掘出点关于他的消息。今天上午咱们这活儿干得漂亮，您知道么，海瑟维克先生！”他搓着双手，一脸得意，接着往下说。“安布罗斯就是在维多利亚接汉纳福德的人，这已基本坐实；麦克弗森（Macpherson）配售过那只药瓶——后来正是在格拉内特屋里、从那只瓶子里验出了毒药——而买主就是他，这也没有疑问。眼下就盼着这片黑地里再透进几分光亮了！”

===Original===

Hetherwick presently found himself closeted with Matherfield and a grave official who, after seeing Matherfield’s credentials and listening to his reasons for his visit of inquiry, began to consult various books of reference. Presently he left the room and was away some time; when he returned he brought with him two slips of paper, which he handed to Matherfield.

“I have had the particulars you require written out for you,” he said, “so you can examine them at your leisure. I﻿—” here he smiled frostily﻿—“I gather that you are somewhat anxious to get in touch with these men?”

“I think it’s extremely probable, sir, that before the day’s over I shall be exceedingly anxious to get in touch with both!” answered Matherfield, with something very like a wink. “More than anxious!”

The grave official nodded and smiled again, and Matherfield and Hetherwick went away. Outside Matherfield looked right and left.

“Mr. Hetherwick,” he said, “it’s well past twelve, and I’d my breakfast before eight﻿—I’m hungry! Let’s turn into the first decent place we see and get a bite and a sup! And we’ll examine these papers.”

===Chinese===

不多时，海瑟维克便发觉自己同马瑟菲尔德一道，同一位神情庄重的官员关在屋里密谈；那位验看马瑟菲尔德的证件，听明他此番查访的缘由，便动手翻检起各种参考名录来。过了一会儿他离屋而去，一去好一阵；回来时手里多了两张纸条，递给马瑟菲尔德。

“您所需的详情，我已着人缮写出来，”他说，“您可从容细看。我——”说到此处他淡然一笑——“我揣想，您二位是有点儿急于同这两位先生取得联络吧？”

“依我看，先生，极有可能不等今天过完，我就要十万火急地同这二位都联络上了！”马瑟菲尔德答道，那副神情跟使了个眼色相差无几。“何止是急！”

庄重的官员点了点头，又笑了一笑，马瑟菲尔德和海瑟维克便告辞出来。到了外头，马瑟菲尔德左顾右盼。

“海瑟维克先生，”他说，“十二点早过了，我八点前吃的早饭——肚子饿啦！咱们瞧见头个体面地方就进去，吃一口、喝一口！顺便把这几张纸细看一遍。”

===Original===

He presently led Hetherwick into the saloon bar of a tavern, and remarking that he had a taste for ale and bread and cheese at that time of day, provided himself with these matters and retreated to a snug corner, whither Hetherwick followed him with a whisky and soda.

“Here’s success to our endeavours, Mr. Hetherwick!” said Matherfield, lifting his tankard. “I’m now firmly under the impression that we’re adding link after link to the chain! But let’s see what we’ve got here in this crabbed writing.”

He laid the slips of paper on the table at which they sat; both bent over them. There were not many words on either, but to Hetherwick they were significant enough in their plain straightforwardness.

> Charles Ambrose, M.B. (Oxon). Medical Officer of Health, Crayport, Lancs, 1903﻿–﻿4; in practice Whiteburn, Lancs, 1904﻿–﻿9; police surgeon, Sellithwaite, WR, Yorks, 1909﻿–﻿12; in practice Brondesbury, London, 1912﻿–﻿18. Struck off Register by General Medical Council for unprofessional conduct, 1918.

===Chinese===

不多时他便领海瑟维克进了一家酒馆的雅座，说自己一天里这个时辰，就好淡啤酒配面包干酪这一口，置齐了这几样，便退进一个暖和安逸的角落；海瑟维克端着一杯威士忌苏打，也跟了过去。

“祝咱们的事马到成功，海瑟维克先生！”马瑟菲尔德举起大酒杯说。“如今我笃信不疑：咱们正给这条链子一环又一环地续上新环！来，瞧瞧这手潦草的字写的是什么。”

他把那两张纸条摊在两人落座的桌上；两人一齐俯身去看。每张纸上字数寥寥，可那份直白平实，在海瑟维克看来已足够触目。

> 查尔斯·安布罗斯，医学学士（牛津）。1903 至 1904 年任兰开夏（Lancs）克雷波特（Crayport）卫生官；1904 至 1909 年在兰开夏怀特本（Whiteburn）行医；1909 至 1912 年任约克郡西骑区塞利斯韦特警医；1912 至 1918 年在伦敦布伦兹伯里（Brondesbury）行医。1918 年因行止不端被医学总会从名册除名。

===Original===

“So much for him!” muttered Matherfield, his cheek bulging with bread and cheese. “I thought it would turn out to be something of that sort! Now t’other!”

> Cyprian Baseverie, L.R.C.P., L.R.C.S. In practice Birmingham, 1897﻿–﻿1902; at Wyborough, Northants, 1902﻿–﻿11; at Dalston, N, 1911﻿–﻿17, Convicted of fraud at Central Criminal Court, 1917, and struck off Register by General Medical Council, 1918.
