“Ho-ho!” exclaimed Matherfield. “Been in the dock already, has he? Well, well, Mr. Hetherwick, we continue to learn, sir! We know still more. Baseverie’s a convicted criminal. Both have been struck off the register. Ambrose was certainly at Sellithwaite﻿—and he’d be there, according to these dates, at the time of the Whittingham affair. A promising pair﻿—for our purpose! What do you think?”

“I’m wondering if the two men know each other,” answered Hetherwick.

“Shouldn’t wonder,” said Matherfield. “Probably they do. Probably they’re mixed up together in this affair. Probably they’re actual partners in it﻿—accessories to each other. But now that I know this much about them, I can find out more, especially about Ambrose, as he was a police surgeon. I can find out, too, what Baseverie’s particular crime was. Defrauding a patient, I should imagine. But I’ll put one or two men on to working up particulars and records of both Baseverie and Ambrose this afternoon, and, of course, I shall go back and thoroughly examine that flat in John Street.”

===Chinese===

“他这一份就交代完了！”马瑟菲尔德咕哝道，腮帮子还鼓着面包干酪。“我早料到会查出这么一号事！再看另一张！”

> 西普里安·巴斯维里，皇家内科医师学会、皇家外科医师学会持照会员。1897 至 1902 年在伯明翰（Birmingham）行医；1902 至 1911 年在北安普敦郡（Northants）怀伯勒（Wyborough）执业；1911 至 1917 年在伦敦北区多尔斯顿（Dalston）执业。1917 年在中央刑事法院被判诈骗罪，1918 年被医学总会从名册除名。

“嚯嚯！”马瑟菲尔德叫了起来。“原来还上过被告席，是吧？好哇，好哇，海瑟维克先生，咱们越查越明白了！如今又多知道一桩：巴斯维里是定过罪的刑徒。两个都被除了名。安布罗斯准在塞利斯韦特待过——照这些年月推算，惠廷厄姆案发作那阵，他正在那儿。冲着咱们这趟差事，这一对儿可真有指望！您意下如何？”

“我在琢磨，这两个人彼此认不认识，”海瑟维克答道。

“这没什么可怪的，”马瑟菲尔德说。“多半认识。多半是一同搅在这桩案子里。多半还是实实在在的同伙——你帮衬我、我帮衬你的从犯。不过如今既摸到了这一步，我就能再往下挖，尤其是安布罗斯——他当过警医，底细好查。巴斯维里犯的具体是哪一桩，也查得出来。要叫我猜，不是诓骗病人就是那一类。今天下午我就派上一两个人，把巴斯维里和安布罗斯两边的详情案底都捋出来；当然，我还要回约翰街，把那套公寓彻底搜上一遍。”

===Original===

“And, I suppose, in view of the evidence supplied by Macpherson, set up a search for Ambrose?” suggested Hetherwick.

“To be sure! We’ll get out a ‘tracked by the police’ notice, describing him to the best of our power,” replied Matherfield. “But I’ll tell you﻿—in my opinion it’ll be a stiff job getting hold of him. If you want my opinion, as a private individual, he’s probably got that secret invention of Hannaford’s and gone off across the Atlantic with it﻿—to turn it into money.”

“That’s very likely,” assented Hetherwick. “But what about Baseverie?”

“I’m not so much concerned about him now,” said Matherfield. “Ambrose seems to be the man I want﻿—first, anyway. But I shall do what I can to get hold of Baseverie. If these Penteney and Blenkinsop people had only come to us instead of laying plans of their own, some good would have been done. I shouldn’t have let the man get away!”

“My belief,” observed Hetherwick, “is that Baseverie and Ambrose are partners in this affair. And﻿—how do we know that they didn’t meet at Dover, and that they haven’t gone off together?”

===Chinese===

“再有，我料想，凭着麦克弗森供出的那些证据，就要张罗缉拿安布罗斯了吧？”海瑟维克提示道。

“那是自然！咱们这就发一纸‘警方查缉’的告示，尽所能把他的相貌开列出来，”马瑟菲尔德答道。“不过有句话我得说在前头——依我看，要拿住此人，是桩扎手的硬活。您要问我个人的私见——多半是他把汉纳福德那项秘密发明弄到了手，带着它漂过大西洋去了——换成钱去喽。”

“十有八九如此，”海瑟维克附和道。“可巴斯维里怎么办？”

“如今我倒不怎么担心他，”马瑟菲尔德说。“安布罗斯似乎才是我要的人——反正先紧着他。不过巴斯维里，能拿住我也尽力去拿。当初彭特尼、布伦金索普这班人若是来投咱们，而不是自家摆弄算盘，早就办成些好事了。我断不会放那个人跑掉！”

“依我看，”海瑟维克说，“巴斯维里同安布罗斯就是这桩案子里的同伙。再说——他们俩没在多佛碰头、没一道远走高飞，咱们又凭什么断定呢？”

===Original===

Still wondering about this, Hetherwick next morning went round to Lincoln’s Inn Fields and asked to see one of the partners. He was shown into the room in which he and Matherfield had had their interview on the previous day. But he found Major Penteney alone. Blenkinsop, remarked the junior partner, had business in the Courts that morning.

“I called,” explained Hetherwick, “to ask if you had any more information about Baseverie’s disappearance at Dover.”

Penteney made a wry face.

===Chinese===

心里还悬着这件事，第二天上午，海瑟维克便绕到林肯律师学院广场，求见事务所的一位合伙人。他被让进头一天他和马瑟菲尔德受接见的那个房间，却发现只彭特尼少校一人在。据这位资浅合伙人说，布伦金索普那天上午因事上法院去了。

“我过来，”海瑟维克说明来意，“是想问问，巴斯维里在多佛失踪一事，你们可有了进一步的消息。”

彭特尼苦起了一张脸。

===Original===

“More vexed than ever about that!” he answered. “Most inexcusably stupid conduct on the part of our man﻿—man we’ve always found so reliable previously. He came back yesterday afternoon, crestfallen, told us all about it, and got a jolly good wigging. He’d done well at first. Tracked his man from Riversreade Court to Dorking, and thence to Redhill, and thence to Dover, after one or two changes. Baseverie put up at some hotel﻿—I forget which﻿—near the harbour; our man, certain that Baseverie was quite unconscious that he was being followed, put up there, too. Nothing happened. He saw Baseverie at dinner that night, saw him in the smoking-room after; in fact, he had a game of billiards with him, and saw him retire to bed; their rooms were adjacent. He felt sure of seeing him at breakfast, but when he went down he found that the bird had flown﻿—flown, said the night porter, before six o’clock; he didn’t know where. Nor could our man trace him at station or pier, or anywhere.”

“Careless sort of watching,” said Hetherwick.

“Worse than careless!” agreed Penteney. “As I said, he caught it hot. But now﻿—”

The telephone bell on Blenkinsop’s desk rang. With a word of excuse Penteney turned to it. A moment later a smothered ejaculation of surprise came from him, followed by a sharp interrogation on his part. Suddenly he turned on Hetherwick.

“Good heavens!” he exclaimed. “What’s all this? This is Lady Riversreade speaking. She says her sister, who came yesterday, and Miss Featherstone have been kidnapped! Kidnapped﻿—this morning!”

===Chinese===

“这事越想越有气！”他答道。“我们派的那个人，办得蠢到不可原谅——这人我们以往一向觉得靠得住。昨天下午他垂头丧气地回来了，把经过一五一十说了，结结实实挨了一顿好训。起初他干得不坏。一路盯梢，从里弗斯雷德府盯到多金，再盯到雷德希尔（Redhill），换过一两趟车，又盯到多佛。巴斯维里在港口附近哪家旅馆落了脚——哪家我忘了；我们那位认定巴斯维里全然不知身后有人缀着，便也住进了同一家。随后风平浪静。当晚他看着巴斯维里用晚饭，饭后又看着他在吸烟室；说实在的，还同他打了一局台球，看着他回房安歇；两人的房间紧挨着。他满以为早饭时定能再见着人，可下楼一看，那鸟儿早飞了——据夜班门房说，六点以前就飞了；飞去了哪儿，门房也说不上。我们那位又在车站、码头，还有什么地方，把人的踪迹寻了个遍，一无所获。”

“这趟盯梢盯得可够马虎的，”海瑟维克说。

“岂止马虎！”彭特尼深有同感。“可是现在——”

布伦金索普桌上的电话铃响了。彭特尼告了声罪，转身去接。片刻之后，他喉咙里迸出一声压住的惊呼，跟着便急促地盘问起来。蓦地，他猛地转向海瑟维克。

“天哪！”他叫道。“这是怎么回事？电话上是里弗斯雷德夫人。她说她妹妹——昨天到的那位——同费瑟斯通小姐叫人绑架了！绑架——就在今天早上！”

===Original===

Hetherwick leapt to his feet with a sharp exclamation﻿—half amazed, half incredulous. But already his thoughts were with Rhona; he saw the dangers of the situation for her as Penteney could not see them.

“Impossible!” he said. “Kidnapped! in broad daylight? And﻿—from there?”

But Penteney was still busy at the telephone, giving and receiving rapid answers.

“Yes, yes!” he was saying. “To be sure!﻿—police﻿—yes! I’m coming straight there now﻿—car﻿—tell the police to get busy.”

He turned sharply to Hetherwick as he laid down the instrument.

===Chinese===

海瑟维克霍地站起，失声惊呼——一半是愕然，一半是不信。可他的心思早已飞到罗娜身上；她眼下处境里的凶险，他看得清楚，彭特尼却看不见。

“不可能！”他说。“绑走了！光天化日之下？还是——从那个地方？”

可彭特尼还在忙着对付电话，急促地问一阵、答一阵。

“是，是！”他正说着。“没错！——警察——对！我这就直接赶过去——汽车——叫警察马上动起来。”

他撂下听筒，猛地转向海瑟维克。

===Original===

“Fear there’s no impossibility about it!” he said. “Lady Riversreade says they were carried off as they crossed from the Court to the Home﻿—she’s heard something of a big car with strange men in it. I’m going down there at once﻿—there’s more in this affair than one sees at first.”

“I’ll come with you,” said Hetherwick. “Where can we get a car﻿—a fast one?”

“Garage close by, in Kingsway,” answered Penteney, hurriedly seizing on one of several greatcoats that hung in a recess. “Here!﻿—get into one of these﻿—you’re about my height, and the air’s still nippy, motoring. Now come on﻿—we’ll be there in under the hour. You know,” he continued, as they left the office and hastened towards Kingsway, “I think I see through something of this already, Hetherwick. These fellows probably believed they were kidnapping Lady Riversreade!﻿—and got her sister in mistake for her. Ransom, you know! The blackmailing dodge failed﻿—now they’re trying this. A desperate and daredevil lot, evidently!”

Hetherwick nodded a silent assent. He was wondering whether or not to tell Penteney that the Miss Featherstone of whom he had just spoken was in reality the granddaughter of the man whose mysterious murder appeared to be the starting-point of the more recent, equally mysterious events. That fact, it seemed to him, would have to come out sooner or later﻿—and there might be possible complications, perhaps unpleasantness, when Lady Riversreade discovered that Rhona had gone to her as a spy. Might it not be well to take Penteney into his confidence and explain matters? But, on reflection, he decided to wait until they knew the exact situation at Riversreade Court; so far, in spite of Lady Riversreade’s news he felt it difficult to believe that two women, one of them, to his knowledge, a girl of character and resource, and the other a woman of the world, used to travelling and to adventure, could be carried off in broad daylight in immediate prospect of two large houses﻿—the thing seemed impossible.

===Chinese===

“只怕这事由不得说不可能！”他说。“里弗斯雷德夫人说，她们俩是从府里往慈善院去的半道上被劫走的——她听说了些风声，说是有一辆大汽车，里头坐着几个生人。我这就赶下去——这桩案子里的名堂，比一眼瞧见的要多。”

“我同你一起去，”海瑟维克说。“上哪儿弄辆车——要快的？”

“近旁就有家车行，在金斯威，”彭特尼答道，一边慌忙从壁龛里挂着的几件大衣中抓下一件。“来！——披上一件——你同我个头相仿，坐汽车跑起来，外头的天还冷飕飕的。这就走——不出一个钟头准到。您知道，”他们出了事务所、快步赶往金斯威的当口，他接着说，“这层机关我已看破了几分，海瑟维克。这伙人八成当自己绑走的是里弗斯雷德夫人！——错把她妹妹当成了她。图的是赎金，懂么！讹诈那一招没得手——如今改试这一手。一望便知，是帮亡命到无法无天的家伙！”

海瑟维克默默点头应了。他正拿不定主意，要不要告诉彭特尼：他方才提到的那位费瑟斯通小姐，其实就是汉纳福德的孙女——而汉纳福德那桩离奇的凶案，看来正是后来这桩同样离奇的事端的起点。这个实情，依他看来，早晚要揭出来——而等里弗斯雷德夫人发觉罗娜是打着卧底的算盘投到她门下的，只怕节外生枝，甚或不欢。何不趁现在就把彭特尼引为心腹，把原委讲明？可再一思量，他决定还是等他们摸清里弗斯雷德府的确切光景再说；尽管里弗斯雷德夫人传来了那样的消息，他至今仍觉难以相信：两个女人——其中一个据他所知是有胆有识的姑娘，另一个是走惯了远门、见惯了风浪的世故妇人——竟会在光天化日之下，在两所大宅近在眼前的当口叫人劫走——这事简直不可能。

## XVII The Torn Labels / 十七 撕裂的标签
===Original===
Penteney strode forward and picked up the telegram; a moment later he passed it over to Hetherwick.

“That’s most unfortunate!” he exclaimed. “And unexpected, too! Of course, the fellow’s slipped off to the Continent.”

Matherfield looked over Hetherwick’s shoulder and read the message.

> Followed him down here last night. Put up at same hotel, but he slipped me and got clear away early this morning. Returning now.
===Chinese===
彭特尼（Penteney）抢步上前，拾起那份电报；不多时，便把它递给了海瑟维克（Hetherwick）。

“这真是太不巧了！”他失声道。“而且万万没有料到！不用说，那家伙已经溜到大陆上去了。”

马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）从海瑟维克肩后探过头来，读了电文。

> 昨夜尾随他到了这里。投宿同一家旅馆，但他甩开了我，今天一清早脱身遁去。这就返回。

===Original===
“You should have employed two men, gentlemen,” said Matherfield. “One’s not enough﻿—in a case of that sort. But it’s as I said before﻿—this man should have been given into custody at once. However﻿—”

He got up from his chair, as if there was no more to be said, and moved towards the door. But halfway across the room he paused.

“You’ll let me know if anybody comes forward about that reward?” he suggested. “It’s more of a police matter, you know.”

The two partners, who were obviously much annoyed by the telegram, nodded.
===Chinese===
“两位先生，你们当初该雇两个人才是，”马瑟菲尔德说道。“办这种事，一个人是不够的。不过，正像我从前说的——这个人早该立即交警方看押起来。然而——”

他从椅子上站起身来，仿佛话已说尽，朝门口走去。可是走到屋子中段，他又停住了。

“要是有人为那笔赏格找上门来，你们会知会我吧？”他提议道。“要知道，这多半是警方的事。”

两位合伙人显然被电报搅得大为懊恼，都点了点头。

===Original===
“We shall let you know﻿—at once,” answered Blenkinsop. “Of course, you’ll regard all we’ve told you as strictly confidential?”

“Oh, to be sure, sir,” replied Matherfield. “It’s not the only private and confidential feature of this affair, I assure you.”

Outside he turned to Hetherwick.

“Well!” he said. “We’ve cleared up a few things, Mr. Hetherwick﻿—or, rather, those two have cleared them up for us. But are we any nearer answering the question that we want answering﻿—who poisoned Robert Hannaford?”
===Chinese===
“我们会知会您——立刻，”布伦金索普（Blenkinsop）答道。“当然，我们把一切都告诉您了，您会严守机密吧？”

“哦，那是自然，先生，”马瑟菲尔德答道。“我向您担保，这桩案子里须守机密的私密情节，可不止这一处呢。”

出了门，他转向海瑟维克。

“好啦！”他说。“咱们弄明白了几件事，海瑟维克先生——或者不如说，是那两位替咱们弄明白的。可是，咱们想要解答的那个问题——究竟是谁毒死了罗伯特·汉纳福德（Robert Hannaford）——咱们离答案就近了一步么？”

===Original===
“I think we are!” replied Hetherwick. “I am, anyhow! Either Baseverie poisoned him﻿—or he knows who did!”

“Knows who did!” repeated Matherfield. “Ah!﻿—that’s more like it. I don’t think he did it﻿—he wouldn’t be so ready about showing himself forward.”

“I’m not so sure of that,” remarked Hetherwick. “From what we’ve heard of him, he seems to be a bold and daring sort of scamp. Probably he thought he’d have a very easy prey in Lady Riversreade; probably, too, he believed that a woman who’s got all that money would make little to do about parting with thirty thousand pounds. One thing’s sure, however﻿—Baseverie knows what we want to know. And﻿—he’s gone!”

“Perhaps﻿—perhaps!” said Matherfield. “And perhaps not. This man of Penteney’s no doubt tracked him to Dover, and there he lost him, but that isn’t saying that Baseverie’s gone on the Continent. If Baseverie’s the cute customer that he seems to be, he’d put two and two together when Major Penteney warned him off Riversreade Court. He’d probably suspect Penteney of setting a watch on him; he may have spotted the very man who was watching. Then, if he’d any sense, he’d lead that man a bit of a dance, and eventually double on him. No!﻿—I should say Baseverie’s back here in town! That’s about it, Mr. Hetherwick. But what’s this? Here’s one of my men coming to meet us. I left word where I should be found.”
===Chinese===
“我想是近了！”海瑟维克答道。“反正我是近了！要么是巴斯维里（Baseverie）下的毒——要么他知道是谁下的！”

“知道是谁下的！”马瑟菲尔德重复道。“啊！——这话才像样。我倒不认为是他亲手下的——他不会那么急着往前台里凑。”

“这我可就不那么有把握了，”海瑟维克说道。“就咱们听到的种种来看，他像个胆大包天、无法无天的流氓。多半他本以为里弗斯雷德夫人（Lady Riversreade）是块到口的肥肉；多半他还以为，一个坐拥那般家私的女人，拿三万镑出来不会怎么在乎。不过有一点是肯定的——巴斯维里知道咱们想要知道的东西。而且——他跑掉了！”

“也许——也许如此！”马瑟菲尔德说。“也许不然。彭特尼手下那人，无疑是一路把他跟到了多佛（Dover），在那里跟丢了，可这并不等于说巴斯维里就去了大陆。巴斯维里若真像看上去那样是个精明角色，那么彭特尼少校那回警告他离里弗斯雷德府（Riversreade Court）远着点的时候，他就该前后一合计，把事情串起来了。他多半疑心彭特尼派了人盯他的梢；说不定连盯梢的那个人，都叫他给认了出来。那么，他但凡有点脑子，就会领着那人兜上好一阵子圈子，末了再折回原路把他甩掉。不！——依我说，巴斯维里这会儿已经回到城里了！就是这么回事，海瑟维克先生。咦，这是什么？我的一个手下迎咱们来了。我动身时留了话，说在哪儿找得着我。”

===Original===
Hetherwick looked up and saw a man, who was obviously a policeman in plain clothes, coming towards them. He was a quiet-looking, stodgy-faced man, but he had news written all over his plain face.

“Well, Marler?” inquired Matherfield as they met. “Got something?”

There was nobody about in that quiet corner of Lincoln’s Inn Fields, yet the man looked round as if anxious to escape observation, and he spoke in a whisper.

“I believe I’ve got that chemist!” he answered. “Leastways, it’s like this. There’s a chemist I tried this morning﻿—name of Macpherson, in Maiden Lane. I showed him the facsimiles of the lost labels on the medicine bottles, and asked him if he could give me any information. He’s a very cautious sort of man, I think; he examined the facsimiles a long time, saying nothing. Then he said he supposed I was a policeman, and so on, and of course I had to tell him a bit﻿—only a bit. Then he said, all of a sudden, ‘Look here, my friend,’ he said, ‘you’d better tell me, straight out﻿—has this to do with that Hannaford poisoning case?’ So, of course, I said that, between ourselves, it had. ‘Isn’t Matherfield in charge of that?’ he said. Of course, I said you were. ‘Very well’ he said. ‘You send Matherfield to me. I’m not going to say anything to you,’ he said. ‘What I’ve got to say I’ll say to Matherfield.’ So I went back to headquarters, and they told me you’d gone to Lincoln’s Inn Fields.”
===Chinese===
海瑟维克抬眼望去，只见一个人朝他们走来，一望便知是个便衣警察。此人生相沉静，面孔呆板，可那张平板的脸上分明写满了新闻。

“怎么样，马勒（Marler）？”两人碰头时马瑟菲尔德问道。“有收获？”

林肯律师学院广场（Lincoln’s Inn Fields）那个清静的角落里四下无人，这人却还左右张望，像是唯恐叫人瞧见，说话用的是耳语。

“我看我是把那家药房找着了！”他答道。“至少，是这么回事。今天上午我跑了一家药房——店主叫麦克弗森（Macpherson），在梅登巷（Maiden Lane）。我把药瓶上那几张残缺标签的摹真照片拿给他看，问他能不能提供点情况。我看他是个极谨慎的人；他把那些摹真照片端详了半天，一言不发。然后他说，他料想我是个警察，如此等等，我自然只好对他稍稍吐露一点——只一点点。于是他突然说道：‘听着，朋友，’他说，‘你最好直截了当告诉我——这事同汉纳福德毒杀案有没有牵扯？’于是，我自然说，咱们私下讲，是有牵扯。‘那案子不是马瑟菲尔德在办么？’他说。我自然说正是。‘很好，’他说，‘你叫马瑟菲尔德到我这儿来。我什么也不会对你讲，’他说，‘我要讲的话，只讲给马瑟菲尔德。’所以我回了总部，他们说您到林肯律师学院广场来了。”

===Original===
“All right, my lad!” said Matherfield. “If you’ve found the right man, I’ll remember you. What’s his name﻿—Macpherson, Maiden Lane? Very good﻿—then I’ll just step along and see him. Not a word to anybody, Marler!” he added, as the man turned away. “Keep close. Now, this is a bit of all right, Mr. Hetherwick!” he continued, chuckling and rubbing his hands. “This beats all we heard at Penteney’s! Only let me get the name and address of the man for whom that bottle of medicine was made up, and I think I shall have taken a long stride! But come along﻿—we’ll see the chemist together.”

The shop in Maiden Lane before which they presently paused was a small, narrow-fronted, old-fashioned establishment, with little in its windows beyond the usual coloured bottles and over the front no more than the name “Macpherson” in faded gilt letters on a time-stained signboard. It was dark and stuffy within the shop, and Hetherwick had to strain his eyes to see a tall, thin, elderly, spectacled man, very precise and trim in appearance, who stood behind the single counter, silently regarding him and Matherfield.

“Mr. Macpherson?” inquired Matherfield. “Just so! Good morning, sir. My name is Matherfield﻿—Inspector Matherfield. One of my men tells me﻿—”

“One moment!” interrupted the chemist. He stepped behind a screen at the rear of his shop and presently returned with a young man, to whom he whispered a word or two. Then he beckoned to his two visitors, and opening a door at the further corner, ushered them into a private parlour. “We shall be to ourselves here, Mr. Matherfield,” he said. “And I’ve no doubt your business is of a highly confidential nature.”
===Chinese===
“好样的，小伙子！”马瑟菲尔德说。“你要是找对了人，我记你一功。他叫什么来着——麦克弗森，梅登巷？很好——那我就这就跑一趟去见见他。对谁也休提一个字，马勒！”那人转身走开时，他又补了一句。“盯紧着点。哈，这可真是妙事一桩，海瑟维克先生！”他接着说，一面呵呵地笑，搓着双手。“这比咱们在彭特尼那边听来的一切都强！只消让我拿到那瓶药是给谁配的名字和地址，我想我就跨出一大步了！走——咱们一起去见见那位药剂师。”

不多时，他们俩在梅登巷一家铺子门前站住了。那是爿门面窄窄的、老派陈旧的字号，橱窗里除了寻常的各色药瓶之外别无长物，门楣上仅有一块年深日久的招牌，上书“麦克弗森”几个褪色的烫金字。铺子里昏暗憋闷，海瑟维克得费劲凝神，才看清那唯一的柜台后面站着一个瘦高个子的上了年纪的人，戴着一副眼镜，外表一丝不苟、干净利落，正默然打量着他同马瑟菲尔德。

“麦克弗森先生？”马瑟菲尔德问道。“正是！早安，先生。敝人马瑟菲尔德——探长马瑟菲尔德。我的一个手下来告诉我——”

“且慢！”药剂师打断道。他走到铺子后头一道屏风背后，不多时领着一个年轻人回来，对年轻人低声吩咐了一两句。然后他向两位来客招了招手，开开最里角上的一扇门，把他们让进一间清静的私室。“在这儿说话，就咱们自己人听得见，马瑟菲尔德先生，”他说。“而且我毫不怀疑，您的来务是桩高度机密的事。”

===Original===
“Something of that sort, Mr. Macpherson,” assented Matherfield, as he and Hetherwick took chairs at a centre table. “But my man’ll have prepared you a bit, no doubt. He tells me he showed you the photographed facsimiles of certain torn labels that are on a medicine bottle which figures in the Hannaford case, and that in consequence of your seeing them you asked to see me. Well, sir, here I am!”

“Aye, just so, Mr. Matherfield, just so, precisely,” replied the chemist, turning up the gas-jet which hung above the table. “Aye, to be sure!” He, too, sat down at the table, and folded his thin long fingers together. “Aye, and you’ll be thinking, Mr. Matherfield, that yon bottle has something to do with the poisoning of Hannaford?”

“I’ll be candid with you, Mr. Macpherson,” answered Matherfield. “But first let me ask you something. Have you read the newspaper accounts of this affair?”

“I’ve done that, Mr. Matherfield﻿—yes, all I could lay hands on.”
===Chinese===
“差不离是那么回事，麦克弗森先生，”马瑟菲尔德应道，同海瑟维克在屋子中央一张桌子旁落了座。“不过我的人想必已经先给您递过一点话了。他告诉我，他把汉纳福德案里那只药瓶上几张撕毁标签的照相摹本给您看了，您看了之后，便要求见我。好了，先生，我来了！”

“哎，正是，马瑟菲尔德先生，正是，一毫也不错，”药剂师答道，拧亮了悬在桌子上方的煤气灯。“哎，可不！”他也在这张桌旁坐下，把瘦长的手指交叠起来。“哎，您八成是在想，马瑟菲尔德先生，那只瓶儿同汉纳福德的中毒有点干系吧？”

“我不妨同您开诚布公，麦克弗森先生，”马瑟菲尔德答道。“不过先容我问您一句。报上关于这桩案子的种种报道，您读过了么？”

“读过了，马瑟菲尔德先生——是的，能弄到手的都读了。”

===Original===
“Then you’ll be aware that there was another man poisoned as well as Hannaford﻿—a man named Granett, who was in Hannaford’s company on the night when it all happened? This gentleman here is the one that was in the Underground train and saw Hannaford die, and Granett make off, as he said, to fetch a doctor.”

“That’ll be Mr. Hetherwick, I’m thinking,” said the chemist, with a polite bow. “Aye, just so!”

“I see you’ve read the reports of the inquest,” remarked Matherfield, with a smile. “Very well, as I say, Granett was found dead later. I discovered a medicine bottle and a glass at his bedside. There’d been whisky in both, but according to the medical experts there had also been poison﻿—the traces, they say, were indisputable. Now, on that medicine bottle were two torn labels﻿—on the upper one, as you see from the facsimile photograph, there’s been a name written﻿—all that’s left is the initial C. and the first letter of a surname, A. All the rest’s gone. And what I want to know is﻿—are you the chemist that made up the medicine or the tonic, or whatever it was, that was in that bottle, and, if so, who is the customer for whom you made it, and whose Christian name begins with C. and surname with A.? Do you comprehend me?”

“Aye, aye, Mr. Matherfield!” answered the chemist eagerly. “I’m appreciating every word you’re saying, and very lucid it all is. And I’m willing to give you all the information in my power, but first I’d just like to have a bit myself on a highly pertinent matter. Now, you’ll be aware, Mr. Matherfield, if you’ve seen the newspapers of this last day or two, that there’s a firm of solicitors in Lincoln’s Inn Fields that’s offering a reward of five thousand pounds﻿—”
===Chinese===
“那么您一定知道，除了汉纳福德，还毒死了另一个人——一个名叫格拉内特（Granett）的，出事那晚正同汉纳福德在一处。这位先生就是当时身在地铁列车里、亲眼看着汉纳福德死去的人；他还看见格拉内特——照此人的说法——跑出去请医生。”

“我想，这位就是海瑟维克先生吧，”药剂师说着，客气地欠了欠身。“哎，正是！”

“看来验尸审讯的报道您也读过了，”马瑟菲尔德含笑道。“很好。正像我说的，格拉内特后来被发现死了。我在他床边发现了一只药瓶和一只玻璃杯。两样里原先都盛过威士忌，可据医学专家们说，里头还下过毒——他们说，那痕迹确凿无疑。喏，那只药瓶上贴着两张撕裂的标签——上面那张，您从这张摹真照片上看得出，原是写着一个姓名的——如今只剩下教名的起首字母 C. 和姓氏的头一个字母 A. ，其余的一概没了。而我想知道的是——那瓶子里盛的东西，是药水也好，补药也好，或是别的什么也好，究竟是不是您配制的？倘若是，您给配的那位主顾是谁——他的教名以 C. 起头，姓氏以 A. 起头？您听明白了么？”

“哎，哎，马瑟菲尔德先生！”药剂师急切地答道。“您说的每一个字我都领会的，而且句句清楚明白。我愿意把我力所能及的情况全数奉告，只是头里我自己想先讨一点情况，是一桩顶关紧要的事。喏，这一两天的报纸您要是看过，马瑟菲尔德先生，您就知道，林肯律师学院广场有家律师事务所，正悬赏五千镑——”

===Original===
“I’m well enough aware of it, Mr. Macpherson,” interrupted Matherfield with a laugh and a sly glance at Hetherwick. “Mr. Hetherwick and myself have just come straight from their office, and what you want to know is﻿—if you give me information will it be the same thing as giving it to them? You want to make sure about the reward?”

“Precisely, Mr. Matherfield, precisely!” assented the chemist eagerly. “You’ve hit my meaning exactly. For, of course, when there’s a reward like yon﻿—”

“If you give us information, Mr. Macpherson, that’ll lead to the arrest and conviction of the guilty party, you can rest assured you’ll get that reward,” said Matherfield. “And Mr. Hetherwick’ll support me in that, I’m sure.”

“I’m satisfied﻿—I’m satisfied, gentlemen!” exclaimed Macpherson, as Hetherwick murmured his confirmation. “Well, it’s a strange, black business, and I’d no idea that I would come to be associated with it until that man of yours called in this morning, Mr. Matherfield. But then I knew! And I’ll shorten matters by telling you, at once﻿—I made up the tonic that was in that bottle!”
===Chinese===
“这个我知道得够清楚的，麦克弗森先生，”马瑟菲尔德笑着打断道，还朝海瑟维克狡黠地瞟了一眼。“海瑟维克先生和我刚从他们的事务所径直过来。您想知道的是——您把情况给了我，是不是就等于给了他们？您想把那笔赏格落实了？”

“正是，马瑟菲尔德先生，正是！”药剂师急切地附和道。“您把我的意思一点就透。因为，当然啦，逢着那样一笔赏格——”

“麦克弗森先生，只要您向我们提供的情况能叫那正凶落网定罪，那笔赏格您放心，准拿到手，”马瑟菲尔德说。“这一点海瑟维克先生也会替我担着的，我信得过。”

“我满意了——我满意了，两位先生！”海瑟维克低声附和确认时，麦克弗森扬声道。“唉，这是桩离奇的、见不得天日的勾当，您那位手下今天早上进店之前，我压根儿想不到自己会同它沾上边，马瑟菲尔德先生。可那一来，我心里就有数了！我也不绕弯子，这就直截了当告诉你们——那只瓶里的补药，就是我配的！”

===Original===
Matherfield rubbed his hands.

“Good!” he said quietly. “Good! And now, then﻿—the critical question! For whom?”

“For a Dr. Charles Ambrose, from a prescription of his own,” replied Macpherson. “It’s a sort of pick-me-up tonic. I first made it up for him two years ago; I’ve made it up for him several times since. The last occasion was about six weeks ago. I have all the dates, though, in my books; I can show you them.”

“Wait a bit,” said Matherfield. “That’s of no great importance﻿—yet. Dr. Charles Ambrose, eh? Have you his address?”
===Chinese===
马瑟菲尔德搓了搓手。

“好！”他轻声道。“好！那么现在——要害的问题！给谁配的？”

“给一位查尔斯·安布罗斯医生（Dr. Charles Ambrose），照他自己开的方子，”麦克弗森答道。“那是一种提神的补药。我头一回给他配是两年前；打那以后又给他配过好几回。末一回约莫是六个星期之前。不过，各回的日子我账上全有；我可以拿给你们看。”

“等一等，”马瑟菲尔德说。“眼下这还不要紧。查尔斯·安布罗斯医生，嗯？您有他的地址么？”

===Original===
“Aye, to be sure!” answered the chemist. “His address is 38, Number 59, John Street.”

“Adelphi!” suggested Matherfield.

“Adelphi, precisely﻿—38, Number 59, John Street, Adelphi,” repeated Macpherson. “That’s in the books, too.”

Matherfield suddenly became silent, staring at the floor. When he looked up again it was at Hetherwick.
===Chinese===
“哎，那自然！”药剂师答道。“他的地址是约翰街（John Street） 59 号 38 室。”

“阿德尔菲（Adelphi）！”马瑟菲尔德提点道。

“阿德尔菲，一毫也不错——约翰街 59 号 38 室，阿德尔菲，”麦克弗森复述道。“这个账上也记着。”

马瑟菲尔德忽然不作声了，眼睛盯着地板。待他再抬起头来，望的是海瑟维克。

===Original===
“Didn’t Granett exclaim that he knew of a doctor, close by, when he rushed out of that train at Charing Cross Underground?” he asked. “Gave the impression that he knew of one close by, anyway?”

“He said distinctly close by,” answered Hetherwick. “Why, are you thinking﻿—”

Matherfield interrupted him with a wave of the hand, and turned again to the chemist. “You’ve seen this Dr. Charles Ambrose?” he asked abruptly.

“Oh, I have, Mr. Matherfield, many a time and often,” replied Macpherson. “But now I come to think of it, not lately.”
===Chinese===
“格拉内特在那班地铁列车抵达查令十字（Charing Cross）站时冲出车厢，不是嚷嚷过他认得一位大夫、就在左近么？”他问道。“反正，他给人的印象是，左近就有他认得的一位大夫？”

“他明明白白说的是就在左近，”海瑟维克答道。“怎么，你是想——”

马瑟菲尔德摆了摆手打断他，又转向药剂师。“这位查尔斯·安布罗斯医生，您见过？”他冷不防问道。

“哦，见过，马瑟菲尔德先生，见过不知多少回，”麦克弗森答道。“不过现在想来，近来倒没有见过。”

===Original===
“When﻿—last?” demanded Matherfield.

“I should think last when he called in and told me to make him another bottle of his tonic,” answered Macpherson, after some thought. “As I said just now, perhaps about six weeks ago. But the books﻿—”

“Never mind the books yet. What’s this Dr. Charles Ambrose like?”

“A tall, handsome man, distinguished-looking﻿—I should say about forty years of age. A dark man﻿—hair, eyes, beard. He wears his moustache and beard in﻿—well, a sort of foreign fashion; in fact, he’s more like a Spaniard than an Englishman.”
===Chinese===
“最后一回——是什么时候？”马瑟菲尔德追问。

“我想，最后一回是他进店来，叫我再给他配一瓶那补药，”麦克弗森想了一想，答道。“就像我刚才说的，约莫六个星期之前吧。不过，账上——”

“账上且慢说。这位查尔斯·安布罗斯医生是个什么样的人？”

“高高的个子，相貌堂堂，气度不凡——依我看，四十岁上下。是个深色的人——头发、眼睛、胡须都是深的。他的髭须和胡子的样式——嗯，带点外国派头；说实在的，他与其说像个英国人，不如说像个西班牙人。”

===Original===
“But﻿—is he an Englishman?”

“He was always taken by me for an Englishman; he speaks like one﻿—that is, like an Englishman of the upper classes. He once told me he was an Oxford man﻿—we’d been talking about universities.”

“Well-dressed man?”

“Aye, he was that! A smart, fine man.”
===Chinese===
“可是——他到底是英国人么？”

“在我，他一向被当作英国人；他说话像英国人——就是说，像上等阶级的英国人。有一回他告诉我，他是个牛津人——那会儿我们正聊着大学。”

“穿着讲究么？”

“哎，讲究得很！是个漂亮体面的人物。”

===Original===
“Did you ever see him in a big, dark overcoat, with a large white silk muffler about his neck and the lower part of his face?”

“Aye, I’ve seen him like that! On chilly evenings. Indeed, that’s another thing he told me﻿—he was subject to bronchial attacks.”

“Muffled himself well up, eh?” suggested Matherfield.

“Aye, just so! He’s been in here like that.”
===Chinese===
“您可曾见过他穿一件深色大外套，脖子和脸的下半截围着一大条白绸围巾？”

“哎，见过他那样儿！在寒凉的傍晚。说真的，他还告诉过我另一件事——他动不动就犯支气管的毛病。”

“把自己裹得严严实实的，嗯？”马瑟菲尔德提点道。

“哎，正是！他就那样儿进过我这店。”

===Original===
Matherfield turned to Hetherwick with a significant glance.

“That’s the man who met Hannaford at Victoria Station that night!﻿—the man that Ledbitter saw, and that nobody’s seen since!” he exclaimed. “A million to one on it! Now then, who is he?”

“You know his name and his address,” remarked Hetherwick.

“Yes﻿—and I know, too, that Mr. Macpherson here hasn’t seen him lately!” retorted Matherfield dryly. “How often, now, Mr. Macpherson, did you use to see him? I mean, did you use to see him at other times than when he came into your shop?”
===Chinese===
马瑟菲尔德意味深长地看了海瑟维克一眼。

“这就是那晚在维多利亚车站（Victoria Station）接汉纳福德的人！——就是莱德比特（Lebitter）见过的、从此再没人见过的人！”他叫道。“这一点，我敢拿一百万对一打赌！那么，他究竟是谁？”

“他的名字和地址，你不是都知道了么，”海瑟维克说道。

“是——我还知道，眼下这位麦克弗森先生近来没见过他呢！”马瑟菲尔德干巴巴地反诘。“那么我问你，麦克弗森先生，往常你隔多久见他一回？我是说——除了他进店来的时候，别的场合你也常见着他么？”

===Original===
“Oh, yes! I’ve seen him in the street, outside,” replied the chemist. “I’ve seen him, too, going in and out of Rule’s, and in and out of Romano’s.”

“In other words,” remarked Matherfield, “he was pretty well known about this end of the Strand. I’m not sure, now, that I don’t remember such a man myself﻿—black, silky, carefully-trimmed beard, always a big swell. But﻿—Mr. Macpherson hasn’t seen him lately! Hm! Do you know if he was in practice, Mr. Macpherson?”

“I could not say as to that, Mr. Matherfield. Seeing that he called himself Dr. Ambrose, I supposed he was a medical practitioner, but I don’t know what his degrees or qualifications were at all.”

Matherfield glanced at a row of books which stood over a desk at the side of the parlour.
===Chinese===
“哦，常见！在外头街上我就见过他，”药剂师答道。“我还见过他进出鲁尔餐馆（Rule’s），进出罗马诺餐馆（Romano’s）。”

“换句话说，”马瑟菲尔德说道，“在斯特兰德街（the Strand）这一头，他算得是颇有几分熟脸了。这么一说，没准儿我自己也记得这么一个人——黑黑的、亮亮的胡子，修剪得整整齐齐，向来一副阔气的派头。可是——麦克弗森先生近来没见过他！唔！麦克弗森先生，您可知道他挂牌行医么？”

“这我说不上，马瑟菲尔德先生。他既自称安布罗斯医生，我自然当他是位行医的，可他有什么学位、什么资历，我压根儿不知道。”

马瑟菲尔德瞥了一眼私室侧壁一张书桌上立着的一排书。

===Original===
“Have you got an up-to-date medical directory?” he asked. “Good! Let’s look the man up. You turn up his name, Mr. Hetherwick,” he went on as the chemist handed down a volume; “you’re more used to books than I am. Find out if there’s anything about him.”

Hetherwick turned over the pages of the directory, and presently shook his head.

“There’s no Charles Ambrose here,” he said. “Look for yourselves.”

Matherfield glanced at the place indicated and said nothing. Macpherson made an exclamation of surprise.
===Chinese===
“您这儿有最新版的医学人名录么？”他问道。“好！咱们查查这个人。海瑟维克先生，你来翻他的名字，”药剂师取下一册书来，他接着说，“你比我惯于查书。查查上头有没有他的条目。”

海瑟维克一页页翻着那部名录，不多时摇了摇头。

“上头没有查尔斯·安布罗斯，”他说。“你们自己看吧。”

马瑟菲尔德朝所指的地方看了一眼，一言不发。麦克弗森却发出一声惊呼。

===Original===
“Aye, well, he may be a foreigner, after all,” he observed. “But I shouldn’t have considered him one, and he certainly told me he was an Oxford graduate.”

“Foreigner or Oxforder, I’m going to know more about him!” declared Matherfield, rising and grasping his stick with an air of determination. “Well, Mr. Macpherson, we’re obliged to you, and if this results in anything﻿—you know! But for the moment﻿—a bit of that caution that you Scotsmen are famous for﻿—eh?”

Outside, Matherfield laid his hand on Hetherwick’s elbow.

“Mr. Hetherwick,” he said solemnly, “we’re on the track﻿—at last! Sure as my name’s Matherfield, we’ve hit the trail! Now we’re going to John Street, Adelphi﻿—and I’ll lay you anything you like that the man’s vanished!”
===Chinese===
“哎，唉，说到底，他兴许真是个外国人，”他评说道。“可我原先怎么也不觉得他像，况且他分明告诉过我，他是牛津的毕业生。”

“外国人也罢，牛津人也罢，我非要再摸出他一些底细不可！”马瑟菲尔德宣告道，站起身来，一把握住手杖，一副斩钉截铁的神气。“好了，麦克弗森先生，我们承你的情，这事若有个结果——你明白的！只是眼下——苏格兰人素来著称的那份谨慎，还得请您拿出几分来——嗯？”

到了外头，马瑟菲尔德把手搭在海瑟维克的臂肘上。

“海瑟维克先生，”他郑重其事地说，“咱们踩上道儿了——到底踩上了！正如我叫马瑟菲尔德一样错不了，咱们是寻着踪迹了！现在咱们这就去阿德尔菲的约翰街——随你押什么，我敢跟你赌——那人准已经没影儿了！”

## XXVI Waterloo / 二十六 滑铁卢

===Original===

The detective, walking a little in advance of his companion, stepped forward to a hall-table and knocked loudly on its polished surface. No answer came. He went further along, to the head of a railed stair which evidently communicated with a cellar kitchen; again he knocked, more loudly than before, on an adjacent panel, and again got no reply. And at that, turning back along the hall, he opened the door of the room which faced upon the street, and he and Hetherwick looked in. A musty-smelling, close-curtained room that, a sort of Sunday parlour, little used, cold and comfortless in its formality. But the room behind it, to which Robmore turned next, showed signs of recent occupancy and life. There was a fire in the grate, with an easy chair drawn near to it; on the table close by lay women’s gear﻿—a heap of linen, with needle and thread thrust in, a workbasket, scissors, thimble; it required no more than a glance to see that the owner of these innocent matters had laid them down suddenly, suddenly interrupted in her task.

“I’ll tell you what it is, Mr. Hetherwick!” exclaimed Robmore abruptly. “This house is empty! Empty of people, anyway.”

“Silent enough, to be sure,” agreed Hetherwick. “The woman﻿—”

“You’ve frightened her by calling here,” said Robmore. “Then she slipped round to Pencove Street. And there she found Ambrose dead! She’s some connection with him and Baseverie, because she possesses a key that admits to that yard. And finding Ambrose dead, she came back here, got her things and cleared out. There isn’t a soul in this house. I’ll lay anything on that!”

“It struck me that this might be the place where the two ladies were detained,” remarked Hetherwick.

“We’ll soon see about that,” declared Robmore. “Come upstairs﻿—we’ll search the place from top to bottom. But stop, downstairs first.”

===Chinese===

侦探走在同伴前面几步，径直走到一张门厅桌前，在光亮的桌面上重重敲了几下。无人应声。他再往前去，来到一道装着栏杆的楼梯口，那楼梯显然通向地下室的厨房；他又在近旁的门板上敲了一回，敲得比方才更响，依旧无人答应。于是他转身顺着门厅折回来，推开临街那间屋子的门，同海瑟维克往里观看。那是一间霉味刺鼻、窗帘紧闭的屋子，类似星期日会客用的客厅，难得启用，陈设拘板，又冷又不舒服。可它后头那间屋子——罗布莫尔接着转向的那间——却留着新近住人过活的痕迹。炉栅里生着火，近旁拉近一把安乐椅；手边的桌上摊着女人的活计——一堆亚麻衣物，针线还插在上面，另有针线篮、剪刀、顶针；一眼便看得出，这些清白物事的主人放下得十分仓促，是干活干到一半突然被打断的。

“我这就跟您直说了吧，海瑟维克先生！”罗布莫尔突然嚷道，“这房子是空的！反正人是一个不剩。”

“静是够静的，没错，”海瑟维克附和道，“那个女人——”

“您上门来找人，把她吓着了，”罗布莫尔说，“随后她便溜到彭科夫街那边去了。在那儿，她发现安布罗斯死了！她跟他和巴斯维里都有牵连，因为她手里有一把能开那院子的钥匙。发现了安布罗斯的死，她回到这儿，收拾起东西一走了之。这宅子里连一个魂儿都没有。这一点，我拿什么都敢打赌！”

“我倒想到，这里没准就是囚禁那两位小姐的地方。”海瑟维克说道。

“这个嘛，咱们很快就能弄个水落石出，”罗布莫尔宣称，“上楼吧——咱们把这宅子上上下下搜个遍。不过慢着，先下楼。”

===Original===

He ran down the stair to the cellar kitchen, with Hetherwick at his heels. And at the door he laughed, pointing within.

“Look there!” he exclaimed. “I told you you’d interrupted things. See! there’s one tea-tray, laid out all ready for two﻿—cups and saucers, teapot, bread and butter cut, cake. There’s another for one. And there’s the kettle, singing away like a bird on a bough. What’s that mean? The woman was going to carry up tea for two, somewhere; t’other tray was for herself. Well, you nipped that in the bud; she’ll have to get her tea somewhere. But﻿—the others? Come upstairs.”

Going back to the hall, he led the way up the main staircase. There were two stories above the ground floor; on the first were rooms the doors of which, being opened, or being found open, revealed nothing but ordinary things: of these rooms there were three, opening off a main landing. But on the next floor there were only two rooms; one was unfurnished: at the door of the other, a few inches ajar, the detective immediately paused.

“Look you there, now, Mr. Hetherwick!” he said, pointing here and there. “Here’s recent work! Do you see that a strong bolt, more like a bar, has been fitted on the outside of this door, and the door itself fitted with a new patent lock, key outside? And, good Lord! a chain as well. Might be in a gaol! But what’s inside?”

He pushed the door open and revealed a large room, fitted with two small beds, easy chairs, a table on which books, magazines, newspapers lay; on the table, too, was fancywork which, it was evident, had been as hastily laid aside as the sewing downstairs. Hetherwick bent over the things, but Robmore went to the one window.

“Gaol, did I say?” he exclaimed. “Why, this is a gaol! Look here, Mr. Hetherwick!﻿—window morticed inside and fitted with iron bars outside. Even if whoever’s been in here could have opened the window, and if there’d been no bars there, they couldn’t have done anything though, for there’s nothing but a high blank wall opposite﻿—back of some factory or other, apparently. But what’s this?” he added, opening a door that stood in a corner. “Um! small bathroom. And this,” he continued, going to a square hatch set in the wall next to the staircase. “Ah! trap big enough to hand things like small trays through, but not big enough for a grown person to squeeze through. Well, I shouldn’t wonder if you’re right, Mr. Hetherwick﻿—this, probably, is where these ladies were locked up. But﻿—they’re gone!”

===Chinese===

他跑下楼梯进了地下室厨房，海瑟维克紧随其后。到了门口，他笑了起来，朝里面指了指。

“瞧那儿！”他喊道，“我说过吧，你们把人家的事儿给搅了。看！这儿有一副茶盘，齐齐整整摆的是两个人的份——茶杯茶托、茶壶、切好的黄油面包、蛋糕。那边还有一副，是一个人的。水壶正唱得起劲呢，活像枝头一只鸟。这说明什么？那女人正要把两个人的茶送到什么地方去；另一盘是她自己的。嘿，这茬儿叫你们掐了头，她的茶只好上别处喝了。可是——另外那几位呢？上楼吧。”

回到门厅，他领头上了主楼梯。地面以上还有两层：第一层的几个房间，把门打开——或者说发现门本就敞着——里头无非些寻常物件；这样的房间共有三间，都开向一方大楼梯厅。可再上一层只有两个房间：一间没有家具；另一间的门虚掩着几英寸宽，侦探立刻在门前停下了脚步。

“喏，您瞧这儿，海瑟维克先生！”他说，东指一下西指一下，“这可是新近干的活儿！您看见没有，这扇门的外头装了一根粗门闩，简直像根门杠，门上本身还装了把新式专利锁，锁眼开在外头。还有，老天爷！另加一条链子。跟蹲大牢似的！可里头到底是什么？”

他推开门，露出一间大屋子，摆着两张小床、几把安乐椅，一张桌上摊着书报杂志；桌上还有针线活，看得出同楼下的缝纫一样，是被匆匆撂下的。海瑟维克俯身细看那些东西，罗布莫尔却走向那扇仅有的窗子。

“我方才说牢房？”他喊道，“嘿，这不就是牢房嘛！瞧这儿，海瑟维克先生！——窗子在里面用暗闩闩死，外头还加了铁条。就算关在这儿的人开得成窗，就算没有铁条，也是白搭，因为对面只有一堵光秃秃的高墙——看样子是哪家工厂的后身。可这是什么？”他一边说，一边推开立在角落里的一扇门。“嗯！一间小浴室。还有这个，”他接着说，走到嵌在楼梯旁墙上的一方传递口前。“啊！这口子递个托盘之类的小东西绰绰有余，大人可钻不过去。嗯，海瑟维克先生，您要说对了也不奇怪——这儿八成就是囚禁那两位小姐的地方。可是——人没了！”

===Original===

Hetherwick was looking round. Suddenly his eyes lighted on a familiar object. He stepped forward, and from a chair near one of the beds, picked up a handbag of green silk. He knew it well enough.

“That settles it!” he exclaimed. “They have been here! This is Miss Han﻿—I mean Miss Featherstone’s bag﻿—I’ve seen her carry it often. These are her things in it﻿—purse, card-case, so on. She’s left it behind her.”

“Aye, just so!” agreed Robmore. “As I say, they all left in a hurry. I figure it out like this: the woman, who, of course, acted as sort of gaoler to these two unfortunate ladies, when she made that discovery round yonder, came back here, got her outdoor things, and cleared off. But before she went, she’d the decency to slip up here, undo that chain, slip the bolt back, and turn the key! Then, no doubt, she made tracks at express speed, leaving the ladies to do what they liked. And they, Mr. Hetherwick, having a bit o’ common sense about ’em, did what I should ha’ done﻿—they hooked it as quick as possible. That’s that, sir!”

Hetherwick thrust Rhona’s handbag into his pocket and made for the door.

“Then I’m off, Robmore,” he said. “I must try to find out where they’ve gone. I’ve an idea probably they’d go to Penteney’s office. I’ll go there. But﻿—you?”

===Chinese===

海瑟维克正四下打量。忽然，他的目光落在一件事物上。他抢步上前，从一张小床边的椅子上拿起一只绿丝绸手提包。这包他再熟悉不过了。

“这下定案了！”他叫道，“她们来过这儿！这是汉——我是说费瑟斯通小姐的提包——我常见她带着它。里头都是她的东西——钱袋、名片盒，等等。她把它落在身后了。”

“是啊，正是这么回事！”罗布莫尔深表赞同，“照我说，他们全是急急忙忙走的。我是这么盘算的：那女人，自然是给这两位倒霉小姐充当牢头的，她到那边撞见了那桩发现，便回到这儿，取了出门的衣物，一走了之。不过临走之前，她倒还讲究体面，摸上楼来，解开链子，抽回门闩，把钥匙一转！然后呢，不用说，拔腿便如飞而去，任凭两位小姐爱怎么办怎么办。她们呢，海瑟维克先生，脑瓜里到底还有几分见识，做了换作我也会做的事——三十六计，走为上计，越快越好。就是这么回事，先生！”

海瑟维克把罗娜的手提包塞进口袋，朝门口走去。

“那我这就动身了，罗布莫尔，”他说，“我得设法打听她们去了哪儿。我料想她们多半会去彭特尼的事务所。我就到那儿去。可你——你呢？”

===Original===

“Oh, I’m going back to Pencove Street,” answered Robmore. “Plenty to do there. But off you go after the ladies, Mr. Hetherwick, there’s nothing you can do round here now. I’ll keep that clerk of yours a bit, and the Jew chap﻿—they might come in. We shall have some nice revelations in the papers tomorrow, I’m thinking, especially if Matherfield has the luck he expects.”

“What are you going to do about this house?” asked Hetherwick as they went downstairs. “Do you think the woman will come back?”

“Bet your life she won’t!” answered Robmore. “Not she! I should think she’s halfway across London﻿—north, south, east or west, by this. House? Why, I shall just lock the front door and put the key in my pocket. We shall want to search this house narrowly.”

Hetherwick bade him good day for the time being, and hurried off to Victoria Street, to fling himself into the first disengaged taxicab he encountered, and to bid its driver go as speedily as possible to Lincoln’s Inn Fields. He was anxious about Rhona﻿—and yet he felt that she was safe. And he was inquisitive, too; he wanted to hear her story, to find out what had happened behind the scenes. He felt sure of finding her at Penteney’s office; she and Madame Listorelle, once released from their prison, would naturally go there.

But the clerk whom he encountered as soon as he rushed into the outer office, damped his spirits at once by shaking his head.

“Mr. Penteney’s not in, sir,” he answered. “He was in until not so long ago, but he got a telephone call and went out immediately afterwards. No, I don’t know who it was that rang him up, Mr. Hetherwick, nor where he went; seemed a bit excited when he went out, and was in a fearful hurry.”

===Chinese===

“哦，我回彭科夫街去，”罗布莫尔答道，“那边有的是事要办。您呢，只管去追那两位小姐，海瑟维克先生，这会儿您在这一带帮不上什么忙了。您那位办事员，还有那个犹太小伙子，我要留他们一阵——兴许用得上。我想，明天报纸上会有几样精彩的披露，尤其是马瑟菲尔德要是交上他指望的那份运气的话。”

“这宅子您打算怎么办？”下楼时海瑟维克问道，“您当那女人还会回来？”

“她要回来才怪呢！”罗布莫尔答道，“她才不会！我估摸这会儿她已经跑到伦敦半道上去了——东西南北，说不准哪一头。宅子？嗐，我把前门一锁，钥匙往兜里一揣，不就结了。这宅子咱们往后还得细细搜查呢。”

海瑟维克暂且与他道了别，急匆匆赶到维多利亚街，一头钻进遇上的头一辆空出租车，吩咐司机尽快开往林肯律师学院广场。他惦记着罗娜——却又觉得她平安无事。他也按捺不住好奇：他想听她讲述经过，弄清幕后到底发生了什么。他确信能在彭特尼的事务所找到她；她和里斯托雷尔太太一旦脱出那座牢笼，自然会到那里去。

可他一冲进外间办公室就撞见的那位办事员，却摇了摇头，顿时浇灭了他的兴头。

“彭特尼先生不在，先生，”对方答道，“前不久他还在，可来了个电话，随后他马上就出去了。不，是谁打给他的，我不知道，海瑟维克先生，他去了哪儿我也不知道；他出去的时候像是有点激动，急得要命。”

===Original===

Hetherwick concluded that Madame Listorelle had summoned Penteney, and that he had gone to meet her and Rhona. He went away, somewhat at a loss﻿—then, remembering that Matherfield had promised to wire from Southampton, he turned towards his chambers. At the foot of the stairs he met his caretaker.

“Been a young lady here inquiring for you, Mr. Hetherwick,” said the man. “Been here twice. I said I didn’t know when you’d be in﻿—any time or no time. She said﻿—but there is the young lady, sir﻿—coming back!”

Hetherwick turned sharply and saw Rhona coming across the square. Hurrying to meet her and disregarding whatever eyes might be watching them, he took both her hands in his in a fashion that brought the colour to her cheeks.

“You’re all right﻿—safe?” he asked quickly.

“Sure?”

“I’m all right and quite safe, thank you,” she answered. “I﻿—I’ve been here twice before, but you were out. I came to borrow some money. I left my bag and purse in﻿—the place where we were locked up, and﻿—”

===Chinese===

海瑟维克断定是里斯托雷尔太太把彭特尼召了去，他找她和罗娜会合了。他离开那里，一时心里没底——随即想起马瑟菲尔德答应过从南安普顿来电报，便转身朝自己的寓所走去。在楼梯脚下，他遇见了替他照管屋子的门房。

“有位年轻小姐来找过您，海瑟维克先生，”那人说，“来过两回了。我说不准您什么时候在——早晚没个准儿。她说——哟，先生，那不就是那位小姐——又回来了！”

海瑟维克霍地转身，看见罗娜正穿过广场走来。他急忙迎上前去，全不顾有什么眼睛在观望，一把握住她的双手，那副情状叫她双颊泛起了红晕。

“你没事吧——平安吗？”他急急问道。

“当真？”

“我没事，也很平安，谢谢你，”她答道，“我——我先前来过两回，你都不在家。我是来借点钱的。我把提包和钱袋丢在——我们被关押的那个地方了，还有——”

===Original===

Hetherwick pulled out the handbag and silently gave it to her. She stared at him.

“You’ve been﻿—there!” she exclaimed. “How﻿—”

“Got in this afternoon, an hour ago,” he answered. “Here, come up to my rooms! We can’t stand talking here. Madame Listorelle﻿—where’s she?”

“I left her at Victoria, telephoning to Major Penteney,” replied Rhona. “She, too, had no money. She wanted me to wait until Major Penteney arrived, but I wouldn’t. I walked here. I﻿—I thought you’d want to know that we’d got out﻿—at last.”

Hetherwick said nothing until they had entered his sitting-room. Then, staring silently at her, he put his hands on Rhona’s shoulders, and after a long look at her, suddenly and impulsively bent and kissed her.

“By gad!” he said in a low voice. “I didn’t know how anxious I was about you until I saw you just now! But﻿—now I know!”

===Chinese===

海瑟维克掏出手提包，一声不响地递给她。她怔怔地望着他。

“你去过——那儿了！”她叫道，“怎么会——”

“今天下午进去的，一个钟头前的事，”他答道，“来，上我屋里去！咱们总不能站在这儿说话。里斯托雷尔太太——她在哪儿？”

“我把她留在维多利亚车站了，她正给彭特尼少校打电话，”罗娜答道，“她身上也没有钱。她要我等彭特尼少校来了再走，可我不肯。我一路走了来。我——我想，你总该想知道，我们总算——脱身了。”

进了起居室，海瑟维克才开口。他默默凝视着她，把双手搭上罗娜的肩头，久久地端详了她一阵，突然按捺不住，俯身吻了她。

“老天作证！”他低声说，“方才一见到你，我才知道自己有多替你悬着心！可是——如今我知道了！”

===Original===

Then, just as suddenly, he turned away from her, and in a matter-of-fact manner lighted his stove, put on a kettle of water, and began preparations which indicated his intention of making tea. Rhona, from an easy chair into which he had unceremoniously thrust her, watched him.

“Liberty!” she said suddenly. “We’re both discovering something. When you’ve been locked up, day and night, for a while﻿—”

“How was it?” he asked, turning on her. “Of course, we know all about the kidnapping﻿—but the rest, until today? Baseverie, of course?”

“Baseverie and another man,” she answered. “A tall, clean-shaven man, whose name we never heard. But Baseverie was the chief villain. As to how it was, they met us at the sunk road at Riversreade, forced us at the point of revolvers into a car, and drove us off to London﻿—to Westminster﻿—and into a house there, the house you’ve been in. There﻿—”

“A moment,” said Hetherwick, who was finding cups and saucers. “The driver of that car? He must have been an accomplice.”

“No doubt, but we never saw him again. We only saw those two and a woman who acted as gaoler and brought our meals. We were fed all right, and they gave us books and papers, and actually provided us with fancy work. But they were inexorable about madame and her jewels. They must have known all about them, because they got her own notepaper﻿—”

===Chinese===

紧接着，他又同样突然地转过身去，一副就事论事的做派，点起炉子，坐上一壶水，动手张罗起来，摆明了是要沏茶。罗娜被他不由分说按进一把安乐椅里，坐在那儿望着他。

“自由！”她突然说道，“咱们俩都算长了一番见识。一个人日日夜夜被关上一阵子之后——”

“到底是怎么回事？”他转过身来问，“绑架的经过我们自然一清二楚——可其余的呢，直到今天的那些？自然是巴斯维里吧？”

“巴斯维里，还有另一个男人，”她答道，“一个高个子、脸刮得干干净净的男人，我们始终没听说过他的名字。可罪魁祸首是巴斯维里。至于经过，他们在里弗斯雷德的凹路上截住了我们，用手枪逼我们上了汽车，把我们载到伦敦——载到威斯敏斯特——弄进那儿的一所房子，就是你进去过的那所。在那儿——”

“慢着，”海瑟维克说，他正翻找茶杯茶托，“那辆汽车的司机呢？他准是同伙。”

“想必是，可我们再没见过他。我们只见着那两个人，还有一个女人，她充当牢头，给我们送饭。饭食倒不曾亏待，他们还给我们书报，居然还备下了针线活。可对太太和她的珠宝，他们毫不通融。珠宝的底细他们必定一清二楚，因为他们弄到了她自用的信纸——”

===Original===

“I know all about that,” said Hetherwick. “I’ll tell you my side of it when you’ve had some tea. Forced her, I suppose, to write the letters?”

“They forced her to do that just as they forced us into the car,” said Rhona, “with revolvers! And﻿—they meant it. I suppose they’ve got the jewels now?”

“Remains to be seen,” replied Hetherwick. “Did Madame Listorelle happen to tell you what those jewels were worth?”

“She talked about little else. Between eighty and ninety thousand pounds. She’s in an awful state about them. But it was literally a question of her life or her jewels. I don’t know what they’d have done with me. But now﻿—I’m all right!”

Hetherwick opened a tin box, and producing a plum cake, held it up for Rhona to inspect.

“What d’you think of that for a cake?” he asked admiringly. “Present from my old aunt in the country﻿—real, proper cake that. Yes,” he went on, setting the cake on the table, “yes, yes; you’re all right now. But, by George﻿—”

===Chinese===

“这些我全知道了，”海瑟维克说，“等你喝过茶，我把这一头的事情讲给你听。那几封信，我料想是逼她写的吧？”

“逼她写信，同逼我们上汽车是一路的手法，”罗娜说，“拿手枪逼的！而且——他们可不是虚张声势。我猜，如今珠宝已经落到他们手里了吧？”

“那还得见分晓，”海瑟维克答道，“里斯托雷尔太太可曾碰巧对你说过，那些珠宝值多少钱？”

“她别的不大谈，谈的净是这个。八万到九万英镑。她为这些珠宝简直急坏了。可那实实在在是要命还是要珠宝的问题。他们打算怎么处置我，我不知道。不过如今——我没事了！”

海瑟维克打开一只铁皮盒，取出一块葡萄干蛋糕，举起来给罗娜过目。

“这样的蛋糕，你说怎么样？”他带着得意的口气问道，“乡下老姑母送的——地地道道、正儿八经的蛋糕。是啊，”他一面把蛋糕摆到桌上，一面接着说，“是啊，是啊；你如今没事了。可是，我的天——”

===Original===

Rhona said nothing; she saw that his relief at seeing her was greater and deeper than he cared to show. She poured out the tea; they sat discussing the recent events until dusky shadows began to fall over the whole room.

“I ought to be getting back to Riversreade,” she remarked at last. “It’s late.”

“Wait a bit!” said Hetherwick, who by that time had told her all he knew. “There’ll be a wire from Matherfield before long. Don’t go down to Riversreade tonight. Telephone to Lady Riversreade that you’re staying in town. Her sister will be there by now, and will have told her everything. Wait till we get the wire from Matherfield; then we’ll go and dine somewhere, and you can put up at your old hotel in Surrey Street for the night. I want you to know what’s happened at Southampton and﻿—”

He broke off as a knock came at his outer door.

“That’ll be Matherfield’s wire,” he exclaimed. “Now then﻿—”

A moment later he came back to her with the message in his hand.

===Chinese===

罗娜没有作声；她看得出，他见她平安而生的宽慰，比他肯表露的更深更切。她斟了茶；两人坐着议论近来的种种事端，直到昏暝的暗影渐渐罩满了整间屋子。

“我该回里弗斯雷德去了，”她终于说道，“天不早了。”

“再等一等！”海瑟维克说，这时他已把自己知道的一切都告诉了她，“马瑟菲尔德的电报说话就到。今晚别回里弗斯雷德。给里弗斯雷德夫人打个电话，就说你留在城里。她的姐姐这会儿该到那边了，会把一切都告诉她。等接到马瑟菲尔德的电报，咱们找个地方吃晚饭，你今夜就在萨里街的老旅馆下榻。我要让你知道南安普顿那边出了什么事，还有——”

话说到一半，外门上响起一阵敲门声。

“准是马瑟菲尔德的电报，”他叫道，“这就——”

片刻之后，他手里拿着电报回到她跟前。

===Original===

“It is from Matherfield,” he said. “Handed in Southampton West six-nineteen. Doesn’t say if he’s got him! All he says is; ‘Meet me Waterloo, arriving eight-twenty.’ Well﻿—”

“I wonder?” said Rhona. “But Baseverie is﻿—”

“Just what Robmore says,” muttered Hetherwick.

“However﻿—” he looked at his watch. “Come along,” he continued. “We’ve just time to get some dinner﻿—at Waterloo﻿—and to be on the platform when the eight-twenty comes in. If only we could see Baseverie in charge of Matherfield and Quigman first it would give me an appetite!”

The vast space between the station buildings and the entrance to the platform at Waterloo was thronged when Hetherwick and Rhona came out of the restaurant at ten minutes past eight. Hetherwick was inquiring as to which platform the Southampton train would come in at when he felt a light touch on his arm. Turning sharply he saw Robmore. Robmore gave him a quiet smile, coupled with an informing wink.

===Chinese===

“是马瑟菲尔德来的，”他说，“南安普顿西站六点十九分挂发。没说他逮没逮着人！只说了一句：‘到滑铁卢接我，八点二十分到。’唉——”

“不知结果怎样呢？”罗娜说，“可巴斯维里他——”

“跟罗布莫尔说的一模一样，”海瑟维克喃喃道。

“不过——”他看了看表。“走吧，”他接着说，“咱们刚好赶得及吃顿晚饭——就在滑铁卢——还能在八点二十的车进站时赶到站台上。要是能先瞧见巴斯维里落在马瑟菲尔德和奎格曼手里，我保管胃口大开！”

八点十分，海瑟维克和罗娜从餐馆出来时，滑铁卢站站房与站台入口之间那片开阔地已经挤满了人。海瑟维克正打听南安普顿的列车进哪个站台，忽觉有人在他臂上轻轻一触。他霍然回头，看见了罗布莫尔。罗布莫尔朝他安然一笑，附带递来一个心照不宣的眼色。

===Original===

“Guess you’re on the same job, Mr. Hetherwick,” he said. “Wire from Matherfield, eh?”

“Yes,” replied Hetherwick. “And you?”

“Same here,” assented Robmore. “Just to say I was to be here for the eight-twenty﻿—with help,” he added significantly. “I’ve got the help; there’s four of us round about. Heard anything of those ladies, Mr. Hetherwick?”

“Here is one of them,” replied Hetherwick, indicating Rhona. “They’re safe. You’ll hear all about it later. But this business﻿—what do you make of Matherfield’s wire? Has he failed?”

“I’ll tell you what I make of it,” answered Robmore. “I think you’ll find that Baseverie is on the train, with Matherfield and Quigman in close attendance. For some reason of his own, Matherfield means to arrest Baseverie here﻿—here! That’s how I figure it. They’ve seen Baseverie there and decided to follow him back to town. As soon as that train’s in﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我猜您也奔着同一桩差事来的，海瑟维克先生，”他说，“马瑟菲尔德的电报，嗯？”

“正是，”海瑟维克答道，“你呢？”

“彼此彼此，”罗布莫尔应道，“电报只叫我到这儿来接八点二十的车——带着帮手来，”他意味深长地补了一句，“帮手我带来了；咱们一共四个人，散在这一带。那两位小姐有下文吗，海瑟维克先生？”

“这里头就有一位，”海瑟维克答道，指了指罗娜，“她们平安。详情回头您自然知道。可这桩事——马瑟菲尔德那封电报，您怎么看？他失手了？”

“我怎么看，说给您听，”罗布莫尔答道，“我看您会发现，巴斯维里就在那趟车上，马瑟菲尔德和奎格曼寸步不离地盯着他。出于他自己的一点道理，马瑟菲尔德打算在这儿逮捕巴斯维里——就在这儿！我是这么盘算的。他们在那边见了巴斯维里，便决意跟着他回伦敦。只等那趟车一进站——”

===Original===

A sudden, sharp exclamation from Rhona interrupted him and made both men turn to her. She clutched Hetherwick’s arm, at the same time pointing with the other hand across the space behind them.

“Baseverie﻿—himself!” she said. “There﻿—under that clock! See! He’s going towards the gates!”

With a swift and unceremonious gesture Robmore laid a hand on Rhona’s shoulder, twisted her round and drew her amongst a group of bystanders.

“Keep out of sight, miss!” he muttered. “He’ll know you! Now, again﻿—which man. That with the pale face and high hat? I see him. Good to remember, too. All right! Stop here, you two. If he moves in this direction, Mr. Hetherwick, move away anywhere. Wait!”

Robmore slipped away. A moment later they saw him speak to a couple of quiet-looking men, who presently glanced at Baseverie. Hetherwick was watching Baseverie, too. Baseverie, quiet, unconcerned, evidently wholly unsuspicious, had taken up a position at the exit through which the Southampton passengers must emerge; he was smoking a cigar, placidly, with obvious appreciation.

===Chinese===

罗娜忽然一声短促的惊呼打断了他，引得两个男人都朝她转过身去。她一把攥住海瑟维克的手臂，同时用另一只手指着他们身后那片空场。

“巴斯维里——他本人！”她说，“那儿——那座钟底下！看！他正朝检票口那边去！”

罗布莫尔手疾眼快，不容分说地一按罗娜的肩头，把她拧过身去，拉进了一堆围观的人群里。

“躲开别露面，小姐！”他低声道，“他认得您！再来——是哪一个。那个脸色苍白、戴大礼帽的？我瞧见了。这张脸也值得记下。好！你们两位就站在这儿。他要是朝这边来，海瑟维克先生，您就随便往哪儿躲。等着！”

罗布莫尔悄然闪开了。片刻之后，他们看见他跟两个外表沉静的男人搭了话，那两人随即朝巴斯维里瞟了一眼。海瑟维克也在盯着巴斯维里。巴斯维里安然自若，不慌不忙，显然毫无戒备，已在南安普顿旅客必经的出口旁占了个位置；他正抽着一支雪茄，悠悠然，有滋有味。

===Original===

“You’re certain that’s the man?” whispered Hetherwick.

“Baseverie? Positive!” declared Rhona. “As if I could mistake him! I’ve too good reason to remember his whole appearance. But﻿—here! Daring!”

“Well,” said Hetherwick, “something’s going to happen! Keep back﻿—keep well back! We can see things from here without being seen. If he caught sight of you﻿—”

Robmore came strolling back and joined them.

“All right!” he murmured. “Four pairs of eyes, beside ours﻿—that’s three pairs more﻿—on him! My men are close up to him, too. See ’em? One, two, three, four! All round him, though he doesn’t know. I shan’t let him go, whether Matherfield turns up or not. Cool customer, eh?”

===Chinese===

“你拿得准是那个人？”海瑟维克悄声问。

“巴斯维里？千真万确！”罗娜断言，“我怎么会认错他！他的整个模样，我有十足的理由铭记在心。可是——瞧！好大的胆子！”

“好了，”海瑟维克说，“有一场好戏要开场了！退后——退得远远的！咱们在这儿看得见动静，人家却瞧不见咱们。他要是望见了你——”

罗布莫尔踱了回来，同他们会合。

“妥了！”他低声道，“不算咱们这双，另有四双眼睛——多出三双——盯着他呢！我的人也贴近他了。瞧见了吗？一、二、三、四！把他围了个遍，他自己还蒙在鼓里。不管马瑟菲尔德来不来，我都不会放他走。够沉得住气的主儿，嗯？”

===Original===

“The train’s due,” said Hetherwick. He had Rhona’s hand within his arm, and he felt it tremble. “Yes,” he whispered, bending down to her, “that’s how I feel. Tense moment, this. But that scoundrel there﻿—”

Baseverie was glancing at the big clock. He turned from it to the platform behind the gates, looking expectantly along its lighted surface. The others looked, too. A minute passed. Then, out of the gloom at the further extremity of the vast station, an engine appeared, slowly dragging its burden of carriages and came sighing like a weary giant up the side of the platform. The passengers in the front compartments leapt out and began filing towards the exit.

“Now for it,” muttered Robmore. “Keep back, you two! My men’ll watch him﻿—and whoever’s here to meet him, for he’s expecting somebody.”

Nothing happened for the first minute. The crowd of discharged passengers, men and women, civilians, soldiers, sailors, filed out and went their ways. Gradually it thinned. Then Hetherwick’s arm was suddenly gripped by Rhona for the second time, and he saw that she was staring at something beyond the barrier.

“There!” she exclaimed. “There﻿—the man in the grey coat and fawn hat! That’s the man who drove the car! See! Baseverie sees him!”

===Chinese===

“车快到了，”海瑟维克说。罗娜的手挽在他臂弯里，他觉出那只手在发颤。“是啊，”他俯下身去对她低语，“我也是这般心情。这一刻真叫人屏息。可那边那个恶棍——”

巴斯维里正抬眼望那座大钟。他随即又从大钟转向栅栏后面的站台，满怀期待地沿着灯火通明的站台望去。其他人也在望。一分钟过去了。随后，从这座大车站远端的幽暗处，一台机车现出身来，缓缓拖曳着它那一串车厢，像个困乏的巨人，喘息着驶近站台一侧。前几节车厢里的旅客跳下车来，开始鱼贯走向出口。

“这就见真章了，”罗布莫尔咕哝道，“你们两个退后！我的人会盯住他——还有前来接他的人，因为他是在等什么人。”

头一分钟里什么事也没有。下车的人流，男男女女，平民、士兵、水手，鱼贯而出，各奔前程。人流渐渐稀落。这时，罗娜第二回猛地攥住海瑟维克的手臂，他看见她正死死盯着栅栏那头的什么东西。

“那儿！”她叫道，“那儿——穿灰衣裳、戴浅黄褐色帽子的那个！就是开汽车的那个人！看！巴斯维里瞧见他了！”

===Original===

Hetherwick looked and saw Baseverie lift a hand in recognition of a young, fresh-faced man, who was nearing the ticket-collectors, and who carried in his right hand a small, square parcel. But he saw more. Close behind this young man came Matherfield on one hand and Quigman on the other. They drew closer as he neared the gate, and on its other side the detectives drew closer to Baseverie.

“Now then,” whispered Robmore, and stole swiftly forward.

It was all over so swiftly that neither Hetherwick nor Rhona knew exactly how the thing was done. Before they had realised that the men were trapped, or the gaping bystanders had realised that something was happening under their very noses, Baseverie and his man were two safely handcuffed prisoners in the midst of a little group of silent men who were hurrying both away. Within a moment captors and captives were lost in the outer reaches of the station. Then the two watchers suddenly realised that Matherfield, holding the square parcel in his hand, was standing close by, a grim but highly satisfied smile in his eyes. He held the parcel up before them.

“Very neat, Mr. Hetherwick, very neat indeed!” he said. “Uncommonly neat﻿—eh?”

But Hetherwick knew that he was not referring to the parcel.

===Chinese===

海瑟维克望去，只见巴斯维里抬手示意，回应一个面色清爽的年轻人；那人正走近收票员，右手提着一只方方的小包裹。可他看见的不止这些。紧贴着这年轻人身后，一边是马瑟菲尔德，一边是奎格曼。他越走近栅门，两人贴得越紧；而在栅门另一侧，侦探们也朝巴斯维里步步收紧。

“瞧好吧，”罗布莫尔悄声道，随即疾步抢上前去。

一切了结得如此迅疾，海瑟维克和罗娜都没弄清事情究竟是怎么办成的。他们还没意识到那两个人已落入彀中，张口呆看的围观者也还没意识到眼皮底下正在出事，巴斯维里和他的同伙已经成了两名戴稳手铐的囚徒，被一小队沉默的人夹在当中匆匆带走了。一转眼的工夫，抓人的和被抓的都没入车站外围的人海。这时，两个观望的人才猛然发觉，马瑟菲尔德正站在近旁，手里提着那只方包裹，眼中含着冷峻而极为满意的笑意。他把包裹举到他们面前。

“干净利落，海瑟维克先生，真是干净利落！”他说，“利落得少见——嗯？”

可海瑟维克知道，他说的并不是那只包裹。

## XIII Who Was She? / 十三 她是谁？

===Original===
Late that night, when Hetherwick was thinking things over, a pounding on his stairs and a knock on his outer door heralded the entrance of Matherfield, who, with an expressive look, flung himself into the nearest easy chair.
“For heaven’s sake, Mr. Hetherwick, give me a drop of that whisky!” he exclaimed. “I’m dead beat﻿—and dead disappointed, too! Such a day as I’ve had after that woman! And what it all means the Lord only knows﻿—I don’t!”
Hetherwick helped his evidently far-spent visitor to a whisky and soda, and waited until he had taken a hearty pull at it. Then he resumed his own seat and took up his pipe.
“I gather that you haven’t had a very successful day, Matherfield?” he suggested. “Hope it wasn’t exactly a wild-goose chase?”
“That’s just about what it comes to, then!” exclaimed Matherfield. “Anyway, after taking no end of trouble she got clear away, practically under my very nose! But I’ll tell you all about it; that’s what I dropped in for. When I went out of that house in St. Mary’s Terrace, she was just turning the corner to the right, Bishop’s Road way. Of course I followed. She went over the bridge﻿—the big railway bridge﻿—and at the end turned down to Paddington Station. I concluded then that she was going up by some early morning train. She entered the station by the first-class booking office; I was not so many yards in her rear then. But instead of stopping there and taking a ticket she went right through, crossed the station to the arrival platform and signalled to a taxicab. In another minute she was in it, and off. Very luckily there was another cab close by. I hailed that and told the driver to keep the first cab in sight and follow it to wherever it went. So off we went again, on another pursuit! And it ended at another terminus﻿—Waterloo!”
“Going home, I suppose,” remarked Hetherwick, as Matherfield paused to take up his glass. “You can get to Dorking from Waterloo.”

===Chinese===
深夜时分，海瑟维克（Hetherwick）正把种种情由在心里反复盘算，楼梯上忽然响起一阵咚咚的脚步，外层房门又跟着被敲响——马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）就此进了门。他满脸意味深长的神色，一进门便瘫进了最近的那张安乐椅。
“看在老天爷的份上，海瑟维克先生，给我倒一口您那威士忌吧！”他嚷道，“我快累散架了——还失望透顶！为了盯那个女人，我今天过的叫什么日子哟！这一切到底是怎么回事，只有老天爷知道——我是不知道！”
海瑟维克给这位显然精力耗尽的访客斟了一杯威士忌加苏打，等他痛痛快快灌下一大口，才回到自己的座位上，拿起烟斗。
“我琢磨着，你这一天怕是干得不大顺当吧，马瑟菲尔德？”他试探道，“但愿不至于真落了场徒劳的空追？”
“可不，结果就差不多是那么回事！”马瑟菲尔德嚷道，“总之，我费了九牛二虎之力，她还是跑得干干净净——简直是从我鼻子底下溜掉的！不过我把原原本本都讲给您听；我顺道进来就为这个。我从圣玛丽台地街（St. Mary’s Terrace）那所房子出来时，她正拐过右手的街角，朝比舍普路（Bishop’s Road）那边去。我当然跟上。她过了桥——就是那座大铁路桥——到桥头便拐下去进了帕丁顿车站（Paddington Station）。我那时断定，她是要乘某班清早的火车动身。她从头等售票处那一侧进了站；那时我落在她身后不过几码远。可她没在那儿停下买票，而是一直穿了过去，横穿车站走到到达站台，招手叫了辆出租汽车。转眼间她已坐进车里，走人了。幸亏近旁还停着另一辆车。我招手唤了那辆，吩咐司机盯住头一辆，它开到哪儿就跟到哪儿。于是我们又上路了，又是一趟追击！到末了，追到了另一个终点站——滑铁卢（Waterloo）！”
“我猜，她是回家去吧。”马瑟菲尔德停下来端酒杯的当口，海瑟维克插话道，“从滑铁卢可以去多金（Dorking）。”

===Original===
“She wasn’t going to any Dorking!” answered Matherfield. “I soon found that out. Early as it was, there were a lot of people at Waterloo, and when she went to the ticket office I contrived to be close behind her﻿—close enough, at any rate, to overhear anything she said. She asked for a first single to Southampton.”
“Southampton!” exclaimed Hetherwick. “Um!”
“Southampton!” repeated Matherfield. “First single for Southampton. She took the ticket and walked away, looking neither right nor left; she never glanced at me. Well, as I said yesterday, I don’t believe in starting out on anything unless I go clean through with it. So after a minute’s thought I booked for Southampton﻿—third. Then I went out and looked at the notice board. Southampton, 5:40. It was then 5:25. So I went to the telephone office, rang up our headquarters and told ’em I was after something and they needn’t expect to see me all day. Then I bought a timetable and a newspaper or two at the bookstall, just opening, and went to the train. There were a lot of people travelling by it. The train hadn’t come up to the platform then; when it came down a minute or two later I watched her get in; she was good to spot because of her tall figure. I got into a smoker, a bit lower down, and in due course off we went, me wondering, to tell you the truth, precisely why I was going! But I was going﻿—wherever she went.”
“Even out of the country?” asked Hetherwick, with a smile.
“Aye, I thought of that!” assented Matherfield. “She might be slinging her hook for anything I knew. That made me turn to the steamship news in the paper, and I saw then that the *Tartaric* was due to leave Southampton for New York about two o’clock that very afternoon. Well, there were more improbable things than that she meant to go by it, for reasons of her own, especially if she really is the Mrs. Whittingham of the Sellithwaite affair ten years ago. You see, I thought it out like this﻿—granting she’s Mrs. Whittingham, that was, she’ll be astute enough to know that there’s no time-limit to a criminal prosecution in this country, and that she’s still liable to arrest, prosecution, and conviction; she’d probably know, too, that this Hannaford affair has somehow drawn fresh attention to her little matter, and that she’s in danger. Again, I’d been working out an idea about her and this man Baseverie. How do we know that Baseverie wasn’t an accomplice of hers in that Sellithwaite fraud? In most cases of that sort the woman has an accomplice somewhere in the background﻿—Baseverie may have been mixed with her then. And now he may have information that has led him to warn her to make herself scarce, eh?”
“There’s something in that, Matherfield,” admitted Hetherwick. “Yes﻿—decidedly something.”

===Chinese===
“她才不去什么多金！”马瑟菲尔德答道，“这一点我很快就弄清了。时间尽管早，滑铁卢车站里已经有一大帮人。她朝售票处走去时，我设法紧贴在她身后——至少也近得能把她的话听个一清二楚。她要了一张去南安普顿（Southampton）的单程头等票。”
“南安普顿！”海瑟维克失声道，“唔！”
“南安普顿！”马瑟菲尔德重复道，“去南安普顿的单程头等票。她拿了票便走开，目不斜视；压根儿没朝我瞥一眼。唉，就像我昨天说的，我这人办事，要么不插手，一插手就非干到底不可。于是略一思量，我也买了去南安普顿的票——三等。随后我出去看公告牌：南安普顿 5:40 开车。当时已是 5:25 。我便去电话间给我们总部挂电话，说我正在追一件事情，一整天都别指望见着我。然后在刚开门的书报摊上买了一份时刻表、一两份报纸，就奔列车去了。乘这班车的人不少。那时车还没进站台；一两分钟后车进站，我盯着她上了车；她身量高挑，好认。我进了靠后一点儿的一节吸烟车厢，时候一到，车就开了——跟您说老实话，我一路纳闷，自己究竟为什么要去！可我反正去了——她到哪儿，我到哪儿。”
“哪怕出国也跟？”海瑟维克含笑问道。
“可不是，这我想过！”马瑟菲尔德应道，“谁知道她是不是正要脚底抹油开溜呢。这么一想，我便去翻报纸上的轮船航讯，果然看到*“塔尔塔里克”号*（*Tartaric*）定于当天下午两点来钟从南安普顿启航开往纽约（New York）。唉，要说她会出于自家的盘算搭这条船走，天下比这更离奇的事儿也有的是——尤其是，倘若她真是十年前塞利斯韦特（Sellithwaite）那桩案子里的惠廷厄姆太太（Mrs. Whittingham）的话。您瞧，我是这么盘算的——姑且认定她当年就是惠廷厄姆太太，那她精明得很，必定知道在这个国家，刑事追诉是没有时效的，她照样可能被捕、被起诉、被定罪；她多半也明白，这桩汉纳福德（Hannaford）的案子不知怎么又把人们的新鲜劲儿引到了她那桩旧事上，她正身处险境。再者，关于她和巴斯维里（Baseverie）这个人，我心里也早有一套想法。咱们怎么知道，在那桩塞利斯韦特骗局里，巴斯维里不是她的同伙？这类案子十有八九，女人背后的什么地方总藏着个同伙——巴斯维里当年也许就同她搅在一处。而如今，说不定他得了什么风声，这才给她递话，叫她避避风头，嗯？”
“这话有些道理，马瑟菲尔德。”海瑟维克承认道，“是啊——确实有几分道理。”

===Original===
“There may be a good deal,” affirmed Matherfield. “You see, we’ve let those newspaper chaps have a lot of information. I’m a believer in making use of the Press; it’s a valuable aid sometimes, perhaps generally, but there are other times when you can do too much of it: it’s a sort of giving valuable aid to the enemy. I don’t know whether we haven’t let those reporters know too much in this case. We’ve let ’em know, for instance, about the portrait found in Hannaford’s pocketbook, and about the sealed packet in which, we believe, was the secret of his patent: all that’s been in the papers, though, to be sure, they didn’t make much copy out of it. Still, there was enough for anybody who followed the case closely. Now, supposing that Baseverie was Mrs. Whittingham’s accomplice ten years ago, and that he’d read all this and seen the reproduction of the portrait, wouldn’t he see that she was in some danger and warn her? I think it likely, and I wish we hadn’t been quite so free with our news for those paper chaps. I’m glad, anyhow, that there’s one thing I haven’t told ’em of﻿—that medicine bottle found at Granett’s! There’s nobody but me, you, and the medical men know of that, so far.”
“You think this woman﻿—Lady Riversreade as she is, Mrs. Whittingham as she used to be﻿—was making off to Southampton, and possibly farther, on a hint from Baseverie?” said Hetherwick ruminatively.
“Put it this way,” replied Matherfield. “Of course, you’ve got to assume a lot, but we can’t do without assuming things in this business. Lady Riversreade was formerly Mrs. Whittingham. Mrs. Whittingham did a clever bit of fraud at Sellithwaite, and got away with the swag. Baseverie was her accomplice. Now then, ten years later Mrs. Whittingham has become my Lady Riversreade, a very wealthy woman. She’s suddenly visited by Baseverie at Riversreade Court, and is obviously upset by his first visit. He comes again. Three nights later she’s seen to come out of a club which he frequents. She spends most of the night in a flat in a quiet part of London, and next morning slopes off as early as five o’clock to a port﻿—Southampton. What inference is to be drawn? That her visit to Southampton has certainly something to do with Baseverie’s visits to her and her visit to Vivian’s!”
“I think there’s something in that, too,” said Hetherwick. “But﻿—we’re on the way to Southampton. Go on!”
“Very good train, that,” continued Matherfield. “We got to Southampton just before eight﻿—a minute or two late. I was wanting something to eat and drink by that time, and I was glad to see my lady turn into the refreshment-room as soon as she left her carriage. So did I. I knew she’d never suspect a quiet, ordinary man like me; if she deigned to give me a glance﻿—she’s a very haughty-looking woman, I observed﻿—she’d only take me for a commercial traveller. And we were not so far off each other in that room; she sat at a little table, having some tea and so on: I was at the counter. Of course, I never showed that I was taking any notice of her﻿—but I got in two or three good, comprehensive inspections. Very good-looking, no doubt of it, Mr. Hetherwick﻿—a woman that’s worn well! But of course you’ve seen that for yourself.”
“You must remember that I’ve only seen her twice,” remarked Hetherwick, with a laugh. “Once at Victoria, when Miss Hannaford pointed her out; once night before last, when it was by a poorish gaslight. But I’ll take your word, Matherfield. Well, and what happened next?”

===Chinese===
“道理兴许还多着呢。”马瑟菲尔德断言道，“您知道，我们让那帮报馆的家伙捞去了不少消息。我一向主张利用报界；这有时是宝贵的助力——也许通常都是。可也有些时候，这事儿做过头就坏了：那简直等于给敌方递送宝贵的情报。我说不好，这回我们是不是让那些记者知道得太多了。比方说，汉纳福德记事本里找到的那张画像，还有那只封了口的纸包——我们相信，他专利的秘密就装在里头——这些都让他们知道了：这一切报上全登过。不过，说句实在话，他们没怎么拿它做文章。可对一个步步紧盯着这案子的人，材料已经尽够了。现在，假定巴斯维里十年前就是惠廷厄姆太太的同伙，假定他读到了这一切、见到了那张画像的翻印版，他难道看不出她处境有险，能不去给她递话？我看十有八九会的，我真懊悔，我们不该对那帮报馆家伙把消息给得那么痛快。好歹有一件事我没跟他们吐露——在格拉内特（Granett）那儿找到的那只药瓶！到眼下为止，除了我、您和几位大夫，没有一个人知道这事。”
“你是说，这个女人——如今是里弗斯雷德夫人（Lady Riversreade），从前是惠廷厄姆太太——是得了巴斯维里的暗示，才奔南安普顿逃去，说不定还去更远的地方？”海瑟维克若有所思地说。
“不妨这么看。”马瑟菲尔德答道，“当然，这里头得姑且认定不少东西，可干我们这行，不认定些事情就没法子办事。里弗斯雷德夫人从前就是惠廷厄姆太太。惠廷厄姆太太在塞利斯韦特干了一票利落的骗局，带着赃物脱了身。巴斯维里是她的同伙。那么，十年之后，惠廷厄姆太太成了如今的里弗斯雷德夫人，一位家私万贯的阔太太。巴斯维里突然到里弗斯雷德府（Riversreade Court）拜访她，头一回登门就显然叫她心绪不宁。他又来了第二回。三夜之后，有人瞧见她从他常光顾的一家夜总会里出来。她在伦敦一处僻静地段的公寓里消磨了大半夜，第二天一早五点钟就溜出门，奔一个港口去——南安普顿。由此该得出什么推论？她这趟南安普顿之行，必定同巴斯维里对她的两回拜访、同她那趟维维安（Vivian’s）之行有干系！”
“我看这话也有几分道理。”海瑟维克说，“不过——咱们这就说到南安普顿了。往下讲！”
“那班车真不赖。”马瑟菲尔德接着说，“我们八点差一点儿到了南安普顿——晚点一两分钟。到那会儿我正想吃点什么、喝点什么，所以一见我这位太太一下车就拐进茶点室，心里挺高兴。我也跟了进去。我知道，她绝不会疑心我这样一个不声不响、相貌平常的人；她要是肯赏我一个眼色——我瞧出来了，这是个神情十分倨傲的女人——也只会把我当成个跑码头的旅行推销员。在那间茶点室里，我们俩离得不算远；她坐在一张小桌旁，喝着茶，吃点点心什么的；我在柜台那儿。当然，我从没显出在留意她——可我上上下下、仔仔细细打量了她两三回。很有姿色，这错不了，海瑟维克先生——是位保养得很好的妇人！不过这个，您自然亲眼见识过了。”
“你可得记住，我统共只见过她两回。”海瑟维克笑道，“一回在维多利亚（Victoria），是汉纳福德小姐（Miss Hannaford）把她指给我看的；一回在前天夜里，还是就着一盏不大亮堂的煤气灯。不过你的话我信，马瑟菲尔德。好，接下来呢？”

===Original===
“Oh, she took her time over her tea and toast,” continued Matherfield. “Very leisured in all her movements, I assure you. At last she moved off﻿—of course I followed, casually and carelessly. Now, as you may be aware, Southampton West, where the train set us down, is a bit out of the town, and I expected her to take a cab. But she didn’t; she walked away from the station. So did I﻿—twenty or thirty yards in the rear. She took her time; it seemed to me she was purposely loitering. It struck me at last why﻿—she was waiting until the business offices were open. I was right in that: as soon as the town clocks struck nine she quickened her pace and made a beeline for her objective. And what do you think that was?”
“No idea,” said Hetherwick.
“White Star offices!” answered Matherfield. “Went straight there, and walked straight in! Of course, I waited outside, where she wouldn’t see me when she came out again. She was in there about twenty minutes. When she came out she turned to another part of the town. And near that old gateway, or bar, or whatever it is that stands across the street, I lost her﻿—altogether!”
“Some exceptional reason, I should think, Matherfield,” remarked Hetherwick. “How was it?”
“My own stupid fault!” growled Matherfield. “Took my eye off her in a particularly crowded part﻿—the town was beginning to get very busy. I just happened to let my attention be diverted﻿—and she was gone! At first I made certain she’d gone into some shop. I looked into several﻿—risky as that was﻿—but I couldn’t find her. I hung about; no good. Then I came to the conclusion that she’d turned down one of the side streets or alleys or passages﻿—there were several about there﻿—and got clean away. And after hanging around a bit, and going up one street and down another﻿—a poor job in our business at the best of times and all dependent on mere luck!﻿—I decided to make a bold stroke and be sure of at any rate something.”
“What? How?” asked Hetherwick.

===Chinese===
“哦，她喝着茶、吃着吐司，那叫一个从容。”马瑟菲尔德接着说，“我向您担保，她一举一动都不慌不忙。末了她总算起身走开——我自然跟上，装得随随便便、漫不经心。这个您或许知道，我们下车的地方是南安普顿西站（Southampton West），离镇子还有一截路，我料想她会叫辆车。可她没有；她出了站，步行。我也步行——落后二三十码。她不紧不慢；依我看，她是有意在那儿磨蹭。到后来我忽然醒悟过来这是为什么——她在等各处办事机关开门。这一点我没猜错：镇上的大钟一敲九点，她立刻加快脚步，照直奔她的目标去了。您猜那是什么地方？”
“猜不着。”海瑟维克说。
“白星航运公司（White Star）的办事处！”马瑟菲尔德答道，“照直奔了去，抬脚就走了进去！我自然在外面守着，挑了个她再出来时瞧不见我的地方。她在里头待了约莫二十分钟。出来以后，她转身往镇子另一头去了。就在那座横跨街面的老城门——或者叫卡子，管它究竟是什么——附近，我把她跟丢了——丢了个干净！”
“我看总有什么特别缘故吧，马瑟菲尔德。”海瑟维克说，“究竟是怎么回事？”
“怪我自己蠢！”马瑟菲尔德咕哝道，“在一段格外拥挤的地方，我把眼睛从她身上挪开了——那会儿镇上已经开始热闹起来。我一不留神，心思岔了那么一下——她就没影儿了！起先我咬定她是进了哪家铺子。我探头看了好几家——尽管这事挺冒险——可就是找不见她。我在那一带转悠；白搭。后来我下了结论：她是拐进了哪条小街、小巷或者夹道——那一带这类岔路有好几条——干脆利落地脱身了。又兜了几圈、一条街一条街地跑上跑下之后——干我们这行的，这营生就算在顶顺当的时候也够呛，全凭运气！——我决定放开胆子干一把，至少先把一件事弄个确实。”
“干什么？怎么干？”海瑟维克问。

===Original===
“I thought I’d find out what she’d gone to the White Star offices for,” replied Matherfield. “Of course, I didn’t want to raise any suspicion against her under the circumstances. But I flatter myself I’m a bit of a diplomatist, and I laid my plans. I went in there, got hold of a clerk who was a likely looking chap for secret keeping, told him who I was and showed my credentials, and asked him for the information I wanted. I got it. As luck would have it, my man had attended to her himself and remembered her quite well. Of course, little more than an hour and a half had passed since she’d been in there.”
“And﻿—what had she been in for?” asked Hetherwick. “What did you hear?”
Matherfield nodded significantly.
“Just what I expected to hear,” he answered. “She’d booked a second-class passage for New York in the *Tartaric*, sailing that afternoon, in the name of H. Cunningham. As soon as I found that out, I knew I should come across her again﻿—there’d be no need to go raking the town for her. I ascertained that passengers would be allowed to go aboard from two o’clock; the boat would sail between five and six. So, having once more admonished the clerk to secrecy and given him plausible excuses for my inquisitiveness, I went off to relax a bit, and in due time sat down to an early and comfortable lunch﻿—a man must take his ease now and then, you know, Mr. Hetherwick.”
“Exactly, Matherfield﻿—I quite agree,” said Hetherwick. “But I dare say your brain was at work, all the same, while you ate and drank?”
“It was, sir,” assented Matherfield. “Yes﻿—I made my plans. I wasn’t going to New York, of course; that was out of the question. But I was going to have speech with her. I decided that I’d watch for her coming aboard the *Tartaric*﻿—being alone, she’d probably come early. I proposed to get her aside, accosting her, of course, as Lady Riversreade, tell her who I was and show my papers, and ask her if she would give me any information about a certain Dr. Cyprian Baseverie. I thought I’d see how she took that before asking anything further; if I saw that she was taken aback, confused, and especially if she gave me any prevaricating or elusive answer, I’d ask her straight out if before her marriage to the late Sir John Riversreade she was the Mrs. Whittingham who, some ten years ago, stayed for a time at the White Hart Hotel at Sellithwaite. And I practically made up my mind, too, that if she admitted that and I saw good cause for it, I’d detain her.”

===Chinese===
“我想去查查清楚，她上白星办事处究竟为了什么。”马瑟菲尔德答道，“当然，在那样的情形下，我不想惹出任何对她的疑心。不过我自夸一句，我多少算有点外交手腕，于是定下了步数。我进了那办事处，物色了一个瞧着像能守密的办事员，告诉他我是谁，亮出证件，向他讨我要的消息。我要到了。也是运气，接待她的正是我这个伙计，他把她记得清清楚楚。当然，她从里头出来，前后统共不过一个半小时多一点。”
“那么——她进去做什么？”海瑟维克问，“你听到了什么？”
马瑟菲尔德意味深长地点了点头。
“正是我料想会听见的。”他答道，“她用 H·坎宁安（H. Cunningham）的名字，订了*“塔尔塔里克”号*当天下午启航开往纽约的二等舱位。我一查明这一层，就知道准会再撞见她——犯不着满镇子翻找她了。我打听明白，旅客从两点钟起可以登船；船在五点到六点之间启航。于是我把那办事员又叮嘱了一遍，叫他守口如瓶，又给我这番刨根问底编了几句说得过去的理由，便走开去松泛了一会儿，到时候寻了个地方，早早地、舒舒服服坐下来用了午餐——人哪，总得时不时歇息歇息，您说是吧，海瑟维克先生。”
“正是，马瑟菲尔德——我完全赞成。”海瑟维克说，“不过我敢说，你吃喝的当口，脑子也照样没闲着吧？”
“没闲着，先生。”马瑟菲尔德应道，“是啊——我定下了方案。去纽约，我自然不去；那想都不用想。可我打算同她搭上话。我决定守着，等她上*“塔尔塔里克”号*——她孤身一人，多半来得早。我打算把她引到一旁——搭话时自然称她里弗斯雷德夫人——告诉她我是谁，亮出证件，然后问她，可愿就一位西普里安·巴斯维里医生（Dr. Cyprian Baseverie）向我提供些情况。我想先看看她听了作何反应，再往下问；倘若我见她猝不及防、方寸大乱，尤其是她给了我什么支支吾吾、躲躲闪闪的回答，我就直截了当地问她：嫁给已故的约翰·里弗斯雷德爵士（Sir John Riversreade）以前，她是不是就是那位惠廷厄姆太太——十年前在塞利斯韦特白鹿旅馆（White Hart Hotel）小住过些时日的那个。而且我差不多也拿定了主意：只要她认下这一层，而我又见出充分的原由，我就把她扣下。”

===Original===
“You meant to go as far as that?” exclaimed Hetherwick.
“I did! I should have been justified,” replied Matherfield. “However, that’s neither here nor there, for I never saw her! I was down at the point of departure well before two, and I assured myself that nobody had gone aboard the *Tartaric* up to that time. I kept as sharp a look out as any man with only one pair of eyes could, right away from ten minutes to two until five-and-twenty past five, when the boat sailed, but she never turned up. Of course you’ll say that she must have slipped on unobserved by me, but I’m positive she didn’t. No, sir! It’s my opinion that she thought better of it and didn’t go﻿—forfeiting her passage money, or a part of it, would be nothing to a woman of her means﻿—or that she was frightened at the last minute of showing herself on that stage!”
“Frightened! Why?” asked Hetherwick.
Matherfield laughed significantly.
“There were two or three of our men from Scotland Yard about,” he answered. “I’m not aware of what they were after; I didn’t ask ’em. But I did ask them to give me a hand in looking out for a lady whom I fully described﻿—which is why I’m dead certain she never went aboard. Now, it may have been that she came down there, knew﻿—you never know!﻿—some of those chaps and﻿—made herself scarce! Anyway﻿—I never set eyes on her. Never, in fact, saw her again after I lost her in the morning. So﻿—that’s where I am!”
“You came back﻿—defeated?” remarked Hetherwick.

===Chinese===
“你竟打算做到这一步？”海瑟维克叫道。
“打算！我那样做完全名正言顺。”马瑟菲尔德答道，“不过，这也无关紧要了，因为我压根儿没见着她！两点之前好一阵，我就到了启航的码头，并且确信到那时为止，没有任何人登上过*“塔尔塔里克”号*。从差十分两点，一直守到五点二十五分船启了航，凭我这仅有的两只眼睛，警戒放得任谁都未必比我更严，可她始终没有露面。您当然会说，她准是趁我没留意溜上了船，可我敢肯定她没有。不，先生！依我看，要么是她回心转意，索性不走了——舱位钱打水漂，或者折上一部分，对家资那样阔的女人算不得什么——要么是到了最后一刻，她害怕在那个台面上抛头露面！”
“害怕！为什么怕？”海瑟维克问。
马瑟菲尔德意味深长地笑了。
“码头一带有我们苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的两三个人。”他答道，“他们来干什么，我不清楚；我没问他们。可我确实请他们帮着留意一位女士——她的模样，我原原本本描述了一遍——正因如此，我才死心塌地认定她压根儿没上船。当然啦，也可能是她到了码头，认出了——谁说得准呢！——那几个人里的哪一个，于是——溜之乎也！总之——我再没瞧见她。事实上，打早上把她跟丢以后，我就再没见过她一面。所以——我眼下的处境就是这样！”
“你回来了——败下阵来了？”海瑟维克说。

===Original===
“Well, if you like to call it so,” admitted Matherfield. “Yes, I came back by the seven thirty-eight. Dog tired! But I’m not through with this yet, Mr. Hetherwick, and I want you to do something for me. This Miss Hannaford, now, is down at Riversreade Court. They’ll be on the telephone there, of course. I want you to ring her up early tomorrow morning, and ask her if she can meet you on important private business in Dorking town at noon. Where shall we say?”
“White Horse would do,” suggested Hetherwick.
“Very well﻿—White Horse Hotel, at noon,” agreed Matherfield. “We’ll go down﻿—for I’ll go with you﻿—by the 10:10 from Victoria. Now please be very careful about this, Mr. Hetherwick, when you telephone. Don’t say anything of any reason for going down to Dorking. Don’t on any account mention Lady Riversreade, in any way. Merely tell Miss Hannaford that you have urgent reasons for seeing her. And﻿—fix it up!”
“Oh, I can fix it up all right,” answered Hetherwick. “Miss Hannaford can easily drive down from Riversreade Court. But I don’t know what you want her for.”
“Wait till morning,” replied Matherfield, with a knowing look. “You’ll see. I’ll meet you at Victoria at ten o’clock, sharp.”

===Chinese===
“唉，您愿意这么叫，就这么叫吧。”马瑟菲尔德认了下来，“是的，我乘七点三十八分的车回来的。累得跟条狗似的！可这件事我还没撒手呢，海瑟维克先生，我要您帮我个忙。您瞧，这位汉纳福德小姐眼下正在里弗斯雷德府。那儿自然通着电话。我要您明天一早给她挂个电话，问她能不能为一桩要紧的私事，正午到多金镇上同您见面。地点说在哪儿好？”
“白马旅馆（White Horse）就行。”海瑟维克提议道。
“很好——白马旅馆，正午。”马瑟菲尔德应承下来，“我们一道下去——我也跟您去——乘维多利亚 10:10 的车。喏，打电话的时候，这事请您千万仔细，海瑟维克先生。下多金去的缘由，一个字也别提。无论如何别提里弗斯雷德夫人，怎么个提法都不行。只告诉汉纳福德小姐，您有急事非见她不可。然后——把事儿敲定！”
“哦，敲定不成问题。”海瑟维克答道，“汉纳福德小姐从里弗斯雷德府驾车下来，很方便。可我不知道你要她做什么。”
“等到明早再说吧。”马瑟菲尔德答道，露出一副心中有数的样子，“您到时候自会明白。十点整，我在维多利亚车站候您，准时不误。”

## II Whose Portrait Is This? / 二 这是谁的画像？
===Original===
Malter himself opened the door of his small private hotel; a quiet, reserved man who looked like a retired butler. He was the sort of man who is slow of speech, and he had not replied to Matherfield’s guarded inquiry about Mr. Robert Hannaford when a door in the little hall opened, and a girl appeared, who, hearing the inspector’s question, immediately came forward as if in answer.

Hetherwick recognised this girl. He had seen her only the previous afternoon in Fountain Court, in company with a man whom he knew slightly﻿—Kenthwaite, a fellow-barrister. Kenthwaite, evidently, was doing the honours﻿—showing her round the Temple; Hetherwick, in fact, in passing them, had overheard Kenthwaite telling his companion something of the history of the old houses and courts around them. And the girl had attracted him then. She was a pretty girl, tall, slim, graceful, and in addition to her undoubted charm of face and figure, she looked to have more than an average share of character and intelligence, and was listening to her guide with obvious interest and appreciation. Hetherwick had set her down as being, perhaps, a country cousin of Kenthwaite’s, visiting London, maybe, for the first time. Anyhow, in merely passing her and Kenthwaite he had noticed her so closely that he now recognised her at once; he saw, too, that she recognised him. But there was another matter more pressing than that﻿—and she had gone straight to it.

“Are these gentlemen asking for my grandfather?” she inquired, coming still nearer and glancing from the hotel proprietor to the two callers. “He’s not come in﻿—”

Hetherwick was glad to hear that the dead man was the girl’s grandfather. Certainly it was a close relationship, but, after all, not so close as it might have been. And he was conscious that the inspector was relieved, too.

“We’re asking about Mr. Robert Hannaford,” he said. “Is he your grandfather﻿—ex-Superintendent Hannaford, of Sellithwaite? Just so﻿—well, I’m very sorry to bring bad news about him﻿—”

===Chinese===
马尔特（Malter）亲自来开他那家小私人旅馆的门；此人安静寡言、不苟言笑，看上去像个退了职的老管家。他本是说话慢条斯理的那类人，关于罗伯特·汉纳福德先生（Mr. Robert Hannaford），马瑟菲尔德（Matherfield）那句措辞谨慎的探问还没来得及作答，小门厅里的一扇门便开了，走出一位姑娘来；她听见了探长的问题，当即迎上前来，仿佛正是应声而至。

海瑟维克（Hetherwick）认出了这位姑娘。就在前一天下午，他还在喷泉庭院（Fountain Court）见过她，同她作伴的是个他略识面熟的人——肯思韦特（Kenthwaite），一位出庭律师同行。看那情形，肯思韦特分明是在尽地主之谊——领着她游览内殿（the Temple）；事实上，海瑟维克打他们身边经过时，还听见肯思韦特正对同伴讲这一带古老宅第和庭院的种种掌故。那时候，这姑娘便已叫他心里留意。她生得俊俏，高挑、苗条、仪态优雅，面庞身段自有一段不待言说的动人风致，瞧上去更有一份超乎寻常的性情与才智，正带着显而易见的兴致与领会，听她那位向导讲解。海瑟维克当时暗自揣度，她多半是肯思韦特乡下来的表亲，兴许还是头一回到伦敦。总之，虽说只是同她和肯思韦特错身而过，他那一眼却把她瞧得十分仔细，此刻便一眼认出了她；他也看出，她认出了他。不过眼下另有一桩事情比这更要紧——而她已经径直奔那桩事去了。
